## I / 一

===Original===
The distinguished-looking elderly gentleman introduced himself and, as usual, nobody caught the name. He wore a suit of fashionable and well-cut clothes, however, and his scarf-pin was a single white pearl in a somewhat quaint setting, its dazzling purity recalling the whiteness of a lovely blonde’s shoulders, as Karstens remarked. Moreover, he at once placed a sum of twenty thousand marks upon the table in front of him.

He had been brought to the club by young Hull, the heir to an industrial concern worth millions, into which his father allowed him to dip freely. Play was started immediately, and the stranger courteously agreed to the game proposed, which was vingt-et-un. The stakes were unlimited, and the first one to hold the bank was Ritter.

At first there was nothing unusual about the game. Gains and losses alternated, but soon it was noticed that Hull was losing, and this began just at the time when it was the elderly gentleman’s turn to play banker. At the start it was hundred-mark notes that Hull lost, but he played on calmly, resigned to his ill-luck. Notes of smaller value were now mingled with the piles of thousands the visitor had put in front of him.

It was only outwardly, however, that Hull appeared undisturbed. He felt a good deal of excitement within, and a veil seemed to be obscuring his mental vision. His banknotes fluttered across to the stranger without his appearing aware of the fact. His senses seemed to be imprisoned in a delicate, invisible web, pressing ever more and more closely upon him.

===Chinese===
那位仪表堂堂的老先生自报了家门，可一如往常，谁也没听清那名字。不过，他身上那套西装时髦而剪裁合身，领带别针是一颗单粒白珍珠，镶在一个颇为古雅的托座里，珠光莹洁夺目，恰如卡斯滕斯（Karstens）所言，令人想起可爱金发美人肩头的莹白。再者，他一落座，就把两万马克放到了自己面前的桌上。

把他带进俱乐部的是年轻的赫尔（Hull），一家身家数百万的工业企业的继承人，父亲任由他随意支取那份产业里的钱。牌局当即开局，陌生客人客客气气地应允了众人提议的玩法——二十一点（vingt-et-un）。赌注不设上限，头一个坐庄的是里特尔（Ritter）。

起初牌局并无异样。输赢交替，可不久众人便注意到赫尔在输——而且恰恰是从轮到那位老先生做庄时开始的。起初赫尔输掉的还是一百马克面额的钞票，他不动声色地接着玩，认下了这背运。如今，一张张面额更小的钞票，陆续混进了客人堆在自己面前的千元大钞堆里。

然而，赫尔的镇定只是做给外人看的。他内里其实激荡得很，仿佛有一层薄纱蒙蔽了他的心智。他的钞票一张张飘向那陌生人，他却浑然不觉。他的感官仿佛被囚在一张细密而无形的网里，那网正越勒越紧地箍住他。

===Original===
He drank a brandy and soda, and then ordered a bottle of champagne. The only effect of that was to make him open another compartment of his pocketbook and bring out the thousand-mark notes which he had procured from the bank that morning. His bad luck became really fantastic. Even when he held good cards it seemed as if in some obscure region of his mind a mysterious warning sealed his lips, and instead of staking a substantial sum, he wagered a trifling amount merely.

It was now the visitor’s turn to pass on the office of banker, but he volunteered to continue to hold it on account of Hull. He said: “If you gentlemen do not object, I will remain banker for a few more rounds. You see how the money seems to cling to me. I am the guest of your hospitable club, so please consider how difficult my position is with regard to Herr von Hull, and grant my request.” His speech was polite, and carefully enunciated, yet there was a masterful ring about the words, as if the speaker would brook no refusal.

The club attendant eyed the guest suspiciously, but he was using the cards provided by the club and fresh packs were opened every time. The play grew more animated. A good deal had been drunk, and several round the table were slightly intoxicated. The guest did not refrain from drinking, and his behaviour was in no way peculiar. He had a steady and lingering glance for everyone who looked at him, and his large grey eyes seemed to have something dominating about them, hardly in accordance with a mere game. His hands were large and fleshy, and as steady as if carved out of wood, while the fingers of the other men, far younger than he, were already quivering with excitement.

Hull continued playing, though his pocketbook grew lighter and lighter. “What is the matter with me?” he continually asked himself. He wanted to rise from the table and miss a round, so that he could get a mouthful of fresh air at the window and gain a little calm from looking into the silent night. But he sat as if glued to his chair, pressing his elbows down on the crimson cloth, and his thoughts escaped his control, falling into a void like that of deep slumber.

===Chinese===
他喝了杯白兰地苏打，接着又要了一瓶香槟。而这唯一的效用，是让他打开皮夹的另一格，掏出那天早晨刚从银行取出的千元大钞。他的背运实在离奇到了极点。哪怕手握好牌，也仿佛在他心底某个幽暗的角落里，有道神秘的警告封住了他的嘴，使他不下重注，只押个区区小数。

这时本该轮到客人把庄家之职让出去，他却主动提出，为赫尔的缘故继续坐庄。他说：“各位先生若不反对，我想再坐几轮庄。诸位也看见了，这钱像是黏在我身上似的。我是贵俱乐部盛情相待的客人，请诸位体谅，我面对冯·赫尔先生，处境有多为难，还望应允我的请求。”他言辞客气，吐字一丝不苟，话音里却带着一股不容分说的腔调，仿佛说话之人容不得半点拒绝。

俱乐部的侍者狐疑地打量着这位客人，可他用的正是俱乐部提供的牌，而且每一局都拆的新牌。牌局愈发热烈。酒已喝了不少，围桌而坐的几位都有了三分醉意。客人并不忌酒，举止也无半点怪异。谁朝他望，他便回以沉静而悠长的一瞥；那双灰色大眼睛里似乎有种慑人的东西，与一场寻常牌局颇不相称。他的手宽大多肉，稳得像木头雕成的，而那些比他年轻得多的人，手指早已激动得发颤。

赫尔继续玩着，皮夹却越来越轻。“我这是怎么了？”他一遍遍问自己。他想离席歇上一轮，去窗口吸一口新鲜空气，望望沉静的夜色，讨得一点安宁。可他像被粘在了椅子上，胳膊肘死死抵着深红色的桌布，思绪脱出了他的掌控，坠入一种沉睡般的空虚。

===Original===
And yet he was not really a reckless player. He was accustomed to reflect and to follow the run of luck, making use of chances that were favourable to him, and reducing his stakes when he saw that the odds were against him.

This evening, however, he seemed to know no bounds. No amount seemed of any value in his eyes, and it appeared as if he were almost glad to lose, and saw his notes change hands with a kind of satisfaction. Something would be sure to happen ere long. The players seemed far too slow in dealing, he thought; they took an endless time in declaring their stakes, and the notes crawled round the table at a snail’s pace.

He drank freely, moreover, and the fancies which he could no longer control were like fiery steeds escaping the driver’s restraining hand and running away into a trackless wilderness. The very air seemed to have been exhausted, and nothing existed for him but the game.

Folks began to discuss his bad luck. He certainly drew unlucky cards, but he was playing his hand badly, and taking unreasonable risks. His friends wanted to restrict the stakes and talk of the final round. At first Hull did not take in what they were saying, and they had to explain their words; then he drew himself up and became furiously angry, shouting in his wrath and beating his fist on the table.

===Chinese===
话说回来，他本不是个不顾死活的赌客。他习惯于思量，习惯于追踪运势的走向，形势于己有利时抓住机会，眼看胜算不利便压低赌注。

然而这一晚，他仿佛全无了节制。什么数目在他眼里都不成其为数目，他几乎像是乐于输钱，带着几分快意看自己的钞票易主。总觉得转眼就要出点什么事。他觉得牌手们发牌发得太慢；报个赌注也要磨蹭上无穷无尽的时间，钞票绕着桌子挪动，慢得像蜗牛爬。

再说他喝酒又喝得凶，那些再也管束不住的幻念，就像挣脱了驭手之手的烈马，撒蹄奔进一片没有路径的荒野。空气本身仿佛也被抽空了，除了这场牌局，天地间再没有别的东西存在。

众人开始议论他的背运。他抓的牌确实不济，可他打得也着实糟糕，净冒些全无道理的风险。朋友们想限制赌注，商量着打完这最后一轮便收场。起初赫尔没听懂他们的话，人家只得把话挑明；他随即挺起身子，勃然大怒，怒喝着，一拳一拳砸在桌上。

===Original===
Then the stranger’s big eyes seemed to withdraw a little from him and the rest; their glance appeared to be directed inward and some of their lustre vanished. He laid down his cards and put his money into his pocket, doing it carelessly, however, as if it were merely a handkerchief. There was one more round to finish. Hull called out, “I’ll play the bank,” and the stranger dealt him the cards. He glanced at them quickly. His total was twenty-one. … Then something happened, something strange and inexplicable. He threw his cards face downwards upon the heap, saying, “I have lost again.”

The guest immediately showed *his* cards. His eyes regained their glitter, he counted his points, named the total, and threw his cards down on the table.

It seemed to Hull as if he were falling from an unsteady foothold down into an abyss below. “What have I been doing?” he asked himself in stupefaction and despair. Now at last he began to see everything as clearly as if he had just come into the room: the three glowing electric globes under their protecting dome, the red-covered, lighted table, his friends, the elderly stranger, the scattered cards and the piles of notes.

“Where have I been? What have I been doing?” he stammered.

===Chinese===
就在这时，陌生人的大眼睛似乎从他和其他人身上收了回去；目光转向内里，眼中的几分光彩随之隐去。他放下牌，把钱揣进口袋，动作漫不经心，仿佛揣的不过是一方手帕。还剩最后一轮。赫尔喊道：“我来做庄。”陌生人便给他发牌。他飞快扫了一眼。点数是二十一。……接着，事情发生了——一件离奇而无法解释的事情。他把牌面朝下扔进牌堆，说道：“我又输了。”

客人立刻亮出*他自己的*牌。他双眼重放光采，数了数点数，报出总数，把牌甩在桌上。

赫尔只觉得自己正从一个不稳的立足点上跌下去，坠向脚下的深渊。“我都在干些什么？”他目瞪口呆、绝望地问自己。直到此刻，他终于把一切都看得清清楚楚，仿佛才刚刚走进这间屋子：护罩下三颗炽亮的电灯泡，蒙着红呢、灯光照亮的牌桌，他的朋友们，那位年老的陌生人，散乱的纸牌和一堆堆钞票。

“我方才在哪儿？我都干了些什么？”他结结巴巴地说。

===Original===
His brain grew alert again, and the thoughts that had been so confused and obscure now became suddenly clear: it was as if he had drawn aside the curtains and let in the light of day. Then he felt a sudden distrust of himself, which made him uneasy. He held his head in his hands awhile, striving to free it from the weight that seemed to encircle it, and then raising himself erect, he said, “What have I been doing? I held twenty-one in my hand, and then someone called out, in my voice, ‘I have lost again.’ Look there!” He snatched the cards he had thrown away from the heap where they lay, and turned them over. They were an ace, a ten, and a knave—twenty-one!

The elderly stranger’s large grey eyes contracted until the pupils were quite small and seemed to be gazing at a far-distant spot. A shudder went through his body; it was perceptible, though hastily subdued. Then his breast expanded and his breath came slowly and with difficulty, as if he were having to pump the air direct into himself.

“Too late!” said he, briefly and decisively.

Hull made a slight gesture.

===Chinese===
他的头脑重新警觉起来，先前那般纷乱晦暗的思绪此刻豁然开朗：就像拉开了窗帘，放进了日光。随即他忽然对自己生出一阵不信任，令他心神不宁。他双手抱头坐了片刻，竭力想挣脱那仿佛箍在头上的重物，随后挺直身子，说道：“我都干了些什么？我手里明明握着二十一点，接着却有人用我的嗓音喊了句‘我又输了’。你们瞧！”他一把从牌堆里抓起自己扔掉的牌，翻了过来。一张爱司，一张十，一张杰克——二十一点！

老先生那双灰色大眼睛收缩起来，瞳孔缩得极小，仿佛凝望着极远的一点。一阵战栗掠过他的全身——虽然被飞快压了下去，仍旧可以察觉。随后他胸膛鼓起，呼吸缓慢而艰难，仿佛必须把空气径直压进自己的身体里去。

“太迟了！”他说，简短而决断。

赫尔微微做了个手势。

===Original===
“My remark had nothing to do with you,” he said quietly; “it concerned myself only. How much do I owe you?” he asked in a friendly tone.

“Thirty thousand marks!”

Hull emptied his pocketbook.

“You must content yourself till tomorrow afternoon with ten thousand and, of course, an I.O.U. for the rest. Will you be so good as to write the amount and your address in this notebook?”

===Chinese===
“我那句话与您无关，”他平静地说，“只是说给我自己听的。我该付您多少钱？”他语调友好地问。

“三万马克！”

赫尔把皮夹掏了个空。

“您只能先收下一万，将就到明天下午，余下的自然补一张借据（I.O.U.）。可否劳驾您把数目和地址写进这个小本子？”

===Original===
When Hull got his little notebook back, he read in it:

> **Balling**, 

 Room 15, Excelsior Hotel.

He passed over his I.O.U., smiling pleasantly as he did so.

“I am ready to give you your revenge, Herr von Hull,” said Balling, as he rose. “Gentlemen, may I offer you my thanks for the evening’s hospitality? Good night!”

===Chinese===
赫尔拿回小本子时，看见上面写着：

> **巴林**（Balling），

 埃克塞尔西奥饭店15号房间（Room 15）。

他把借据递了过去，递的时候面带愉快的微笑。

“我随时愿意奉陪，让冯·赫尔先生翻本，”巴林起身说道。“诸位先生，容我为今晚的款待致谢。晚安！”

===Original===
He said this almost abruptly, but in so decisive a tone that it brought the others to their feet. Karstens offered him his car.

“No, thank you; my own is waiting for me.”

He walked away somewhat stiffly, as though tired out, and vouchsafed no further farewell of any sort. The club attendant conducted him to the outer door.

“Hull, you are off your head,” said Karstens, when the stranger had left the room.

===Chinese===
这话说得近乎唐突，语气却如此果决，众人不由得都站了起来。卡斯滕斯提出把自己的车让给他。

“不必了，谢谢；我自己的车在等着。”

他离去时步子有些僵硬，像是筋疲力尽，此外再没做任何道别的表示。俱乐部侍者把他送到大门。

“赫尔，你脑子出毛病了。”陌生人离开房间后，卡斯滕斯说。

===Original===
“What did really happen?” asked Hull quietly.

“Ask your purse!”

“My pocketbook is empty. Who won all my money?”

“Your friend there,” said Karstens, pointing to the door.

“My friend! I never set eyes on him before! How did he get here?”

“Hull, you certainly are needing the services of a good physician. Emil, bring the telephone directory.” Karstens turned over the leaves. “Here we are: Dr. Schramm, Psychopathological treatment, 35, Ludwigstrasse. …”

===Chinese===
“到底出了什么事？”赫尔平静地问。

“问问你的钱包吧！”

“我的皮夹空了。我的钱全叫谁赢去了？”

“你那位朋友呗。”卡斯滕斯指着门说。

“我的朋友！我这辈子从没见过他！他是怎么到这儿来的？”

“赫尔，你确实需要一位好医生来照看了。埃米尔（Emil），把电话簿拿来。”卡斯滕斯哗哗地翻着。“有了：施拉姆医生（Dr. Schramm），精神病理治疗，路德维希大街35号（Ludwigstrasse 35）。……”

===Original===
“I don’t understand your joke, my dear Karstens.”

“Well, who brought this fine vingt-et-un player here but you?”

“That is not true, Karstens.”

“Go to No. 35, Ludwigstrasse, my dear fellow, and quickly too.”

“Of course it was you who brought him, Hull,” said another.

===Chinese===
“我不明白你这个玩笑，亲爱的卡斯滕斯。”

“得了，把这位二十一点好手带到这儿来的，不是你还能是谁？”

“这不是真的，卡斯滕斯。”

“去路德维希大街35号（No. 35）吧，亲爱的朋友，而且要快。”

“当然是您把他带来的，赫尔。”另一个人说。

===Original===
“*I?* *I* brought him? At any rate, I don’t remember a thing about it, but it may be so.”

Hull then withdrew, exhausted and stupefied, brooding over the problem which had so strangely and suddenly opened up before him that evening.

When he awoke, towards morning, he had a dim and fleeting remembrance, and he seemed to recall the stranger sitting at the same table with him in the Café Bastin. He had an idea that they had been talking together, and that it was about the theatre, but what they had said, and which theatre it was about, he had not the slightest idea. In the dim recesses of his mind he recalled merely the sensation of a dazzling reflector that seemed to throw its beams upon him during the conversation. Sleep was no longer possible, but, try as he would to pierce these elusive fragments of memory and penetrate to the reality behind them, he was quite unable to make anything out of them.

===Chinese===
“*我？*是*我*带来的？反正我一点儿也记不起来了，不过也许是吧。”

赫尔随即退了席，精疲力竭，神思恍惚，一路上苦苦思索着当晚如此离奇、如此突兀地在他面前展开的那道难题。

天快亮时他醒来，脑中残留着一点模糊而转瞬即逝的记忆：他仿佛记得，那陌生人曾与他在巴斯廷咖啡馆（Café Bastin）同桌而坐。他隐约觉得两人交谈过，谈的是戏剧，可说了些什么、说的是哪家剧院，他毫无头绪。在心底幽暗的角落里，他只记得交谈时仿佛有一面耀眼的反光镜把光束打在他身上的那种感觉。再睡是睡不成了，可无论他怎样想刺透这些飘忽的记忆碎片、探到它们背后的实情，都全然理不出个所以然来。

---

===Original===
The next afternoon brought him no enlightenment either. By four o’clock he had obtained the twenty thousand marks, and he made his way to the Excelsior Hotel. At his request a telephone message was sent to Room 15. Herr Balling was there, he was told, and requested the gentleman to send up his card. This Hull did, following close upon it.

In the middle of Room 15 he found a man whom he had never seen in his life before. He was a short, stout, clean-shaven man, apparently an American. He made a very stiff bow.

“I beg your pardon,” said Hull. “I must have been directed wrongly. I wanted Room 15.”

“This is it,” said the other.

===Chinese===
第二天下午同样没有给他带来任何启悟。四点钟前后，他筹到了那两万马克，便动身前往埃克塞尔西奥饭店（Excelsior Hotel）。应他的要求，一通电话打到了15号房间（Room 15）。有人告诉他，巴林先生在，并请这位先生先把名片递上来。赫尔照办了，人紧跟着名片上了楼。

在15号房间正中，他见到一个平生从未见过的男人。那人五短身材，体态肥硕，胡子刮得干干净净，看模样是个美国人。他极生硬地鞠了一躬。

“恕我冒昧，”赫尔说，“一定是有人给我指错了路。我要找的是15号房间。”

“这儿就是。”对方说。

===Original===
“Then Herr Balling must have given me the wrong number.”

“My name is Balling.”

“This time I am *not* dreaming, I am in full possession of my senses,” said Hull to himself, and then aloud, he continued: “But the mystery can soon be explained. Did you write this?” and he extended the notebook in which the stranger of the previous night had written his name and address.

“Certainly not,” replied the stout man.

===Chinese===
“那一定是巴林先生把号码写错了。”

“我就叫巴林。”

“这回我*可没有*做梦，我的神智清清楚楚。”赫尔暗自想道，随后又出声接着说：“不过这个谜很快就能解开。这是您写的吗？”说着，他递过那个小本子，昨夜那位陌生人正是在上面写下了姓名和地址。

“当然不是。”胖子答道。

===Original===
“Then I am not in your debt to the tune of twenty thousand marks?”

“My time is very limited, and I am expecting a friend on business,” said the other, looking at his watch.

“I will make way for your friend, sir, at once, and will only put one more question to you. It is not my fault that I am bothering you; I have been misled in some way.”

The other nodded.

===Chinese===
“那么，我并不欠您两万马克的债了？”

“我的时间十分有限，正等一位朋友来谈生意。”对方说着看了看表。

“我这就给您的朋友让位，先生，只想再问您一个问题。打扰了您并非我的过错，我是在什么地方被人引入了歧途。”

对方点了点头。

===Original===
“Possibly you are acquainted,” Hull went on, “with a gentleman of about sixty, with large grey eyes, a big nose and white whiskers. He wears good and well-cut clothes and a tall grey hat, and his name is also Balling.”

“I can only repeat that I know nothing about him,” said the Balling of No. 15.

Hull thereupon took his leave. Downstairs he asked whether there were a second Herr Balling in the hotel, but the answer was “No.” Had Room 15 been occupied by any Herr Balling who had just left? “No.” Was the writing in the pocketbook known? Again there was a negative reply. “For the first time in my life,” thought Hull, “I find myself unable to pay a debt of honour.”

Gradually he became uneasy. What a mysterious affair this was! Nothing of the kind had ever occurred before. He had won money and lost it again … sometimes much, and at other times little. He had been in financial straits. He had had some trouble about a girl he cared for. Once, indeed, he had been seriously wounded in a duel. Yet all that was comprehensible and straightforward, so to speak. But this tale of Herr Balling and the twenty thousand marks had some mystery or other behind it. He had forgotten that it was he who introduced the stranger to the club. He had played as if he had lost his head. He had incurred a debt of twenty thousand marks, and his creditor had furnished a name and address which did actually exist but were not his, and, moreover, he would not have the money. …

===Chinese===
“您或许认识，”赫尔接着说，“一位六十岁上下的先生，灰色大眼睛，大鼻子，白色络腮胡。他衣着考究合身，戴一顶灰色大礼帽，名字也叫巴林。”

“我只能再重复一遍，我对此人一无所知。”15号房间（Room 15）的这位巴林说。

赫尔随即告辞。到了楼下，他问旅馆里是否还住着第二位巴林先生，回答是“没有”。15号房间可曾住过什么刚刚离开的巴林先生？“没有。”皮夹里那笔迹可有人认得？回答依旧是否定的。“我这辈子头一回，”赫尔想，“竟然还不起一笔赌债了。”

他渐渐不安起来。这是何等蹊跷的一桩事！这样的事从未有过。他赢过钱，又输掉过……有时多，有时少。他手头拮据过。他为一个心爱的姑娘惹过麻烦。他甚至有一次在决斗中身受重伤。可那些事都还说得明白、来得直截了当。而这个巴林先生和两万马克的故事，背后却藏着某种谜。他忘了，把那陌生人引进俱乐部的正是他自己。他打得像丢了魂。他欠下两万马克的债，而债主留下的姓名地址虽确有其人其地，却不是他本人的，何况，他还拿不出这笔钱来。……

===Original===
If it had not happened that Hull had no mistress at the moment, he could have talked this affair over. He pondered over it alone while he walked along Lenbach Square and the Promenade, looking everybody in the face in the hope that he might encounter the distinguished stranger among them. He went to the Café Bastin and scanned all the faces there. He sat down at a table and waited to see whether the *genius loci* would be favourable to him and recall the vanished recollections; but nothing came of it, and he stood up again, a prey to increasing uneasiness. It seemed as if in the invisible depths behind him another power, extraneous to himself, was pursuing him, pressing down upon him, trying to jump on his back as a monkey might do, and lead him into unlucky adventures of some kind or another.

Hull forced himself to return to his lonely bachelor chambers. There he met Karstens, and greeted him with relief. But Karstens at once asked:

“Well, has your memory returned?”

“My dear fellow, there’s something wrong with me!”

===Chinese===
倘不是赫尔眼下没有情妇，这本是可以找人细谈的事。他独自一人边走边琢磨，沿着伦巴赫广场（Lenbach Square）和散步大道一路走去，端详每一张迎面的脸，盼着能在人丛里撞见那位气度不凡的陌生人。他去了巴斯廷咖啡馆，把那里的面孔一张张扫过。他在一张桌旁坐下，等着看此地的守护灵（genius loci）是否会眷顾他，唤回那些消逝的记忆；结果一无所获，只得重新站起身来，愈发忐忑难安。仿佛在他身后看不见的深处，有另一种外在于他的力量在追逐他、压迫他，想像猴子那样蹿上他的背脊，领着他走向这样或那样的厄运。

赫尔强迫自己回到冷清的单身寓所。他在那儿遇见了卡斯滕斯，如释重负地向他招呼。卡斯滕斯却当即问道：

“怎么样，记性回来了吗？”

“亲爱的朋友，我这人出了毛病！”

===Original===
“With the twenty thousand marks?”

“No, there they are!” and he tapped his breast-pocket. “Nobody wants them, it appears. There is a Herr Balling in Room 15 at the Excelsior, but he isn’t my man, and we’ve never met before. He has never played vingt-et-un, and nobody owes *him* twenty thousand marks. I can’t get rid of the money, and it makes me feel creepy! Something is going to happen to me. Who *is* there near me whom I cannot see? There’s certainly something wrong with me!”

“Come to the club! Perhaps your Herr Balling will go there to fetch the money himself.”

“Yes, but what about the real Balling in No. 15?”

===Chinese===
“两万马克出了毛病？”

“不，钱在这儿呢！”他拍了拍胸前的口袋。“看样子没人想要它们。埃克塞尔西奥饭店15号房间（Room 15）是有一位巴林先生，可他不是我要找的那个人，我们素未谋面。他从没玩过二十一点，也没人欠*他*两万马克。这笔钱我脱不了手，弄得我心里发毛！我身上要出事了。我近旁到底有谁，是我看不见的？我这人肯定出了什么毛病！”

“去俱乐部吧！没准儿你那位巴林先生会亲自上那儿取钱去。”

“是啊，可15号房间里那个真正的巴林，又怎么说？”

===Original===
“Well, that’s certainly odd, I grant you. Come along.”

“All right. Perhaps he’ll be there.”

In the club that night there was no play. The curious circumstance had so worked upon the members’ imagination that no one felt the need of trying his luck. Hull was overwhelmed with well-meaning or obtuse advice.

“Emil,” said one of them to the attendant, “what did his car look like?”

===Chinese===
“唔，那确实古怪，我承认。走吧。”

“好吧。也许他会在那儿。”

那天夜里，俱乐部没有开赌。这桩蹊跷事把会员们的想象力撩拨得厉害，谁也提不起碰运气的兴致。赫尔被种种好心或是迟钝的忠告淹没了。

“埃米尔，”其中一个对侍者说，“他的车是什么模样？”

===Original===
“An excellent one, Herr Baron, a twenty-horsepower at the least—a closed car with a body like a royal cradle, if one may use such a comparison nowadays … so smooth and well rounded and polished. It started off with a great bound, and soon vanished. It was a first-class car. I kept a close eye on the gentleman, and I saw that he had the devil’s own luck when he played against Herr von Hull. He played quite straight, however.”

They learnt nothing more about the stranger. Nobody came either to the club or to Hull’s rooms to ask for the twenty thousand marks or to offer him his revenge.

A few days afterwards Hull made acquaintance with a girl who was performing jazz dances in the Bonbonnière. She was partly Mexican, she told him. She soon effected a diversion in his thoughts, and in her company he rapidly got rid of the twenty thousand marks which he could not pay over to the stranger.

“It seems as if you were meant to give the money to a woman instead of to a man,” remarked Karstens, when he told him that he was now free of his worries once more.

===Chinese===
“好车一辆，男爵先生，少说也有二十马力——一辆厢式轿车，车身像只御用的摇篮，如今还兴不兴这么打比方，且不管它……那么圆润流畅，打磨得锃亮。它猛地一蹿便起了步，转眼就没了影。那是辆头等的好车。我眼睛一刻没离开那位先生，看得出来，他跟冯·赫尔先生对阵时，运气好得邪门。不过他打得规规矩矩，一点手脚也没做。”

关于那陌生人，他们再没有打听到任何消息。无论俱乐部还是赫尔的住处，都没有人来讨那两万马克，也没有人来向他讨一场翻本局。

几天之后，赫尔结识了一位在邦博尼埃尔（Bonbonnière）表演爵士舞的姑娘。她告诉他，她身上有一部分墨西哥血统。她很快便把他的心思引向了别处；有她作伴，那笔付不出去的两万马克，也飞快地从他手里散尽了。

“看来这笔钱哪，命里注定是该交给一个女人，而不是一个男人。”卡斯滕斯听他说自己重又没了烦忧时，如此评论道。

## II / 二

===Original===
About a fortnight later the circles to whom the life of the day is only a wearisome burden till the hour of play arrives, when the nerve-tension is once more excited, were all agog with the stories of a stranger who simply loaded himself with money wherever he chanced to play. The tales varied constantly. At one time the stranger was a young sportsman, at another a worthy provincial; now he was a fair-bearded man looking like an artist, and again a robber and murderer who had escaped from justice. Some said he was a dethroned prince, others that he was a Frenchman. Another time they declared him to be a citizen of Leipzig, who was smuggling pit-coal from the Saar into Bavaria by way of Switzerland, or profiteering on the money exchange with New York and Rio de Janeiro. There was endless variety in the descriptions, but the imagination put the various forms together and made one personality out of them.

Circles that were exclusive had ceased to exist. Money was a key that opened all doors, the wearing of a fur coat could conceal any calling, and a diamond scarf-pin shed lustre on any character. A man could go into whatsoever company he desired.

There was no longer any sense of security, and the mysterious gambler might turn up in any place, at any time. He might be anybody’s neighbour. The authorities were constantly notified of swindling players, and though in no case could their swindles be proved, their luck was so continuous that it did not seem possible for it to be due to ordinary play.

===Chinese===
大约两个星期之后，那些把白昼的光阴只当作难耐重负、唯有等到赌局开场、神经再度绷紧之时方才觉得活着的人，全都沸沸扬扬地传说着一个陌生人的故事——此人无论碰巧在哪里上场，都能大把大把地把钱往身上揽。传闻层出不穷，说法随时在变：一会儿说这陌生人是位年轻的花花公子，一会儿又说他是个体面的外省人；忽而说他是个蓄着浅色胡须、貌似艺术家的人，忽而又说是个逃脱了法网的强盗杀人犯。有人说他是位被废黜的亲王，也有人说他是法国人。还有一回，人们又断言他是莱比锡的一个市民，正取道瑞士把萨尔的坑煤走私进巴伐利亚，再不然就是在同纽约和里约热内卢的货币套汇中大发横财。种种描述千变万化，没有穷尽，然而人们的想象力却把这形形色色的模样拼合起来，捏成了同一个人。

昔日壁垒森严的排他圈子已经不复存在。金钱成了一把能打开一切门户的钥匙，一件皮大衣可以遮掩任何营生，一枚钻石领带夹能替任何品性增光。一个人想跻身什么样的交际场，就能跻身什么样的交际场。

再没有丝毫安全感可言，那个神秘的赌客随时随地都可能出现。他说不定就是任何一个人的邻座。当局不断接到关于出千赌客的举报，虽然没有一起作弊行为得到证实，可那些人的好运如此连绵不断，实在不像是寻常赌技所能解释的。

===Original===
Through the Bonbonnière lady Hull frequently spent his evenings in places where gambling was indulged in. He heard much about this swindler at play, and from many different quarters, for theatrical folk are always particularly interested in anything out of the common, especially where masquerading is concerned. But Hull’s brain was of a matter-of-fact and ordinary kind. He did, indeed, still think about the twenty thousand marks, but mostly with the comfortable reflection that they had been used in a very different way from that for which they were destined. Now that the story of his forgetfulness had ceased to haunt him he had become quite convinced that his friends had played an elaborate trick upon him, that his I.O.U. and the twenty thousand marks had been discharged, and the only disreputable part in the affair had been played by Balling, who, on account of Emil’s watch upon him, had not felt himself secure. His astonishment was all the greater, therefore, when a certain Herr von Wenk was announced and the story of that night’s escapade was brought up once more.

Hull refused to discuss the matter, but the visitor told him he was a State Attorney and showed his credentials. In the most polite way possible he continued to question him, saying that his official status obliged him to pursue the inquiry. Had Hull been able to communicate with Cara Carozza, his *chère amie* from the Bonbonnière, instead of having to face this man by himself, he would have known what to say and how much to conceal. He was greatly enamoured of Cara Carozza, and by no means inclined to go into this matter and rake up bygones for the sake of the country’s morals.

===Chinese===
托那位邦博尼埃尔（Bonbonnière）女郎的福，赫尔（Hull）常在纵情聚赌的场所消磨他的夜晚。关于这个赌桌上的骗子，他从四面八方听到了许多传闻，因为戏剧界人士对一切超乎寻常的事向来格外热衷，何况又牵涉到假面乔装。只是赫尔的头脑务实而平庸。那两万马克他确实还惦记着，不过多半带着一种心安理得的回味：这笔钱已经派上了同原本用途截然不同的用场。如今健忘一事既已不再纠缠他，他早已深信不疑：那是朋友们精心设计的一场恶作剧，他的借据和那两万马克早已两讫，整件事里唯一不光彩的角色是巴林（Balling），此人因为被埃米尔（Emil）盯着，感到自身难保。因此，当一位冯·文克先生（Herr von Wenk）登门求见、那晚荒唐事的旧账被重新翻出来时，他就愈发大吃一惊了。

赫尔拒绝谈论此事，但来客亮明了自己的检察官身份，并出示了证件。他以尽可能客气的方式继续盘问，说是自己的公职身份令他不得不将调查进行下去。倘若赫尔当时能先同他在邦博尼埃尔的那位亲密女友（*chère amie*）卡拉·卡罗扎（Cara Carozza）通个气，而不是独自面对这个人，他本该知道该说什么、该隐瞒多少。他深爱着卡拉·卡罗扎，绝不肯为了国家的风化去翻旧账。

===Original===
“You will pardon my introducing a personal note, but I understand that you are very intimate with Mdlle. Cara Carozza, of the Bonbonnière?”

“Good Lord! He knows that, does he?” ejaculated Hull to himself.

“Can you make me acquainted with this lady? It would further the task which the State has laid upon me, but I would ask you to introduce me to her as a private individual. It is unnecessary to assure you that I take you for a man of irreproachable character and quite above suspicion. Nothing is known to the detriment of the lady, either. You will be able to render a service to the country and perhaps to yourself as well. Henceforward you are under the direct protection of the police. Do not be uneasy; it is possibly quite an unnecessary precaution. You can rest assured that you will not suffer in any way through the services you may be able to render to the general public and the State.”

“What am I to gather from all this, sir?” said Hull hesitatingly.

“You must have come to some conclusion about your extraordinarily lucky opponent?”

===Chinese===
“请恕我夹带一点私人话题——据我所知，您同邦博尼埃尔的卡拉·卡罗扎小姐过从甚密？”

“天哪！这个他都知道？”赫尔在心里失声惊呼。

“您能把我引荐给这位女士吗？这将有助于国家交付给我的任务。不过，我想请您以私人身份介绍我同她认识。用不着我向您保证，我把您看作一个品行无可挑剔、全然不受怀疑的人。对这位女士，也没有任何有损名誉的记载。您将能为国家效劳，说不定也能为您自己效一份劳。从今往后，您处于警察的直接保护之下。不必不安，这多半完全是一种多余的防范。请您放心，无论您能为公众和国家出什么力，您都不会因此受到任何损害。”

“那么，我该从这些话里得出什么结论呢，先生？”赫尔迟疑地说。

“对于您那位运气好得离谱的对手，您总归有过某种看法吧？”

===Original===
“To be quite candid, I did feel uneasy for a time, Herr von Wenk. There seemed to be something very mysterious about the affair. Finally, I imagined that my forgetting that I had brought the stranger to the club was a feeble joke on the part of my friends.”

“But the Herr Balling in the hotel, who was quite different from the Balling at the club?”

“That certainly is a mystery to me still, but a false address is often given for the purpose of evading payment. In this case, however, it occurred in order to avoid receiving twenty thousand marks.”

“May it not be explained,” continued the State Attorney, “by the fact that this elderly gentleman had been cheating in some way? He was set on his guard by some fact unknown to you, and contented himself with the money he had already won. He gave a name which occurred to him, and of which he had some knowledge. Unless, of course, the Balling in the Excelsior was the Balling from your club, disguised. But you say that the one was short and stout and the other of rather imposing presence. Do you still play, Herr von Hull?”

===Chinese===
“坦白说，有一阵子我心里确实不踏实，冯·文克先生。这件事似乎透着十足的蹊跷。到后来我想，我竟忘了是自己把那陌生人带进俱乐部去的，这不过是朋友们开的一个无伤大雅的玩笑。”

“可是旅馆里那位巴林先生，同俱乐部里的巴林判若两人，这又怎么说？”

“这在我至今确实还是个谜。不过人们常常留假地址来躲债。只是这一回，留假地址却是为了不去收取两万马克。”

“可不可以用这样的事实来解释，”检察官接着说，“这位上了年纪的先生当时正在用什么手法出千？某件您不知道的事情叫他起了戒心，于是他见好就收，满足了已经赢到手的那笔钱。他随口报出一个名字，一个自己略知底细的名字。当然，除非埃克塞尔西奥（Excelsior）饭店里的那个巴林，就是您俱乐部那个巴林乔装改扮的。可您说过，一个又矮又胖，另一个却仪表堂堂。您如今还赌吗，冯·赫尔先生？”

===Original===
“A little, now and again.”

“With Mdlle. Carozza, perhaps? I am on friendly terms with one of your intimates, with Karstens. He will introduce me to you, and we shall be able to renew our acquaintance socially. You must not be prejudiced by the fact that it has had an official beginning. I hope to be able to count you on my side.”

The barrister took his leave, and returned to his official chambers.

===Chinese===
“偶尔还玩两把。”

“多半是同卡罗扎小姐一起吧？我同您的一位至交卡斯滕斯（Karstens）交情不错。他会引荐我们认识，往后我们便能在社交场合重续这份交情。您不必因为事情带着公务色彩的开头而心存芥蒂。我希望能把您算作我这一边的人。”

这位检察官起身告辞，回他的官署去了。

===Original===
A month previous to this occurrence, in a lawsuit in which he was professionally engaged, Wenk had first noted the extent to which the gambling fever possessed the city. He himself liked the nervous excitement and the appeal to the imagination afforded by the relations between judge, counsel and accused in the course of his calling. In earlier years he had been a regular cardplayer. He was not a passionate lover of games of chance, but he enjoyed the opportunity of testing the effect of play upon his own self-control, of observing his fellows and noting the enticement afforded by the devious course of luck.

During the lawsuit above mentioned he realized what a danger to the people lay in gambling. The change from war conditions to a state of affairs which afforded the nation little relief from tension had not sobered its imagination, but rather excited it yet more strongly. Perhaps, in the first instance, the war news was largely responsible for extravagant fantasies. For a week, sometimes a month, at a time the reports were like a lottery for the whole nation. Then a fateful movement was set on foot by which whole districts of people were seized with a passion for gambling, a movement designed by the military authorities to induce them to replete the army coffers. Increased wages were offered to the war workers and money was flung into manufacturing concerns. Commerce of all kinds was affected ere long, and everywhere the floodgates were opened. When goods grew more and more scarce, money overflowed all its channels. Wenk saw clearly that the folks in high places who had believed they could purchase the soul of a nation for money were to blame for the tragic outcome of the war as far as Germany was concerned, and so, too, were they responsible for the political development. Instead of the ideal of an immortal soul prepared for any and every renunciation as long as it fulfilled its duty to the community, they had set up an idol—money—and the whole nation was worshipping it.

Then the war came to an end. Money decreased in value and the idea of it played a yet more dominant part in the life of a nation now deprived of its success and brilliance in the world outside. Hundreds of thousands had become accustomed to a life of inaction, and for many years now it had been nothing but pure chance whether they lived or died. Their only preoccupation had been to exercise authority over others and to live entirely on their nerves. They brought with them to the more stable conditions of life the gambling spirit born of their war experience. They had grown accustomed to taking risks, and they continued to rely on luck. They resumed their former mode of life, but brought to it the atmosphere of their recent experiences, transferring the nerve-racking and hazardous existence of those days to the conditions which now obtained. To some extent this was inevitable, but those who looked beyond the present and wanted to see a new era of prosperity dawn must strain every nerve and exercise the strictest self-denial. Thus only could there be hope of recovery.

===Chinese===
在此事发生的一个月前，文克（Wenk）在一起因职务经手的诉讼案里，头一回注意到赌热已把这座城市攫取得多么深。他本人就喜欢自己这一行中法官、律师与被告之间那种关系所带来的神经亢奋和对想象的撩拨。早年间，他是个牌桌上的常客。他并非酷爱碰运气的赌戏，而是乐于借牌局检验赌博对自己自制力的作用，乐于观察同侪，留意运道那迂回莫测的走势散发出怎样的诱惑。

在上述诉讼案的过程中，他认识到赌博对人民是怎样的一种危险。从战争状态转入一个几乎没能让国民的紧张得到些许缓解的时局，并未使国民的头脑冷静下来，反倒把它的想象力刺激得愈发亢奋。或许首先该在很大程度上归咎于战时的新闻：一度一连一星期、有时一连一个月，战报对整个民族来说就像一张彩票。随后，一场命运攸关的运动开场了：整片整片地区的居民被赌博的狂热攫住，这场运动出自军方的谋划，为的是诱使民众去填满军队的钱柜。战时工人拿到了加薪，金钱被大把抛进制造企业。形形色色的商业不久都受到波及，各处的闸门纷纷洞开。当货物越来越稀缺，金钱便漫过了一切河道。文克清楚地看到，那些自以为能用金钱买下一个民族灵魂的庙堂诸公，对德国战争结局的悲惨负有罪责，对政治的演变也同样难辞其咎。他们不曾树立这样的理想——一个不朽的灵魂，只要能对共同体尽到义务，便随时准备作出任何舍弃——而是竖起了一尊偶像——金钱——于是举国上下都对它顶礼膜拜。

后来，战争结束了。金钱贬值，而对于一个失去了外部世界的成功与光彩的民族，金钱这个观念在生活中所占的支配地位反倒愈发凸显。几十万人已过惯了游手好闲的日子，何况多年来，他们的生死存亡本就全凭纯粹的运气。他们唯一的心事，就是对他人行使权威，全凭神经过活。他们把战争经历中孕育出的赌性，带回了较为安稳的生活境况。他们已习惯于冒险，便继续仰仗运气。他们重操旧日的生活方式，却给它注入了近事的气息，把那些岁月里熬人心神、吉凶难卜的生存状态，移植到眼下的境况中来。这在某种程度上势所必然；但凡想放眼将来、盼一个新的繁荣时代破晓的人，都必须绷紧每一根神经，实行最严格的自我节制。唯有如此，才有康复之望。

===Original===
The great lawsuit had afforded Wenk one example after another of the development of this spirit of gambling, and in its course had taken him frequently into the company of those who lived but for, and by, games of chance. His convictions were well grounded and his recognition of the national danger constantly confirmed to an alarming extent. In the attics and basements folks were gambling for five-mark pieces, and on the first floors for five thousand. They laid their wagers in the streets and the lanes, at home and abroad. They gambled with cards, with goods, with ideas and with enjoyments, with power and with weakness, with themselves and with their nearest and dearest.

At this period, too, people who were not naturally given to hazardous risks, who were habitually calm and self-reliant, were wont to be guided by chance conditions and circumstances, instead of combating them where necessary.

Wenk was an official who had reached his thirty-eighth year in a peaceful and well-ordered career. During the war he had volunteered for the Flying Corps, because he had a love of sport and remembered the fascination which the element of danger had held for him in early youth. The experience had fired his imagination, and he returned to his career with more impetuous feelings than had been his when he quitted it. The lawsuit against the gamblers, and all he had learned in the course of it, had excited him considerably. He had gone at once to the head of the Police Department, had described what he had seen and experienced, and represented to him that this new disease must be combated if the whole body were not to be destroyed. As money lost its value and the necessities of life increased, the nation could do nothing but seek to augment its mass of paper currency by trying first one speculation and then another. The connection between supply and demand required both time and work before it could become normal again, and so by degrees it had come about that the pulsations of commercial life were regulated merely by chance.

===Chinese===
那场大诉讼给文克送来一个又一个这种赌性滋长的实例，办案过程还常常让他与那帮为赌而生、以赌为业的人周旋。他的信念根底扎实，而他对民族危殆的认识又一再得到印证，其程度令人心惊。阁楼里、地窖里，人们为几个五马克的硬币赌得天昏地暗；一楼厅堂里，一注便是五千。大街小巷，国内国外，处处有人下注。他们赌纸牌，赌货物，赌主意，赌享乐；赌权势，也赌软弱；押上自己，也押上至亲骨肉。

也正是在这个时期，连那些天性不近冒险、素来沉稳自恃的人，也惯于听凭偶然的境遇和情势摆布，而不在必要之处奋起抗争了。

文克是个官员，年届三十八，仕途平顺安稳。战争期间他志愿加入了航空兵部队，因为他素来喜好冒险的运动，又记得少年时代危险因素对自己曾有怎样的魅惑。这段经历点燃了他的想象，重返仕途时，他的心绪比当初离职时更为激越。那桩控告赌徒的案子，以及办案过程中他了解到的一切，都令他大为振奋。他当即去见警察总署的长官，讲述了自己的所见所历，并向他申明：这种新疾病若不与之斗争，整个躯体必将溃烂。随着货币贬值、生活必需品涨价，国民无计可施，只好指望靠一桩接一桩的投机，去增多手里的纸币。供求之间的联系需要时间和劳作才能恢复正常，于是渐渐地，商业生活的脉搏竟全凭偶然来调节了。

===Original===
The Minister smiled; he was new to his office. He said, “The nation is sound enough; you are a pessimist!”

But Wenk replied, “It is diseased and rotten! How can it be healthy, after such years and such a life?”

Then the Minister, who felt his position somewhat insecure and was willing to try anything that might lead to stability, yielded the point, and created a new post, which Wenk at once took over.

===Chinese===
部长微微一笑；他到任未久。他说：“这个民族健康得很，您是个悲观主义者！”

文克却答道：“它已经病了、烂了！经过那样的岁月、那样的生活，它还怎么可能健康？”

这位部长自觉地位未稳，凡有可能带来稳定的办法都愿意一试，于是让了步，新设了一个职位，文克当即接掌。

===Original===
The erstwhile State Attorney and official was at once caught up in the vortex of his new office. He devoted all his time and energies to it. He did not establish himself in an armchair in a comfortable well-furnished room, but began to build up his position from the very bottom, became a police-spy and a detective, unwearied in his efforts to collect all the evidence he could lay his hands on. He did it all himself, and when he realized, as he soon did, the slight extent of his own powers when pitted against the widespread national vice, he conceived the idea of recruiting a guard and rallying force from the ranks of the victims.

Accordingly, he began with men whose wealth was not displayed in their houses, but who, through their connection with the social order which had come to grief, had been forced into the opposition, both as human beings and as politicians. He knew that none were more responsible for the existing state of affairs than these men, because, at a time when resistance was a necessity, they had been cowardly and kept out of the way. But he knew, too, that in them a new force of decision had come to birth, that they longed to make good where they had failed.

Above all, there were the rich young men without any profession. In the disorganization brought about in the country by the depreciation and disorder of the currency, they were unable to carry on life as before. Their society was permeated by the “new rich,” who made use of them because they allowed themselves to be made use of.

The State Attorney von Wenk had turned to his whilom comrades, from whom the divers duties of his office had long separated him, and the man whom he had first encountered and won over to his side was Karstens. It was from him that he had learned all the circumstances of Hull’s strange and suspicious gambling adventure. He compared Hull’s story with the other material which he had hastily collected. Fresh complaints were constantly being made about swindlers who worked so cleverly that no taint of suspicion could attach to them, yet who won so consistently that it was not conceivable that this could be merely luck. From some similarities in detail in the various stories Wenk was inclined to refer all these cases to a band of swindlers operating in concert, and he even had the idea that it might all be the work of one man. But this was hardly more than an impression. Hull’s experience was the strangest and most mysterious of all these cases, and it was fraught with the greatest danger, but Wenk had a notion that therein lay the solution to all the rest.

===Chinese===
这位昔日的检察官和官员，一脚踏进了新职务的漩涡。他把全部时间和精力都投入其中。他没有安坐在陈设考究的舒适房间里的扶手椅上，而是从最底层着手经营自己的位置，亲自做起了警探和侦探，不知疲倦地搜罗一切能到手的证据。事必躬亲；而当他——这为时不久——意识到自己的力量同这场遍布全国的恶习相较何其单薄时，他萌生了一个念头：从受害者的队伍里，招募一支护卫和集结的力量。

于是，他先从这样一些人着手：他们的财富并不显露在宅邸门面上，却因同那个已然倾覆的社会秩序有牵连，无论作为人还是作为政界人物，都被逼进了反对派一边。他知道，对眼下这种局面，没有人比这些人更难辞其咎，因为在必须抵抗的时刻，他们怯懦地退避了。但他也知道，一种新的决断力量已在他们身上萌生，他们渴望在跌倒过的地方弥补前愆。

尤其是那些没有任何职业的富家子弟。在这个因货币贬值和紊乱而陷于失序的国度里，他们无法照旧度日。他们的交际场被“暴发户”渗透，后者利用他们，正因为他们甘愿被利用。

检察官冯·文克转向了昔日的一班故交——种种职务早已使他同他们疏远——而他头一个遇上并争取过来的便是卡斯滕斯。他正是从卡斯滕斯那里，得知了赫尔那场离奇而可疑的赌博冒险的全部始末。他把赫尔的叙述同自己仓促搜集来的其他材料加以比对。新的控告不断递上来，控告那些手法巧妙得叫人抓不到一丝疑点、赢钱却又稳定得令人无法相信只是运气的骗子。从种种叙述在细节上的某些相似之处，文克倾向于把所有这些案子归为一个协同作案的诈骗团伙，他甚至闪过一个念头：这一切说不定统统出自一人之手。不过这几乎只是一种印象罢了。在所有这些案子里，赫尔的经历最离奇、最神秘，凶险也最大，然而文克有一种预感：其余一切案子的谜底，恰恰藏在这桩案子里。

---

===Original===
After Wenk’s departure, Hull held a long argument with himself. The uncompromising yet thoroughly courteous way in which Wenk had effected an entrance had made an impression upon him. He guessed what the official desired, for he himself was often dissatisfied with his way of living, although his love of ease usually made him drive such thoughts away.

Under ordinary circumstances he would have pursued his usual search for enjoyment without restraint or reflection, either until considerations of health had set a limit to his dissipations or until a marriage, either arranged or entered into voluntarily, had caused him to “range himself.”

Hull by no means approved of the course of affairs in Germany which had led to the Treaty of Versailles. He at once asked himself, “Where were you in 1918, when the retreat began? and earlier still, when it first began to be planned? Are not you, Hull, and all your kind, responsible for it?” … That was what Herr von Wenk’s words had implied.

But Hull found in himself no trace of such individuality as might have saved the situation, and he dismissed these ideas from his mind. He drove to see Cara Carozza and told her of Wenk’s visit.

===Chinese===
文克走后，赫尔在心里同自己长久地辩论。文克那种寸步不让却又彬彬有礼的登门方式给他留下了印象。他猜得出这位官员想要什么，因为他自己也时常不满于自己的生活方式，只是贪图安逸的习性总让他把这类念头驱开。

换在寻常时世，他会照旧毫无节制、不假思索地追逐享乐，直到健康方面的考虑给他的放浪划定界限，或者一桩婚事——无论是安排的还是自愿的——叫他“安顿”下来。

对于德国步步走向《凡尔赛条约》的那些作为，赫尔绝谈不上赞成。他随即扪心自问：“1918 年大撤退开始的时候，你在哪里？更早些，撤退刚刚开始筹划的时候，你又在哪儿？赫尔，难道不正是你，还有你们这一类人，对此负有责任吗？”……冯·文克先生那番话的含意，正是如此。

但赫尔在自己身上找不出半点足以力挽狂澜的个性，便把这些念头从心里打发掉了。他驱车去见卡拉·卡罗扎，把文克来访的事告诉了她。

===Original===
“For God’s sake, don’t get us mixed up with your State Attorney, dear Eddie,” said she.

“But … but … do we cheat? Are we dishonest? Are we profiteers, or climbers? We merely keep ourselves going. What are you thinking about, darling?”

“Eddie, a game of cards in full swing—someone holding the bank—closed doors, and a State official looking on! That might prove a hanging matter!”

“But I promised him I would bring you!”

“More fool you!” she exclaimed. “You ought to have got out of it somehow. Elsie is bringing her friend today, and we are going to Schramm’s. Karstens has already telephoned that he will be there.”

“Then Wenk would be coming anyhow, so *that’s* all right, as it happens!”

===Chinese===
“看在上帝份上，别让你那位检察官把我们搅和进去，亲爱的埃迪（Eddie）。”她说。

“可是……可是……难道我们作弊了吗？我们不讲信义吗？我们是发横财的投机商，还是攀高枝的钻营小人？我们不过是糊口度日罢了。你在胡思乱想些什么呀，宝贝？”

“埃迪，牌局正酣——有人坐庄——门扉紧闭，旁边还站着个国家官员看着！这可是够得上绞刑的事！”

“可我答应过他，要带你去见他！”

“那你更是傻瓜！”她嚷道。“你本该想个法子推托掉。埃尔西（Elsie）今天要带她的朋友来，我们要上施拉姆（Schramm）餐馆去。卡斯滕斯已经来过电话，说他也会到场。”

“那文克横竖也要去的，这么一来，倒正凑巧！”

===Original===
The headwaiter of Schramm’s little restaurant, recently opened in one of the residential streets and decorated throughout in most eccentric style by a modern professional, led Karstens and Wenk from the dinner-table to a box at the rear. Thence a winding stair led to a room which had no other exit and seemed to have no windows of any kind.

In the middle of the room stood a fairly large table of an oval shape, but so arranged that every occupant of an armchair was sitting in a hollowed-out niche of his own, with the leaves of the table under his elbows on both sides. The table was formed of quaint, curiously veined Kiefersfeld marble. In the middle only was there a perfectly white oval left. Around the table, behind the players’ chairs, the floor was raised and the walls furnished with full-length reclining lounges, upon which rested crushed-strawberry-coloured cushions with black designs. A large shade of polished glass attached to a brass electrolier hung low over the table and reflected the electric light bulbs which gleamed forth from silver brackets. The walls above the strawberry-coloured cushions were inlaid with the same warm marble as that on the table.

Wenk was introduced to Cara Carozza.

“I could not keep the secret, Herr von Wenk. I was obliged to tell my lady friend here. Please don’t be vexed with me!”

===Chinese===
施拉姆的小餐馆新近在一条住宅街开张，里里外外由一位时髦的行家以极为怪诞的风格装潢一过。领班侍者把卡斯滕斯和文克从餐座引到后面的一个包厢，从那里，一道盘旋的楼梯通向一个房间——它别无出口，看上去也不见任何样式的窗户。

房间正中摆着一张相当大的椭圆形桌子，构造却颇为特别：每把扶手椅上的坐客都各自陷在一个凹龛里，桌子的活动翻板正好垫在他两侧的肘下。桌子由纹理奇诡的基弗斯费尔德（Kiefersfeld）大理石制成，唯独正中留出一方纯白的椭圆。桌子四周、赌客座椅的后面，地面垫高了一层，墙上装着齐身长的卧榻，榻上陈放着带黑色纹样的碎莓红色软垫。一盏黄铜组合吊灯上装的大抛光玻璃灯罩低低悬在桌面上方，映照着自银质灯托中绽亮的电灯泡。莓红色软垫上方的墙壁上，镶着与桌面同一种暖色的大理石。

文克被引见给了卡拉·卡罗扎。

“我没能守住这个秘密，冯·文克先生。我不得不告诉了这位女朋友。请您别见怪！”

===Original===
Wenk gave a slight bow, in which there was a trace of annoyance.

Baccarat was being played. Karstens turned to Wenk: “The young man with the fair beard is the only stranger. All the others play here regularly.”

Wenk glanced at the stranger and met his eyes. He noticed that they were fastened on him, and he immediately looked beyond and above them, but he felt that the stranger had noticed they were speaking of him. Whenever he looked at him again he found that his eyes were fixed on the table.

The stranger played a quiet, restrained game. He frequently lost. Then Wenk ceased to pay attention to him and turned to the others, whom he watched in turns. They all had their eyes fastened on the white oval, whereon the cards were being dealt. They seldom looked in any other direction. There were gentlemen in evening dress, ladies in *décolletée*, expensively and fashionably attired. The passion for gambling had seized and carried them all away.

===Chinese===
文克微微一躬，躬身之间透着一丝不快。

玩的是巴卡拉（baccarat）。卡斯滕斯转向文克：“那个浅色胡须的年轻人是唯一的外人，其余各位都是这里的常客。”

文克瞥了那陌生人一眼，正与对方的目光相接。他察觉那双眼睛正定在自己身上，便立刻把视线越过他们，投向更高更远处，但他感觉到，陌生人已经发觉他们在谈论自己。此后每当他再看过去，总见那双眼睛只顾盯着桌面。

那陌生人打得安静而克制，输牌是常事。于是文克不再留意他，转而观察其余的人，轮流打量。他们个个把目光钉在那方白色椭圆上，牌正一张张发向那里。他们很少朝别的方向看。在场的是些穿燕尾服的先生、身着袒胸夜礼裙（*décolletée*）的女士，衣饰华贵入时。赌的狂热把他们统统攫住，席卷而去。

===Original===
“It is none of these,” said Wenk to himself, “so it can only be that young man with the sandy beard.”

He began to study him afresh, but only to find that the latter returned his gaze. Wenk then turned his attention to Cara Carozza. He saw her wholly given over to her game, sitting next to Hull, to whose money she helped herself when she lost. If she won, however, she added the winnings to her own heap. In the player on her other side Wenk thought he recognized a well-known tenor from the State Theatre, whose picture often appeared in shopwindows.

“Is that Marker?” he asked Karstens, who nodded in reply.

Wenk won a trifling sum. He played only till he had persuaded himself that there was no work for him here. Then he gave up his place to an elderly gentleman who had already been sitting behind him for some time, boring him by remarks upon his method of play. He seated himself on one of the lounges and watched the play for a short time longer. Then he took his departure, Karstens accompanying him. Hull remained with the Carozza girl.

===Chinese===
“要找的不在这些人之列，”文克暗自思忖，“那就只能是那个沙色胡须的年轻人了。”

他重新端详起此人，却发现对方正回望着他。文克于是把注意力转向卡拉·卡罗扎。他见她整个人沉在牌局里，坐在赫尔身旁，输了便从赫尔的钱里自行取用；赢了，却把彩头添进自己那一摞。在她另一侧的赌客身上，文克觉得认出了一位国家剧院的名角男高音，此人的照片常出现在商店橱窗里。

“那位是马尔克（Marker）吗？”他问卡斯滕斯，后者点头作答。

文克赢了一小笔。他只玩到自己确信在此无事可办为止，随后便把座位让给一位老先生——此人早在他身后坐了半晌，对他的打法评头论足，令他生厌。他在一张卧榻上落座，又看了一阵牌局，然后起身告辞，卡斯滕斯陪同前往。赫尔留在了卡罗扎姑娘身边。

===Original===
When Wenk had descended a few steps he looked back at the table. It seemed as if the fair-bearded man with large mouse-grey eyes followed his departure eagerly, and then directed an urgent and threatening look towards Carozza, but it might have been only an illusion.

When Wenk reached the foot of the stairs, he unexpectedly found himself for a moment face to face with a lady who had already laid her hand on the balustrade to ascend. He looked right into her eyes and started back in amazement, while he inclined his head, as if doing homage, before he passed on. He wanted to say to Karstens, “I have never seen so beautiful a woman!” but that seemed to him like betraying a secret, and, consumed with desire, he bore her image with him as he made his way silently through the deserted streets. When at home, he soon fell asleep, but the two mouse-grey eyes, which were far older than the carefully arranged sandy beard, seemed to fasten on his breast as he slept. They appeared to be trying to colour the ace of hearts with his own lifeblood.

When he awoke next morning he was conscious of nothing but an intense longing to meet once more the lady he had encountered on the stairs.

===Chinese===
文克下了几级楼梯，回头望向牌桌。那个生着一双大而呈鼠灰色的眼睛的浅色胡须男子，似乎正急切地目送他离去，随后又朝卡罗扎投去急迫而威吓的一瞥——不过，这也可能只是错觉。

文克走到楼梯脚下时，冷不防与一位女士打了个照面，她的手已经搭上扶栏，正要上楼。他直直地望进她的眼睛，惊愕得向后一仰，随即俯首致意，仿佛在行臣服之礼，然后才继续前行。他想对卡斯滕斯说：“我从未见过这般美丽的女人！”可这话在他看来无异于泄露一个秘密；他被欲望煎熬着，把她的形象揣在身旁，默默穿过空寂无人的街道。到家之后，他很快入睡，可那双鼠灰色的眼睛——那双眼远比梳理得一丝不苟的沙色胡须苍老——仿佛在他熟睡时牢牢盯住他的胸口，似乎正想用他自己的心头血，去染红那张红桃爱司。

第二天早晨醒来，他心中只剩下一件事，一个强烈的渴望：再见一见楼梯上遇见的那位女士。

## III / 三

===Original===
The next night Wenk was invited to a *soirée musicale* in the neighbourhood of Schramm’s restaurant. A young pianist was performing modern fantasies. Wenk was bored, became fidgety and was the prey to wandering thoughts. It seemed to him as if he were neglecting some special opportunity elsewhere. He grew so uneasy that he finally slipped away, merely leaving a card of apology for his hostess.

He reached Schramm’s and was about to pass quickly by. Then it occurred to him to look up at the first floor of the villa where the new restaurant and gaming-house was established, and try to see the windows of the little room in which he had played the previous night. The ground-floor windows were large, and through their old-gold curtains a faint light gleamed, but the four windows on the first floor showed no signs of occupation. Yet he said to himself, “Behind those unlighted windows there gleams light﻿ ﻿… *her* light,” and he went in, full of hope that he might encounter the mysterious lady who had so bewitched him.

The headwaiter approached him at once, took hat and coat, whispering, “The marble table?” and looking closely at the visitor as he did so. Wenk gave a nod of assent, and the headwaiter rapidly preceded him to the back, Wenk following more leisurely. Then he was led up the winding stair.

===Chinese===
第二天夜里，文克应邀到施拉姆餐馆附近一处宅邸参加一场音乐晚会。一位年轻的钢琴师正在演奏现代幻想曲。文克听得乏味，渐渐坐立不安起来，心思四处游荡。他总觉得，自己正在别处错失什么特别的机会。他越来越心神不宁，终于悄悄溜走，只给女主人留下了一张致歉的名片。

他来到施拉姆餐馆门前，本想快步走过。转念之间，他抬起头去望那幢开设着新餐馆兼赌场的小楼二层，想找一找昨夜他赌钱的那间小屋的窗户。底层的窗子很大，古金色的窗帘后面透出微弱的光亮，而二层的四扇窗子却不见丝毫有人光顾的迹象。可他心里却对自己说：“那几扇没有灯光的窗户后面正闪着光……是她的光。”于是他走了进去，满心盼望能遇见那位令他如此痴迷的神秘女子。

侍者领班立刻迎上前来，接过他的帽子和外套，低声问道：“还是大理石桌那间？”说这话时，他一边将来客细细打量了一番。文克点头应允，领班快步在前引路往屋子后面去，文克不紧不慢地跟着。随后他被引上了那道盘旋的楼梯。

===Original===
The first person he saw at the gaming-table was the sandy-bearded man. He sat in his niche, his broad shoulders bent forward, with his eyes fixed in a steady gaze upon a player opposite. His attitude was that of a beast of prey who has already played his victim and is only waiting to pounce upon him. He seemed to be all sinews﻿—at least, that was the impression he made upon Wenk, who started back at his aspect.

There was one empty seat. Wenk took it and drew out his pocketbook. An idea crossed his mind, that something special had occurred at the table. He saw all the players cowering over the little heaps of money in front of them, and yet there was in them all a distinct, even if unintentional, glance given to one among their number.

The sandy-bearded stranger was holding the bank, and now he looked up. Wenk noticed how, at first, annoyed at the disturbance, he raised his eyes towards him, and then it was clearly noticeable that his face quivered. At the same moment, however, he closed his jaws so firmly that his beard stood out round them. The rest was a mere impression, but this Wenk saw clearly. A shudder went through him as if at some sudden and dangerous encounter. At this moment the “banker” displayed the cards he held. Someone said, “Basch has lost again!” All turned to look openly at the pale thin man whom they had been furtively regarding when Wenk entered.

With a quiet and drowsy movement Basch pushed the notes lying on the oval in front of him over to the stranger. He grabbed at them like a bird of prey. The loser sank back in his seat, and in the same slow and dreamy way he brought out a fresh thousand-mark note and laid it in front of him.

===Chinese===
他在赌桌旁看到的第一个人，便是那个蓄着沙色胡须的男人。他坐在自己的凹龛里，宽厚的肩膀前倾，目光一动不动地盯住对面的一名赌客。那副姿态，活像一头已经把猎物戏耍够了、只等扑上去撕咬的猛兽。他看上去浑身筋腱虬结——至少，他给文克的印象是如此，文克一见他这副模样，不由得倒退了半步。

桌上还有一个空位。文克坐下来，掏出钱夹。一个念头掠过脑海：这张桌子上发生过什么特别的事情。他看见所有赌客都伏在自己面前一小堆一小堆的钱上，然而他们每一个人又都朝着自己人中间的某一位，投去分明可见、却又出于无心的一瞥。

那沙色胡须的陌生人正坐着庄，此刻他抬起了眼。文克注意到，他起初是怎样因这场搅扰而恼火地朝自己抬眼，随后又可以清楚地看出，他的脸抽搐了一下。但与此同时，他把下颌咬得死紧，胡须都从腮边绷了出来。其余的不过是一种感觉，唯独这一点文克看得真切。一阵寒战穿过他的全身，仿佛猝然撞见什么危险人物似的。就在这时，“庄家”亮出了手中的牌。有人说：“巴施又输了！”众人这才公然转过头去，打量那个脸色苍白的瘦削男人——文克进门时，他们一直在偷偷打量的正是此人。

巴施用一个安静而困倦的动作，把摆在面前椭圆上的钞票推给了那陌生人。那人像猛禽一般一把攫了过去。输家瘫回座位，又以同样迟缓、梦游一般的姿态摸出一张新的一千马克钞票，摆在自己面前。

===Original===
“How much are you losing now?” said a lady from the divan behind Basch. “You will have a lucky life. When one loses to *that* extent! I regard you as a champion. You must establish a record.﻿ ﻿… In losing, you know! Then you will be so lucky in life that I shall want to.﻿ ﻿…” She broke off in embarrassment. Then Wenk, with a delicious tremor in his veins, recognized the speaker as the lady whom he had so abruptly encountered on the stairs on the previous evening.

“Get ready to stake,” said the sandy-bearded man in a harsh voice, drowning the speaker’s concluding words.

Basch had not answered her. As the banker called out, he merely made a movement of his hand over his thousand-mark note, a movement as if he were secretly conjuring it to do his bidding.

He looked at his cards; it was his turn, and no one else was punting.

“Do you take one?” said the banker sharply.

===Chinese===
“您到现在输了多少啦？”巴施身后长榻上一位女士开口道，“您会有一辈子好运的。一个人能输到*这种*地步！我把您看作冠军。您该创下一项纪录才好。……输钱的纪录，您知道！到时候您一生的运气会好得叫我都想……”她难为情地收住了话头。这时，文克血管里涌过一阵甜美的震颤——他听出来了，说话的正是昨晚他在楼梯上仓促撞见的那位女士。

“准备下注。”沙色胡须的男人粗声说道，把她的尾音整个淹没了。

巴施没有答她的话。庄家喊话时，他只把手在那张一千马克的钞票上抹了一下，那动作仿佛在暗暗念咒，要这张钞票听命于他。

他看了看自己的牌；轮到他了，别人都没有下注。

“要不要牌？”庄家厉声问道。

===Original===
Basch shook his head dreamily. Wenk noticed Cara Carozza’s auburn-tressed head behind one the spectators, but his glance always returned to the other woman.

The banker bought a Court card and disclosed his own hand. He had only a total of four. Basch, too, with a feverish movement, laid his on the table. His points were but three.

“He plays as if he were drugged!” whispered Wenk’s neighbour. “To hold three, and yet not take a card! What folly!”

As he raked in his gains the sandy-bearded stranger gave a hasty glance at Wenk. The latter felt himself pitted against the winner. He increased his stakes, won, then lost for several rounds, and won again.

Basch continued to lose every time. By degrees Wenk ranged himself more and more on his side. He staked his money as if it were a weapon for Basch against the stranger, a weapon with which to strike him down.

===Chinese===
巴施恍恍惚惚地摇了摇头。文克瞥见卡拉·卡罗扎那颗红褐色的脑袋在一名看客身后探着，可他的目光总是不由自主地回到另一位女士身上。

庄家补了一张牌，是张花牌，随即亮出自己的牌——总共只有四点。巴施也带着一阵狂乱急促的动作，把自己的牌摊到桌上。他的点数不过三点。

“他打牌的样子像中了麻醉药似的！”文克的邻座低声说，“手里拿着三点，居然还不肯要牌！多蠢啊！”

沙色胡须的陌生人把赢来的钱耙拢过去时，匆匆瞥了文克一眼。文克觉得自己已同这位赢家较上了劲。他加大了赌注，赢了，接着连输几局，又赢了回来。

巴施依旧局局皆输。渐渐地，文克不知不觉把自己越来越多地摆到了他那一边。他把钱押上去，仿佛那是替巴施投向陌生人的武器，一件要将对方击倒在地的武器。

===Original===
Wenk noticed that the latter looked at no one but himself and Basch. He therefore accepted the challenge, and threw himself eagerly and wholeheartedly into the struggle, impelled by some mysterious power that incited him against the banker. He forgot himself altogether, and no longer played for the purpose of observing and discovering. He abandoned himself to the game and played like all those whom he had come to rescue from the gaming-table. He even forgot the lovely lady. When he first realized this, he was ashamed, and for the first time during the evening he glanced round the room to see whether Hull were there.

But it was not Hull who now sat behind Cara Carozza. Wenk’s search was vain; Hull was not present. Cara sat with a stranger behind a player with whom she was sharing the stakes. Then Wenk came to himself. He stopped playing and at once left the hall, sorely vexed with himself. When he was on the winding stair he turned and saw that the stranger with the fair sandy beard was also rising from the table.

Wenk had ordered his car to call for him at the house where the musical party was held, and did not remember this till he had walked some distance. Then he retraced his steps and drove home. He went to bed at once, but he could get no sleep, for the thought continually recurred that he had made a mistake to come away, that he ought to have stayed and talked to Basch.

===Chinese===
文克注意到，对方除了自己同巴施之外，谁也不看。于是他应下了这场挑战，把全副身心扑进这场搏斗之中，被一股神秘的、挑动他与庄家为敌的力量驱使着。他完全忘乎所以，早已不是为了观察和查探才下注。他沉溺在赌戏里，打得同那些他本要把他们从赌桌前拯救出来的人一模一样。他甚至连那位可爱的女士也忘掉了。待他初次意识到这一点时，不禁羞愧起来，一晚上头一回环顾室内，看看赫尔在不在。

可如今坐在卡拉·卡罗扎身后的并不是赫尔。文克白找了一阵；赫尔不在场。卡拉和一个陌生人坐在一位赌客背后，同那人合股下注。这时文克才猛然醒悟。他收手不赌，立刻离开了大厅，对自己懊恼不已。走上盘旋楼梯时，他回头一望，看见那淡黄沙色胡须的陌生人也正从桌边起身。

文克原是吩咐了自己的车到举办音乐晚会的那户人家去接他，走出好一段路才想起这茬。于是他折返回去，坐车回了家。他立刻上了床，却怎么也睡不着，心里翻来覆去只有一个念头：自己不该离开，早该留下来同巴施谈谈。

===Original===
He got out of bed again and went through a bundle of depositions in order to quiet his conscience. In going through these documents, written by men who were strangers to him, he got the impression that all of them, losing so much that they could not but ascribe it to foul play, must have sat at the gaming-table very much as Basch did. Had he remained and behaved in a sensible fashion, he would have had an opportunity of seeing for himself at first hand what had hitherto reached him through the testimony of others.

Then Wenk became thoroughly discouraged. “I must set to work in quite another way,” he said to himself. “Goodwill and industry are not sufficient. Self-denial and inexorable self-discipline and a little more cunning are necessary! I must make use of every ruse that my opponent displays.﻿ ﻿… I must make use of disguise and secret spying. I must be prepared to stake myself on the game﻿ ﻿… must be myself the snare, if I do not want to be caught in it like a silly pigeon.﻿ ﻿… A State official with a false beard﻿ ﻿… a Browning concealed in his fist﻿ ﻿… a jockey-cap, a tall hat, a wig, and so on, like the cinema stage.﻿ ﻿…”

In the looking-glass he contemplated his clean-shaven face, finding that when he made grimaces, drew down the corners of his mouth, stretched his jaws, and tried the effect of a beard made out of paper shavings, his features lent themselves very well to disguise.

===Chinese===
他又下了床，翻检一叠证词，好让良心安定下来。读着这些出自素不相识者之手的文书，他得到一个印象：这些人个个输得那么惨，除了归咎于骗局之外别无解释，他们当年坐在赌桌前的模样，必定同巴施相差无几。倘若他留下来，行事得体，本可以亲眼获得第一手的见闻，而不必像至今这样，只凭他人的证词了解一切。

随后文克彻底灰了心。“我得换一套完全不同的干法。”他对自己说，“光凭好心与勤勉是不够的。还需要克己，需要铁面无私的自律，还要再多一点狡猾！对手使出的每一招，我都要拿来为己所用。……我必须借助化装改扮和暗中刺探。我得准备把自己押上赌局……我自己就得成为那只罗网，免得反倒像只蠢鸽子似的落进网里。……贴着假胡子的国家官员……拳头里暗攥一支勃朗宁……骑师帽、大礼帽、假发套，诸如此类，就像电影舞台上那一套。……”

他对着镜子端详自己刮得干干净净的脸，发现无论是做怪相、把嘴角往下扯、伸张下颌，还是试垫上一把纸屑做的假须看效果，他这张脸都很适合化装易容。

===Original===
The next day he procured a complete outfit from the Criminal Investigation Department. With the help of a Secret Service expert, he tried all the necessary arts, learned to plaster on a beard, to alter his complexion, make himself look younger or older, change his appearance by scars, and so on. He could now make up as a country cousin, a dispatch-bearing cyclist, a taxicab driver, a porter, waiter, steward, window-cleaner, an “unemployed,” and other characters. In the morning he made an exhaustive examination of the criminal museum which the police had collected, studied the photographs he found there, returned to his various makeups, and worked with the zeal of a fanatic.

Thus the day passed, and by evening he felt he had become a stronger man. He was at once more discreet and yet more daring. He would have liked to make a tour at once of all the gaming-houses in the city.

He went only to Schramm’s, however. He had long been considering whether he should not appear there in some sort of disguise, more for the purpose of making a trial of it and learning to feel at home in it than for actually starting upon his work. He was still more anxious to go in the hope of meeting the sandy-bearded man again and seeing him play, for he was desirous of atoning for his shortcomings of the previous evening, which had left a painful impression upon his mind. He would have liked to meet Basch again and talk to him about the evils of gambling, from which he had suffered so much. He went, therefore, just as he was.

===Chinese===
第二天，他从刑事调查部弄来了一整套行头。在一位特工专家的帮助下，他把种种必要的技艺演练了一遍，学会了粘贴胡须、改变面色，让自己显得年轻些或苍老些，用疤痕变换相貌，等等。如今他已能化装成乡下亲戚、送递公文的骑车信使、出租汽车司机、搬运工、侍者、管事、擦窗工、“失业汉”以及其他种种角色。上午，他把警方收藏的犯罪博物馆仔细察看了一遍，研究了那里存档的照片，回过头又继续摆弄他的各色化装，干得像狂热的信徒一般起劲。

这一天就这样过去了。到了晚上，他觉得自己成了一个更强大的人。他变得愈加审慎，却又愈加大胆。他恨不得立刻把全城的赌场都巡视一遍。

不过，他只去了施拉姆餐馆。他早就盘算着要不要化点装再去，倒不是为了正式着手办事，主要是想先试一试手段，练到穿戴自如。他更急于前往，还是盼望再见一见那个沙色胡须的男人，看他打牌，因为前一晚的失误在他心头留下了痛楚的印象，他一心想加以弥补。他也愿意见到巴施，同他谈谈赌博的祸害——巴施已为此吃了这么多苦头。于是他未加任何化装，就这么去了。

===Original===
It was already late when he got there. Hull was present, but he saw neither the fair-bearded stranger nor Basch. He only heard that the former had left immediately after him, a fact which all had noticed. After he left, Basch had remained sitting as if utterly prostrate. He had not played again, and suddenly he vanished. No one knew him well. He had never been to Schramm’s before.

The lady who sat behind him estimated that his losses must have been thirty to thirty-five thousand marks. The blond stranger had won it all, but he did not win until he began to hold the bank. Everything had been absolutely in order. The attendant who furnished the cards was thoroughly reliable.

While talking about the previous night’s play they stopped their game. Then Cara said:

“There are people who are born players, and if they take only one card in their hands it is sure to be an ace. They can do what they like; the power is stronger than they are; it is their guiding spirit, their God.”

===Chinese===
他到那里时已经很晚了。赫尔在座，但他既没有见到那淡色胡须的陌生人，也没有见到巴施。他只听说，前者紧跟着他之后便离了场——这事所有人都注意到了。他走后，巴施还一直呆坐着，仿佛整个人都垮了。他没有再赌，随后便突然不见了。没有人同他熟识。他从前从未到过施拉姆餐馆。

坐在他身后的一位女士估计，他输掉的总有三万到三万五千马克。这笔钱全教那金发的陌生人赢了去，不过那人是直到坐庄才开始赢的。一切都规规矩矩，毫无破绽。供牌的侍役是绝对可靠的。

众人谈论着前一夜的牌局，牌也停了下来。这时卡拉说道：

“有的人是天生的赌徒，哪怕手里只抓起一张牌，那也准是一张爱司。他们想怎样就怎样；那股力量比他们自己还强大；那是他们的引路神灵，是他们的上帝。”

===Original===
But Elsie did not agree with her. She thought that every player once in his life came upon a series of lucky days. They lay stretched out before him, handed to him by his good fairy, for every man had a good fairy. One must not give up expecting to meet with those times of good fortune, for one day one could gather in the winnings as quickly as ripe apples in the autumn.﻿ ﻿…

No one knew the man with the sandy beard. Basch had brought him to Schramm’s, and the first evening they had gone away together. He might be a dethroned prince, he was so imperious and abrupt in his speech. A dethroned prince in want of money, no doubt.

“I have a strange feeling,” said Hull, “as if I had already played against him once.﻿ ﻿…”

“Nonsense!” said Cara.

In his mind the fancy grew stronger. “It is not so much that I have played with him, but as if he had done me some very serious internal injury, affecting my very blood; but how, and when, and where, I have no idea. It must have been in a dream.”

===Chinese===
埃尔西却不同意她的话。她认为，每个赌徒一生之中总会碰上一连串走运的日子。那些日子在他面前铺展开来，是他的善良仙女亲手递到他手里的，因为每个人都有自己的善良仙女。人不该放弃遇上那种好运时光的指望，因为终有一天，你会把赢来的钱财像秋天的熟苹果一样，飞快地一一收进筐里。……

没有人认识那个沙色胡须的男人。是巴施把他带到施拉姆餐馆来的，头一晚两人还是一道走的。他说不定是位被废黜的亲王，言谈那样专横峻急。多半是位手头拮据的废黜亲王吧。

“我有一种奇怪的感觉，”赫尔说，“仿佛从前已经同他交过一次手。……”

“胡说！”卡拉道。

他心里这个念头却愈发强烈了。“倒不像是同他赌过牌，倒像他把我内里伤得很重，伤到了我的血；可是怎么伤的、什么时候、在哪儿，我全然不知。一定是在梦里。”

===Original===
“He has evil eyes,” said a woman’s voice, which Wenk seemed to recognize. He looked in that direction, but with the bright light on the table the corner seemed as dark as a cave and he could descry no one.

Cara answered the voice in the darkness in a tone that seemed to have anger in it: “Evil eyes! What do you mean by that? Surely at the gaming-table no one looks like a saint!”

From the corner there came the words, “He seemed to look at Basch like a beast of prey eyeing his victim!”

“That was exactly the impression he gave me!” exclaimed Wenk.

He at once rose hastily and went to the corner, entered the dark niche and started back, for the speaker was the beautiful unknown! A glow suffused Wenk’s features and his heart began to beat violently, as if its strokes must be heard. Then he pulled himself together, saying, “I really must be mad! I am searching for a criminal and am about to fall in love with someone whom I may have to send to prison tomorrow. This is really idiotic!” He recovered his presence of mind, bowed to the stranger and said:

===Chinese===
“他有一双恶毒的眼睛。”一个女人的声音说道，文克似乎听得出这声音。他朝那个方向望去，可桌面上灯火通明，把那个角落衬得如同洞穴一般幽黑，什么人也辨认不出。

卡拉朝黑暗中那声音答话，语气里像是含着怒意：“恶毒的眼睛！你这话是什么意思？赌桌边上可没有人长得像圣徒！”

角落里又传来那句话：“他看巴施的神情，就像猛兽盯着自己的猎物！”

“他给我的正是这个印象！”文克脱口喊道。

他立刻匆匆起身朝那个角落走去，跨进幽暗的凹龛，不由得怔退了一步——说话的正是那位美丽的无名女子！一阵红晕涌上文克的脸，他的心猛烈跳动起来，仿佛那心跳声必定会被人听见。他随即定住心神，暗自说道：“我准是疯了！我在搜查一名罪犯，却眼看要爱上一个明天也许就得由我送进监狱的人。这实在荒唐！”他恢复了镇定，向那陌生女子欠身一礼，说道：

===Original===
“I should be greatly interested, madam, to hear how you reached a conclusion which so exactly resembles my own?”

“It cannot be anything else,” said the lady, smiling, “than an unusual evidence of secret sympathy between me and a State official!”

“She knows me, then!” said Wenk to himself in astonishment. “But how could that come about, except through Cara Carozza? A State official, guardian and representative of the law, and avenger of any breach of it, himself violating its rules! It was absolutely fantastic. Yes, it must have been the Carozza girl.” From the niche he looked into the brilliantly lighted room, where the dyed tresses of the dancer gleamed forth between the heads. “So it was you!” he said to himself; “you want to bring my plans to nought, you good-for-nothing!﻿ ﻿…”

Then he remembered the glance the blond had given her that first evening, and he ended, “You are his decoy!” Now he realized the connection between them. It was the dancer who brought the blond his victims. He breathed a threat: “Just you wait; I am taking it all in!”

“Our agreement seems to have struck you forcibly,” said the lady, interrupting his thoughts.

===Chinese===
“夫人，我很想听一听，您是怎样得出一个同我的想法如此吻合的结论的？”

“这只能是，”女士微笑着说，“我同一位国家官员之间存在隐秘共鸣的不寻常的证据！”

“这么说，她认得我！”文克暗自吃惊。“可这怎么可能呢？除了通过卡拉·卡罗扎，还能有别的途径？一位国家官员，法律的守护者与代言人、一切违法行为的复仇者，自己却在违犯法律！这真是荒诞至极。是的，一定是卡罗扎姑娘。”他从凹龛里望进灯火通明的大厅，舞女染过的发丝在一颗颗人头之间闪闪发亮。“原来是你！”他心里说；“你想叫我的计划落空，你这个没用的东西！……”

接着他记起头一晚那金发男人投向她的眼色，于是断然认定：“你就是他的诱饵！”此刻他明白了两人之间的关系。正是这舞女，在替金发男人引来一个又一个牺牲品。他从牙缝里无声地发出威胁：“等着瞧吧；这一切我都记下了！”

“看来我们的一致意见给了您不小的震动。”女士打断他的思绪，说道。

===Original===
“As a matter of fact, my thoughts were wandering, and I beg your pardon, madam,” said Wenk; “it is incomprehensible that any strange influence should be able to intervene in *your* neighbourhood, but it can be explained, nevertheless.﻿ ﻿…”

He did not continue. Two ideas suddenly obtruded themselves. This lady was undoubtedly an excellent observer. If only he could procure her help! But the other thought stirred his pulses. Why not abandon all this searching and spying and following after criminals, and strive to win the love of a woman such as this, beautiful as a queen and stately as a goddess! Then he felt her touching his arm hastily.

“Don’t speak,” she whispered, “I beg of you!”

At the same moment Wenk saw three gentlemen entering the circle of light in the room. The first was a young man whom he knew by sight, for a few days previously he had noticed him at an exhibition of Futurist paintings, as the buyer of the most unusual and bizarre of these. He had asked the name of the purchaser, and the attendant had replied, “Graf Told bought them. There he is,” pointing to the young man, who had just now entered the room.

===Chinese===
“不瞒您说，我方才走了神，请您原谅，夫人。”文克说，“居然会有什么异样的力量插足于*您*的身侧，这实在叫人费解，不过终究还是解释得通的。……”

他没有说下去。两个念头突然闯进脑海。这位女士无疑是一位极高明的观察者。要是能求得她的协助就好了！可另一个念头又搅得他心潮起伏：为什么不抛下这一切搜查、窥探、追踪罪犯的营生，去赢得这样一位女子的爱情——她美如王后，端庄如女神！这时，他觉出她急急地碰了碰他的手臂。

“别作声，”她低语道，“我求您了！”

就在同一时刻，文克看见三位绅士走进室内灯光的圈子里。走在头里的是个年轻人，他见过此人：几天前，在一场未来主义绘画展览上，他注意到这人买下了其中最奇特、最怪诞的几幅。他当时问过买主的名字，管理员答道：“是托尔德伯爵（Graf Told）买下的。喏，就是他。”手指着那个年轻人——此刻他正好走进屋来。

===Original===
“Herr von Wenk,” said the lady in a whisper, “will you do me a great favour?”

“With pleasure, madam. I am at your command.”

“I am anxious to leave this room within the next few minutes without being seen. Can you help me to do this?”

“Certainly,” said Wenk.

“How can I accomplish my purpose?”

“That is quite simple. You see the entrance to that staircase; it is only a few steps to it. You must look at it well, to be able to find it in the dark. I am certain that I know where the electric light switch is. It is just over the first section of the stairs. I will go there and turn it out, and you can make use of the darkness to gain the staircase. When you have passed me I will stand directly in the way of anyone who tries to follow you or to reach the switch.”

===Chinese===
“冯·文克先生，”女士低声说，“您能帮我一个大忙吗？”

“乐意效劳，夫人。听凭您的吩咐。”

“我想在几分钟之内离开这间屋子，不被人瞧见。您能帮我做到吗？”

“当然可以。”文克说。

“我该怎样着手呢？”

“这很简单。您瞧见那道楼梯的入口了；离这儿不过几步路。您要好好看清它的位置，好在黑暗中也找得到。电灯开关在哪儿，我心里有数。就在楼梯第一段的上方。我走过去把灯关掉，您就趁黑摸到楼梯口去。等您走过去之后，无论什么人想跟上来，或是想抢到开关跟前，我都会径直拦在他面前。”

===Original===
“Splendid! Thank you very much.”

Her escape was safely made. When Wenk saw the lady had reached the bottom, he turned the light on again and entered the room with a light laugh, saying, “Please forgive me; I did it for a joke, and I did not realize you would be in total darkness.”

They all laughed, but the dancer was standing, pale and disturbed, at the head of the winding stair, which she had reached at one bound. She recovered herself quickly and returned to Hull, begging him to drive her home. Wenk accompanied them.

As they were about to leave the gaming-hall, Wenk saw the headwaiter hand Hull an envelope. He went to an empty table beneath a lamp, opened it, and drew out a little note. It seemed as if an invisible thrust had sent him staggering. Cara went up to him, but he crumpled up the note, stuffing it into his pocket, and rose and followed the others out.

When they had reached the street they parted, but Hull turned and came back to Wenk, saying, in a voice trembling with excitement, “I *must* speak to you. This very night! Can you see me at your rooms in an hour’s time? It is something horrible; I am being shadowed!”

===Chinese===
“好极了！多谢您。”

她安然脱身了。文克见女士已到楼梯底下，便重新开了灯，轻笑着走进屋来，口里说道：“请原谅；我不过是开个玩笑，没想到会让大家陷入一片漆黑。”

众人都笑了，只有那舞女面色苍白、心神不定地站在盘旋楼梯的顶端——她是一步抢上来的。她很快定下神，回到赫尔身边，求他送她回家。文克陪着一同走了。

正要离开赌厅时，文克看见侍者领班递给赫尔一个信封。赫尔走到灯下一张空桌旁，拆开信封，抽出一张小字条。仿佛挨了一记无形的重击，他踉跄了一下。卡拉朝他走去，他却把字条揉成一团塞进口袋，起身随着众人出去了。

到了街上，众人分了手，赫尔却又转身折回来，走到文克跟前，声音激动得发颤：“我*必须*同您谈谈。就在今夜！一小时后能在您寓所见我吗？出了一件可怕的事；我被人盯梢了！”

---

===Original===
“Look at this!” said Hull, as he entered Wenk’s rooms an hour later. With a despairing gesture he flung an envelope on Wenk’s table. The latter opened it and drew a small card from it. On it there stood:

> **Herr Balling**, 

 I.O.U. 20,000 (twenty thousand) marks payable November 21st, 4 p.m.
> Edgar Hull.

“My I.O.U.,” said Hull in a toneless voice, and after a pause, “Look at the other side!”

On the reverse side Wenk read: “You are warned. The reason I did not take your twenty thousand marks is my affair alone. The transaction lies between you and me. Play is play, and no State Attorney has anything to do with it.”

===Chinese===
一小时后，赫尔走进文克的寓所。“看看这个！”他做了个绝望的手势，把一个信封摔在文克的桌上。文克拆开信封，从中抽出一张小卡片。上面写着：

> **巴林先生**：
>
> 兹欠 20,000（两万）马克，定于 11 月 21 日下午 4 时偿还。
> 埃德加·赫尔。

“我的借据。”赫尔声音平板地说，顿了顿，又道：“看看背面！”

文克在背面读到：“你已受到警告。我不取走你那两万马克，缘由只与我本人有关。这笔交易只在你我之间。赌归赌，检察官无权过问。”

===Original===
Wenk was staggered. “Yes, yes, yes,” he said, and found no other words to express the storm which raged within him. Then after a while, as he collected himself, he said:

“We sat near him, you and I! We could have seized him by the arm, one on each side, you﻿ ﻿… and I! Do you understand?”

“I am shadowed!” whispered Hull, who seemed to have no thought of anything except his immediate danger.

“Do you understand? Do you know who Balling is? Your Balling? your distinguished old gentleman? It is the man with the fair beard who was at Schramm’s. He is your Herr Balling! Good heavens!﻿ ﻿… We could have put our hands on his shoulder!”

===Chinese===
文克被震住了。“是，是，是。”他说，再找不出别的话来表达胸中翻腾的风暴。过了一会儿，他定下神来，说道：

“我们就坐在他身旁，你和我！我们本可以一人一边抓住他的胳膊，你……和我！你明白吗？”

“我被人盯梢了！”赫尔低声说，他似乎除了眼下的危险，什么也顾不上去想了。

“你明白吗？你知道巴林是谁吗？你的那位巴林？你那位体面的老先生？他就是施拉姆餐馆里那个淡色胡须的男人。他就是你的巴林先生！天哪！……我们本来一伸手就能按住他的肩膀！”

===Original===
Hull merely gasped. Now he knew why the sandy-bearded man had seemed familiar to him; his were the large, fierce grey eyes!

“Yes,” he said, “it *is* the same man!”

“He has disappeared,” exclaimed Wenk; “he no longer comes to Schramm’s. And as for you, Herr Hull, we shall henceforth have you under our special care, but you must endeavour to meet our wishes and be constantly on your guard.”

===Chinese===
赫尔只是喘着气。如今他知道，那沙色胡须的男人为何看着眼熟了；那双又大又凶的灰眼睛，正是此人的！

“是的，”他说，“*正是*同一个人！”

“他失踪了。”文克大声说，“他不再来施拉姆餐馆了。至于您，赫尔先生，从今往后我们将把您置于我们的特别保护之下，但您也必须尽力配合我们的要求，时刻保持警惕。”

## IV / 四

===Original===
Hull departed, and Wenk, alone with the impressions which the evening’s experiences had left on his mind, asked himself, “Why did the fair unknown try to get away so secretly? Have I made another mistake? Has my helping her in her flight placed a weapon in the hand which would strike at me and my work?”

His agitation increased, but he dismissed his doubts of the lady. No, he felt he could rely upon her. And now the realization of the connection between Hull and the gambler, and all the other stories about the latter, set his mind working in a fresh direction, and other ideas began to develop. He seemed to hear the beating of the wings of some new and mighty force that was invading his life. Conflicts were going on in his physical nature, his fantasy, his nervous energy and endurance. His knowledge of men and his dominion over them were being put to the test. Thinking fiercely, he smoked cigar after cigar, and clouds of smoke surrounded him. It was springtime, storm, sunshine, and again storm, in his blood. His muscles were engaged in an imaginary and heroic conflict with mysterious and mighty giants who were seeking to strangle his fellow-men. He had seized one of them by the false reddish beard which he had assumed, in semblance of humanity.

From the town, lapped in slumber, it seemed as if the spirit of the age rushed into his room﻿—an age fraught with dangers, demands, and tension of all kinds. It demanded men﻿—demanded of all men all their ambition, self-discipline, intelligence, selflessness﻿ ﻿… selflessness. It should take him! There he was, free alike from arrogance and from indolence! Might there not be, he asked himself in his ecstatic monologue, a new democracy which should redeem the past? Was that the goal towards which the present gloom was leading mankind? Was he rising on the stormy wave? He would no longer drift along, striving to help his country as a mere idealist. No, he would stand firm on his feet; struggle, contest, but not submit! Freed from thoughts of self, he would expend the last drop of his blood to become what he had learned to be; he would yield all he had to give, to the very last red drop.

===Chinese===
赫尔走了，文克独自回味着这一晚的经历在心头留下的种种印象，暗自问道："那个淡色头发的陌生女子，为什么要那样隐秘地脱身？我是不是又犯了一个错误？我助她出逃，会不会是把一件武器交到了某只手里，而它终将打击我和我的事业？"

他心绪激荡，愈来愈难平静，但还是打消了对那位女士的疑虑。不，他觉得自己可以信赖她。此刻，认识到赫尔与那个赌徒之间的关联，再加上关于后者的种种传闻，他的思路转上了一个新的方向，别的念头开始成形。他仿佛听见了某种全新而强大的力量的振翅之声，正侵入他的生活。他的血肉、他的想象、他的神经能量与耐力，处处都在经历冲突。他对人的洞察与驾驭正经受着考验。他思绪奔涌，一支接一支地抽着雪茄，云雾般的烟气环绕着他。他的血液里正是春天，一阵风暴，一阵晴日，又是一阵风暴。他的肌肉正投身一场想象中的英勇搏斗，对手是那些意欲扼杀他同胞的神秘而强大的巨人。他已经一把揪住了其中一个的胡子——那撮用来装出人形的假红胡须。

从沉睡中的城市里，仿佛有一种时代精神冲进了他的房间——一个充满危险、苛求与种种紧张的时代。它需要人——要求所有人都拿出全部的抱负、自律、才智、无私……无私。它正该找他这样的人！瞧，他就在这儿，既无骄矜，亦无怠惰！他在狂喜的独白中自问：会不会有一种新的民主，能够救赎过去？眼下这片阴霾，正把人类引向那个目标吗？他自己是不是正乘着怒涛扶摇而起？他再也不要随波逐流，只凭一腔理想主义去报效国家。不，他要双脚站稳；去斗争，去较量，绝不屈服！抛开了私心杂念，他甘愿流尽最后一滴血，去成为他所学要成为的人；他甘愿献出自己所有的一切，直到最后那殷红的一滴。

===Original===
It was not his career that was at stake, but that which all men have in common, both in conflict with each other and in helping one another. It was the surge of humanity in which mortals, for good or ill, were engulfed in a gloom which none could dominate and subdue. In that night of reflection the lawyer saw the criminal no longer as a being of an inferior order. He envisaged him as a man whose pulses raced madly along, his senses stirred by the powers of hell; a man whose lusts and appetites, demon-fed, should overreach themselves and be brought to nought, and he, Wenk, should save and deliver him. The fighter should gain the ascendancy over his adversary.

In imagination Wenk was now struggling with the blond stranger, and in him he had a powerful opponent. He suspected even more than he already knew. If he could relieve mankind of him, he would have accomplished something by which he could advance further.

The song which Wenk’s heart had been singing for the last two hours suddenly seemed to be familiar to him, and in astonishment he realized that the state to which he had now come had been foreshadowed in his boyhood’s days, even before his university career, his military training, and his entry into law, when as yet no idea of the justification of humanity had fired his blood. Thinking over his lonely bachelor existence, without any womanly influence, he felt a strange, sad yearning for the father who had died long before.

===Chinese===
悬于一线的不是他的前程，而是所有人共有的东西——无论在彼此冲突之中，还是在互相扶助之中。那是人性的巨浪，凡人在其中或沉或浮，被一片谁也无法主宰和降服的阴霾吞没。在那反思的一夜里，这位法律家不再把罪犯看作天生低人一等的生物。他把罪犯看作一个人：脉搏疯狂奔涌，官能被地狱的势力搅动；这个人的贪欲和渴念靠魔鬼喂养，终将玩火自焚、归于毁灭——而他，文克，将去拯救他、解救他。斗士应当压倒他的对手。

此刻在想象中，文克正与那个金发陌生人角力，而在他身上，文克遇到了一个强大的对手。他疑心事情的真相比他已知的还要多。倘若他能替人类除去此人，他便完成了一件足以让自己更上层楼的事业。

两个钟头以来文克心中一直在吟唱的那支歌，忽然听来竟有些耳熟；他惊异地意识到，自己如今抵达的这种境界，早在少年时代便已露过端倪——那还在他上大学、受军训、投身法律界之前，那时"为人类正名"的念头尚未点燃他的热血。回想自己孑然一身的独居岁月，身边从无一点女性的影响，他心头涌起一阵奇异而哀伤的渴念，想念那位早已故去的父亲。

===Original===
The next day Wenk asked Hull to procure for him a list of all the secret gambling-dens, the addresses of which might be obtained with the help of Cara, who was au courant of such matters. He made Hull promise, however, that he would not speak to the girl of himself in this connection.

Wenk visited these places evening by evening. He went disguised as a rich old gentleman from the provinces. He had chosen this disguise, first of all, because he had an excellent example of it in an elderly uncle whom he merely had to copy. The old gentleman gave the impression that he was thoroughly enjoying all his experiences of the great city.

Wenk had some accomplices among Karstens’ acquaintances. He begged them to make it widely known that he, the “country cousin,” was a man of fabulous wealth, which when once settled down, he intended to use to the full. He thought that thus he might entice the gambler from Schramm’s and others bent on plunder, that his wealth would be the candle to these night moths. At times he played carelessly for half an hour, adapting himself to the character of the game; then he would win considerable sums, only to lose them again next time. With all this he never lost sight of his own affairs or his neighbours’, and during the game his brain was working busily with a keenness which brought its own satisfaction.

===Chinese===
第二天，文克要赫尔替他弄一份所有秘密赌窟的名单，这些地址可以借卡拉之手弄到，她对这类事情消息灵通。不过他让赫尔保证，在这件事上决不向那姑娘提起他本人。

文克一夜接一夜地探访这些地方。他乔装成一位外省来的阔绰老绅士。之所以选定这副装扮，首先是因为他有一位上了年纪的叔父，是个现成的绝佳范本，照着模仿就行。这位老绅士给人的印象是：大都会的种种阅历，他正享用得有滋有味。

在卡斯滕斯的熟人圈子里，文克安插了几个帮手。他请他们四处张扬：他这位"外省阔佬"家资豪富，一旦安顿下来，便打算尽情挥霍。他心想，这样一来，也许能把施拉姆餐馆的那个赌徒以及别的一心劫掠的家伙引出来，他的财富正是诱这帮夜蛾子的烛火。他不时漫不经心地玩上半个钟头，随赌局的脾性下注；时而赢下可观的一笔，下次又尽数输回去。尽管如此，对自己和邻座的动静他始终不曾放过，赌局进行间，他的头脑高速运转，那份敏锐本身就带着一种快意。

===Original===
