One evening during the second week in which he was pursuing this course, he came to a gaming-house in the centre of the city which, from the style of its habitués, who appeared more downright than in some of the other places, seemed to promise him something out of the common. There he saw an old gentleman sitting at the card-table, his attention being drawn to him on account of his horn spectacles. These were of unusual size. The old gentleman was addressed as Professor. When he took his cards in hand, he removed his horn spectacles, exchanging them for eyeglasses of an uncommon shape.

Then Wenk noticed that the spectacles now lying on the table were not the usual type of modern horn spectacles, but were of tortoiseshell, very artistically designed. The old gentleman slipped them into a large shagreen case, dotted over with green points. All his movements were very leisurely, so that Wenk had ample time for his observations. “Those are Chinese spectacles,” he thought to himself, recalling his own journey to China, which he had made before the war. The recollection surged up so powerfully that he uttered aloud what he had really only intended to say to himself.

The Professor, who sat opposite him, nodded to him and said in a firm voice, which he had not expected to hear from so aged a mouth, “They are from Tsi-nan-fu!”

He repeated the name, stressing it and separating the syllables, “Tsi-nan-fu.” It was as if the name had a rhythm and recollection behind it which affected him strongly, and which he enjoyed in the mere repetition of the syllables. He looked across at Wenk, as if his eyes in their large glasses were sending him a challenge. Wenk at once felt some strange connection with the old Professor.

===Chinese===
这样进行到第二个星期的某个晚上，他来到市中心的一家赌场。从常客们的做派看，这里的人比别处某几家的更直来直去，这地方似乎向他预示着某种不同寻常的东西。在那里，他看见一位老绅士坐在牌桌旁，首先攫住他注意的是那副角质框眼镜，大得出奇。人们尊称这位老绅士为教授。他拿牌上手时，便摘下角质框眼镜，换上一副样式少见的眼镜。

这时文克才注意到，放在桌上的那副眼镜并非时下常见的角质框样式，而是玳瑁做的，做工十分精巧雅致。老绅士把眼镜滑进一只硕大的鲛革眼镜盒里，盒面上缀着点点绿斑。他的一举一动都从容不迫，文克因此有充裕的时间细细观察。"那是中国眼镜。"他心里想着，忆起自己战前去中国的那次旅行。回忆汹涌而至，竟使他脱口说出了原本只打算在心里想想的话。

坐在他对面的教授朝他点了点头，用一种斩钉截铁的嗓音说道——他没料到如此苍老的口中会发出这样的声音："这眼镜是济南府（Tsi-nan-fu）来的！"

他又把那名字重复了一遍，一字一顿，重重咬着音："济——南——府。"这名字背后仿佛藏着某种节奏和记忆，深深撼动着他，单是反复念叨这几个音节，他就觉得受用。他隔着桌子望向文克，那双藏在大镜片后面的眼睛，仿佛正向他递来一封挑战书。文克当即感到自己与这位老教授之间有了某种奇特的关联。

===Original===
“Tsi-nan-fu,” said the harsh voice again, as if with special meaning; indeed, as if he wanted to hurl the three syllables at something, some invisible goal behind Wenk﻿—to reach, three times over, an invisible point in the obscurity straight above his head beyond the circle of electric light.

Wenk involuntarily raised his hand to the back of his head and turned round. Was he seeking the spot towards which the three syllables were projected, and had they reached their goal? When he looked round he observed that behind his neighbour at the gaming-table sat the lady whose mysterious flight from Schramm’s he had assisted. It seemed as if she were regarding him mockingly, and he did not know what course to pursue with regard to her, but at that moment he felt that cards were being dealt to him, and he turned again to the table to take them up. As he did so he began to feel sleepy, and felt dimly that the staring eyes of the Professor were somehow responsible for this. He forgot the beautiful unknown, and strove to banish his lassitude, sitting bolt upright and gazing at the green shagreen cover of the Chinese spectacle-case. It seemed as if the eyes of the old Professor, larger than ever behind his glasses, were fixed vaguely upon him, and some dim recollection of past days of travel flitted into his mind. One morning on his journey to China, through the porthole of his cabin he had seen a narrow strip of coastline between sky and sea, and knew it for the delta of the Yang-tse-kiang. Yes, it was the Yang-tse-kiang.

Pursuing this recollection, Wenk named his stake, won it, and left his money lying. A comfortable sense of drowsiness pervaded him, and he stretched himself out, enjoying it. Then he became wide awake once more, played his game, and continued his watch. The players were holding the bank in turns, and it seemed to Wenk as if he were only awaiting the moment when the old gentleman should take it over. “Why am I waiting for that?” he asked himself. “How strange it is that I should be. There are feelings that one cannot trace to their source.”

===Chinese===
"济南府。"那生硬的嗓音又说了一遍，仿佛别有深意；那口气简直像是要把这三个音节掷向什么东西，掷向文克身后某个看不见的目标——要一连三回，命中他头顶正上方、电灯光圈之外黑暗中那个无形的点。

文克不由自主地抬手摸向自己的后脑勺，转过身去。他是在找那三个音节射向的所在吗？它们命中目标了吗？回头一望，他发觉牌桌上自己邻座的身后，坐着那位女士——正是他曾协助她从施拉姆餐馆神秘出逃的人。她似乎正带着讥诮打量他，他不知该拿她怎么办才好；可就在这时，他发觉牌发到了自己手上，便又转回牌桌去取牌。就在这时，他开始觉得困倦，隐隐感到教授那双凝视的眼睛与此脱不了干系。他忘了那位美丽的陌生女子，竭力驱走倦意，腰背笔直地坐着，眼睛盯着那只中国眼镜盒的绿色鲛革盒面。老教授的那双眼睛在镜片后面显得愈发巨大，仿佛正恍惚地盯在他身上，一些昔日旅行的模糊记忆掠过他的脑海。去中国的旅途上，一天清晨，他曾透过舱室的舷窗，望见天海之间一线狭窄的海岸，认出那就是扬子江（Yang-tse-kiang）的三角洲。是的，那是扬子江。

顺着这段回忆，文克报出了赌注，赢了，赢的钱仍留在原处。一种舒坦的睡意漫上全身，他舒展四肢，安然受用。随后他又完全清醒过来，接着赌，继续观察。赌客们轮流坐庄，文克觉得自己仿佛只是在等待老绅士接庄的那一刻。"我为什么要等这个？"他问自己。"我竟会这样，多奇怪啊。有些感觉，人是追不出根源的。"

===Original===
He finally decided that he was awaiting that moment because the Professor with the Chinese spectacles was the most interesting person present, and that this waiting sprang from a feeling of rapport and sympathy with him.

As the evening proceeded, this secret bond between him and the unknown Professor grew stronger still. “It is childish and sentimental,” he told himself; “what is it going to lead to?”

Then the old gentleman took the bank, and Wenk seemed to be released﻿—released from a ridiculous and unnatural tension. “Now things will be all right,” he thought. He staked a small sum, trying to indicate thereby that he was no opponent of the banker, and that it was only for form’s sake he played against him.﻿ ﻿… He won, for he held eight points, and then he ascertained that he had staked a much bigger note than he had intended to. Therefore he put his stake and his winnings together and ventured both. He drew a king and a five. When he held a five he never bought another card, and this rule was so firmly established in his mind that when asked to say Yes or No, he did not even answer.

“You are taking a card?” were the words he heard in his fit of abstraction. They were uttered by a deep, compelling voice, and seemed almost threatening in tone. Strangely, too, they seemed to him to proceed from the spot behind and above him which had been the goal of the sounds “Tsi-nan-fu.”

===Chinese===
他最后认定，自己之所以等待那一刻，是因为这位戴中国眼镜的教授是在场最有趣的人物，这份等待源于一种与他意气相投、惺惺相惜的感觉。

随着夜晚渐深，他与这位素昧平生的教授之间的这根暗线愈绷愈紧。"这真是孩子气，多愁善感。"他对自己说，"这能有什么结果呢？"

随后老绅士接过了庄家之位，文克顿时觉得自己如释重负——从一种可笑而不自然的紧张中解脱了出来。"这下一切都会顺利了。"他想。他押了一小笔，想以此表明自己并非庄家之敌，同他对赌不过是走个过场。……他赢了，因为他手里有八点；接着他才发现，自己押出去的钞票面额比原先想的大得多。于是他把赌注连同赢头一齐押上，再试一把。他摸到一张K和一张五。凡是手里拿着五点，他从不买牌，这条规矩在他心里根深蒂固，以致当人家要他答"要"还是"不要"时，他索性一声不吭。

"您要牌吗？"——恍惚之间，他听见了这样一句话。那是一个低沉而带有强制力的嗓音说出的，语调近乎威胁。奇怪的是，在他听来，这话音仿佛正是从"济南府"三个音节所射向的那个目标——他身后上方的那一点——发出来的。

===Original===
Then he whispered hesitatingly, “Please!” and at the same instant he seemed to dissociate himself inwardly from this decision, but it was too late. He had drawn a five, and that, added to the cards he held, totalled more than twenty-one and made his hand worthless.

The banker’s hand showed a queen and a four, and as he had taken no other card, he had won the round.

“The country cousin is losing!” said a woman’s voice.

The hasty ejaculation astonished Wenk. He turned round again, trying to penetrate the obscurity; then he grew uneasy, and at the same time he seemed to feel the beating of wings above his eyes. Yes, they were wings, and he himself was in a birdcage. And now a seven was dealt him. “That’s no good,” something seemed to say to him, although it was almost certain to win. But Wenk resisted the suggestion, and said distinctly, “No other card for me!” It seemed as if it were almost death to him to have to utter these words.﻿ ﻿… He felt as if lightning-stabs were compelling him to close his eyes. Then in the last struggle of his will against his unnatural weariness he saw the Professor’s hand resting on the cards. It pressed the upper one with a slight trembling movement, in evident desire of giving it him, and it seemed as if a secret and burning stream passed from this hand to him, seeking to *compel* him to take the card, although he had already declined it.

===Chinese===
于是他迟疑地低声道："请给牌！"就在这一瞬间，他内心里仿佛又同这个决定割裂开来，可是太迟了。他摸到一张五，加上手里原有的牌，总点数超过了二十一点，这一手成了废牌。

庄家的牌面是一张Q和一张四；他不再另要牌，赢了这一局。

"那位外省阔佬输钱了！"一个女人的声音说。

这声脱口而出的惊呼令文克愕然。他又回过头去，想看透那片昏暗；随即他不安起来，同时又仿佛感到有翅膀在自己眼睛上方扑动。是的，那是翅膀，而他本人正身处一只鸟笼之中。这时一张七发到了他面前。"这张不妙。"仿佛有什么在对他这么说，尽管这牌几乎稳赢。但文克抵抗住了这个暗示，一字一句地说道："我不要再要牌了！"说这几个字，他几乎觉得要耗尽性命。……他感到仿佛有一道道闪电刺来，逼他闭上眼睛。随后，在意志对抗那反常疲惫的最后一搏中，他看见教授的那只手正搭在牌上。那只手带着微微的颤动按住最上面的一张，分明是想把牌递给他；仿佛有一道隐秘而灼热的潜流从这只手传到他身上，竭力*迫使*他把牌接过去，尽管他已经拒绝了。

===Original===
Recognizing this, he was suddenly wide awake. It seemed as if the chains destined to fetter his soul had fallen from before him, and he now faced the Professor fearlessly, seized with an incomprehensible and strangely earnest misgiving with regard to him. He was tempted to spring up and beat the beckoning fingers away from the card.

“You are taking a card?” said the deep, stern voice, as if issuing an order. It was the voice he had already heard from behind him. Then Wenk, in an unusually loud tone, said firmly and indignantly, “No, I have already declined!”

The large eyes behind the glasses remained fixed, gazing at him for the space of a second, then shrank back like hounds before a more powerful assailant. The old gentleman leaned slightly forward, asked for brandy and water, and shortly afterwards requested to be allowed to give up the bank and leave the game. He felt suddenly indisposed, he said.﻿ ﻿

They all busied themselves with him, crowding round his seat, but Wenk remained in his chair. He was struck by the connection between his little experience and the old gentleman’s attack of faintness. Were they indeed connected? He felt as if he were responsible for the Professor’s collapse. It seemed as if he had subconsciously come into conflict with him, and that this fainting-fit was the result of their struggle. He was considering how he could help him. Then he felt in his waistcoat-pocket and brought out his little bottle of smelling-salts. He took the stopper out and handed it across, saying, “Perhaps these salts may be of use? I have just﻿ ﻿…” but he was surprised to find that the old gentleman had already departed.

His earlier misgivings returned. He rose quickly and pushed his way through the crowd. He wanted to follow the man and bring him back. Someone suddenly stopped him, saying something incomprehensible, as if he, Wenk, were responsible for the Professor’s condition; but Wenk’s hand went to the revolver in his breast-pocket. Cara Carozza advanced towards him; he pushed her hastily aside, dragging the other with him. Then with his disengaged hand he violently wrenched himself free of his assailant’s grip, and hurried to the corridor which formed the dimly lighted side-entrance. He heard footsteps behind him as he entered it, hastened forward, closed the door behind him after passing through, and soon gained the side street where the motorcars were waiting.

===Chinese===
认出这一点，他霍然完全清醒了。仿佛那副要来禁锢他灵魂的锁链已在他面前散落，此刻他毫无惧色地面对着教授，心里生出一种难以名状、异乎寻常的郑重戒惧。他几乎要跳起来，把那几根招引的手指从牌上打落。

"您要牌吗？"那低沉而严厉的嗓音又响了，像在下命令。这正是他方才从身后听到过的声音。于是文克以一种异乎寻常的高声，坚决而愤然地说道："不，我已经拒绝过了！"

镜片后面那双大眼睛凝然不动，逼视了他一秒钟，随即缩了回去，如同猛犬遇上更强大的进袭者。老绅士微微前倾，要了一杯掺水白兰地，不多一会儿便请求准许他放弃庄家之位、退出牌局。他说自己突然感到不适。……

众人都围着他忙作一团，他的座位四周挤满了人，文克却仍坐在自己的椅子上。他心里琢磨着，自己方才那段小小的经历与老绅士这场晕厥之间有何关联。它们当真有牵连吗？他觉得自己仿佛对教授的虚脱负有责任。似乎他在潜意识里同教授较量了一场，这场晕倒便是两人角力的结果。他正思量怎样才能帮上忙，便伸手到马甲口袋里，摸出自己那小瓶嗅盐。他拔开瓶塞，隔着人递过去，说道："这嗅盐也许管用？我刚刚……"却惊愕地发现，老绅士早已离去。

先前那阵疑惧又回来了。他霍然起身，从人群中挤出去。他想追上那个人，把他带回来。有人突然拦住他，说着些莫名其妙的话，仿佛教授的境况该由他文克负责；可文克的手已伸向胸前口袋里的左轮手枪。卡拉·卡罗扎朝他迎面走来；他仓促把她推到一旁，连带着也推开了拦他的那个人。然后他用腾出来的那只手猛力挣脱对方钳住的手，疾步奔向那条充当昏暗侧门的走廊。他一进走廊便听见身后有脚步声，忙加快脚步，穿出去后随手带上门，很快便到了停着汽车的侧街上。

===Original===
By the light of a lantern he saw the old gentleman, bent and bowed no longer, but with hasty and powerful stride about to enter a car. He saw his own chauffeur drawing up to the kerb, and called to him in a low voice, “Follow that car!”

They flew after it. It was a large and powerful car, but as it was still early in the evening, there was a good deal of traffic, and it could not travel at its full speed, consequently they were close behind it. They were soon caught up in a stream of cars and taxicabs coming from one of the theatres, so that Wenk could follow quietly and without exciting suspicion right to the Palace Hotel. The Professor’s car stopped in front of it, and before Wenk’s car came to a standstill he saw the other enter the vestibule hastily. He gave a fleeting glance round. Wenk hastened after him, but happened to be caught in the stream of those entering, who hid him from sight. He saw the Professor rapidly open and read a telegram at the hotel bureau, and while he was reading it Wenk had time to select a favourable spot for observing him. Thus he saw that the old gentleman, raising his eyes from his telegram, gave a furtive glance round, then went quickly to the lift, opened the door and disappeared within it; but Wenk had noticed that there was a lift attendant sitting inside.

He waited till the light signalled where the lift had stopped, and saw it was on the first floor; then he rang for it to descend.

===Chinese===
借着一盏提灯的光亮，他看见那位老绅士——腰也不弯了，背也不驼了——正迈着急促而有力的步子走向一辆汽车，眼看就要登车。他看见自己的司机正把车靠向路缘，便低声唤道："跟上那辆车！"

他们飞驰着追了上去。那是一辆宽大而马力强劲的汽车，但夜色尚早，路上车马正多，它开不出全速，因此他们紧咬在后面。不多时，两辆车便汇入从一家剧场散出的一股汽车与出租车的洪流，文克得以不动声色、不启疑窦地一路追到宫殿饭店（Palace Hotel）。教授的车在饭店门前停下，文克的车还没停稳，他已看见那人急匆匆跨进门厅。那人飞快地朝四周扫了一眼。文克急忙跟上去，恰好裹进进门的人流里，人流遮住了他的身形。他看见教授在饭店服务台前迅速拆开一封电报读了起来；趁他读电报的工夫，文克得以挑中一个便于观察的有利位置。就这样，他看见老绅士的目光离开电报，贼也似的朝四下里溜了一眼，随即快步走向电梯，拉开门，消失在里面；而文克早已注意到，电梯里坐着一名司梯的侍者。

他等到指示灯显示出电梯停靠的楼层，看清是二楼，便揿铃唤它下来。

===Original===
“First floor!” said he to the boy, and they went up alone.

“Wasn’t it the gentleman in No. 15 who just went up?” he asked.

“No, sir; it was the Dutch Professor in No. 10.”

“Ah, then my eyes must have deceived me,” he said. “Thank you;” and he proceeded slowly along the corridor. He came to No. 10, lingered a moment there, then went on and looked backward, hearing a door open. It might have been No. 10. He waited, stooping down and busying himself with his shoelace, and when he heard the door shut again, he turned round. Then he saw that on the mat in front of No. 10 there was a pair of shoes.

He went back, an unusual idea having occurred to him. He would knock at the door and ask the old gentleman whether he had recovered from his indisposition, and then take him unawares, for he felt he had enough to go upon to arrest him. The idea seemed to him both a bold and promising one, but when he stood in front of No. 10 again, he saw that the shoes outside the door were women’s shoes, and he gave up the thought. Then he went downstairs and asked to see the hotel manager. He showed him the necessary credentials and asked about the gentleman in No. 10. The hotel list was brought.

===Chinese===
"到二楼！"他对那侍者说，两人单独上了楼。

"刚才上去的，是不是15 号房的那位先生？"他问。

"不是，先生；是10 号房的那位荷兰教授。"

"啊，那准是我看走眼了。"他说，"谢谢你。"说罢便顺着走廊缓缓走去。他走到10 号房门前，在那里逗留了片刻，又往前走，忽听得一扇门开了，便回头看去。那可能就是10 号房的门。他停下脚，弯下身摆弄鞋带，等到听见门又关上，才转过身来。这时他看见，10 号房门前的地垫上摆着一双鞋。

他折了回去，心里冒出一个不寻常的主意。他要敲开房门，问问老绅士贵体是否安好，然后乘其不备将他拿下，因为他觉得，要逮捕此人，自己手里的凭据已经够了。这主意在他看来既大胆又有胜算，可等他再站到10 号房门前，却发现门外那双鞋是女人穿的，便打消了这个念头。于是他下楼，求见饭店经理。他出示了必要的证件，打听10 号房的那位先生。住客名册取来了。

===Original===
“No. 10, you see, sir, is Professor Grote, from The Hague.”

“According to your book he is staying here alone.”

“That is so, your honour.”

“Is he always alone here, or now and then with a female companion?”

“I do not allow anything of that sort, your honour. We are very strict about our guests’ respectability.”

“Well, I can only say that this guest, in spite of his size, has uncommonly small feet.”

===Chinese===
"先生您瞧，10 号房住的是格罗特教授（Professor Grote），海牙来的。"

"照你们的登记簿，他是一个人住在这里。"

"是的，长官。"

"他在这里总是一个人，还是时不时有个女伴同住？"

"这种事我们是不容许的，长官。对住客的名声体面，我们查得非常严。"

"那么我只能说，这位客人虽然身量高大，那双脚却小得出奇。"

===Original===
“What does your honour mean by that?”

“He wears ladies’ shoes.”

“Ah now, sir, you are joking.”

“Well, come with me, my good fellow, and see for yourself.”

===Chinese===
"长官这话是什么意思？"

"他穿的是女人的鞋。"

"哎呀，先生，您这是说笑吧。"

"那好，跟我来，伙计，亲眼去看看。"

===Original===
They went upstairs together. In front of No. 10 they saw a pair of elegant high-heeled shoes of the latest fashion.

Then Wenk cocked his revolver and went in without the formality of knocking. He entered the room quickly, the hotel manager following him. The light was on, but the room was empty. Both the windows were closed and the bathroom adjoining had none. Wenk searched the cupboards, bed, and drawers, but nowhere was any clue to be found. He hurried down to the street, but the stranger’s car had disappeared.

He made the manager inquire who had left the hotel within the last ten minutes. “Nobody but the secretary,” said the commissionaire. At that moment the secretary came from behind a partition, ready to leave the hotel. The man looked at him in amazement.

===Chinese===
两人一同上楼。10 号房门前，摆着一双时新款式、雅致的高跟鞋。

于是文克扳起左轮手枪的击锤，连敲门的礼数都省了，径直闯了进去。他迅速进入房间，饭店经理跟在身后。灯亮着，房间里却空无一人。两扇窗户都关着，相邻的浴室也没有窗户。文克搜了衣橱、床铺和抽屉，哪里都找不到一点踪迹。他急忙奔到街上，可那陌生人的汽车已经不见了。

他让经理去查问，过去十分钟内有谁离开了饭店。"只有秘书先生出去过。"门房说。正在这时，那秘书从一道隔板后面走出来，正预备下班离店。经理惊异地打量着他。

===Original===
“You here again! You only left a few minutes ago.”

“*I* did? I was in the bureau till this very minute,” answered the employee.

Then Wenk knew all he needed to know, and the circumstance was fully explained. For the purposes of disguise the man who had disappeared had prepared the outfit of someone well known in the hotel. He had put a woman’s shoes at his door, for he conjectured, and rightly too, that the pursuer, before he entered the room, would go back to the bureau and inquire about the mystery of the feminine footwear, and he had made good use of the time this took. It was evident to Wenk that he was dealing with a mastermind. He was astonished at the dexterity with which he worked. It immediately recalled the doings of the blond stranger at Schramm’s, and Hull’s Herr Balling.

On his homeward way, and after he reached his chambers, Wenk thought over all he knew about the bearded blond, and tried to compare it with the impressions made on him by the Professor. But, strangely enough, although many details concerning the gambler at Schramm’s were firmly and indelibly fixed in his mind, his impressions of the Professor were wavering and indistinct, although he had encountered him but an hour before.

Moreover, he grew drowsy and it seemed to him as if he had to recover from some more than ordinary fatigue which he had undergone in the course of the day. He began to undress, and a lassitude, almost like that caused by the loss of blood, overcame him. That feeling of an inward lightness of body which had seemed so comfortable when he recalled it at the close of their contest, the nervous tension after the last occurrence, together with the sensation of faintness, now took possession of him entirely. He yielded to it and fell asleep before he had quite finished undressing. In his dream it seemed as if a mysterious and magic castle had been built up all round him, and he knew that if he could interpret the three syllables “Tsi-nan-fu,” or locate that hole in the wall whither The Hague professor’s voice projected them, he would find the key to unlock the door of the enchanted castle.

===Chinese===
"你怎么又在这儿！你几分钟前刚走的。"

"*我*走了？我一直在办公室里待到这会儿。"那名职员答道。

到这时，文克把该知道的都弄清楚了，这桩怪事也得到了圆满解释。为了乔装改扮，那个失踪的人事先备下了一位在饭店里人尽皆知者的行头。他在门口摆了一双女鞋，因为他料定——而且料得一点不错——追捕的人进房之前，必定会折回服务台去查问这双女人鞋的蹊跷，而这段工夫就被他充分利用了。文克明白，自己面对的是一个首屈一指的犯罪大脑。那人行事手法之娴熟令他骇然。这立刻让他想起施拉姆餐馆那个金发陌生人的行径，还有赫尔的那位巴林先生。

归途之上，回到寓所之后，文克把所知的一切关于那个蓄须金发男子的情形在脑子里过了一遍，想拿来同教授留给他的印象做个比较。可是奇怪，尽管关于施拉姆餐馆那个赌徒的诸多细节在他脑海里刻得又深又牢、无法磨灭，他对教授的印象却摇曳模糊，虽然不过一个钟头之前他才同此人打过照面。

不仅如此，他还困倦起来，仿佛需要从这一日里一场超乎寻常的劳乏中恢复过来。他开始宽衣，一阵近乎失血后的虚弱感攫住了他。那种体内轻飘飘的感觉——先前回想起两人较量收场时还觉得无比舒坦的那种感觉——连同最后一幕之后的神经紧张和眩晕之感，此刻把他整个人都占据了。他任其摆布，衣服还没脱完便睡着了。梦里，仿佛有一座神秘魔幻的城堡在他四周拔地而起；他知道，只要能破译"济南府"这三个音节，或是找到海牙教授的嗓音把它们投掷进去的那处墙洞，他就能找到开启这座魔堡之门的钥匙。

## IX / 九

===Original===
At four o’clock there was a telephone call from Frankfurt. “George Strümpfli, artist, was born in Basle in 1885, and lived at the address indicated from January 1st to December 10th last year. He has now gone abroad, his whereabouts being unknown. In the records he is entered as of Swiss nationality, and he is a married man.”

From the register of the town inhabitants Wenk learnt that Cara Carozza was described as follows: “Maria Strümpfli, formerly Essert, known as Cara Carozza, dancer, born in Brunn, May 1, 1892, arrived in Munich from Copenhagen.”

Wenk wondered how the pronunciation of “Georsh” instead of George could have arisen, for both these people were South Germans by speech, and “Georsh” was only heard in North Germany.

===Chinese===
四点钟，法兰克福来了电话。“艺术家格奥尔格·施特吕姆普夫利（George Strümpfli），1885 年生于巴塞尔（Basle），去年 1 月 1 日至 12 月 10 日居于所示地址。现已出境，下落不明。登记册上载为瑞士籍，已婚。”

文克又从市民登记册上查得，卡拉·卡罗扎的名下登记如下：“玛丽亚·施特吕姆普夫利，原姓埃塞特（Essert），以卡拉·卡罗扎之名行世，舞者，1892 年 5 月 1 日生于布伦（Brunn），由哥本哈根抵达慕尼黑（Munich）。”

文克暗自纳闷，“格奥尔格”（George）怎么会被念成“格奥尔什”（Georsh），因为这两人口音都是南德腔，而“格奥尔什”这种念法，只有在北德才听得到。

===Original===
He went again to see the dancer, who was now in a prison cell.

“I don’t want anything to do with you,” she said in a harsh voice to Wenk. “You say you are going to help me, and yet you put me in prison.”

“It was not I: that is a mistake on your part. It is the examining counsel, as I told you at once. I am only here to clear up one difficulty in the case, and that is the name you called out. That is the point at issue.”

“Indeed! you seem rather concerned about the verdict.”

“Yes, of course we are. If you were prepared to help us we might get over the difficulty. Let me see, you said your husband’s name was Carl﻿ ﻿… Carl Strümpfli, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
他又去探视那位舞女，她如今已关进了监狱牢房。

“我不想跟您有任何瓜葛。”她冲文克厉声说道，“您口口声声说要帮我，却把我扔进了监狱。”

“那不是我：您弄错了。是预审法官的安排，我当初就跟您讲明了。我来只为澄清本案的一个疑难，就是您喊出的那个名字。争议的症结正在这里。”

“是嘛！您倒挺关心判决结果。”

“我们当然关心。您若肯帮我们一把，这道难关未尝不能渡过。让我想想，您说过您丈夫名叫卡尔……卡尔·施特吕姆普夫利，对吧？”

===Original===
“In case you forget it again, his name is George.”

“He is a Swiss?”

“You have evidently been inquiring about him.”

“Certainly,” said Wenk. “And so he is called George. Now tell me, although you may think it a foolish question, had you any special name for him?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
“省得您再忘掉：他的名字是格奥尔格。”

“他是瑞士人？”

“您分明去打听过他了。”

“当然。”文克说，“所以他叫格奥尔格。那么请告诉我——您也许会觉得这问题问得蠢——您可曾用什么特别的名字称呼过他？”

“没有。”

===Original===
“You never called him anything but.﻿ ﻿…”

“George. No, only George. When can I get away from here?”

“Ah, that depends upon the examining counsel.”

“Well then, he ought to be here. It is shameful that a well-known artiste like me should.﻿ ﻿…”

“You see, unfortunately everything must take its prescribed course. ‘Without respect of individuals,’ as the legal phrase runs. I cannot promise you any more than my own help.”

===Chinese===
“除了……您从没叫过他别的？”

“格奥尔格。没有，只有格奥尔格。我什么时候能离开这儿？”

“唉，那要看预审法官了。”

“那他就该到这儿来。像我这样一位知名艺人，竟然……真是岂有此理。”

“您瞧，很不巧，一切都得按规定的程序走。用法律上的话来说，‘不徇个人情面’。除了我本人的助力，我无法许诺您更多。”

===Original===
“You are going away again? And without me?”

“For the moment I cannot do anything else.”

The dancer turned away.

===Chinese===
“您又要走了？还不带上我？”

“眼下我别无他法。”

舞女转过身去。

===Original===
Wenk went to the scene of the crime. He had previously studied the list of those living in its vicinity, and especially those in the Finkenstrasse. He took two plainclothes policemen with him, one of them being the constable who had pursued the criminals as far as the park wall. They examined the wall by daylight; it showed scratches from the tips of shoes, and on the top was a trace of blood. Possibly someone had been lifted up who grasped the top with his hands. In the clear February day the light fell pitilessly on that trace of the murdered Hull.

Wenk entered the houses, many of which, he perceived, led at the back to the park. He spoke to all their occupants separately. Some had heard a noise in the night, but they did not consider that anything unusual, and in the houses themselves, as they told Wenk, they had heard nothing.

===Chinese===
文克前往案发之地。他事先已研究过那一带的住户名单，尤其是芬肯街（Finkenstrasse）沿街的住户。他带上两名便衣警察，其中一名便是当晚追击歹人、直追到公园墙下的警员。众人在白昼光线下察看那堵墙：墙上留有鞋尖蹬出的划痕，墙顶上有一抹血迹。也许有人被托举上去，双手扒住了墙沿。二月的晴日里，天光无情地照在那道血痕上——遇害的赫尔留下的痕迹。

文克走进一幢幢宅子，发觉其中不少的后面都直通公园。他把住户逐一分开询问。有人说夜里听见过响动，但并不觉得有什么反常；至于宅子里面，他们对文克说，什么也没听见。

===Original===
He examined the park on the other side of the wall. There was nothing to be seen there beyond a trace of many footprints in one spot, where they had apparently jumped down, for some of the impressions were fairly deep. But this spot had been raked, and carbolic acid thrown upon it. There was an empty tin near, which from its smell had evidently contained carbolic. This precaution was doubtless taken in case the police hounds should be requisitioned, and it might have been put there beforehand, but he did not quite understand the reason, and decided to test it by means of a hound. It took up the scent in the Jägerstrasse, ran to the wall and jumped up on it, but when they lifted it on the other side it went no further. It turned away in disgust at the smell of the carbolic, ran up and down the wall and then back again, always in the same direction, and yet always as if irresolute. It tried to spring into the air.

Wenk had it lifted over the wall again, but when the hound was on the top, and the man on the other side ready to receive it, it escaped from him and ran, barking furiously, along the top. It did not run far, but remained in one spot, barking, with its head downwards, towards the yard of one of the houses, trying to jump down there. Then with one spring the hound was over, running towards the house, where it stood still at the outer wall. This Wenk examined closely, perceiving marks of scratches occurring at regular intervals upwards. Here undoubtedly people had climbed up by means of a ladder, and the tracks led to a window on the first floor. The room it belonged to was empty, and he asked the people of the house how long it had been so. Then all the other lodgers were astonished, for they said it was occupied. One of them exclaimed, “But Georsh is living there!”

Wenk’s heart gave a sudden leap.

===Chinese===
他察看了围墙另一侧的公园。那里什么也没有，只有一处地上留着杂沓的脚印，他们显然是从那里跳下去的，因为有几枚印痕相当深。但这一处已被耙平，还泼了石炭酸。旁边丢着一只空铁皮罐，从气味判断，原先装的显然就是石炭酸。这一手防范，无疑是为防警方调遣警犬，也可能是事先布置下的；但个中缘由他还看不透，于是决定牵一条警犬来试。那狗在耶格街（Jägerstrasse）衔起嗅迹，一路追到墙边，一跃而登上墙头；可人们把它抱到墙那头，它却再不肯前行。它厌恶地避开石炭酸的气味，沿着墙头跑上跑下，又折回来，始终朝着同一个方向，却又始终像踌躇不决。它一次次向空中跃扑。

文克让人再把它举过墙去；可警犬一到墙顶，那一头的人也张臂待接，它却挣脱出去，狂吠着沿墙顶奔逃。它跑出没多远便停在一处，低头朝着某一座宅子的院子狂吠，想往那儿跳下去。转眼间警犬一跃而过，直奔那座宅子，在宅子的外墙跟前站定。文克凑近细察这面墙，看出自下而上留着间隔匀称的抓蹭痕迹。无疑，有人在此架梯攀过，痕迹直通二楼的一扇窗。窗内那个房间空空如也，他便问宅中之人，这房间空了多久。众房客一听都愕然，因为据他们说，那房间是有人住的。其中一个失声嚷道：“可格奥尔什就住在那儿呀！”

文克的心猛地一跳。

===Original===
“Who?” he said quickly. “What was his name?”

Again the answer was “Georsh.”

“Did you know him?” he asked of one woman.

“Certainly I knew Georsh!” she replied.

===Chinese===
“谁？”他急急问道，“他叫什么名字？”

回答依旧是“格奥尔什”。

“您认识他吗？”他问其中一名妇人。

“格奥尔什我怎么会不认得！”她答道。

===Original===
“Was that his surname?”

“I don’t know.”

“Who used to call him that?”

“The fellows who were always coming to see him.”

“So his name was George?” went on Wenk, desirous of being quite certain.

===Chinese===
“那是他的姓吗？”

“不知道。”

“都管他这么叫？”

“老来找他的那帮家伙，都这么叫他。”

“这么说，他名叫格奥尔格？”文克追问，只求十足确定。

===Original===
“No, he was called Georsh,” answered one of them.

“Has he lived here long?”

Nobody knew exactly; some thought it was about a year, but he was hardly ever at home. He tried to get a description of the man, but then a curious fact came to light. Even about the colour of his hair they could not agree. One said he was blue-eyed, another declared his eyes were dark. He was rather tall and thin, and dressed like a sailor. Again, he looked rather like an athlete.

===Chinese===
“不是，他叫的是格奥尔什。”其中一人答道。

“他在这儿住得久吗？”

谁也说不准；有人说大约一年了，但他难得着家。文克想请他们描述此人的相貌，结果却冒出一桩怪事来：连他头发的颜色，众人都谈不拢。一个说他是蓝眼睛，另一个一口咬定他是深色眼睛。他个子高挑清瘦，穿戴像个水手；时而又说，他看上去倒像个体魄健壮的运动员。

===Original===
“What was he then? What was his calling?”

“They said he was a commercial traveller.”

It was curious that there was no mention of this Georsh as an occupant of the house; he was not on the list given to Wenk.

===Chinese===
“那他究竟是什么人？干的哪一行？”

“他们说他是个旅行推销员。”

奇怪的是，住户名册上压根没有这个格奥尔什；交给文克的那份名单上，也没有他。

===Original===
Wenk went to the Town Register Office, and with the help of the officials he ascertained that one occupant of the house had been a George Hinrichsen from the Elbe district. He had left the place about a month before, and said he was going to Ravensburg, and after that the room had been taken by a commercial traveller named Poldringer.

It was quite clear to Wenk that Hinrichsen and Poldringer the traveller were one and the same person. It was just a month ago that Hull had had that memorable conversation with Wenk. And Hinrichsen and Poldringer were the same individual as the murderer of Hull, or at least the person who directed the murder, and it was his name that the dancer had called out. Possibly the direction Hinrichsen had taken in departure also agreed with this, for Constance lay near Ravensburg, and Switzerland could be reached from there.

Wenk telegraphed to the Constance head-office, with special reference to the passport stations. A few hours later the police officials there telegraphed back that a man named Poldringer had notified his arrival there. He gave Bavaria as his native State, and this had struck the registering official as curious, because the man used a dialect that was unmistakably North German. On that account the police kept him under surveillance. They ascertained that he frequented the society of people who were suspected of smuggling goods across the Swiss frontier. He often travelled by the steamer to Lindau. “Expect me today in Constance,” telephoned Wenk finally.

===Chinese===
文克前往市民户籍登记处，在办事员的协助下查明：那座宅子曾住过一个易北河地区来的格奥尔格·欣里希森（George Hinrichsen）。此人约一个月前离去，说是要前往拉文斯堡（Ravensburg）；此后那个房间由一个名叫波尔德林格（Poldringer）的旅行推销员租下。

在文克看来，欣里希森与推销员波尔德林格分明是同一人。恰恰一个月前，赫尔才同文克有过那次难忘的谈话。而欣里希森也好、波尔德林格也好，正是杀害赫尔的凶手本人，至少也是幕后主持那场谋杀的人——舞女当时喊出的正是这个名字。或许欣里希森离开时取道的方向也与此相合：康斯坦茨（Constance）就在拉文斯堡左近，从那里便可进入瑞士。

文克致电康斯坦茨警察总局，特别提请各护照检查站留意。几小时后，当地警员回电：一个名叫波尔德林格的人已前来申报户口。他自报原籍巴伐利亚，登记官员顿觉蹊跷，因为此人一口北德方言，错不了。为此警方将他置于监视之下。他们查明，他常混迹于涉嫌向瑞士边境走私货物的人群之中，还常搭轮船去林道（Lindau）。“今天在康斯坦茨等我。”文克最后在电话里说。

===Original===
Wenk immediately prepared for a journey. He could reach Constance before night if the little monoplane belonging to a friend of his, which was always at his service, were ready for a flight. He telephoned to him and ascertained that it was.

At four o’clock he departed, and in the deepening twilight he descended at the Petershaus aerodrome near Constance. The police described the locality in which these profiteers and smugglers were to be found. He disguised himself as a chauffeur and went to one of their inns to get some supper. He addressed one man whom he thought to be of their party, saying that he could get hold of two cars, and also some sort of export licence, as long as it wasn’t looked at too closely, but if he had the help of one or, better still, of two bold fellows it could be done quite easily. There would be a profit of about ten thousand in it, for the cars were bought in the autumn of 1918 and had been kept hidden ever since. They were first-class cars that had belonged to two generals.

The other did not take long to consider. He would broach the matter to a friend of his, and the three of them would soon pull it off. They went together later to another tavern, which the friend often frequented, but he did not appear.

===Chinese===
文克当即准备动身。倘若他一位朋友的那架小单翼机可以起飞——那架飞机向来随他调用——他天黑之前便能抵达康斯坦茨。他打电话过去，确认可以。

四点钟他启程，在渐浓的暮色中降落于康斯坦茨近郊的彼得斯豪斯（Petershaus）机场。警方向他描述了这帮暴利贩子和走私客出没的地段。他化装成汽车司机，走进一家他们常去的酒馆吃晚饭。他看中一个像是他们一伙的人，凑上去攀谈，说自己能弄到两辆汽车，还能搞到一份类似出口许可证的东西——只要没人细看就行；再有一两个胆大的伙计相助，两个更好，这事便轻而易举。这里头大约有一万马克的赚头，因为这两辆车是 1918 年秋天买的，此后一直藏着不露面。都是一流好车，原属两位将军所有。

对方没怎么犹豫。他要向一个朋友提起这桩买卖，他们仨很快就能办成。随后两人一同去了那朋友常光顾的另一家酒馆，可他没有露面。

===Original===
“What is his name?” asked Wenk. “Perhaps I know him.”

“He is called Ball, but you may have known him under some other name. Most of us find it convenient to have one or two different names here; you know all about that, don’t you?”

“Of course I do,” said Wenk.

===Chinese===
“他叫什么名字？”文克问，“说不定我认识他。”

“他叫巴尔（Ball），不过您知道的，兴许是他别的名字。我们这号人在这儿备下一两个不同的名字是常事，图个方便——这里头的门道您都懂，不是吗？”

“这我自然懂。”文克说。

===Original===
Then he grew suddenly pale, for just then a man entered, in whom he thought he recognized the chauffeur who had driven him to Schleissheim in the car filled with poison gas. Everything was at stake. Wenk’s disguise was rather a sketchy one. Supposing this man were the Ball they were expecting! If he came to their table and sat down, he would probably recognize Wenk, and the whole story would come out. He employed all his powers to regain his self-control, and tried to disguise his features by contracting his facial muscles. He had already taken the precaution of seating himself in a dark corner.

But the newcomer sat down at some distance from him at a large table where several young fellows were already sitting. He had his back to Wenk, but the lawyer felt he must not venture any further, and promising a rendezvous for the next evening, he hastily took his leave.

He went to the police-station, stated where he had been, and described the suspected man. The sergeant of police sent for a constable, who said that according to the description the man must be Poldringer.

===Chinese===
这时他倏地脸色发白：恰好有个人走进酒馆，他觉得认出此人正是当日开着那辆满车毒气的汽车、把他送往施莱斯海姆（Schleissheim）的司机。一切都押在这上头了。文克的化装本就草率。万一此人正是他们等的那个巴尔呢！倘若他走过来在桌边坐下，多半会认出文克，全盘皆输。他使出全部定力恢复镇定，又收紧面部肌肉，想让相貌变个样子。亏得他早有防备，拣了个昏暗的角落坐下。

但新来的那人却在离他挺远的一张大桌旁落座，桌边已经坐着几个年轻人。他背对着文克，可这位律师觉得再不敢冒险逗留，遂约定明晚再碰头，匆匆告辞而去。

他去了警察局，说明自己到过哪里，又描述了那个嫌疑人的相貌。警长唤来一名警员，此人听完描述说，这人必是波尔德林格无疑。

===Original===
“Could we be certain of that? I should like the fact established during the night. But I beg of you to proceed cautiously in the matter, for this man is armed at all points!” urged Wenk.

Then he thought it would be better not to go there, said the constable. It was but a small town, and all the police officials, even the plainclothes men, knew these profiteers. His sudden appearance might give the alarm.

“Well then, I must manage without that. Do you know where he lives?”

===Chinese===
“能确凿无疑吗？我希望今夜之内坐实此事。但我恳请诸位务必谨慎行事，这个人可是全副武装、处处设防！”文克力陈道。

那警员说，如此说来最好还是别上那儿去。这不过是座小城，全体警务人员连便衣都算上，都认得这帮暴利贩子。他骤然露面，恐怕会打草惊蛇。

“那好吧，没有这一手我也能办。你知道他住在哪儿吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly.”

“Then take me there at once.”

The sergeant took Wenk to a byway where stood a shabby old inn, which was divided into many courtyards at the back. Wenk at once recognized that it would be extremely difficult to carry through any arrest here without a large body of police, and so many constables could not be quickly and easily procured in a small town like this.

===Chinese===
“知道。”

“那就立刻带我去。”

警长带文克来到一条僻巷，巷里立着一家破旧的老客栈，宅子后头分隔成好几个院落。文克一眼看出，不调动大批警力，想在此地实施逮捕几乎不可能；而在这样一座小城，这么多警员既难以迅速调集，也无法悄悄瞒过众人。

===Original===
Opposite the house was an iron-foundry. Here Wenk spent the next forenoon in company with a constable who knew Poldringer, the two concealing themselves behind a dust-begrimed window.

When, about eleven o’clock, the man whom Wenk knew as the sandy-bearded man’s chauffeur came out of the house, the constable nudged him, saying, “That is Poldringer!”

“That’s my man!” said Wenk.

===Chinese===
客栈对面是一家铸铁厂。第二天上午，文克便在厂里度过，陪他的是一名认识波尔德林格的警员，两人藏身在一扇积满尘垢的窗户后面。

十一点钟光景，那个被文克认作沙色胡须男子司机的家伙从宅子里出来，警员用胳膊肘碰了碰他：“那就是波尔德林格！”

“正是我要找的人！”文克说。

===Original===
In the afternoon he had a consultation with the head of the Criminal Investigation Department. Wenk said it was not a case of arresting one man, but of getting rid of the whole gang, for here in Constance, as one might say, there was but one division of the army whose general headquarters was in Munich, and until one could lay hold of the leader it was not worth while to secure a dozen or so of his accomplices. Wenk advised their not making use of the announcement of a reward of five thousand marks for information (which had been drawn up contrary to his wish), but rather that they should keep a close watch upon what they now knew to be one of the haunts of the gang. That would be the safest way of entrapping their leader, for if they seized the chauffeur now, his master would receive emphatic warning. And this man, Wenk told them, was undoubtedly one of the most daring criminals to be met with in the last ten years. It was not only a money reward, but fame, that might be looked for, and the constables all promised to do what they could.

In the evening Wenk met the young man who was going to help him get rid of the cars at a big profit. His friend had left the town, he said, for things had gone badly of late. Switzerland was overdone with German goods, and the German authorities seemed to be regaining their control of the Lake. They might soon be starving, he said. But *he* knew what to do. He wasn’t going to starve, and sooner than be driven out of the place by hunger, he would join the Foreign Legion. Then at least he would be safe from the German authorities. He could fill his belly in peace, and if he were shot down it would be as a free man, whereas if he stayed here he was bound to end in quod.

Wenk asked what he had to do to get into the Foreign Legion.

===Chinese===
下午，他与刑事侦查科的负责人商议。文克说，这不是抓一个人的问题，而是要端掉整个团伙；可以说，康斯坦茨这边不过是那支军队的一个师，司令部设在慕尼黑；拿不到统帅，先捉住十来个党羽毫无意义。文克建议，不要启用那份悬赏五千马克征集线索的通告（它违背文克的心意拟就），而应密切监视这个现已查明为团伙巢穴之一的地方。那才是诱捕其首领最稳妥的路数；因为若此刻拿下那司机，他的主子便会得到响亮的警讯。而此人，文克告诉他们，无疑是近十年来所能遇到的最胆大包天的罪犯之一。这桩差使指望到手的不止是一笔赏金，还有名声；警员们无不答应竭尽全力。

傍晚，文克见到那个要帮他高价脱手汽车的年轻人。那人说，他的朋友已经离城，因为近来市面很不景气。瑞士的德国货已经泛滥成灾，德国当局似乎正在重新把博登湖（Lake Constance）管控起来。他说，他们眼看就要挨饿了。可*他*知道该怎么办。他是不会挨饿的；与其被饥饿从这地方撵走，他宁可去投外籍军团（Foreign Legion）。那样至少德国当局拿他没辙。他能安安稳稳填饱肚子；就算吃枪子儿，也是作为自由人倒下的；可要是留在这儿，迟早免不了蹲班房。

文克问，要进外籍军团得怎么办。

===Original===
“Oh, that’s easier than ever it was,” answered the man. “Before the war you had to go to Belfort, but now that’s not necessary﻿—you can join up here.”

“Well, that’s a good thing to know. What’s the address of their headquarters?”

“Oh, you only need go to the Black Bull and ask for Poldringer, or else come in the evening to the tavern we went to yesterday, for he was sitting there. He had got a lot of them at his table, and I told him I’d think it over. If our honk-honk business comes off, I shan’t need to, though, but we can’t get hold of that d﻿—﻿—﻿d Ball; he’d want to stand in with us, but I expect he’s got something good on somewhere else. By the way, Poldringer was asking after you last night. You must belong to his part of the country, eh? He said he thought he knew you, but I told him you were from Basle and wanted to get two cars across, and he said, ‘Oh, then it can’t be the man from Munich,’ but I thought to myself a man might have been in Munich and yet be in Basle now, eh, mate?”

===Chinese===
“哦，这可比从前什么时候都容易。”那人答道，“战前你还得跑一趟贝尔福（Belfort），如今用不着了——在这儿就能入伙。”

“哈，这倒是个有用的消息。他们的总部在哪儿？”

“哦，您只消上‘黑公牛’（Black Bull）酒馆找波尔德林格，要么晚上去咱们昨天去的那家酒馆，他昨天就坐在那儿。他桌边围了一大帮人，我跟他说我得考虑考虑。不过，要是咱们那档子汽车买卖做成了，我就用不着去了；可就是寻不着那个该死的巴尔！他本来该入咱们一股的，我看他八成在别处寻着了好买卖。对了，昨天夜里波尔德林格还打听您来着。您准是他老乡吧，呃？他说他觉得认得您，可我告诉他，您是巴塞尔人，想运两辆车过去，他就说：‘哦，那就不会是慕尼黑来的那个人了。’可我心里想，一个人兴许既在慕尼黑待过，如今又到了巴塞尔，呃，伙计？”

===Original===
“I’ve never been in Munich,” said Wenk; “he must have mistaken me for someone else.”

“Well, it’s all the same thing, anyhow! We’ll get those cars through, eh? By the way, can you stand me a trifle of ready on the job?”

“A fifty?” asked Wenk.

“Oh well, if it’s not inconvenient, I’d like two fifties.”

===Chinese===
“我从没去过慕尼黑。”文克说，“他准是把我认成别人了。”

“嗐，反正都是一回事！咱们把那两辆车弄过去，呃？对了，这活儿您能先支我一点儿现钱吗？”

“五十？”文克问。

“哦，要是方便的话，我想借两张五十的。”

===Original===
“One’s all I can spare at the moment,” said Wenk, pulling a fifty-mark note out of his waistcoat-pocket.

“You needn’t be afraid of showing your purse, even if it has a hole in it,” remarked the man.

“You wouldn’t buy any more with fifty out of my purse than you can with that one!”

“Well, all right; no offence! Where are you staying?”

===Chinese===
“眼下我匀得出一张。”文克说着，从马甲口袋里抽出一张五十马克的钞票。

“您就是钱包上有个窟窿，也不必怕掏出来给人看哪。”那人说。

“就算从我钱包里再掏五十，您能买到手的，也不会比您手里那张多！”

“行行行，别见怪！您住哪儿？”

===Original===
“In Barbarossa,” said Wenk, at a venture.

“Oh, if the folks there get hold of you, you won’t get out of their clutches, I can tell you! You go to the Black Bull. They’ll look after you properly there, and everything is arranged so that you can fly off as easily as these greenbacks will. Not a trace left behind!”

Next morning Wenk flew back to Munich. His trip had been successful, and the journey in the pure clean air, cold though it was in the upper regions, invigorated him. He felt as if he were gathering the threads together in his hand and they were about to form a vast and invisible net, and he, the fisherman, felt himself ready and able to drag it in.

===Chinese===
“巴巴罗萨（Barbarossa）。”文克随口诌了个名目。

“哦，那地方的人要是缠上您，您可就脱不了身喽，我告诉您！您去‘黑公牛’吧。那儿的人会把您伺候得妥妥帖帖，什么都安排得好好的——您想开溜，就跟这些票子一样容易，一点痕迹也不留！”

次日清晨，文克飞回慕尼黑。此行大有所获；高空虽寒，一路纯净清爽的空气却令他精神一振。他觉得，仿佛千条丝线正在他手中收拢，即将织成一张巨大而无形的网；而他，这个撒网人，自觉胸有成竹，只待收网。

---

===Original===
An hour before Wenk took up his stand at the grimy window of the iron-foundry opposite the Black Bull, the following conversation was carried on between Constance and Munich:

“Hulloa, Dr. Dringer speaking. Who is there?”

“Hulloa, this is Dr. Mabuse. What is it, please?”

===Chinese===
就在文克到“黑公牛”对面那家铸铁厂、在油腻污暗的窗后开始蹲守的一小时之前，康斯坦茨与慕尼黑之间进行了如下一通电话：

“喂，我是德林格医生（Dr. Dringer）。您是哪位？”

“喂，我是马布斯博士。有何见教？”

===Original===
“The invalid seems to be staying here. I am not quite certain yet that it is he, but I thought I recognized him. I am anxious for instructions.”

“That’s very strange. He was in Munich to my certain knowledge just about four o’clock yesterday. What time did you think you saw him, Doctor?”

“At half-past seven!”

“But the express does not leave until 7 p.m. and only reaches Lindau at 11 p.m. Even if he had used a car he could not possibly have reached Constance by half-past seven!”

===Chinese===
“那位‘病人’似乎下榻在本处。我还不能完全确定就是他，但我自觉认出来了。我亟盼指示。”

“这就怪了。据我所知，昨天四点钟他还明明在慕尼黑。医生，您觉得自己是在几点钟见到他的？”

“七点半！”

“可是快车要到晚上 7 点才发车，抵达林道已是 11 点。就算他坐汽车，7:30 也绝到不了康斯坦茨！”

===Original===
“It is possible that I may have been mistaken, but hardly likely. I can’t at once abandon the idea that it was the lunatic we are searching for.”

“Well, in any case, my dear colleague, prosecute your inquiries, and if you are convinced, use the safest means at your command.”

“You mean the strait-waistcoat, Doctor?”

“Certainly, for you know he is dangerous to the community. Have you any other news? What about those neurotic patients?”

===Chinese===
“我有可能看错，但可能性很小。我一时撇不下这个念头：那就是我们正在寻找的那个疯子。”

“那么，无论如何，亲爱的同行，请继续查证；一旦确信属实，就用您手上最稳妥的手段。”

“您是指拘束衣吧，博士？”

“正是，您知道，他对社会是个危险人物。还有别的消息吗？那几位神经症患者怎么样了？”

===Original===
“They are quite ready to go to the sanatorium, and they start tomorrow.”

“Good. That’s all, thank you. My best wishes to you, Doctor.”

Mabuse went up and down his room in considerable excitement. How could it be possible that the State Attorney, who was still in Munich at 4 p.m. should have been seen in Constance at 7:30 p.m.? Might not George be mistaken?

===Chinese===
“他们都已收拾停当，明天就动身去疗养院。”

“好。就这些，谢谢。祝您一切顺利，医生。”

马布斯在房间里来回踱步，激动难平。那位检察官下午 4 时还在慕尼黑，怎么可能在晚上 7:30 被人看见出现在康斯坦茨？会不会是格奥尔格弄错了？

===Original===
He dressed himself as a messenger and repaired to the Amandastrasse, where Wenk had his chambers. He rang his doorbell, and a servant opened to him.

“Can I see the State Attorney?” he asked.

“He is not at home. Give me the letter.”

===Chinese===
他乔装成送信人，前往文克设寓的阿曼达街（Amandastrasse）。他按响门铃，一个仆人开了门。

“我能见一见检察官先生吗？”他问。

“他不在家。信给我吧。”

===Original===
“I was to give it to him personally.﻿ ﻿… When will he be back?”

“I do not know.”

“Has he gone away for long, or shall I be able to hand him the letter this afternoon?”

===Chinese===
“这信我得当面交给本人。……他什么时候回来？”

“我不知道。”

“他出门要很久吗？还是今天下午我就能把信交到他手上？”

===Original===
“His honour did not leave word.”

“Ah, then I must rely on you,” said the messenger. “You will be sure to deliver the letter, won’t you?”

“Certainly, give it here,” and the man glanced at the address, but it was directed to the State Attorney, Dr. Müller, and he said, “You are making a mistake. Herr von Wenk is the barrister who lives here.”

===Chinese===
“老爷没有留话。”

“啊，那我只能仰仗您了。”信差说，“您一定会把信送到，是吧？”

“当然，给我吧。”仆人瞥了一眼信封上的地址，可信是写给检察官米勒博士（Dr. Müller）的，便说：“您弄错了。住在这儿的是律师冯·文克先生。”

===Original===
“Good heavens, so they’ve given me the wrong number! I always say, ‘Write it down, gentlemen.’ And so I’ve made a mistake here. Where does the gentleman I want live?”

“I don’t know him at all.”

“Well, there’s nothing for it but to go back! Good morning!”

===Chinese===
“天哪，他们把门牌号报错了！我老是跟人说：‘先生们，写下来。’这不，我就弄岔了。我要找的那位先生住在哪儿？”

“我根本不认识他。”

“唉，那只好回去喽！早安！”

===Original===
The pseudo-messenger went off, knowing only half he wanted to know. On the way, enlightenment came to him. “Of course,” he said to himself, “he must have gone by aeroplane, and I can guess why.﻿ ﻿…”

For an instant a mist swam before his eyes, so acutely did he feel this discovery of his. For the first time he measured his adversary’s powers. No one had ever used such means against him before. George had not yet sent off the discharged smugglers. Were they the reason of this hasty visit to Constance? Had his—Mabuse’s—band of watchers failed him? The matter became more difficult and dangerous every day, and recently several agents of the Foreign Legion had been discovered and arrested.

“If Wenk has the whole gang imprisoned,” thought Mabuse, “one of them might blab enough to bring the inquiry home to me, and then for the first time I shall no longer be safe. I must have him got out of the way.﻿ ﻿… Why did George let him go, if he had even a suspicion that it might be the lawyer? A plague upon the softheartedness that allowed him to escape us at Schleissheim! My life is not safe until he is wiped out of existence! I shall have to prepare for flight, and I will be off to the Swiss frontier unless I know for certain by eight o’clock tonight whether George is arrested or not. Where did George see him? If I only knew that, for it all depends upon that! I am consumed with impatience, and my hatred of this destroyer of my peace is burning me like a fever. Supposing I never reach my kingdom of Citopomar!”

===Chinese===
这个假信差走开了，想知道的事只探到了一半。路上，他豁然醒悟。“当然，”他自语道，“他一定是坐飞机去的，缘故我也能猜到几分……”

刹那间，一层薄雾在他眼前浮动——这一发现竟让他刺痛如斯。他头一回掂量出了对手的能耐。此前从没有人对他使过这样的手段。格奥尔格还没有把那批被开释的走私客送走。莫非他们就是这次康斯坦茨仓促之行的缘由？他——马布斯——豢养的那班眼线，难道失灵了？事情一天比一天棘手，一天比一天凶险，而近来外籍军团已有好几名密探被识破逮捕。

“倘若文克把整伙人都关进了大牢，”马布斯思忖，“其中哪一个嘴巴不严，漏出的东西就够把查办引到我头上，到那时我头一回不再安全。必须除掉他。……格奥尔格既然疑心那可能是位律师，为什么还放他走？该死的妇人之仁，竟让他在施莱斯海姆从我们手里溜掉！不把他从这个世界上抹去，我的性命就一天不得安稳！我得做好出逃的准备——今晚八点之前若不能确知格奥尔格是否被捕，我就动身去瑞士边境。格奥尔格是在哪儿见到他的？我要是知道就好了，一切都系在这上头！我心焦如焚，对这个毁我安宁的人的仇恨，正像热病一样炙烤着我。万一我永远到不了我的锡托波马尔王国（Citopomar）呢！”

===Original===
Then Mabuse went home again, carrying a parcel for himself under his arm. He must be prepared for all eventualities. Should his dwelling be already secretly watched by the police, he was a messenger who had something to deliver, and there were cigars in the parcel. But his chambers were empty, and there was nothing suspicious in the neighbourhood.

That evening he did not leave his house again. It was safer for him to see from the window who was coming to him than to find, on returning after absence, that someone had effected an entry and was watching at the window for him. He must be ready for anything that might happen!

He spent the evening in examining his finances. There was yet six months’ work to be accomplished in Germany before he had the amount he had decided would be necessary. There he knew the ground well, and anywhere else it would take at least a year to accomplish the same result. The languages he was conversant with necessitated his being in countries where German and English were known. Six months! The words throbbed in his brain, and the blood mounted to his heart. “I shall stay!” he said aloud in his lonely room, and it seemed as if the defiance these words awoke rang through him like the blow of the hammer on the anvil.

===Chinese===
随后马布斯回了家，胳膊底下夹着一个给自己捎的包裹。他必须对一切不测有所防备。万一住处已受警方暗中监视，他就是一个有物投递的信差，包裹里装的是雪茄。但他的寓所空无一人，左近也没有丝毫可疑迹象。

当天晚上他没有再出门。坐在窗前看清来客是谁，总比外出归来时发现有人已破门而入、正守在窗边等他来得安全。他必须对一切可能的变故严阵以待！

这个晚上，他用来盘点自己的财务。还得在德国干上六个月，才能挣足他认定必需的数目。在德国，他熟门熟路；换到任何别的地方，要取得同样的成果，至少得花上一年。他通晓的语言，也限定他只能待在通用德语和英语的国度。六个月！这三个字在他脑子里突突直跳，热血涌上心头。“我留下！”他在空寂的房间里朗声说道；这几个字激起的决绝，仿佛铁锤砸在砧上的轰鸣，铮铮贯穿他的全身。

===Original===
Next morning at half-past seven there was an urgent telephone call from Constance. “Doctor Dringer speaking! I am sorry, but I fear I have misled my esteemed colleague. There is no further trace to be seen. Everything is in readiness for departure, and the other patients are prepared for their journey.”

“It was a pity, Doctor. Ring up again this evening!”

“You swine!” Mabuse growled between his teeth at his window, looking in the direction of Wenk’s chambers. “If it were only for this half-hour of uncertainty, you should pay for it with your life! The first attempt failed through a mere accident. There shall be no accident the next time!”

===Chinese===
次日清晨七点半，康斯坦茨来了紧急电话。“我是德林格医生！很抱歉，恐怕我误导致爱的同行了。眼下再看不到任何踪影。出发的一切均已就绪，其余的病人也做好了上路准备。”

“可惜了，医生。今晚再来电话！”

“你这头猪！”马布斯伫立窗前，朝着文克寓所的方向咬牙低吼，“哪怕只为这半个钟头的悬心，你也得拿命来偿！头一回失手，纯属偶然。下一回，绝不容再有偶然！”

===Original===
Mabuse left his house on foot, went to one of the fashionable hotels, and asked for the general manager, Herr Hungerbühler. Yes, he was there, and would be found in Room 115, he was told.

When Mabuse entered the room unannounced, it was empty. “Spoerri!” he called softly. Then a cupboard door opened and Spoerri came out.

“Wenk seems to be in Constance. George has just telephoned to me. Look after the matter. How is Cara getting on in prison?”

===Chinese===
马布斯步行出门，走进一家时髦旅馆，求见经理洪格比勒先生（Herr Hungerbühler）。他得到的答复是：在的，可以在 115 号房间找到他。

马布斯不待通报便走进房去，房里空无一人。“施佩里（Spoerri）！”他轻声唤道。随即一扇柜门打开，施佩里从里面走了出来。

“文克似乎在康斯坦茨。格奥尔格刚才来了电话。这件事你去料理。卡拉在狱中怎么样？”

===Original===
“It would be safer if she were out of the way altogether. Dead men tell no tales!”

“No, I have already told you once, she is safer alive than dead,” answered Mabuse quickly.

“In any case, I have got one of the warders under my control.”

===Chinese===
“若能让她彻底出局，才更安全。死人不开口！”

“不，这话我已对你说过一回：她活着比死了更保险。”马布斯当即答道。

“无论如何，我已经拿住了牢里的一名看守。”

===Original===
“Why?”

“To contrive her escape, if she’s to be allowed to live!”

“Fool!” cried Mabuse angrily. “I tell you she is safer where she is. If they were to break open her mouth with a crowbar she would never say anything. Stop talking such d﻿—﻿—﻿d nonsense. She is to come out when I leave Europe, not before! I came to tell you that I give you a month to get rid of Wenk. I make it so long, so that it may be safely undertaken. Make a note of the date, for he’s not to live a day longer than that!” and Mabuse went off, without a word of farewell.

===Chinese===
“做什么用？”

“万一容她活下去，就安排她越狱！”

“蠢货！”马布斯怒喝，“我告诉你，她待在原地最安全。就是用撬棍撬开她的嘴，她也绝不会吐露半个字。别再讲这种混账废话。等我离开欧洲时再放她出来，此前不行！我来是要告诉你：限你一个月除掉文克。把期限放得这么宽，是为求办得稳妥。把日子记下来——他多活一天也不行！”说罢马布斯扬长而去，连一句道别的话也没有。

## Translator's Note / 译者说明

===Original===
Since there is no actual equivalent of the position of *Staatsanwalt* it is almost impossible to find an English rendering that conveys its full meaning. In part the duties assimilate those of the Public Prosecutor, but in England we can hardly conceive of an official of high judicial status personally identifying himself with his cases to the extent of disguising himself and playing the part of a detective. Wenk's position appears to combine some of the offices which would here be delegated to various individuals acting more or less independently as subordinates to a higher and single authority. He was a barrister and an LL.D., and a person of some influence, however, as his threat to the governor of the women's prison, and his treatment of the night editor prove.

In accordance with modern German usage I have adhered to the original in dropping the "von," when intimates of the same social class are speaking to or of each other, maintaining it in the more formal intercourse and the reports tendered by social inferiors.

Readers should note that in the currency now prevailing, the amounts staked by Hull and his friends would be tens of thousands, and his total losses would run into many millions.

===Chinese===
由于「检察官」（*Staatsanwalt*）一职在英语中并无真正对等的职位，要想找到一个能传达其全部含义的英文译名，几乎是不可能的事。就职责而言，其一部分与公诉人（Public Prosecutor）相近，但在英格兰，我们很难设想一位具有崇高司法地位的官员，会亲自投入自己的案件到这般地步——竟然乔装改扮，扮起侦探的角色。文克的职位，似乎把在此间通常会分派给若干人等担任的职务合于一身；这些人各自行事，颇多独立，同为更高一元权威的属下。不过，他身为出庭律师（barrister），兼有法学博士学位（LL.D.），也是个颇具影响的人物——他对女子监狱典狱长的威吓，以及他对夜班编辑的处置，便是明证。

依照现代德语用法，笔者遵循原文处理：同一社会阶层中关系亲近的人彼此称呼或谈及对方时，略去「冯」（von）字；而在较为正式的交往场合，以及社会地位较低者呈递的报告中，则保留该字。

读者应留意：按现今通行的币值折算，赫尔及其友人所下的赌注当以数万计，而他的全部输金则高达数百万。

## V / 五

===Original===
For the next few evenings Wenk did not visit any gaming-house. As his own chauffeur, dressed in leather cap and coat, he drove round the city, bringing his car to a standstill before one or other of its well-known resorts, and observing, from the security of the driver’s seat, the people who entered or left it.

On one occasion, when he was driving to the first of these houses and proceeding slowly along the Dienerstrasse, he was held up by a block in the traffic. While he was waiting, he saw in a tobacconist’s, just in front of which his course was arrested, something which caused his pulses to beat at double time. It was he, the sandy-bearded man! He had his back turned and was buying cigars, but it was certainly he! He was making his choice slowly and carefully as if he defied the danger of being recognized. There was a car in front of the door. Wenk examined it closely, but it was unfamiliar to him. He copied its number down.

Once the chauffeur left it, in order to do something to the back of the car, Wenk, who was behind him, called to him; the man looked up, but put his hands to his mouth as if to signify that he was dumb.

===Chinese===
接下来有几个晚上，文克没有再踏进任何一家赌场。他自己充当司机，戴上皮帽、穿上皮外套，驾车在城里兜转，把车停在这家或那家著名销金窟门前，从稳当的驾驶座上，观察着进进出出的人影。

有一回，他正驾车前往其中最近的一家，沿着迪纳大街缓缓行驶，却被一阵车流堵住了去路。等待之际，他看见正前方拦住他去路的那家烟草店里有一幕情景，令他脉搏狂跳，加倍搏动。是他，正是那个沙色胡须的男人！他背对着街，正在买雪茄，可那分明就是他！他慢条斯理、仔仔细细地挑选着，仿佛全然不惧被人认出来的危险。店门口停着一辆汽车。文克把它细细打量了一番，却并不认得。他把车牌号抄了下来。

有一回那司机下了车，走到车后去摆弄什么；文克在他身后喊了一声，那人抬起头来，却把双手捂在嘴上，像是要表示自己是个哑巴。

===Original===
The man in the shop took up his parcel and turned to the door, but the face he disclosed to Wenk was one he had never seen before. People pushed between him and Wenk, so that he saw him for a moment only. Just then the block was released, the string of cars drove on, and the one in front of him set off at a bound, as if hastening to get away from pursuit.

Wenk, however, could not shake off his conviction. He followed. As soon as the other car was free of the rest, it increased its speed, and bore off to the Maximilianstrasse. Wenk was unable to keep up with it. The street was empty throughout its length, and when he had reached the square at the end he saw that the car in front was turning down Wiedenmeierstrasse. He still followed, the distance between them always increasing, but in the moonlight he never lost sight of his quarry throughout the length of the street. When he reached the Max Joseph Bridge, he saw that the car in front was making use of the wide square on the other bank of the Isar to make a detour, and suddenly, with its engines throbbing, it came back across the bridge and drove past him. It then drove again down the Wiedenmeierstrasse, which it had just ascended.

This was certainly a suspicious circumstance, and Wenk did all he could to gain upon the other car, and turned round while still on the bridge. Again the other turned into the Maximilianstrasse, and as it was now teeming with traffic, Wenk was able to bring his own vehicle close up.

===Chinese===
店里的那人拿起纸包，转身朝门口走来，可他露给文克看的那张脸，却是一副他从未见过的面孔。行人在他与文克之间来回穿行，文克只看了他片刻。就在这时，堵塞的车流疏通了，一长串汽车向前驶去，他前面那辆车猛地一蹿而出，仿佛急着甩脱追撵似的。

然而文克怎么也摆脱不掉心中的确信。他跟了上去。前面那辆车一脱开车流，便加快速度，拐向马克西米利安大街。文克追赶不上。整条大街空空荡荡，待他赶到街尽头的广场，只见前车正拐进维德迈尔大街。他仍旧跟着，两车距离越拉越大，但在月光下，沿着整条长街，他始终没有跟丢目标。等他赶到马克斯·约瑟夫桥，只见前车正借伊萨尔河对岸的开阔广场兜了个圈子，突然引擎突突狂吼着折回桥来，从他身旁疾驰而过，随即又驶下它方才上来的那条维德迈尔大街。

这情形实在可疑，文克竭尽全力追赶前车，人还在桥上便调转了车头。前车再次拐进马克西米利安大街，此时街上车流正密，文克得以把自己的车紧紧跟上。

===Original===
The strange car came to a halt outside a theatre of varieties. Wenk sprang from his car, and when the stranger left his and, turning his back on Wenk, entered the theatre, he felt the same overpowering conviction that it really was the blond—it could be no other.

In feverish excitement Wenk pushed past the people and got into the theatre. He saw that he would overtake the stranger in the foyer, so he waited among the rest, certain that the other would have to pass by him.… But when he did, Wenk saw a broad, clean-shaven man, with a heavy mouth and large staring eyes. The face was quite unknown to him, and coolly and indifferently the large eyes glanced at him. Disappointed and disgusted, Wenk passed by, intending to go out to his waiting car.

A few late arrivals detained him in the proximity of the cloakroom. It was exactly eight o’clock, and the signal that the curtain was about to go up was already being given. At this moment Wenk realized what a difficulty there would be and what excitement would be created were he to arrest his man then and there. Unwilling to let his quarry escape him, he turned once more, and then saw the other disengaging himself from a group of men who were pushing forward to the pit, making his way quietly to the left-hand entrance to the boxes. This led to the five ground-floor boxes, as Wenk knew. He quickly made up his mind and bought a seat in one of them for himself. It was the last to be had, and the plan showed him that each box held five persons.

===Chinese===
那辆陌生的汽车在一家杂耍剧院门外停了下来。文克跳下车。当那陌生人下了自己的车、背朝文克走进剧院时，他心里又涌起同样压倒一切的确信：正是那个金发男人——不可能是别人。

文克激动得近乎狂热，从人群里挤进了剧院。他看出自己会在前厅赶上那陌生人，便混在众人中间等着，断定对方必定要从他身边经过……可当那人走过时，文克看到的却是一个身宽体阔、脸刮得精光的男人，生着一张厚重的嘴和一双凸出的大眼睛。这张脸他全然陌生，那双大眼睛冷淡漠然地朝他瞥了一下。文克又失望又厌恶，径直走了过去，打算出去回到自己等候着的汽车里。

几个迟到的看客把他耽搁在了衣帽间近旁。此时恰好八点，即将开演的信号已经发出。就在这一刻，文克意识到，倘若当场就把这人逮捕，将会遇到多大的难题、掀起多大的骚动。他不愿让猎物从手中溜走，便又转过身来，恰好看清那人正从一群争着往正厅挤的男人中脱身出来，悄悄走向左手边的包厢入口。文克知道，这条通道只通向底层的五个包厢。他当机立断，在其中一间包厢里给自己买了个座位。那是最后的一个空位，座位图上显示，每个包厢容纳五人。

===Original===
Going back to his car, he crept inside, and there changed into evening dress. From the box-office he telephoned his chauffeur to come for the car, and then returned to his box.

It was dark when he entered it, and he tried, but without success, to distinguish the stranger’s features in the dim light. When the light went up again he was equally unsuccessful in tracing him anywhere among the twenty ladies and gentlemen sitting in the lower boxes. It was altogether incomprehensible. This corridor led to the five boxes only, and they were five or six feet above the pit. How had his quarry escaped him?

Now thoroughly uneasy, Wenk hastened to the street to see whether the stranger’s car was still there. To his relief he found it there.

He breathed more freely, and turned to go to his own car and remain there until he could pursue the other, but as he noticed the strange car again, he saw that it had a taximeter. He had looked at the car well before, and was certain that it had no register. Without further reflection, Wenk approached the chauffeur, saying, “Are you disengaged?”

“Yes, sir,” said the chauffeur.

===Chinese===
他回到自己的汽车旁，钻进车厢，在里面换上了晚礼服。他从售票处打电话叫他的司机来把车取走，随后便回到自己的包厢。

他走进包厢时里面一片漆黑，他在昏暗中竭力辨认那陌生人的容貌，却徒劳无功。灯光再度亮起时，他想在楼下包厢里那二十位先生女士中间寻到此人，同样一无所获。这简直匪夷所思。这条走廊只通向那五个包厢，而且包厢高出正厅五六英尺。他的猎物是怎么从他手里逃脱的？

此刻文克彻底不安起来，急忙赶到街上，去看那陌生人的车还在不在。令他宽慰的是，车还在原地。

他呼吸顺畅了些，转身朝自己的汽车走去，打算守在那儿，直到能继续跟踪对方为止。可当他再次留意那辆陌生的汽车时，发现车上装了计程器。先前他已把这辆车看得分明，确信它原先并无此物。文克不加多想，径直走向司机，开口道：“您有空吗？”

“有空，先生。”司机答道。

===Original===
Wenk entered the car, giving his own address. During the drive he intended to consider his next move; then it suddenly occurred to him that the man, who had been dumb when in the Dienerstrasse, answered instantly when spoken to here.

The automobile drove on; a sweetish scent pervaded its interior, which affected Wenk’s mucous membranes.

Something *was* wrong then! “A little while ago he was dumb, now he can talk,” reflected Wenk. “Before it was a private car; now it is plying for hire like a taxicab. What is it that smells so strongly?” His nostrils and eyelids seemed to be on fire.

In order to decide what the odour was, Wenk drew one or two deep breaths. Then he tried to open the window, for he found the smell unbearable. What *did* it smell of? He raised his arm, but he saw that it would not rise to its full extent; it did not obey his will. At the same moment it seemed as if a heavy block were pressing on his eyes. Then dread seized him in a fiery grasp. No longer capable of resistance, he began to bellow furiously, flung himself down and kicked with his foot at the handle of the door, but without being able to find it.

For some few seconds he lay on the floor of the car, with occasional gleams of consciousness. Then these were finally extinguished, and complete insensibility overtook him, while the car continued its mad race through the streets.

===Chinese===
文克上了车，报了自己家的地址。行车途中，他本想盘算下一步的行动；随即他猛然想起，这个在迪纳大街上装聋作哑的男人，此刻在这里被人一问便应声作答。

汽车继续行驶；一股甜腻的气味在车厢里弥漫，刺激着文克的黏膜。

果然有蹊跷！“方才他还是个哑巴，现在却能说话了。”文克心中暗想，“先前这是一辆私人汽车，现在却像出租车一般揽客营业。这么冲鼻的到底是什么气味？”他的鼻孔和眼皮火烧火燎起来。

为了辨明这是什么气味，文克深深吸了一两口气。接着他想打开车窗，因为这气味已令他无法忍受。这到底的是什么味儿？他抬起手臂，却发现手臂抬不到位；它不听他的使唤了。就在同一瞬间，仿佛一块沉重的压板压上了他的双眼。随即，恐惧像烧红的铁钳般攫住了他。他再也无力抵抗，狂怒地吼叫起来，扑倒下去，用脚去踹车门的把手，却怎么也找不着它。

有那么几秒钟，他躺在车厢的地板上，意识时时闪过几线微光。随后这些微光也终于熄灭，彻底的失去知觉吞没了他，而汽车仍在街头继续着它的狂奔。

===Original===
The chauffeur drove with the unconscious form of the drugged State Attorney throughout the darkness to Schleissheim. There he propped him up on a bench, and then drove back to Munich. In the Xenienstrasse he halted before a residence standing alone. Upon a brass plate might be read:

> **Dr. Mabuse**,
> Neurologist.

A man of massive build, covered by a fur coat, came rapidly out of the house and through the little front garden to the car. “He is lying in the Schleissheim Park,” said the chauffeur. “Here is the notebook you wanted.”

===Chinese===
司机载着被麻醉后不省人事的检察官，一路穿过黑暗开到施莱斯海姆。他把检察官扶坐在一条长椅上，随后折回慕尼黑。在克塞尼恩大街，他在一幢孤零零的宅子前停了车。门上一块铜牌上写着：

> **马布斯博士**，
> 神经科医生。

一个身材魁梧的男人裹着皮大衣，快步走出宅子，穿过小小的前园来到车旁。“他躺在施莱斯海姆公园里。”司机说，“这是您要的记事本。”

===Original===
“Did you remove the gas-flask from the car?”

“Yes, Doctor.”

“Drive on!”

But at this moment a woman, closely muffled up, came out of the darkness and stepped towards the car. She held on to the door, murmuring beseechingly, “Dearest!”

Mabuse turned in annoyance. “What do you want? Are you begging?”

The woman answered him gently and sadly. “Yes, begging—for love!”

===Chinese===
“你把车上的毒气瓶取走了吗？”

“取了，博士。”

“开车！”

可就在这时，一个裹得严严实实的女人从黑暗中走出来，朝汽车走近。她扶住车门，哀告似的低声唤道：“最亲爱的！”

马布斯恼怒地转过身。“你要干什么？你是在乞求吗？”

女人温柔而哀伤地答道：“是的，乞求——乞求爱情！”

===Original===
“You know my answer.”

“But remember the past. Why should this be?” implored the voice.

Mabuse, in wrath, exclaimed, “The past is past. Your part is to obey. My orders are clear, and there is nothing between Yes and No. You have heard from George what my wishes are. Drive on, George!”

He was already in the car. The woman fell back to the garden railings, covered herself up again, and called after the retreating car, “But if I cannot stop loving you?”

===Chinese===
“你知道我的答复。”

“可你想想从前。为什么竟会这样？”那声音恳求道。

马布斯勃然大怒，喝道：“过去的事已经过去了。你的本分是服从。我的命令清清楚楚，是与否之间没有任何余地。我的意愿你已从乔治那里听明白了。开车，乔治！”

他已坐进车里。女人跌退到花园的栏杆旁，重新裹紧了身子，朝着远去的汽车喊道：“可要是我止不住爱你呢？”

===Original===
Then a second car pulled up close beside her. A man sprang out and advanced towards her, saying threateningly, “What do you want here? Oh, oh! it’s you, Cara! Well, have you spoken to the Doctor?”

She nodded despairingly.

“There’s nothing to be done. His will is like a sledgehammer, therefore don’t oppose it. So long! I must go after him.”

And Cara Carozza gathered her disguising garments about her and went away in grief, downcast and heavyhearted, to sacrifice herself for him.

===Chinese===
这时第二辆汽车在她身旁停了下来。一个男人跳下车，朝她逼近，语气咄咄逼人：“你在这儿想干什么？哦，哦！是你啊，卡拉！怎么样，你跟博士说了吗？”

她绝望地点了点头。

“没辙了。他的意志就像一柄大锤，别去顶撞它。回头见！我得追他去了。”

卡拉·卡罗扎裹紧她那一身伪装的衣裳，悲悲切切地走开了，垂头丧气、心事重重，为了他去牺牲自己。

---

===Original===
“Where are we?” inquired Mabuse through the speaking-tube.

“Past Landsberg!” answered George.

The plans in Mabuse’s head succeeded one another as rapidly as the trees in a forest in which he wandered continually further. Ever more steps to climb, more gulfs to cross! Were they really plans after all? Were they not dreams? he asked himself, suddenly checking the thoughts that were racing through his mind.

===Chinese===
“我们到哪儿了？”马布斯对着传话管问道。

“过了兰茨贝格了！”乔治答道。

马布斯脑子里的计划一个接一个地更迭，快得如同他越走越深的那座森林里掠过的树木。永远有更多的台阶要攀、更深的沟壑要跨！这些当真是计划吗？难道不是梦吗？他问自己，随即猛然勒住了在脑海中飞驰的思绪。

===Original===
“Five million Swiss francs are now worth about twenty-five million lire, i.e. five million Italian five-lire pieces. Each of these weighs twenty grams. Five million, will that be enough? It’s a good idea, for the gain on every five-lire piece which I buy at today’s rate with Swiss francs is four francs; therefore the total gain will be four million Swiss francs. Against that the costs are thirty percent. Good! Each one, I said, weighs twenty grams. Now, how many kilograms are there in five million times twenty grams? A hundred million grams? Why cannot I think out these simple calculations clearly? Am I afraid of anything?”

Yes, there again he found himself in another forest. “Am I afraid, really afraid? If I am, I shall come to grief. After all, who is Hull? Who is Wenk? What absurdity! *I*… afraid?”

He collected his ideas, and sent these thoughts packing.

“A hundred million grams make a hundred thousand kilograms. According to the district he is in, a smuggler can carry from ten to fifteen kilos every time. How many men am I employing in this work alone? The whole amount must be brought from Italy to the Southern Tyrol and thence to Switzerland within a month. The Austrian frontiers are easier, even if I have to employ twice the number. Spoerri reckoned the risk to be only three percent, according to the police reports, as against ten percent by Lake Constance or the Ticino frontier, where the Customs officials, even in peacetime, used to regard everybody with suspicion.”

===Chinese===
“五百万瑞士法郎如今约值两千五百万里拉，也就是五百万枚意大利五里拉硬币。每枚重二十克。五百万枚，够不够？这主意不错，因为按今日的汇率用瑞士法郎买进，每枚五里拉可赚四法郎；这样总利润就是四百万瑞士法郎。而成本要占百分之三十。好！我说了，每枚重二十克。那么，五百万乘二十克是多少公斤？一亿克？这么简单的账我为什么算不清楚？我是害怕什么了吗？”

是啊，他发觉自己又置身于另一座森林之中。“我害怕吗，当真害怕？若是害怕，我就要栽跟头了。说到底，赫尔算什么？文克算什么？多么荒唐！*我*……会害怕？”

他收拢心神，把这些念头统统打发走了。

“一亿克就是十万公斤。看地界不同，一个走私犯每次能背十到十五公斤。光这一项活计我就用了多少人？全部数目必须在一个月之内从意大利运到南蒂罗尔，再从那里运进瑞士。奥地利边界好走些，哪怕我得用上双倍的人手。据警察方面的报告，施佩里估算风险只有百分之三；相比之下，走博登湖或提契诺边界要有百分之十，那边的海关人员即便在太平年月，也惯于对每个人都疑神疑鬼。”

===Original===
Mabuse’s imagination threatened to run away with him again. Should he not try to sleep?

“Where are we?” he called through the speaking-tube.

“At Buchloe!” was the reply.

The distance from Buchloe to Röthenbach was eighteen kilometres.

“That will take two hours,” he reflected; “then we shall do it comfortably. At 2 p.m. we must be at Schachen, and before that we meet Spoerri at Opfenbach and Pesch on the Lindau Hill. After that we shall be practically in Schachen, and there will be no chance of sleep.”

But he could not regain control of himself. Wenk’s attempt at pursuit oppressed him. In the Palace Hotel he had only had ten minutes’ start of him.

===Chinese===
马布斯的想象力眼看又要脱缰而去。他何不试着睡上一觉？

“我们到哪儿了？”他对着传话管喊道。

“到布赫洛厄了！”是回答。

从布赫洛厄到勒滕巴赫有十八公里。

“那得走两个钟头。”他盘算着，“这样正好从容不迫。下午两点我们必须到沙兴，在那之前要在奥普芬巴赫和林道山见施佩里和佩施。此后就等于已经到了沙兴，再没有睡觉的工夫了。”

可他无法重新控制住自己。文克那番追逐令他胸口发闷。在宫殿饭店，他不过比文克早走十分钟。

===Original===
He did not want to acknowledge it, even to himself. He began to reckon that to smuggle five million five-lire pieces from Italy and the Southern Tyrol through Vorarlberg to Switzerland would require two hundred and fifty people on each frontier. That was five hundred men for the smuggling alone. If he reckoned the buyers and the Bolzano collectors as well, it was really seven hundred. With their families he might consider that he was keeping, roughly, about four thousand people. That was a small township. A little town lay in his grasp, pledged to evil purposes, working in dark nights, stealing along mysterious byways, avoiding the revolvers of Customs officials, working stealthily, steadily, at his will. They had no thought either, but of him, the owner of the money, the employer and dictator, the possessor of all power and force. They ventured their lives for him, but he had never seen one of them. How would it be if he were to see and converse with them, appearing abruptly before them when they were in the midst of their enterprise? They would imagine themselves to be caught, until they should have realized that it was he, their master and employer, who stood amongst them.

Four thousand people; it was a whole district. But in Citopomar it would be something very different when he traversed the virgin forests and had the Botocudos and all the other tribes directly under his thumb, and had left this insignificant beggarly little continent behind him! There his word alone would be law. There, in Citopomar, the dream of his boyhood would be fulfilled—a dream which had already begun to be realized on that large and desolate island which lay cradled in the ocean yonder. There he had owned men; there wild Nature was his alone; as a conqueror he had sailed the waters; his blood and sinews governed men; his will was imposed on Nature; the palms of his planting yielded him a luxuriant growth of wealth—sheer gold. He could despise it because he did not need it, for there he was free, free as a king, a deity!…

But the war had driven him out of his Paradise and sent him back to the despised continent of Europe. He could not endure life in these European countries. He felt as if he were confined in a pasture, eating grass like dumb, senseless cattle ate their predestined, accustomed grass. No, he could not live thus! Therefore by undermining State organization he was preparing a State for himself, with laws which he alone made, with powers vested in himself over the souls and bodies of men. By means of his accomplices he was collecting the money wherewith to establish his empire in the primeval forests of Brazil, the Empire of Citopomar.

===Chinese===
他不愿承认这一点，哪怕是对自己。他开始盘算：要把五百万枚五里拉硬币从意大利和南蒂罗尔经福拉尔贝格偷运进瑞士，每条边界需要二百五十人。单是走私就得五百人。若把收货人和博尔扎诺的集货人也算上，实际上是七百人。连他们的家眷一起算，他养着的大约就有四千人。那俨然是一座小镇。一座小小的城镇攥在他的手心里，效命于邪恶的目的，在黑夜里干活，沿着神秘的僻径潜行，躲开海关人员的左轮手枪，依他的意志隐秘而持续地劳作着。他们心里也别无他念，只有他——钱的主人、雇主兼独裁者、一切权柄与力量的占有者。他们为他舍命冒险，他却连其中一个人也没见过。倘若他去见见他们、同他们说说话，在他们干得正酣时突然出现在面前，那会是如何光景？他们会以为自己落网了，直到认出来站在他们中间的正是他，他们的主人和雇主。

四千人；那是一整个地区。可在锡托波马尔，情形将截然不同——他将穿越原始森林，把博托库多人和其他所有部族直接捏在掌心，把这块微不足道、乞丐般寒酸的小小大陆甩在身后！在那里，他一言即是法律。在那里，在锡托波马尔，他少年时代的梦想将得偿所愿——这个梦想，早已在那座安卧于远方大洋之上的荒凉大岛上开始变为现实。在那里，他曾拥有人；在那里，蛮荒的自然只属于他一人；他曾以征服者的姿态巡弋水域；他的血与筋骨统治着人们；他的意志强加于自然；他亲手栽种的棕榈为他捧出繁茂的财富——纯纯粹粹的黄金。他能鄙薄这一切，因为他不需要它，在那里他是自由的，自由得像一位国王，一位神祇！……

可战争把他逐出了他的乐园，将他扔回这遭他鄙夷的欧洲大陆。他无法忍受在这些欧洲国家里生活。他觉得自己像被圈在牧场上，像那些喑哑无知的牲口一样，嚼着命中注定、习以为常的草料。不，他不能这样活着！于是他暗中蛀蚀国家组织，为自己经营另一个国家——法律由他独自制定，统辖众人灵魂与肉体的权柄尽归于他。他借着手下的党羽敛聚钱财，以便在巴西的原始丛林里建立他的帝国——锡托波马尔帝国。

===Original===
He was self-sufficing. What were men to him? He scattered them at will. Yonder, however, in the future, in Citopomar, there would be none who *could* oppose him.

By degrees, as these thoughts ran away with him, Mabuse fell asleep, his limbs reclining on the cushions and his fantasies soaring above all material things. For two long hours he slept, sunk in the darkness of his dreams.

Then it seemed as if a little hammer were striking his skull, always on the same spot. It was annoying, and it was unheard of. He had only two hours between Buchloe and Röthenbach in which to sleep. Who had dared to strike his head with this hammer?

All at once he was wide awake. The hammer was the whistle of the speaking-tube.

===Chinese===
他自给自足。人于他何有？他随心所欲地将人驱散。而在那边，将来，在锡托波马尔，将没有任何人*能够*与他作对。

这些思绪载着他越走越远，渐渐地，马布斯睡着了，四肢瘫在软垫上，幻想翱翔于一切有形之物之上。他沉沉睡了漫长的两个钟头，深陷于梦的黑暗之中。

随后，仿佛有一柄小锤在敲击他的颅骨，总是敲在同一个地方。这真恼人，也是闻所未闻的事。从布赫洛厄到勒滕巴赫，他只有两个钟头可睡。谁敢用这柄锤子敲他的头？

他猛然完全醒了过来。那柄锤子是传话管的哨音。

===Original===
“Well?” called out Mabuse.

“There is a car behind us.”

“What are its marks?”

“There is a grey patch on the right lamp.”

“What is the time?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”马布斯喊道。

“后面有辆车。”

“有什么记号？”

“右车灯上有一块灰斑。”

“现在几点了？”

===Original===
“Half-past one.”

“And where are we?”

“Two kilometres from Röthenbach.”

“Pull up! It is Spoerri.”

The car stopped, and immediately its lights went out, and so did those of the car which followed. Then it drove close up and stopped. There was a cough heard.

===Chinese===
“一点半。”

“我们到哪儿了？”

“离勒滕巴赫两公里。”

“停车！是施佩里。”

汽车停了下来，车灯随即熄灭，后面那辆车的灯也灭了。接着那辆车驶到近旁，停住。黑暗里传来一声咳嗽。

===Original===
“Come here!” said Mabuse.

Someone came out of the darkness. Mabuse had drawn the revolver from his coat-pocket. The car-driver turned on a small electric lamp, and its gleam disclosed a man wrapped in a large cloak.

“Spoerri?”

“Yes, Doctor.”

The pistol was returned to its place.

===Chinese===
“过来！”马布斯说。

有人从黑暗中走了出来。马布斯已从大衣口袋里拔出左轮手枪。司机拧亮一盏小电灯，微光里显出一个裹在宽大斗篷里的男人。

“施佩里？”

“是我，博士。”

手枪放回了原处。

===Original===
“Spoerri, wait here a quarter of an hour, or else drive to Schachen by another route. You must arrive shortly after me, between half-past one and two o’clock. I have decided on some great changes that I want to tell you of before we go to Switzerland. Anything else?”

“Everything is in order. I have another hundred kilos of cerium in the car.”

“Good. Between half-past one and two o’clock!”

They drove on. As their road approached the Austrian frontier, which was patrolled by officials, their lights were extinguished for a while, but in Schlachters they shone out again, and the village was soon left behind them.

Halfway to Lindau, where forest and hill meet, they stopped again.

===Chinese===
“施佩里，你在这儿等一刻钟，要不就绕另一条路开到沙兴去。你必须在我之后不久到达，一点半到两点之间。我已定下几项重大变动，想在去瑞士之前告诉你。还有别的事吗？”

“一切就绪。我车上又装了一百公斤铈。”

“很好。一点半到两点之间！”

他们继续赶路。当道路临近有巡逻人员把守的奥地利边界时，车灯熄灭了一阵，到了施拉赫特斯又重新亮起，村子很快被甩在了身后。

在去林道的半路上，森林与山丘相接的地方，他们又停了下来。

===Original===
“Anybody there?”

“No, Doctor.”

“Not Pesch?”

“I don’t see anybody.”

Mabuse quitted the car impatiently.

“I will punish him for this. I *will* have my people punctual!”

===Chinese===
“有人吗？”

“没有，博士。”

“佩施不在？”

“我谁也没看见。”

马布斯不耐烦地下了车。

“我要为此罚他。我*非要*我的人准时不可！”

===Original===
He waited on, and the minutes crept by. Mabuse slapped his thigh angrily. To keep him waiting! That a smuggler should dare to do such a thing! He was consumed with impatience, and felt as if his dignity were impeached. That a smuggler should keep him, the master, waiting!

Five minutes later a car, with faint lights, issued from the junction road and stopped on the highway.

“Pesch!” exclaimed Mabuse.

A man turned from the open car.

“Yes, Doctor, here I am. It is Pesch.”

“It is 1:45 a.m., and you were due at 1:35.”

===Chinese===
他等着，时间一分钟一分钟地爬过去。马布斯气愤地拍着大腿。竟敢让他等！一个走私犯竟敢做出这种事！焦躁啃噬着他，他只觉得自己的尊严受到了冒犯。一个走私犯，竟敢让他这个主人干等！

五分钟后，一辆亮着昏暗车灯的汽车从岔路上驶出，停在了大路上。

“佩施！”马布斯喊道。

一个男人从敞篷车里转过身来。

“是，博士，我在这儿。是佩施。”

“现在是凌晨1:45，你本该1:35到。”

===Original===
“Oh, a matter of ten minutes doesn’t count. *I’ve* had to wait often enough!” answered the voice in the darkness in a defiant tone.

“If I had a horsewhip here I’d cudgel you soundly. Ten minutes mean fifteen kilometres advance upon a pursuer, you fool! You are earning two thousand marks from me tonight.”

The other answered boldly, “And with my help *you* are earning twenty thousand!”

“Five hundred thousand more likely, you blockhead,” said Mabuse; “but that’s nothing to do with you. The only question here is who is master and who servant.”

“You are not my master,” said the other.

“I am not?… you say so, do you?” he thundered. “Very well, you can get along home. I don’t want you any more—never any more!”

===Chinese===
“哦，十分钟的事儿，不值一提。*我*等人的时候可多了去了！”黑暗中那声音带着挑衅的口吻答道。

“我要是手边有根马鞭，非好好抽你一顿不可。十分钟，就意味着比追你的人多跑出十五公里，你这蠢货！今晚你要从我这里挣两千马克。”

对方硬邦邦地回答：“靠了我的帮忙，*你*挣的是两万！”

“更像是五十万，你这呆子。”马布斯说，“不过这与你不相干。这里唯一的问题是：谁是主人，谁是仆人。”

“你不是我的主人。”对方说。

“我不是？……你竟这么说，是吗？”他雷霆般喝道。“很好，你滚回家去吧。我不再用你了——永远不再用！”

===Original===
He turned to his car and got in; then said hastily in a threatening tone, “If you feel inclined to send any anonymous information to the authorities, you’ll remember that there is a fir-tree growing in the wood, and there’s room for you to hang there like your colleague Haim. Drive on, George!”

The car started off again.

In the neighbourhood of Schachen, where stately houses with upper stories made cars appear less striking, they found a park gate open, and without any difficulty George found his way along the dark drive leading to the villa. The lights were extinguished.

While Mabuse and George were still standing on the doorstep Spoerri arrived.

When Mabuse opened the door and turned on the light, he saw that Spoerri was dressed as a monk.

===Chinese===
他转身钻进自己的车里，随即又急急地以威胁的口吻补上一句：“你要是心血来潮，想给当局递什么匿名情报，就记着树林里长着一棵冷杉，那上面有的是地方吊你，就像吊你的同行海姆一样。开车，乔治！”

汽车重新启动，驶了出去。

在沙兴一带，建有楼层的气派宅第使汽车不那么惹眼，他们发现一道园门敞着，乔治毫不费力地循着通往别墅的幽暗车道开了进去。车灯熄了。

马布斯和乔治还站在门阶上，施佩里到了。

马布斯开门拧亮电灯时，看见施佩里一身修士打扮。

===Original===
“It is a mere accident,” said Spoerri. “I had to go to Switzerland in a hurry, and down there in the Rhine valley a cowl is more useful than even a genuine frontier pass. The last pass I had is in St. Gallen, and you know that I had to leave there hastily. But I had left the list of securities with Schaffer, and he brought them to me at Altstetten today. It is not safe to send such things by post nowadays.”

When he said this they were sitting in a large, well-furnished dining-room. George served the supper, brought ready prepared from Munich, and warmed up on the electric stove. Still eating, Mabuse said:

“We will liquidate on the lake itself, and thus we shall gain five points more than on land, according to the lists. I have bought five million Italian five-lire pieces. They are coming to the Southern Tyrol, and must be taken to Switzerland by way of the Vorarlberg. You must look after that, Spoerri. The Italian agent is Dalbelli, in Meran. You must go there tomorrow. I give you a month to do it in, and then we shall start a fresh district. Switzerland is now strongly against the importation of silver, and so there is less competition. We shall get enough of the five-lire pieces in Italy, and I have tried to do it with French silver too, but since the Treaty of Versailles there are so many fresh business combines in France, and they give nobody anything because the majority of them have not been in trade before. Have you not noticed that?”

===Chinese===
“纯属偶然。”施佩里说，“我当时得赶紧去瑞士，在下面莱茵河谷，一身修士道袍比货真价实的边境通行证还管用。我的上一张通行证留在了圣加仑，您知道，我当时不得不仓促离开那里。不过我把有价证券的清单留在了沙弗尔那里，他今天在阿尔特施泰滕给我送了来。如今这种东西走邮政可不保险。”

说这话时，两人已在一间陈设考究的大餐室里坐定。乔治端上晚饭——是从慕尼黑做好带来的，在电炉上热过。马布斯边吃边说：

“我们就在湖上交割，这样照行市能比在陆地上多赚五个点。我买下了五百万枚意大利五里拉硬币。它们将运到南蒂罗尔，必须经福拉尔贝格运进瑞士。这件事你去办，施佩里。意大利方面的代理人是梅拉诺的达尔贝利。你明天就去。我给你一个月期限，之后我们要开辟新的地界。瑞士如今严禁白银进口，所以竞争也少些。五里拉硬币在意大利我们能弄到足够的数目；法国银币我也试过，可自《凡尔赛条约》以来，法国冒出了那么多新起的联合商行，他们谁的好处也不给，因为其中大多数从前根本没做过买卖。这一点你没有留意到吗？”

===Original===
Spoerri nodded, making some inward calculation.

“Stop your calculations till I have done talking,” said Mabuse sharply, and Spoerri looked up in confusion.

Mabuse continued: “My confidential agent in the Government has informed me that meat-control will be abolished in Bavaria next month, but the matter will be kept dark. The difference in the prices prevailing in Bavaria and in Würtemberg is an enormous one, and for the first few weeks of decontrol it will still be very considerable. It would be a good thing to begin buying up now, however, and you can say that I am prepared to lay out ten million marks. Buy as much as you can get hold of; haste is wisdom in this respect.
