Inquire of Meggers in Stuttgart about the sales, and see that we have enough people for the transport. Everything must be completed within three days of giving the orders. We shall want from a thousand to twelve hundred head of cattle, and look out for beasts of good quality. No sheep or pigs—the risk is too great. Reckon it up for yourself before you do anything further. We get thirty percent on our purchase, and therefore we can allow ten percent on expenses. You must reckon more correctly than you did about the salvarsan.”

===Chinese===
施佩里点点头，心里打着算盘。

“把你的算盘收起来，等我讲完再说。”马布斯厉声道，施佩里惶惑地抬起头来。

马布斯继续说道：“我在政府里的密探告诉我，巴伐利亚下个月将取消肉类管制，但此事要保密。眼下巴伐利亚与符腾堡之间的差价大得惊人，取消管制的头几个星期里差价仍会相当可观。不过，现在就开始收购是件好事，你可以放话出去，说我准备投放一千万马克。能抓到多少就买多少；在这种事情上，快就是精明。销售情况去问斯图加特的梅格斯，并留意我们有足够的人手搞运输。一切必须在下单之后三天之内办妥。我们需要一千到一千二百头牛，要挑上等好牛。绵羊和猪不要——风险太大。再往前走一步之前，你先自己把账算清楚。我们进货有百分之三十的赚头，因此开销上可以容许百分之十。你算账得比上次砷凡纳明那回算得准些。”

===Original===
“That time I hadn’t calculated.…”

“Exactly, you hadn’t calculated correctly. Pesch is withdrawing; let him be closely watched by the Removal Committee, for he is impulsive, and if he plays the slightest trick he can be strung up beside Haim. By the way, they haven’t found *him* yet.… How much did you pay for the cerium?”

“It was dearer than.…”

“Everything always is dearer than… the Poles or the Bolsheviks can get it. How much?”

“Fifty marks.”

“Fifty Swiss francs then. They must have it, so don’t yield a stiver!”

===Chinese===
“那一回我没算过账……”

“正是，你是没算对。佩施要退出；让清除委员会把他盯紧，他这人冲动，只要耍一丁点花招，就可以吊到海姆旁边去。顺带说一句，他们到现在还没找着*他*呢。……铈你花了多少钱？”

“比……的价贵。”

“什么都是老一套的‘比……贵’——比波兰人或布尔什维克弄到手的价钱贵。到底多少钱？”

“五十马克。”

“那就付五十瑞士法郎。他们非买不可，一个子儿也别让！”

===Original===
He whistled into the speaking-tube under the table.

“George there?” he called out. “Everything in order?… Good. The *Rhine* is waiting, Spoerri. George, you are to be pilot; don’t forget the securities. That’s all for the present”; and turning again to Spoerri, “You’ll be in no danger in going to Zürich, Spoerri, will you?”

“I am all right as soon as I’ve passed the Customs, and then I go on as a priest.”

“If you travel by the *Rhine*, you’ll avoid the Customs; you can take charge of the securities and put them in the bank, to the account of Salbaz de Marte, mining engineer. Here is the list: a million in German Luxembourg stock, two million German Colonial Loan, five hundred thousand-mark notes. These are to be changed at once into milreis; that gives a better exchange than either dollars or Swiss francs. Inform Dr. Ebenhügel that fresh securities have been deposited, and that I want him to make use of the first favourable opportunity and sell for milreis.… There is one rather difficult matter to settle: the disposal of the people who have been working for me in Constance. If they are unemployed.…”

===Chinese===
他对着桌下的传话管吹了一声哨。

“乔治在吗？”他喊道。“一切就绪？……很好。*莱茵号*在等着呢，施佩里。乔治，你来当领航员；别忘了那些证券。眼下就这些。”他又转向施佩里：“你去苏黎世不会有什么危险吧，施佩里？”

“一过海关我就没事了，然后我就以神父的身份继续赶路。”

“你若乘*莱茵号*走，就避开了海关；证券由你负责，存进银行，记在矿业工程师萨尔巴兹·德·马尔特名下。清单在这儿：一百万的德国卢森堡股票，两百万的德国殖民公债，五百张一千马克的纸币。这些要立即兑换成米尔雷斯；换它比换美元或瑞士法郎的汇率都合算。通知埃本许格尔博士，又有新证券存入了，要他抓住第一个有利时机出手，换成米尔雷斯。……还有一件颇为棘手的事要了断：处置那些一直在康斯坦茨为我效力的人。要是他们失了业……”

===Original===
“Many don’t want to work any longer, in any case,” said Spoerri.

“I know. Those are the folks who have all they want; there’s nothing to fear from *them*. With my help they have got their own houses and are free of debt. But sometimes I have been obliged to take any workers I could get, and those who don’t own their houses should be carefully watched. The powder magazine is at Constance, for the young fellows live there, and if we suddenly withdraw these high wages from them, there is nothing for them to do but steal, and in a week’s time they’ll find themselves in prison and will be blabbing everything in their rage. Talk to George about this, and see what is to be done. He’s going there tomorrow. The safest thing would be to pack them off into the Foreign Legion. Go and see Magnard as soon as you have finished up in Zürich and Meran. Don’t forget to claim the commission for them. Give it to George, who can divide it among those concerned.… Authorize Böhm to sell the three motorboats that we have on the lake besides the *Rhine*. *That* always bears the ensign of the Royal Würtemberg Yacht Club and therefore is unnoticed. Keep the *Rhine* in this neighbourhood for any emergency. The boat can do sixty kilometres if it is well handled. Let us go.”

George was waiting outside. The three men felt their way through the darkness to the landing-stage, where they could hear the boat’s engines throbbing.

===Chinese===
“反正很多人已经不想再干了。”施佩里说。

“我知道。那些人是要什么有什么的家伙；*他们*不足为惧。靠了我的帮忙，他们置下了自己的房子，还清了债。可有的时候，我也不得不抓着什么人就用什么人，那些没置下房子的人要仔细盯着。火药桶就在康斯坦茨，因为那帮年轻小伙子住在那儿，一旦我们突然撤掉这份高工钱，他们除了偷窃别无出路，不出一个星期就会进监狱，怒火中烧之下会把什么都抖搂出来。这件事你去跟乔治商量，看看怎么办。他明天就去那儿。最稳妥的办法是把他们打发进外籍军团。你在苏黎世和梅拉诺办完事，就马上去见马尼亚尔。别忘了替他们把佣金领出来。交给乔治，让他分给有关的人。……授权伯姆，把除*莱茵号*之外我们在湖上的三条摩托艇卖掉。*莱茵号*常年挂着符腾堡皇家游艇俱乐部的会旗，因此不惹眼。把*莱茵号*留在这一带以备急用。这条艇若是操纵得当，能跑六十公里。走吧。”

乔治在外面候着。三个人摸着黑走到码头上，听得见艇上引擎的低鸣。

===Original===
“You have followed out my orders and there’s nothing on board?” said Mabuse.

“Nothing but the cerium.”

“Take it out then. I am not a dealer in scrap-iron!”

George hastened forward. Three men were busy in the gloom. Then Mabuse and Spoerri went on board and the boat started, going cautiously through the night. The engine scarcely throbbed. There was a slight vibration in the cabin where Mabuse sat, wrapped in his fur coat; then he went to the deck aft, and impatiently forward to the engine. After they had travelled for a while, he listened intently. It seemed to him as if through the sounds made by his own boat a noise reached his ears.

===Chinese===
“你按我的命令办了，船上什么都没有吧？”马布斯说。

“除了铈什么都没有。”

“那就搬下去。我可不是废铁贩子！”

乔治快步上前。昏暗中有三个人正忙着。随后马布斯和施佩里上了艇，艇便启动了，小心翼翼地驶入夜色。引擎几乎听不出声响。马布斯裹着皮大衣坐在舱里，只觉出轻微的颤动；随后他走到艇尾甲板上，又不耐烦地走到前面的机器旁。行驶了一阵之后，他凝神细听。他仿佛觉得，透过自家艇身发出的声响，有一阵动静传进他耳中。

===Original===
“Stop!” he cried suddenly.

George stopped the engine, and the sounds outside ceased. They started again, and immediately the sounds on the water, now on the right and again on the left, were heard once more. Mabuse went on the foredeck, where the noise of the engine was not so distinct. From there he could hear them quite distinctly.

“We are pursued, or at any rate under surveillance,” he thought. “Can it be that lawyer-detective Wenk?” Calmly, yet defiantly, he got his pistols ready. In the darkness he tried to discern what flag the *Rhine* was carrying, but it was impossible to find out.

“Spoerri,” he called out softly, and Spoerri came out of the cabin. “What are we travelling as? Don’t you hear that we are being followed?”

“No, no,” said Spoerri, “we are a Swiss patrol-boat tonight. I heard that the Germans were about, so I ordered the three other boats to act as convoy. One is travelling behind us, the others on each side. Nobody could reach us; we are already in Swiss waters.”

===Chinese===
“停艇！”他突然喊道。

乔治停下引擎，外面的声响也随之消失。艇重新启动，水上的声响立刻又起了，忽而在右，忽而在左。马布斯走上前甲板，那里引擎的噪声不那么明显。从那儿，他听得一清二楚。

“我们被跟踪了，至少是受到了监视。”他想道。“难道会是那个律师侦探文克？”他不动声色，却又带着挑衅，把几把手枪都备好了。黑暗中他竭力想辨清*莱茵号*挂的是什么旗，却无法看清。

“施佩里。”他轻声唤道，施佩里从舱里走了出来。“我们现在是以什么身份航行？你没听见我们被人跟踪了吗？”

“没有，没有。”施佩里说，“今晚我们是一艘瑞士巡逻艇。我听说德国人出动了，所以命令另外三条艇担任护航。一条跟在我们后面，其余两条一左一右。谁也接近不了我们；我们已经进了瑞士水域。”

===Original===
“How much a year do you earn in my service, that makes you take such care of me?” said Mabuse spitefully.

“Quite enough,” answered Spoerri; “but that is not why I do it.”

“Why then? Are you enamoured of my person, or is it merely the Christian charity that it suits you Swiss folk to assume since the war?”

“Yes,” said Spoerri simply.

“I have three and a half millions here in my dispatch-case. If you dared to, you would strangle me, but you don’t dare, and that is all there is about it. That is your pure humanity and love. During the last year you have had somewhere about eighty-five thousand, six hundred and seventy-seven marks or more from me.… Is that enough to stifle the desire to murder a man?”

===Chinese===
“你在我手下当差，一年挣多少钱，值得这般细心看护我？”马布斯刻毒地说。

“相当可观了。”施佩里答道，“可我并不是为了这个。”

“那为什么？你是迷恋我这个人，还是自开战以来你们瑞士人披挂上身正合体的那种基督徒慈悲？”

“是。”施佩里简单地答道。

“我的公文包里就有三百五十万。你要是敢，早就掐死我了，可你们不敢，事情的全部底细就在这里。这就是你们纯粹的仁义与慈爱。过去一年，你从我手里拿走了大约八万五千六百七十七马克，兴许还多。……这点钱，够不够把杀人的欲望闷熄？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Spoerri once more.

“Then you are a slave—my slave. Do you hear me? You are my slave.”

“I hear you.”

“Shall I slap your face? No; I won’t touch your slave-skin with my own. I just spit in the air.”

“Into the sea. You won’t pick a quarrel with anybody. There is no point of honour on the Lake of Constance.”

“Point of honour is an expression that doesn’t exist. A point is no larger than a squashed fly, and that’s the extent of a man’s honour—yours too, eh? You have some honour, even if the Lake of Constance has not?”

===Chinese===
“够。”施佩里又说了一遍。

“那你就是个奴隶——我的奴隶。听见了吗？你是我的奴隶。”

“我听见了。”

“要不要我扇你耳光？不必；我才不屑亲手碰你那身奴隶皮。我只朝空中啐一口。”

“啐到海里去。您跟谁也寻不了衅。博登湖上没有名誉攸关的问题。”

“‘名誉攸关’这个词压根儿不存在。一个‘点’，不过压扁的苍蝇那么大，人的名誉也就这么大——你的也一样，嗯？博登湖没有名誉，你总还有一点吧？”

===Original===
“I have never measured it.”

“Speak sense when you talk to me. I won’t stand your tomfoolery.”

“We are getting close to the shore.”

“Are you shirking, fellow?”

“No.”

“You dog!” said Mabuse in a stifled voice, in growing wrath. “I feel hatred tingling in my fingertips. I shall grasp you by the throat, you cur, you cowardly cur, and I shall annihilate you just as the electric current in the American death-chair does, you miserable wretch!”

===Chinese===
“我从来没量过。”

“跟我说话要像个明白人。你这套蠢话胡缠我可不领受。”

“我们快靠岸了。”

“你想开溜吗，家伙？”

“不想。”

“你这条狗！”马布斯的声音憋在喉咙里，怒气一阵紧似一阵。“我觉出仇恨在我指尖发麻。我要掐住你的喉咙，你这畜生，你这懦怯的畜生，我要像美国电刑椅上的电流那样把你灭掉，你这可怜的渣滓！”

===Original===
At this moment the engine stopped. For some time the sounds of the boats behind them had ceased.

“Why have we stopped?” asked Mabuse angrily. “I gave no orders.”

“There is no signal from the shore.”

Then Mabuse came to himself again. He stood up, gnashing his teeth, and asked:

“What is the matter?”

“We must wait. We can always rely on Solly. There is something wrong.”

===Chinese===
就在这时，引擎停了。一阵子以来，后面那几条船的声响早已沉寂。

“为什么停船？”马布斯怒气冲冲地问，“我没下过命令。”

“岸上没有发信号。”

这时马布斯才回过神来。他站起身，咬着牙问：

“怎么回事？”

“我们只能等。索利那边向来靠得住。一定是出了什么事。”

===Original===
“Let us wait! Have you weapons ready?”

“Yes, but if we don’t get the signal, we’d better get into the skiff. Then we can row back to the other boats.”

Behind Romanshorn a searchlight began to play, throwing a beam of light into the sky. It moved lower and peered about through the darkness, probing closely and lingering in places, then was directed towards the waters in the middle of the lake. It rose in the sky once more and then fell pitilessly on the very spot where Mabuse’s boat was lying. His knees trembled under the tension.

===Chinese===
“那就等！武器备好了吗？”

“备好了，可要是信号不来，我们最好上小艇。那样可以划回其他船去。”

罗曼斯霍恩后方，一盏探照灯亮了起来，一道光柱直插夜空。光柱压低了，在黑暗里四下逡巡，细细探查，不时在某处流连，随后转向湖心的水域。它再度升上天空，接着便无情地落在马布斯那条艇停泊的正上方。紧张之中，他的双膝直打颤。

===Original===
Suddenly, however, the shaft of light was fixed on a house standing out prominently in Romanshorn, just where the new church stood on a hill, and those in the boat perceived that the other craft must be far beyond on the other side of the point, and did not signify any danger. Their boat remained in darkness. In the railway-station on the shore lamps hung here and there at some distance from each other, and their reflections gleamed fitfully on the black waters. Then Mabuse said sternly:

“No, we’ll stay here! Tell George to get the pneumatic gun fastened to the engine.”

Spoerri sprang to do his bidding.

Under the cushions there was a poison-gas installation. Mabuse opened the nozzle. The wind was from the southwest and therefore favourable to his purpose. He prepared masks for himself and his companions and tried their fastenings.

Then he saw on shore a light which shone out brightly and was at once extinguished, then came again and flickered and was still. The engine started again, and the boat was soon in the channel, gliding under the trees, where it finally came to a standstill. The engine was silent, and a man ashore threw out a cable. Then Mabuse heard someone say, “Dr. Ebenhügel.”

===Chinese===
然而那道光柱突然钉在了罗曼斯霍恩一处格外醒目的房子上，正立在山丘上新教堂所在的位置，艇上的人这才明白，那条船必定远在岬角另一侧的前方，并不构成什么危险。他们的艇仍隐在黑暗中。岸上的火车站里，灯盏三三两两地悬着，相隔有些距离，倒影在黑色的水面上明灭不定。这时马布斯严厉地说：

“不，我们留在这儿！叫乔治把气动炮固定到引擎上。”

施佩里一跃而起，去执行他的命令。

坐垫下面藏着一套毒气装置。马布斯拧开了喷嘴。风从西南方来，正合他的用意。他给自己和同伴们备好面具，试了试系带。

随后他看见岸上亮起一盏灯，灿然一亮，旋即熄灭，接着又亮起，闪了几闪，归于沉寂。引擎重新启动，艇很快驶进水道，在树影下滑行，终于停住。引擎静默下来，岸上有人抛过来一根缆绳。接着马布斯听见有人通报：“埃本许格尔博士。”

===Original===
“Yes,” he ordered, “let him come on board.”

A dim form stepped across the gangway.

“It is I, Ebenhügel, Doctor. I have just come from Zürich. It is on account of my car that Solly did not give the signal punctually. The Customs authorities are on the watch every night with their cars now. Did you get my wire? There is something wrong, for the clerk has sent a warning. He could not tell us what was up, but from some reply to one of his superiors he gathered that it came to the Consulate headquarters in Zürich from the Munich Criminal Investigation Department.”

===Chinese===
“好。”他吩咐道，“让他上船。”

一条模糊的身影跨过跳板。

“是我，埃本许格尔，博士。我刚从苏黎世来。索利没能准时发信号，就是为了我这辆车。如今海关人员每夜都开着车巡守。您收到我的电报了吗？出事了，那位职员递来了警报。他说不清到底出了什么事，但他从给某位上司的一封复文里探得，这警报是从慕尼黑刑事调查部发到苏黎世领事馆总部的。”

===Original===
“So,” said Mabuse, closing his jaws firmly, “my lord Wenk is on the track, is he? Just you wait a while, my fine official!” Then, turning to Ebenhügel, he continued, “I am constantly in danger, but I’ve never come to grief yet.”

“I meant to say that this danger can only be averted in Munich. If anything goes wrong, they must not be able to put the responsibility on us here in Zürich.”

Mabuse answered roughly, “What do you mean by that?”

“This affair is of great importance for several people.”

“For whom then?”

“For myself, for example!”

===Chinese===
“哦。”马布斯说，牙关咬得紧紧的，“文克大人嗅到踪迹了，是吧？那您就稍候片刻，好一位体面的官老爷！”随后他转向埃本许格尔，继续说道：“我时刻身处险境，可至今还没栽过跟头。”

“我想说的是，这危险只有在慕尼黑才能消解。万一出了岔子，绝不能让他们把责任压到我们苏黎世这边的头上。”

马布斯粗声粗气地回答：“你这是什么意思？”

“这件事对好几个人都事关重大。”

“对谁？”

“比如对我！”

===Original===
Mabuse waved his hand with a threatening gesture of dismissal, while the other stood breathless.

“*I* have not been drinking,” said Mabuse. “How came you to alter my plans for such a trifle?”

“I thought it was necessary to warn you. The post is being watched, and people are not reliable.”

“Who is to convince me that *you* are reliable? You are one of the people too.”

“Our common interests should convince you, Doctor. I merely meant to tell you that it is from Munich that the danger threatens. You would be safe in Switzerland. You have accumulated wealth which allows you to live wherever you like. Stay here; you will be safe among us.”

===Chinese===
马布斯挥手作出威胁的逐客之势，对方屏息立于原地。

“*我*可没有喝酒。”马布斯说，“这么点鸡毛蒜皮，你怎么敢改动我的计划？”

“我以为必须警告您。邮政正受人监视，人心也靠不住。”

“谁能让我相信*你*靠得住？你也不过是众人中的一个。”

“我们共同的利益应该能说服您，博士。我只是想告诉您，危险来自慕尼黑。您在瑞士会安然无恙。您已积下万贯家财，爱住在哪儿就住在哪儿。留下来吧；在我们中间您会很安全。”

===Original===
“A lot *you* know about that! Your business is to look after my investments, nothing else. You are but my manager. Enough on that head. Is there anything else to tell me?”

The lawyer described his latest financial operations to Mabuse, who took down the descriptions furnished him. Then he walked backwards and forwards alone on the foreshore for five minutes, to ease himself after his long sitting.

“Is Spoerri still there?” he asked. “Spoerri, you need not go to Zürich. Ebenhügel will take the portfolio with him. We will go back to Schachen together.”

Upon the return journey Mabuse could not remain still in one place. He was constantly backwards and forwards on the small deck. The three convoys were again throbbing in their neighbourhood, their sounds drowned in the ghostly darkness. Suddenly Mabuse called through the speaking-tube to George, demanding brandy. Spoerri heard the order and shrank in terror.

===Chinese===
“这上头*你*懂得可真多！你的本分是照管我的投资，别无其他。你不过是我的经理人。这个话头到此为止。还有别的事要告诉我吗？”

这位律师向马布斯讲述了他最近的几桩金融操作，马布斯把他的讲述一一记录下来。随后他独自在湖滩上来回踱了五分钟，舒散久坐后的疲惫。

“施佩里还在吗？”他问。“施佩里，你不必去苏黎世了。公文包由埃本许格尔带去。我们一起回沙兴。”

归途中，马布斯在一处地方安静不下来。他不停地在小小的甲板上来回走动。三条护航艇又在附近的水域低鸣，声响淹没在鬼影幢幢的黑暗里。突然，马布斯对着传话管喊乔治，要白兰地。施佩里听见这道命令，吓得缩作一团。

===Original===
In the half-hour which the passage took, Mabuse drank the bottle empty. He was drunk when they landed, and he staggered through the darkness towards the house in front of them, having issued orders that they were not to follow for five minutes.

“We want more drink,” said he, when they were in the dining-room. “George, bring drinks!”

George shuddered, for he knew that the more the doctor drank, the more violent and unreasonable he became. Spoerri himself was always obliged to drink till he lost his senses. They drank champagne and brandy mixed in equal parts.

===Chinese===
这趟航程的半个钟头里，马布斯把一瓶酒喝了个底朝天。登岸时他已醉了，摇摇晃晃地穿过黑暗朝眼前的宅子走去，并且下了命令，叫他们五分钟之内不许跟上来。

“我们还要酒。”他们进到餐室后他说，“乔治，拿酒来！”

乔治打了个寒噤，因为他知道，博士喝得越多，就变得越发狂暴、越发不可理喻。施佩里自己则总是被迫奉陪，喝到不省人事。他们喝的是香槟和白兰地对半掺的酒。

===Original===
“This is liquid gold,” stuttered Mabuse thickly. “Here, George, bring bigger glasses! Let’s have the goblets. Spoerri, take a draught. You fool of a courier, drink; drink it down, you dog. Down with it into your currish throat! Now then, another! Drink till you can’t hold any more in that carcass of yours. I love to see you drink till you’re sick!”

Spoerri drank until everything swam round him and he lapsed into unconsciousness.

===Chinese===
“这是流体的黄金。”马布斯舌头发僵，说话含混不清。“来，乔治，拿大杯来！把大酒杯拿来。施佩里，喝一口。你这蠢货信差，喝呀；喝下去，你这狗。灌进你那条狗喉咙里去！来，再来一杯！喝到你那副皮囊再也装不下为止。我就爱看你喝到呕吐！”

施佩里直喝得天旋地转，陷入了昏迷。

===Original===
“And you, my lord Wenk! A State Attorney in Munich! Your notebook! *Your* orders to the Criminal Investigation Department, forsooth! Just wait a moment, my fine gentleman! We’ll begin with Herr Hull, for he was the first.… (Drink, Spoerri, can’t you, you miserable country bumpkin, drink; drink as I do!) Let me see—Hull, yes, Edgar Hull, 34, Hubertusstrasse. Away with him, his turn first. George will look after it, and you can help him. The Carozza girl can contrive it. Find your accomplices. Write it down, it is the order of the… Prince. (Drink it down, now!) Of the Prince, have you written that? Which prince, do you say? The Prince, the Emperor of Citopomar, in Southern Brazil. A word from his mouth and a thousand women lie bathed in their blood, five hundred men are reduced to impotence. One single word and a whole edifice totters! Don’t simper, you fool, or I’ll dash your brains out with this goblet!”

He flung the vessel down, shattering it in pieces, and with the fragments he threatened Spoerri.

“I… I am writing it,” stammered Spoerri.

“A thousand women and five hundred men,” shouted Mabuse.

===Chinese===
“还有你，文克大人！慕尼黑的一位检察官！你的记事本！竟是*你*向刑事调查部发号施令，好大的威风！且稍待片刻，我的好先生！我们先从赫尔先生开刀，他是头一个……（喝呀，施佩里，你不会喝吗，你这可怜的乡巴佬，喝；照我这样喝！）我想想——赫尔，对了，埃德加·赫尔，34，胡贝图斯大街。把他除掉，他排头一个。乔治会去料理，你可以帮衬他。卡罗扎姑娘能想出办法。去找你们的同伙。写下来，这是……亲王的命令。（现在就喝下去！）亲王的命令，写下没有？你问是哪位亲王？那位亲王，锡托波马尔的皇帝，在南巴西。他口中一个字，一千个女人便浴血倒地，五百个男人便废作无能。单单一个字，一座大厦便摇摇欲坠！别讪笑，你这蠢货，不然我就用这大酒杯砸出你的脑浆来！”

他把酒杯掼在地上，摔了个粉碎，又抓起碎片威胁施佩里。

“我……我在写。”施佩里结结巴巴地说。

“一千个女人，五百个男人。”马布斯吼道。

===Original===
“Doctor,” said Spoerri hesitatingly, struggling with the intoxication overcoming his senses, “I did not hear; I do not know this Hull. What am *I* to do with him? 34, Hubertusstrasse.… Do you really mean *me*, Doctor?”

Then Mabuse all at once stood upright, intoxicated as he was. “Yes, you!” he thundered, and then gave Spoerri a heavy blow with his fist, full on his forehead, knocking him senseless to the floor. “I am going to bed, George,” he shouted, overcome with rage. He left Spoerri lying where he was, and went out.

When he came into the dining-room again next morning, Spoerri was sitting there. Mabuse had breakfasted in bed.

“Show me your notes!” he ordered in a harsh voice. He ran through them quickly, found Hull’s address traced in drunken characters, and returned the book to Spoerri. “That’s all right,” said he, and Spoerri fawned upon him like a cur watching to avoid a kick.

===Chinese===
“博士。”施佩里迟疑地说，一面同正淹没他神智的醉意搏斗着，“我没有听见；我不认识这个赫尔。*我*拿他怎么办？34，胡贝图斯大街。……您当真是对*我*说吗，博士？”

这时马布斯虽然醉着，却猛地直起了身。“对，就是你！”他吼雷似的说，随即一记重拳打在施佩里的额头上，把他打得不省人事，直挺挺倒在地板上。“我去睡了，乔治。”他怒不可遏地喊道。他撇下施佩里就地躺着，自己走了出去。

第二天早晨他再进餐室时，施佩里已坐在那里。马布斯是在床上用的早餐。

“把你的记录拿来我看！”他声音严厉地命令道。他飞快翻了一遍，找到了那行醉后歪斜笔迹写下的赫尔地址，便把本子还给施佩里。“这就对了。”他说。施佩里像条留神躲踢的狗一样对他阿谀讨好。

===Original===
That attitude of his did Mabuse good; it soothed and reconciled him, and he became talkative. Spoerri was quietly delighted to find the master friendly towards him, to know that the dread will of this imperious man inclined him to be amiable, as if recognizing his devotion.

“Spoerri,” said the Doctor, “I shall go to Constance with you. We mustn’t let those young men do anything stupid!”

Spoerri brightened up. “Oh, when they once see you, Doctor, there’ll be no trouble at all.”

===Chinese===
施佩里这副姿态让马布斯受用；它抚慰了他，令他心平气和，他的话也多了起来。施佩里则暗自欢喜，因为见得主人对他和颜悦色，知道这位专横之人那令人生畏的意志竟对他生出和善之意，仿佛是认可了他的忠心。

“施佩里。”博士说，“我要跟你一起去康斯坦茨。我们可不能让那些年轻人干出蠢事来！”

施佩里顿时眉开眼笑。“哦，只要他们一见到您，博士，就什么乱子都不会有了。”

===Original===
The two men remained all day long at the villa. Mabuse drank, but no longer compelled Spoerri to do so. By midday he was already intoxicated. Spoerri, tired out by the carousal of the previous night, watched over Mabuse devotedly. He tried many simple devices to persuade him to stop drinking, but Mabuse soon saw through them, and ordered full bottles to be brought and no tricks to be played. Alcohol was a necessity to him; it inflamed his wild and evil spirit, and in the fantasies of intoxication he found all his great ideas. There was no thwarting of his will from without, and when drunk he felt himself enclosed as in a castle of the *Arabian Nights*. Nobody could understand that to him alcohol was the bringer of magic, the stream which intensified life and gave him creative power. He bathed in it as he might do in the love of some fair woman, yielding himself to it wholly, bridging chasms, attempting new feats, working unrestrainedly and overcoming all obstacles.… He became a law unto himself, a world of which he was the sun.

“Spoerri, how do you like Europe?” he stuttered.

===Chinese===
两个人在别墅里待了一整天。马布斯喝着酒，但不再强迫施佩里喝了。到中午他就已经醉了。施佩里被前一夜的狂欢弄得筋疲力尽，仍一心一意地看护着马布斯。他试了许多简单的小法子劝他停杯，马布斯却很快看穿，吩咐把满瓶的酒拿来，不许再耍花招。酒精于他是必需品；它点燃他狂野凶邪的心性，在醉态的幻梦里，他一切伟大的构想纷至沓来。他的意志不容外界违逆，醉倒之时，他觉得自己仿佛围在一座*《天方夜谭》*的城堡之中。没有人能够明白，酒精于他是魔法的赐予者，是奔涌生命、赋予他创造力的洪流。他沐浴其中，如同沐浴在某个美丽女子的爱里，把自己整个交付出去，跨越深渊，尝试新的壮举，无拘无束地纵横驰骋，扫清一切障碍。……他成为自身的立法者，成为一个以他为太阳的世界。

“施佩里，你觉得欧洲怎么样？”他口齿含混地问。

===Original===
“Oh, very much, Doctor,” answered Spoerri unreflectingly.

Then Mabuse broke out vehemently, “You shall *not* go to Citopomar, to my Empire! Europe is a filthy, lousy country, fit for none but grubs and earthworms. It is the home of parasites, of all creepy, crawly creatures, but when I am in Citopomar—**Citopomar**… Spoerri, I shan’t take you with me. I am going to sleep now, and will see you later.”

He staggered out, and lying in his bedroom on his bed, fully dressed, he felt for a few moments as if he were himself the universe, beyond and above all bounds and limits, the power of his will surging over him as a stream of molten lava, bearing him with it towards the day when, in his distant kingdom, his power would be supreme over man and beast, and all Nature be subjugated to his impulses.

In the evening, when the twilight was descending, they drove to Constance. Mabuse was sober, silent and morose. His imagination was already busy and his nerves reacting to his stern resolves, as he thought of the crowds of young men in the town, which seemed but a mere speck on his horizon—men who had been working for him since the Armistice. From this very town he himself had made a new start when the war had driven him from his own vast plantations in the Solomon Isles back into the European vortex, and he could find nothing better to do than work for medical examinations and exchange his career in the Pacific for that of a doctor in a town of Southern Germany.

===Chinese===
“哦，好得很，博士。”施佩里不假思索地答道。

马布斯随即勃然发作：“你*休想*去锡托波马尔，去我的帝国！欧洲是个肮脏的、生虱子的地方，只配蛆虫和蚯蚓栖身。它是寄生虫的老巢，是一切蠕蠕爬行东西的渊薮，可等我到了锡托波马尔——**锡托波马尔**……施佩里，我不会带你去的。我现在要去睡了，回头再见。”

他踉跄着走出去，和衣躺在卧室的床上，有几个瞬间，他觉得自己就是宇宙本身，超越一切边界与限度，意志之力如一道熔岩之流在他周身奔涌，载着他驶向那一天——在他遥远的国度里，他的权力将凌驾于人与兽之上，整个自然都将臣服于他的欲念。

傍晚，暮色四合时分，他们驱车前往康斯坦茨。马布斯清醒着，沉默而阴郁。他的想象已然活跃起来，神经也随着他冷酷的决心绷紧反应，因为他想到城里那一群群的年轻人——那城在他的地平线上不过是一个小点——那些人自停战以来一直在为他效力。当年战争把他从所罗门群岛上自家的辽阔种植园赶回欧洲的漩涡，他正是从这座小城重新起家的；他找不到更好的事由，只好替人体检行医，把他在太平洋上的营生，换成了南德意志一座小城里的医生职业。

## VI / 六

===Original===
Wenk was awakened by a feeling of chilliness, which set him shivering. He pulled his cloak round him, under the impression that the coverlet had slipped off his bed, but he soon became aware of his error. He sat up, feeling giddy and at first unable to recall anything. He came slowly to himself and then he perceived where he was and saw the castle buildings gleaming through the darkness.

He rose hastily and moved away, but he was still dazed, and had to jump about to get any warmth into his body. What could the time be? He felt for his watch, but it was not there, and then he went hastily through his pockets. His purse was missing; so, too, were his pocketbook and his official notebook. He had fallen into the hands of thieves. The strange thing was how it could have happened that he had escaped with his life?

Then sudden dread seized upon him. He held his head in his hands, setting his jaws firmly, striving to subdue his feeling of despair. His notebook was missing, and in this were to be found addresses, reports, information, data, plans of all kinds.﻿ ﻿… The very first thing he recalled about it was the opening page, on which the Hull affair was fully set forth.﻿ ﻿… Wenk now hurried straight forward. If only he could recover his notebook! He rushed on till he was out of breath, then stopped and asked himself, “What shall I do? Go to the nearest railway-station? But what is the time? It may be one o’clock, it may be five. How am I to tell? And when would the first train start? It might mean waiting in front of a closed railway-station for four, even five hours!” Then he reflected that if he were to wake anybody in the castle he would have to submit to questioning. No; that would do no good. Should he make a fuss about it? It was clear that the chauffeur had acted in obedience to the blond stranger’s orders. Had the latter really penetrated his disguise and laid his plans so cautiously and cleverly beforehand, or was it the usual thing that anyone who appeared in any way suspicious should at once be put to the test in this way? Could it be merely theft, and the book have been taken from him by accident? He realized that when he seated himself on the cushions he must have set the gas-current free, for there was no gas in the car when he got in. That had been arranged, then. No, it wasn’t that way either. It was something both simpler and safer. The driver could open the gas valve from his seat. Of course that was the way of it.

Thinking thus, Wenk reached the highroad, only half-conscious of his resolve to proceed to Munich on foot. He went as fast as he could, but every now and then he had to stop and wait till a feeling of giddiness had passed. That must be the effect of the gas. What sort of gas could it be that operated so rapidly and yet did so little harm? His foes might just as easily have used a deadly gas, then they would have got rid of him altogether. Why did they use a stupefying gas merely? Was it meant for a warning to him?

===Chinese===
文克被一阵寒意冻醒，冻得直打哆嗦。他还当是被子从床上滑落了，便把斗篷往身上拢了拢，但很快就发觉自己弄错了。他坐起身来，只觉头晕目眩，起初什么都想不起来。他慢慢回过神来，才认出自己身在何处，看见城堡的楼宇在黑暗中泛着微光。

他霍然起身，挪步走开，人却仍是昏昏沉沉，只得蹦跳几下，让身上生出点暖意来。现在是什么时候了？他伸手摸表，表不在了，于是急忙把口袋挨个翻了一遍。钱袋没了；记事簿和公务笔记本也一同不见了。他落到了窃贼的手里。奇怪的是，他怎么竟能逃得性命？

接着，一阵恐惧突然攫住了他。他双手抱头，咬紧牙关，竭力压下心头的绝望。笔记本不见了，而那里面记着地址、报告、情报、资料、各种各样的计划。……他最先想起的，就是扉页那一页——赫尔一案在那里写得一清二楚。……此刻文克只顾埋头匆匆向前赶。要是能把笔记本找回来就好了！他一路狂奔，直到上气不接下气才停住，自问道："我该怎么办？去最近的火车站吗？可现在几点了？也许一点，也许五点。我怎么知道？头班车又要等到什么时候？说不定得在一座大门紧闭的火车站前干等四个钟头，甚至五个钟头！"随即他又想到，若是去惊动城堡里的什么人，自己就得受一番盘问。不，那没有好处。要不要为此大闹一场？明摆着，那司机是奉金发陌生人之命行事。那家伙究竟是真的识破了他的乔装、事先布置得这样周密巧妙呢，还是说，凡是形迹可疑的人，一律先要这样试探一番，不过是他们的惯技？会不会只是单纯的盗窃，那本子不过是顺手误拿的？他明白了，自己坐上坐垫的时候必定是放出了瓦斯——他上车时车里本来没有瓦斯。那么说，那是事先安排好的。不，也不对。有个法子既更简单又更稳妥：司机坐在自己的座位上就能打开瓦斯阀门。自然就是这么回事。

这样一路想着，文克走上了公路，才半清醒地意识到自己已打定主意徒步走去慕尼黑。他竭力快走，却不时得停下来，等那一阵眩晕过去。那必定是瓦斯的作用。那会是种什么瓦斯，见效这样快，害处却这样小？他的仇家满可以同样轻易地用一种致命的瓦斯，那样就能把他干脆除掉。他们为什么只用一种迷醉性的瓦斯？是要给他一个警告吗？

===Original===
Now, at any rate, his notebook was in their hands, and perhaps they wanted nothing more of him than that. It was but an attack on his little notebook. Whose names were to be found there? Karstens’, for one﻿ ﻿… and an account of all the occurrences in the gaming-houses with the sandy-bearded man and the old Professor, and in the Palace Hotel likewise. All the places where gambling was carried on were noted there too. It was clearly only his notebook that they wanted, and that they had succeeded in getting, but the book he had lost had meant a good deal to him.

He went faster and faster by the sleeping houses, past the peaceful suburbs and into the quiet approaches of the town. The byways, in which traces of snow still lay, seemed like dragons creeping through the night, bent on spying in the ghostly light on those who went by, and Wenk shuddered at the thought. But when a tramcar drew near he felt more at ease. He soon recognized where he was and hastened to his own chambers. He was thoroughly exhausted when he reached home, threw himself fully dressed upon his bed and became unconscious once more, not awakening until the evening.

The first idea which occurred to him then was that henceforth his life was at stake. He accepted it calmly, for since he was combating evil, it was natural that it should be so. The conflict would be played out on the borderland between existence and annihilation, and for one moment he wondered whether it were worth while to go on. But only for a moment. He immediately told himself that there could be no question of hesitation here. Such men are like beasts escaped from a menagerie, and it was his task, his duty, the justification for his existence, to help to make them powerless for evil. There must be no fear of men, no fear of the body any more than he had had of the soul, since his mind had once succeeded in grasping the crisis through which his country was passing. Since he too had been a witness of its genesis, he must help to overcome its effects.

Yet one more thought. Was he a match for his opponent? Must he not fortify himself if he were henceforward to pit his life and strength in such a struggle? His adversary seemed to have the advantage of him, for he worked in the dark. Were his own hands strong enough to seize and hold the evil powers advancing upon him and to crush them? Had he the strength to fight the age, for his opponent was more than a cheat, a criminal﻿—he was the whole spirit of the age, a spirit torn through the catastrophe of the war from the hellish depths where it was created, to fall upon the world and the homes of men. He realized that against such an opponent he must spread his nets more widely if he hoped to ensnare him. He must have an organization equal to the criminal’s own. He must not, as hitherto, consider it sufficient to rely on his confederates, those who were entirely of one mind with himself. He must seek his helpers in the enemy’s camp.

===Chinese===
无论如何，如今他的笔记本已落在他们手里，也许他们对他的所求也就到此为止了。这不过是对他这本小本子的袭击。那里面都记着谁的名字？就有卡斯滕斯的……还有与那个沙色胡须的男人、与那位老教授在赌场里种种经过的记载，宫殿饭店里的也有。所有设赌的地方也都记在里面。他们要的显然只是他的笔记本，而这一点他们得手了；只是他丢掉的这本册子，对他非同小可。

他贴着一座座沉睡的房屋越走越快，走过安宁的郊外，走进城边寂静的入口。小路上还残存着积雪的痕迹，恍如一条条在黑夜里爬行的龙，存心要在幽冥的光里窥探过路的人；想到这里，文克不寒而栗。但等到一辆电车驶近，他心里踏实了些。他很快认出了自己所在的位置，急忙赶回自己的寓所。到家时他已筋疲力尽，和衣倒在床上又失去了知觉，直到傍晚才醒。

他醒来后浮起的头一个念头，便是从今往后他性命悬于一线。他平静地接受了这一点：既然他在与邪恶作战，事情本该如此。这场较量将在生存与毁灭的交界地带进行，有一瞬间他自问是否还值得继续下去。但也只是一瞬间。他随即告诉自己，此处绝无迟疑的余地。这号人如同逃出兽笼的猛兽，而协助使他们无力为恶，正是他的任务、他的职责、他存在的理由。人不足惧，肉体也不足惧，正如灵魂于他也已不足惧——自从他的头脑终于把握了国家正经历的这场危机。既然他也亲眼见证了这场危机的生成，他就必须协助战胜它的后果。

还有一个念头。他配得上自己的对手吗？从今以后要把生命和力气都押上这样一场斗争，他能不先加固自己的力量吗？他的仇敌似乎占着上风，因为那人在暗处行事。他自己的双手，够不够强劲，能抓住那步步逼近的邪恶势力、牢牢按住并将它碾碎？他有没有力量与整个时代作战？因为他的对手不止是骗子、罪犯——他就是整个时代的精神，一个在大战的浩劫中被从孕育它的地狱深处撕裂出来的精灵，扑向世界，扑向人的家园。他明白，面对这样的对手，要想把他罗网住，就必须把网撒得更开。他必须拥有一支足以与罪犯相匹敌的组织。再不能像以往那样，以为只依靠自己的同志——那些与自己完全一条心的人——就足够了。他必须到敌人的营垒里去寻找帮手。

===Original===
At once he thought of the lady whose strange and questionable escape he had assisted. He drove quickly to Schramm’s. Yes, there she was, but, as usual, a spectator merely. He sat down beside her.

“You are not playing, sir?” said she.

“No, your example has made watching more interesting than playing to me.”

“Watching,” laughed the lady lightly, “when carried on by a high legal official is not good﻿ ﻿… for the players!”

===Chinese===
他立刻想到了那位女士，他曾协助过她那桩蹊跷而令人生疑的脱身。他驱车赶到施拉姆餐馆。不错，她果然在那里，但和往常一样，只是个看客。他在她身旁坐下。

"您不下场玩玩吗，先生？"她问。

"不玩了，有您的榜样在前，看牌对我来说比打牌更有意思。"

"看牌，"那位女士轻轻笑道，"由一位高级司法官员来进行的看牌，可不是什么好事……对打牌的人来说！"

===Original===
Wenk had a slight suspicion that this was said with a double meaning, but whether mockingly or warningly he could not decide; in any case, it was said to serve the purpose of some other, who possibly was sitting there at play. Perhaps they worked secretly in partnership.

He observed her closely, but she sat quietly idle. Her bright eyes roved in all directions. He said to her, feeling his way:

“You have yourself seen a high legal official caught in the toils of the gaming-devil. His jurisdiction is troublesome to the other player!”

He said “the *other*,” and waited to see whether she would start, or twitch nervously, or give the player some sign or other. But she did none of these things, merely remained still and accepted his words with a friendly smile.

“She is a beautiful woman,” he thought, “and there is some secret reserve strength in her. Men play for money, but it would be more worthy of their manhood to play for such a woman as this.”

===Chinese===
文克隐隐疑心这话语带双关，可那是讥讽还是警告，他拿不准；无论如何，这话说出来是为了给另一个人效力，而那人此刻也许正坐在桌边打牌。说不定他们正在暗中合伙。

他细细观察她，她却安安静静地闲坐着，一双明眸向四面八方逡巡。他试探着对她说：

"您亲眼见过一位高级司法官员落进赌魔的罗网。他的管辖权，叫别的玩家伤脑筋呢！"

他说的正是"别的"二字，一面等着看她会不会惊跳起来，会不会神经质地抽动，或者给那位玩家递个什么暗号。可这些她一样都没有做，只是不动声色，含着友好的微笑领受了他的话。

"她是个美丽的女人，"他想，"她身上藏着一股秘而不宣的后备力量。男人们为钱而赌，可要为这样一个女人去赌，才更配得上他们的男子气概。"

===Original===
After a few moments she leaned towards him, saying lightly and with a playful impressiveness:

“I was present when Basch lost so heavily!”

“I know you were,” said Wenk, astonished and inquiringly.

“And you were playing then, too.”

“Yes, I was playing. I have just confessed it!”

“Ah, but I mean you were really playing *then*! The first evening, when you came with Hull, you took part in the game, but you were not really playing. And the evening when the old Professor was there﻿—well, I don’t quite know, there was some sort of atmospheric disturbance﻿ ﻿… wasn’t there now?” she said, turning to him with a melting and wholly feminine gesture of friendliness.

===Chinese===
过了片刻，她朝他俯过身来，轻声说道，语气俏皮而意味深长：

"巴施输得那么惨的那一回，我就在场！"

"我知道您在场。"文克说，语气又惊又疑。

"那一晚您也在打牌。"

"不错，我在打牌。我刚刚招认过了！"

"啊，可我的意思是，那一晚您是*真*在打牌！头一个晚上，您同赫尔一起来的时候，您也入了局，可您并不是真打。而老教授在的那一晚呢——嗯，我说不太清，空气里总有些什么在扰动似的……不是吗？"她说着，转向他，那姿态温柔妩媚，全是女人的亲热劲儿。

===Original===
Wenk was taken aback. He replied:

“That evening when the old Professor was there? What old Professor?”

“The evening you came as a country cousin,” she answered roguishly.

At last Wenk comprehended that she had recognized him, and his face showed his disappointment, but she begged him not to mind her having found him out.

“You were well disguised,” she said, “but I could not believe that here in Munich there would be two such quaint little monkeys on a cherry-tree, conjured so cleverly by a Chinese jewel-cutter out of an amethyst. When I first saw the ring, flanked on each side by stupid diamonds on stupid fingers, I noticed it with pleasure.”

===Chinese===
文克吃了一惊。他答道：

"老教授在的那一晚？什么老教授？"

"您扮成外省阔佬来的那一晚嘛。"她调皮地答道。

到这时文克才明白，她认出了他；他脸上露出失望的神色，她却求他别把被识破这桩事放在心上。

"您乔装得很好，"她说，"可我没法相信，在慕尼黑这里竟会有两只这样古怪的小猴子，蹲在同一棵樱桃树上，由中国珠宝匠用一颗紫水晶那么巧妙地变出来。头一回看见那枚戒指，夹在两侧愚蠢的钻石中间、戴在愚蠢的手指上，我就满心欢喜地注意到了它。"

===Original===
Wenk looked at her, awaiting something more. Who could she be?

“At any rate, it struck me as curious that there could be two men, even in such circles as ours”﻿—here she glanced round the table﻿—“who had some amount of taste.﻿ ﻿…”

“Your sarcasm,” said Wenk, entering into her vein, “does not require either Yes or No, for the fact that you noticed my ring and so correctly guessed its origin proves that you belong to a very different circle from the one you find yourself in here.”

“Oh, I was a stewardess on a steamer bound for Asiatic ports, but the war has taken both our ships and our calling from us!”

“May I then hazard the suggestion that you have withdrawn from your former calling at some advantage to yourself?”

“Oh, I am not stupid!” she smiled back.

===Chinese===
文克望着她，等着下文。她能是什么人呢？

"不管怎么说，我觉得奇怪的是，居然能有两个男人——即便是在我们这样的圈子里"——说到这里她扫了一眼牌桌——"多少还有点品味。……"

"您的挖苦，"文克说着，接上了她的口风，"本不需要回答是或不是，因为您注意到了我的戒指、又如此准确地猜到它的来历，这本身就证明，您所属的圈子，跟您眼下混迹的这个圈子全然不同。"

"哦，我原本在一条开往亚洲各港的轮船上当女乘务员，可这场战争把我们的船和我们的行当都夺走了！"

"那恕我冒昧揣测一句：您从原先的行当里抽身的时候，给自己捞到了不小的好处吧？"

"哦，我可不傻！"她笑着回敬。

===Original===
“There is nothing which it is more unnecessary to assure me of, Countess.”

There was a momentary flutter in the beautiful woman’s eye, and an imperceptible something within her seemed to come to a standstill. Had he known who she was and wanted to play with her a little, and would he now blazon abroad the fact that she frequented such places secretly?

Wenk laughed aloud.

“Or can it be that the coroneted handkerchief comes from the trunk of some countess travelling to Asiatic ports, as Sherlock Holmes would argue? No, dear lady, we are quits. We shall both comport ourselves more circumspectly in future when we are among our fellow-mortals. I shall put a stupid diamond on my finger, and you will use a monogram without a coronet on your handkerchiefs, Countess.﻿ ﻿…”

“Hush!” she said, in agitation.

===Chinese===
"这一点最不必向我保证，伯爵夫人。"

美人眼里掠过一丝慌乱，她体内有什么不可察觉的东西似乎骤然凝住了。他莫非已经知道了她的身份，想跟她逗弄一番？他现在会不会把她在暗中出入这种地方的事张扬出去？

文克朗声笑了。

"再不然，莫非那块缀着冠冕的手帕，是从某位往亚洲各港去的伯爵夫人的行李箱里弄来的，就像夏洛克·福尔摩斯会推论的那样？不，亲爱的夫人，咱们两清了。今后混在凡人中间，你我行事都得更谨慎些。我会往手指上套一颗愚蠢的钻石，您呢，就在手帕上改用不带冠冕的花押字，伯爵夫人。……"

"嘘！"她激动地说。

===Original===
“But even such precautions would serve no turn!”

“I do not understand you.”

“You force me to pay you compliments. I am seeking vainly for a suitable way of expressing myself so that I may convey to you my conviction that the ‘countess’ in you cannot anyhow be suppressed.”

“He will be asking me to sup with him directly,” she said to herself. “He evidently wants to start a romance,” and the idea amused her. From sheer exuberance of energy she had come hither, seeking nothing in her masquerade but relief from boredom, and lo! she had landed a prize like this!

“At any rate, I need not have taken a circuitous route to Schramm’s!” she said laughingly.

===Chinese===
"可连这样的小心也是白费！"

"我不明白您的意思。"

"您这是逼着我说恭维话。我正苦苦寻找一个合适的说法，好让您明白我确信的一件事：您身上的'伯爵夫人'，是怎么也压不住的。"

"他马上就要请我共进夜宵了。"她暗自想道。"他显然想开始一段风流韵事。"这念头让她觉得有趣。她纯粹是精力过剩才到这里来的，乔装改扮别无所求，只为解解闷，瞧，竟钓到了这样一条大鱼！

"不管怎么说，我原不必绕那么大的弯子到施拉姆餐馆来！"她笑着说。

===Original===
At the gaming-table nothing sensational was going on. She decided to feint with him, and said sarcastically:

“You try to disguise your compliments as well as you do yourself, Herr von Wenk. I am obliged to accept them, since they take me unawares.”

“I merely mean,” persisted Wenk, “that the removal of the coronet from your monogram cannot remove the stamp of nobility from your brow.”

“I hope you are still masquerading!”

“As an enraptured reader of sentimental romances, you mean? In any case, dear lady.﻿ ﻿… But is this quite the place to carry on a conversation which aims at a more serious turn?”

===Chinese===
牌桌上没有什么刺激的场面。她决意跟他虚晃一枪，讥诮地说：

"您把恭维话包装得跟您本人一样好，冯·文克先生。我只好照单收下，谁让它们攻了我个措手不及。"

"我不过是说，"文克坚持道，"从花押字上摘掉冠冕，并不能从您的额头上抹去高贵的印记。"

"但愿您现在还在演戏！"

"您是说我此刻正扮着一个读伤感小说读得如痴如醉的读者？随便怎么说都行，亲爱的夫人。……不过，一场想要转入正经话题的谈话，在这个地方进行，合适吗？"

===Original===
She answered, looking him up and down haughtily and deliberately: “Does that mean that you are inviting me to sup with you?”

“I would certainly not venture to do that,” said Wenk hastily, recognizing her meaning. He saw that she suspected him of desiring to establish an intrigue, and that he would begin it in the ordinary way of a champagne supper. “Now,” said he to himself, “if I am to win her over, I must act in such a way as not to deceive her and yet not fulfil her expectations, and since she thinks she has guessed me aright, I must not allow her a feeling of superiority over me. I do not want her to think me a blockhead. The coronet on the handkerchief seems genuine enough, and she does not come here for money, for she never plays. Therefore someone present, or an adventure of some sort, must account for her being here, and if I am to win her to my side I must prove myself stronger than the unknown attraction here,” he argued.

“What have you to offer me?” she asked in a frivolous tone; but Wenk seemed to find something real behind the thoughtless manner, and he answered intuitively, fearing defeat as soon as the words had left his lips:

===Chinese===
她答话了，高傲而不动声色地把他上下打量了一番："您这是在请我共进夜宵吗？"

"那我是万万不敢的。"文克赶紧说，明白了她的意思。他看得出，她疑心他想跟她建立一段私情，而且料定他会按老套路用一顿香槟夜宵来开场。"现在，"他暗自盘算，"若要把她争取过来，我的行事就得既不欺骗她，又不遂了她的期待；她既认为已经把我猜透，我就绝不能让她生出高我一等的感觉。我不想被她当成个蠢货。手帕上的冠冕看样子货真价实，她也不是为钱而来——她从来不打牌。那么，她到这里来，必是为了在场的某个人，或是为了一场什么奇遇。要赢她到我这边来，我就必须证明自己比这里那个不为人知的吸引力更强大。"他这样推断着。

"您能给我什么呢？"她用轻浮的口气问；但文克似乎在这漫不经心的做派背后察觉到了几分真意，他凭直觉应道，话一出口便担心落败：

===Original===
“I can offer you a great adventure, a really great adventure!”

“With you?” she rejoined, equally without pausing for reflection. “As a lover or as an agent of the State?”

“With me﻿—as a detective!”

“*Can* you?” she asked disdainfully.

“Shall I give you proofs? Last night I was decoyed into a car and left in the freezing cold lying on a bench in the Schleissheim Park, stupefied by gas. Today, but twenty-four hours later, I am aware that the man who did this, or ordered it to be done, is the same whom you saw playing recently as the old Professor, and that this same learned old fellow is also the sandy-bearded man to whom you saw Basch lose his money here.”

===Chinese===
"我能给您一场大冒险，一场真正的大冒险！"

"同您一起？"她同样不假思索地接口道。"以情人的身份，还是以国家特工的身份？"

"同我一起——以侦探的身份！"

"您*行*吗？"她鄙夷地问。

"要我拿证据给您看吗？昨天夜里，我被人诱进一辆汽车，被瓦斯迷倒，扔在施莱斯海姆公园的长椅上挨冻。今天，不过二十四小时之后，我已经知道，干出这件事、或是下令干这件事的人，正是您前不久见过的那个扮作老教授打牌的人；而这位博学的老先生，也就是您亲眼看着巴施在这里把钱输给他的那个沙色胡须的男人。"

===Original===
“Is that true?” she asked in a serious tone.

“Absolutely.”

“The man﻿ ﻿… with the reddish beard﻿ ﻿… who﻿ ﻿… sat﻿ ﻿… there?”

“The man who sat opposite Basch like a beast of prey!”

“And what am I﻿ ﻿… what have I to do with it?”

“To help me find this man, from whom others must be rescued.”

===Chinese===
"这是真的？"她正色问道。

"千真万确。"

"那个……留着微红胡须的……在那边……坐过的男人？"

"那个像一头猛兽似的坐在巴施对面的男人！"

"那我算什么……这件事跟我有什么相干？"

"帮我找到这个人——还有别的人，正等着从这个人手里被救出去。"

===Original===
“I can’t help admiring him!”

“I do not minimize his powers, but there are powers which are evil in their influence.”

“And yet more really human and greater than those that are called good!” she cried; and her bosom, slender and youthful as a girl’s, swelled as she confronted Wenk.

“Ah, now I understand you, dear lady. Listen. Not more really human or greater, for power is power. One display of it cannot be measured by another; it is only its essence we can judge. Everything is human, the good as well as the bad. Evil forces only reap their advantage through the destruction of good ones, and this advantage is for the destroyer alone. The forces of good benefit all without yielding their possessor that gross material gain which he who practises evil strives to attain. Which is the nobler? That is what you must ask yourself, and if there is an exuberance of energy in your temperament which you cannot make use of in the class of society to which you belong, and yet do not desire to keep inactive.﻿ ﻿… However, these people are beginning to notice our talk. I expect the blond has his spies everywhere. Allow me to take leave of you and request an opportunity of continuing this conversation.”

===Chinese===
"我忍不住佩服他！"

"我并没有贬低他的力量，可是有的力量，它的影响是恶的。"

"可它们比那些称作善的力量更真实、更有人性，也更伟大！"她喊道；她那少女般纤细年轻的胸脯起伏着，迎面望着文克。

"啊，现在我明白您了，亲爱的夫人。听着。谈不上更真实、更有人性，也谈不上更伟大，因为力量就是力量。力量的一种施展无法拿另一种来度量；我们所能评判的，只有它的本质。一切都是人性的，善也罢，恶也罢。邪恶的力量只有通过摧毁善的力量才能收获它的好处，而这好处只归毁灭者一人。善的力量造福众人，却不给它的拥有者带来那种种行恶之徒拼命追求的粗鄙的物质收益。哪一种更高贵？这是您必须自问的。倘若您的天性里有一股过剩的精力，在您所属的社会阶层里无从施展，而您又不甘让它闲置。……不过，这些人开始注意我们的谈话了。我看那金发家伙到处都安插着眼线。请容我告辞，并请您给我一个继续这次谈话的机会。"

===Original===
“Come and see me tomorrow; come at teatime please. Ask for Countess Told, at Tutzing.”

She gave him her hand. Wenk, to whom her name supplied the clue to that mysterious flight when Count Told had entered the room, kissed her slender fingers, yielding himself momentarily to her charm and beauty, and toying with the foolish notion of abandoning his chase of criminals and yielding to the pursuit of this woman. With these thoughts in his mind, he said farewell.

Left to herself, the Countess reflected: “We women have no imagination. I was looking for an adventure among these gamblers absorbed in their play, and when it presented itself I imagined it was but an intrigue. But *this* is a man, indeed! He devotes his life to his task, and no man can give more than his life, and there is nothing greater or more beautiful than life. If only I had the chance of doing likewise!” She resolved to follow Wenk’s leading and do all that she could to help him.

===Chinese===
"明天来看我吧；请下午茶的时候来。到图青找托尔德伯爵夫人。"

她向他伸出手。她的名字为文克解开了一个疑团——那回托尔德伯爵进屋时，她那场神秘的出逃。他吻了吻她纤细的手指，一时间沉醉于她的魅力与美貌之中，心里玩味着一个荒唐的念头：抛下对罪犯的追猎，转而追求这个女人。他带着这些念头道了别。

只剩下自己一人时，伯爵夫人思忖道："我们女人没有想象力。我到这些沉迷于牌局的赌徒中间寻找一场奇遇，奇遇当真来了，我却只当它是一段私情。可是*这才*是一个真正的男人！他把生命奉献给自己的事业，而一个人至多献出生命，再没有比生命更伟大、更美好的东西了。但愿我也有机会这样做！"她决意听从文克的引领，尽其所能帮助他。

---

===Original===
Among his letters next morning Wenk noticed a small registered parcel. He opened it, to find his watch and his purse with the money intact. The notebook alone was missing, and on a card these words were typed:

> “I am no ghoul. The things my subordinate took from you in error are returned herewith. I am keeping the notebook because its contents concern me.﻿—**Balling.**”

Wenk was scarcely surprised. This man had thousands at stake; what were a few beggarly hundreds and a gold watch to him? He did not need this to convince him that it was really himself, and more particularly his notebook, that was concerned. He put away both watch and purse and let his thoughts linger on the alluring Countess.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，在一堆信件里，文克注意到一个小小的挂号包裹。他拆开一看，里面是他的表和钱袋，分文未动。唯独笔记本不见了，附着一张卡片，打着这样几行字：

> "我不是食尸鬼。我的手下误从您身上取走的东西，兹随函奉还。笔记本我留下了，因为里面的内容与我有关。——**巴林**"

文克几乎并不惊讶。这人干的是成千上万的营生；区区几百块寒酸小钱和一块金表，于他算得了什么？用不着这一手来让他相信，对方真正在意的正是他本人，尤其是他那本笔记本。他把表和钱袋收好，任由思绪在那个迷人的伯爵夫人身上流连。

===Original===
In the afternoon he was received at her house, a mansion sumptuously arranged, but in a style that offended Wenk, for since yesterday his ideas of the Countess had made considerable advance, and it would have been pleasant to find himself more in sympathy with her tastes than this home of hers evidenced.

In the very entrance-hall the walls had been painted all over in Cubist forms and conventional designs tortured into weird shapes in endless succession, with splashes of colour here and there, as if to create an impression of the ardent temperament of the designer. “You are cold and passionless,” said he to himself; “of so calculating and cold a nature that if one among you disappears, the others have not enough red blood in their veins to notice his absence?”

The butler, the dark severity of whose livery was lightened by small silver buttons and blue lappets, took his hat and coat from him and announced him to the Countess, who was sitting at the tea-table.

“We shall not be alone long,” she said; “my husband will be home at five o’clock.”

===Chinese===
下午，他到她府上做客。宅邸陈设豪华，风格却让文克生厌，因为从昨天起，他对伯爵夫人的想法已经大有进展，倘若她的品位能比这座居所所显示的更与他相投，那本会是一件令人愉快的事。

就在门厅里，四壁从头到尾画满了立体主义的图形，传统纹样被扭曲成种种怪形，层出不穷，这里一点那里一撇地溅着颜色，仿佛要给人造成一种印象：设计者有着怎样火热的性情。"你们是冰冷而无情的，"他暗自说道，"你们的本性这样斤斤计较、这样冷，你们中间哪怕少了一个人，其余人血管里也没有足够的热血去察觉他的消失？"

管家接过他的帽子和外套，进去向坐在茶桌旁的伯爵夫人通报；管家那一身深色制服的严峻，被小小的银纽扣和蓝色饰襟冲淡了几分。

"我们单独待不了多久，"她说，"我丈夫五点钟回家。"

===Original===
But the decorations of the house had made Wenk feel unsympathetic, and before he answered he cast a hasty glance at the walls of the room. The Countess noticed it.

“That is all my husband’s doing,” she said. “To me it appears simply hideous. What are you to make out of it, if one paints a peasant, indicates some freshly painted barns, and then tells the beholder that it is a symphony of Beethoven’s? However, everyone to his taste﻿—or are you perhaps a Futurist also?”

“I cannot say that,” said Wenk, “but you seem to imply that they are the only moderns. Yet all men in secret speak the same language. Our freedom to express ourselves comes only from individuality!”

“You want to be free?” said the lady. “Are you not your own salvation? Does not your calling, your expenditure of energy, give you your inner freedom? There is no salvation from without!”

===Chinese===
可是这宅子的装饰已让文克心生疏离，他答话之前，先朝房间的四壁匆匆扫了一眼。伯爵夫人注意到了。

"这全是我丈夫搞的，"她说，"在我看来简直丑陋不堪。画一个农夫，点几笔刚刷过漆的谷仓，然后告诉观者这是贝多芬的一首交响曲——这叫人怎么理解？不过，各人有各人的口味——莫非您也是个未来主义者？"

"这我谈不上，"文克说，"不过照您的说法，仿佛唯有他们才算现代人。其实所有的人在内心深处说着同一种语言。我们表达自我的自由，只能来自个性！"

"您想要自由？"夫人说，"您不就是自己的救赎吗？您的天职，您精力的付出，难道没有给您内在的自由吗？救赎从不来自外部！"

===Original===
“That is quite true,” said Wenk simply; and the womanly image which had haunted him since yesterday, and which seemed to be lost on entering this house, once more returned to his mind. “It is really what we were talking of yesterday, this balance of the forces of good and evil, and I wanted to talk to you about that again today.”

“I understood you aright,” answered the Countess. “I will confess to you that at first I thought you were on the search for an intrigue, and the idea amused me considerably, for God knows I seek something very different in the gaming-houses.”

“You will find what you are seeking in my work, Countess,” rejoined Wenk quickly.

Suddenly the butler, in his black livery, with its blue lappets and silver buttons, appeared noiselessly, and bent down whispering something to his mistress.

“My husband!” said the Countess to Wenk, fixing a steady and lingering glance on him, and as the Count came forward she introduced the two men.

===Chinese===
"这话很对。"文克坦率地说。自昨天起一直萦绕在他心头的那幅女性形象——走进这座宅子时仿佛丢掉了——此刻又回到了他的脑海。"这正是我们昨天谈的，善与恶两种力量的平衡，今天我想就这个再同您谈谈。"

"我没有理解错。"伯爵夫人答道，"我向您坦白，起初我以为您是在找一段私情，这念头让我觉得挺有趣，因为天知道，我在赌场里寻的是完全不同的东西。"

"您要找的东西，我的事业里就有，伯爵夫人。"文克立刻接口道。

忽然，那管家无声无息地出现了，黑制服，蓝饰襟，银纽扣，俯身对女主人低语了些什么。

"我丈夫来了！"伯爵夫人对文克说，目光定定地、久久地盯住他；伯爵走上前来，她介绍了两位男士。

===Original===
Count Told was an extremely thin man, and gave an impression of excessive sprightliness. He was surprisingly young and very fashionably dressed. He gesticulated a good deal, and the movement of his hands gave prominence to a ring he wore, set with an unusual gem, such as Wenk had never before seen.

It might have been a flame topaz, with streaks of bloodred across it, trailing off into milky whiteness at the edges and emphasizing the clear honey colour of the transparent stone. In the middle of it, just where its lightning rays were most dazzling, was a tiny pearl, an islet, hardly larger than a freckle, but of a blue that put the sapphire into the shade, and.﻿ ﻿…

Thus Wenk was thinking to himself, unable to keep his eyes from the jewel.

“It is a trifle too big for my hand,” said the Count, answering his visitor’s unspoken thoughts, “but the stone is so﻿ ﻿… how shall I describe its originality? Well, I can only say that it is like a recital by Endivian, whom you doubtless know, and it was he who gave it to me. He brought it back from Penderappimur.”

===Chinese===
托尔德伯爵是个瘦得干瘪的男人，给人一股过分活泼跳脱的印象。他年轻得惊人，衣着十分入时。他说话时频频打手势，双手的动作让他手上那枚戒指格外显眼：戒上镶着一颗异样的宝石，文克从未见过那样的东西。

那也许是一颗火焰托帕石，横贯着血红的条纹，边缘渐渐淡入乳白，愈发衬出这透明石头澄澈的蜜色。宝石正中，恰在它闪电般的光芒最炫目之处，嵌着一粒小小的珍珠，像一座小岛，大不过一颗雀斑，那蓝色却连蓝宝石也要黯然失色，还有……

文克就这样出神地想着，眼睛离不开那颗宝石。

"戴在我手上是稍大了些，"伯爵答道，竟应中了客人未出口的心思，"可这石头实在太……叫我怎么形容它的独特别致呢？这么说吧，它就像恩迪维安的一场朗诵会——您想必知道此人——这颗宝石正是他送我的，是他从彭德拉皮穆尔带回来的。"

===Original===
“Is he the fashionable jeweller nowadays?” asked Wenk, who seemed somewhat at sea.

“Herr von Wenk,” said the Countess gravely, “Endivian is the fashionable young Goethe of this season.” Then she laughed. “No! Endivian the poet received the jewel at the Court of Artimerxes II, instead of the goblet, from the poem of his spiritual father﻿ ﻿… you know it, ‘Give me no golden chain’﻿ ﻿… and when he returned, he announced in Germany, much as the Pope announces the Golden Rose, that his greatest admirer should have it. The choice fell upon my husband. It would have been better if he had given it me.”

“Why don’t you enthuse about him as I do?” asked the Count, with a pleasant smile, looking at her very tenderly as he spoke.

“Peter Resch dedicated his rubbish to him, and that was enough for me,” was the Countess’s laughing retort.

===Chinese===
"他是眼下走红的珠宝商吗？"文克问，显得有些摸不着头脑。

"冯·文克先生，"伯爵夫人正色道，"恩迪维安是本季走红的年轻歌德。"说到这里她笑了。"不对！诗人恩迪维安这颗宝石，是在阿尔提墨克西斯二世的宫廷里领受的，用以代替他精神之父诗中的那只金杯……那首诗您知道——'别赠我金链'……他回到德国后宣布——很有点像教皇颁赐金玫瑰那样——这颗宝石将归他最热烈的崇拜者。结果选中的是我丈夫。他要是一开始就送给我，倒更好些。"

"你为什么不像我一样为他倾倒呢？"伯爵问，脸上带着愉快的微笑，说话时无限温柔地望着她。

"彼得·雷施把自己那些破烂题献给了他，这对我来讲就足够了。"伯爵夫人笑着回敬。

===Original===
“Pooh, Peter Resch, indeed!” said the Count. “He is one of the Impressionists who has arrived. By the way, dearest, I have got something new.”

“From the Jennifer gallery?”

“Can one get a real picture anywhere else? There is nothing left.﻿ ﻿… And one has a clear and incontestable and direct impression. If the artistic temperament would only renounce colour﻿ ﻿… it would be the beginning of really abstract thought, of the detachment from everything which needs the help of another consciousness to interpret its vision.”

The Countess replied, with apparent earnestness: “Thank Heaven, we do get a little further. If in the realm of music, too, genius had any prospect of renouncing the crash of sound when it desires to express itself, the world would soon be attaining its aim.”

===Chinese===
"呸，还彼得·雷施呢！"伯爵说，"他是功成名就的印象派之一。对了，亲爱的，我又弄到一件新东西。"

"从珍妮弗画廊买的？"

"除了那儿，还能在别处弄到真正的画吗？好东西一点不剩。……而且人得到的是一种清晰、无可争议、直截了当的印象。艺术气质要是肯舍弃色彩就好了……那将是真正抽象思想的发端，是从一切需要借助他人意识来解读其所见之物中解脱出来的开端。"

伯爵夫人以貌似认真的口吻答道："谢天谢地，我们总算又前进了一点。倘若在音乐领域，天才也有望在想表达自己时舍弃那一片轰响，世界很快就要达到目的了。"

===Original===
The Count went on enthusiastically: “A sublime atmosphere of space﻿ ﻿… in two blues﻿ ﻿… which project into the cosmogony and play upon each other between storm and lightning.﻿ ﻿…

“Whereupon the Almighty leaves His seat, dear Herr von Wenk, saying, ‘My creation has surpassed Me; I take My leave!’ ”

The conversation continued in this tone for awhile, and an hour later Wenk took his leave. He felt depressed as he drove home, but he had hardly sat down to dinner when a note was brought him, and he read:

> Dear Herr von Wenk,
> I am sorry that our meeting today fell out differently from the one we had planned. That is not why I am writing to you, however, for we can continue our conversation in another place and at another time. But you may have left our house under the impression that my husband was “nothing but a fool,” and in his wife’s eyes too, and that would have been my fault, so I want to entreat you not to allow yourself to take up a depreciatory attitude. It is true that the Count buys Futurist pictures, but that must be understood more or less symbolically. I have always found that the more “foolish” a man appeared at one’s first encounter with him, the more approachable he became when one met him in his more serious moments.
> Au revoir﻿ ﻿… but when, and where?
> Yours sincerely, 

 **Lucy Told**.

===Chinese===
伯爵兴致勃勃地继续说："一种崇高的空间氛围……用两种蓝色……直投射进宇宙创生论里，在风暴与闪电之间彼此激荡。……"

"于是全能的上帝就从宝座上起身了，亲爱的冯·文克先生，说道：'我的造物已超越了我；就此告退！'"

谈话就这样继续了一阵，一个钟头后，文克告辞而去。归途中他心境抑郁，但晚饭刚坐下，便有人送来一张短简，他读道：

> 亲爱的冯·文克先生：
> 很遗憾，我们今天的会面同原先计划的走了样。不过我写这封信倒不为此，我们的谈话尽可以在别的地方、别的时间继续。只是您离开舍下时，心里也许存着一种印象，觉得我丈夫"不过是个蠢货"，在他妻子眼里也是如此——若真这样，那就是我的过错了。所以我恳求您，不要让自己采取一种轻慢的态度。伯爵确实购买未来派的画，可这一点多少得从象征意义上去理解。我一向发现，初次见面时显得越"蠢"的男人，在他较为严肃的时刻遇见时，就越容易接近。
> 再会……可是何时？何地？
> 您真诚的
> **露西·托尔德**

===Original===
“So Lucy is her name? And indeed she is rightly called Light. If she were *my* light of life!﻿ ﻿… Oh, what a fool I am,” said he, as he felt an unaccustomed warmth steal over him﻿—a warmth for which he always yearned.﻿ ﻿… Then he stood up, shaking off these delicious tremors, and saying sternly to himself, “This is a pretty way to reach a criminal﻿ ﻿… through falling in love with a beautiful woman.”

The telephone rang: “Hull speaking!”

Hull told him that a new gaming-house had been opened, and he really must visit it. The saloon was not only arranged to accommodate a large number of people, at least a hundred, but it had certain mechanical contrivances which could turn it into a music-hall if the police were to appear. He did not know how it was done, but Cara had written to him about it and she was always au courant of any new sensation of this kind. They were going there, and taking Karstens with them, but Hull did not know the address of this place, and they would trust to Cara’s guidance. Of course, she knew nothing about his writing to Wenk.

A rendezvous was arranged, and at ten o’clock Wenk drove to the Café Bastin, whence they were to set out.

===Chinese===
"这么说，露西就是她的名字？果然名副其实——露西（Lucy），'光'。倘若她是*我*的生命之光呢！……唉，我多么愚蠢。"他自语道，只觉一股久违的暖意悄悄爬遍全身——一种他始终渴求的暖意。……随后他站起身来，抖落这阵美妙的战栗，严厉地对自己说："想追到罪犯，这倒是个好办法……爱上一个美丽的女人。"

电话铃响了："我是赫尔！"

赫尔告诉他，一家新赌场开张了，他实在非去不可。那间厅堂不但布置得能容纳大批人——至少一百个——还装着一些机关，一旦警察露面，就能把它变作一间音乐厅。机关是怎么弄的，他不知道，是卡拉写信告诉他的，这类新鲜轰动的玩意儿她永远消息灵通。他们打算去，还带上卡斯滕斯，只是赫尔不知道那地方的确切地址，全指望卡拉领路。当然，卡拉对赫尔写信给文克的事一无所知。

双方约好了碰头，十点钟文克驱车前往巴斯廷咖啡馆，他们将从那里出发。

## VII / 七

===Original===
The house they entered lay on the border of the inner city, in one of the mean, sordid streets leading to Schwabing. Its outward appearance, like its neighbours’, showed an unimposing façade. It was one of those shops having lodgings above, and the sliding shutters over the shop were drawn to the ground. It was too dark to read the name, but Wenk noticed the number, that of his birth-year—’76.

They entered a dirty stairway in which hung a dusty globe, which gave an indifferent light to the changing population who inhabited such houses as these, and then ascended two flights of stairs. A heavy door opened before them, and in a corridor at the side a light shone out over the miserable staircase. The corridor ran alongside the staircase; it was completely empty: a cheap and shabby black and white drugget ran throughout its length, and its walls were covered with faded paper-hangings.

“This is lively,” said Cara, “but just wait a moment!”

Then a small door opened from the corridor and a light streamed forth into the gloomy darkness. They looked upon a swelter of luxury. There was a little foyer with cushions and curtains, cloakroom accommodation, little restaurant tables, etc. There was the odour of prepared foods and the popping of champagne corks. People they did not know were sitting there. The visitors laid aside hats and coats and went through into the restaurant.

===Chinese===
他们走进的那所房子坐落在内城边上，位于一条通往施瓦宾（Schwabing）的破落龌龊的街上。外表同左邻右舍一样，一副不起眼的门脸。这是那种楼上带住家的铺面房，店堂的推拉护窗板直落到底。天太黑，招牌上的名字看不清，但文克留意到了门牌号码——恰好是他出生的年份：’76。

他们走进一道肮脏的楼梯间，里面悬着一盏蒙尘的球形灯，给这类房子里来来去去、走马灯似的住客洒下一片无动于衷的光；随后上了两层楼梯。一扇厚重的门在他们面前打开，旁侧走廊里透出灯光，照着寒碜的楼梯。走廊与楼梯平行，空空荡荡：一条廉价寒酸的黑白色粗呢地毯贯通到底，墙上贴着褪色的墙纸。

“这儿可真热闹，”卡拉说，“不过且再等一等！”

这时走廊上一扇小门打开，灯光倾泻而出，涌进阴沉沉的黑暗里。眼前是一片奢华的热浪。小小的前厅里备有软垫和帷幔，有存衣处，有餐馆式的小桌等等。空气里飘着烹制好的菜肴香味，还有香槟开瓶的脆响。一些他们不认识的人生在那里。来客们脱下帽子和大衣，径直走进里面的餐厅。

===Original===
Yes, there things looked different. On entry, the place recalled the promenade of a well-known Théâtre de Variétés in Paris. Through little peepholes or from the boxes one could see a smooth surface gleaming with light. This was the gaming-table, and it was of immense size. In the middle there was a circular opening in which was placed a large revolving chair. It was the seat for the croupier. Around the table the places for the players were arranged like boxes. Every box—there were some single ones, some for two and some for four persons—lay shut off from the rest and in darkness, and all were furnished with comfortable seats. People could be entirely separated from each other by a curtain, and a grating, like those of the Parisian theatres, could be drawn at will. The players might gamble there as securely as if masked, and, without being recognized or even seen, could indulge their passion for the tables.

Two miniature rails led from each seat to the croupier, and upon these stood a little truck. This was to carry the stakes down and later bring the winnings back. The sum was made known by sliding numbers displayed on a board. The pressure of a button sent each vehicle to its destined spot.

On the dome above the table, in the circle formed by the boxes, were the *petits chevaux* in varied colours. The little brass horses had been carved by a Cubist, and painted in their various colours with highly glazed enamel. They were set in motion by a crank turned by the croupier. In the middle, beneath the horses, there hung a little searchlight which, lighted from below, reflected light upon the dome, and in this light they ran with the dome as a background. This was painted in the colours of the spectrum arranged alternately, so that there was always a dark horse against a light colour and a light one against a dark colour, followed by their shadows. This gave the effect of promiscuity which was intensified the faster they ran. The goal was formed by a thin strip of tiny electric lights let into the dome, and every box had an arrangement of mirrors by which its occupants could clearly recognize the winner.

===Chinese===
不错，到了里面，气象便截然不同了。一进门，这地方便令人想起巴黎一家著名杂耍剧场（Théâtre de Variétés）的观众散步厅。透过一个个小小的窥孔，或者从包厢里，可以望见一片平滑的台面熠熠生光。那便是赌台，其大无比。台子正中有一个圆孔，孔里安着一把宽大的转椅，那是荷官（croupier）的座位。赌台四周，赌客的席位布置得如同一个个包厢。每个包厢——有单人的，有双人的，也有四人的——都与其余各处隔绝，隐在黑暗里，座座都配着舒适的座椅。拉上帘子，人与人之间便可彻底隔开，还可以随意拉下一道格栅，就像巴黎剧院里的那种。赌客们在里面赌起来稳当得如同戴着面具，不被人认出、甚至不被人看见，便可纵情沉溺于赌台之癖。

每个座位通向荷官处都铺着两条微型铁轨，轨上停着一辆小斗车，用来把赌注送下去，再把赢来的钱送回来。数额由一块板子上滑动排列的数字牌显示。按钮一按，每辆小车便驶往它的目的地。

赌台上方的穹顶上、包厢围出的圆圈里，是五彩缤纷的「小马」（petits chevaux）。这些黄铜小马出自一位立体派艺术家之手，以色彩各异的亮釉绘成。荷官摇动曲柄，它们便奔跑起来。正当中、小马群下方悬着一盏小探照灯，自下打光，把亮光反射到穹顶上，小马便在这片光里以穹顶为背景奔跑。穹顶涂着光谱七色、交替排布，因此总有一匹深色马衬在浅色底上、一匹浅色马衬在深色底上，身后拖着各自的影子。这便造成一种纷杂交错的观感，跑得越快越加浓烈。终点是嵌进穹顶的一线小电灯，每个包厢都装有一套反光镜，包厢里的人凭它可以清清楚楚认出获胜的马。

===Original===
Wenk and his companions took their places in a box for four, which seemed to have been reserved for them. Cara and Karstens sat in front, the two other men behind.

When the boxes were all filled, the croupier gathered his elegant evening dress about him, and slowly began to revolve in his seat, as if on a mechanical rotating disk, while he delivered the following oration:

“Ladies and gentlemen, this is the Go-Ahead Institute. The Go-Ahead has in itself the roots of vigour and success. We live in times of change, and our undertaking is designed to suit all comers. Here you can play alone, or as a pair, or in company. You can play alone, because you can have a box for one person only, like the charming lady of whom I can see no more than the red heron’s feathers in her coiffure. If you think that for good luck two heads are better than one, you can seclude yourself from your fellows like yonder elegant cavalier and his lady; and if you choose to play in company you are equally invisible from my point of view. In the dome, ladies and gentlemen, you will find our game, the game of the house, I may venture to call it, although every other game is equally at your service. There you see the *petits chevaux* of the Go-Ahead Institute. One of the first artists of our day, whose work you are constantly encountering in exhibitions and periodicals, has designed them for the ‘Go-ahead,’ and placed them here, and we have united art with technique, the strongest product of the age. The reflecting apparatus allows everyone from any place whatsoever to see at once and quite distinctly whether his horse is in at the finish. Allow me to demonstrate to you, by a mere turn of the handle, the very artistic and effective play and counterplay which is developing in the dome. There was once a man who had no shadow, but that cannot be said of our *petits chevaux*. Notice, I beseech you, the extremely artistic effect produced when substance and shadow thus unite in a piece of work which in its resourcefulness and originality does the greatest credit to the artist of our house.…”

===Chinese===
文克和同伴们在一间四人包厢里落座，这包厢似乎是特意给他们留的。卡拉和卡斯滕斯坐在前排，另外两位男士坐在后面。

各包厢坐满之后，荷官拢了拢他一身考究的晚礼服，在座位里缓缓转动起来，仿佛坐在一只机械转盘上，同时发表了下面这篇演说：

“女士们，先生们，这里是前进馆（Go-Ahead Institute）。‘前进’二字本身，便孕育着活力与成功的根苗。我们生活在一个变革的时代，本馆的营生为四方来客量身打造。在这里，您可以独赌，可以成双，也可以结伴。您可以独赌，因为您可以包下一间单人包厢，就像那位迷人的女士——我只望得见她发髻间红鹭的羽饰。倘若您相信凑好运道，两个人头比一个强，您尽可以像那边那对风雅的骑士与他的淑女一样，与同伴隔离开来；而您若愿意结伴同赌，在我的位置看您也同样隐形。女士们，先生们，在穹顶上，您将看到我们的赌戏——我斗胆称之为本馆的馆戏，尽管其他各种玩法也一应听凭尊便。您看到的，就是前进馆的「小马」。当今第一流的艺术家之一——他的作品您在展览和画刊上随时可以见到——为‘前进馆’设计了这些小马，把它们安放在这里；我们将艺术与技术、这个时代最强有力的产物，合为一体。反光装置使任何人从任何位置都能立刻一清二楚地看到自己的马是否率先冲线。请允许我仅凭一转摇柄，为您演示穹顶上正在展开的这场极富艺术效果、互为攻守的游戏。从前有个人没有影子，可这话万万说不到我们的「小马」头上。请您务必留意，当实体与影子如此交融于一处时那种极致的艺术效果——这件作品以其匠心与独创，为本馆的艺术家赢得了最大的荣光。……”

===Original===
He turned the crank, and horses and shadows chased each other with kaleidoscopic effect. It formed a pretty and a fanciful picture. Slowly the horses came to a standstill.

“I had staked on that one,” exclaimed a woman’s voice as the cream-coloured bay stopped beneath the goal, and in its head the eyes gleamed forth like stars. They were formed of small electric lamps.

The croupier said: “I will not detain you much longer from trying your luck, dear madam. I have only now to introduce to you the epoch-making novelty of the Go-Ahead Institute. What would you do, ladies and gentlemen” (here he raised his voice), “if the police were suddenly to intrude upon you and rob you of your money and your freedom on account of your forbidden game? You need have no anxiety on that score. We have hit upon an arrangement which might be called a *garde-police*. The Go-Ahead Institute may await the police quite calmly. They may be surrounded and inundated by the police. With a pressure of my little finger I can turn the whole police force of the city away from you and let them go ahead elsewhere. Look here!”

===Chinese===
他摇动曲柄，小马和影子便你追我赶，恍如万花筒。这幅图景又美丽又奇幻。小马渐渐停了下来。

“我押的就是它！”一个女人的声音喊道。那匹奶油色的栗毛小马停在终点线下，马头上一双眼睛如星辰般闪亮——那是几盏小电灯做成的。

荷官说道：“亲爱的夫人，我不再多耽搁您试运气了。现在我只消向诸位介绍前进馆划时代的新发明。女士们，先生们，倘若警察突然闯进来，以您玩禁赌之戏为由，夺走您的钱财和自由，您将如何是好？”（说到这里他提高了嗓音）“这一点诸位尽可放宽心。我们想出了一套布置，不妨称之为‘防警术’（garde-police）。前进馆尽可以从从容容地恭候警察。任凭他们把这里围个水泄不通。我只消小指一按，就能调开全城警力，打发他们上别处‘前进’去。请看！”

===Original===
He raised his hand, then lowered it with affected impressiveness, pressing his forefinger down upon the black knob near him. A moment later the surface of the table was set in motion, and it began to sink. It moved rapidly and noiselessly, and the speaker sank down with it. The boxes remained stationary, but from the dome the little horses and the coloured circles descended—came past the boxes; the dome followed, and a few minutes later a quartet of nude twelve-year-old children were to be seen dancing, upon a new stage, to the strains of fiddles and harps, which began to resound from some invisible quarter. A body of men, dressed in the uniform of the city police, trooped into the boxes, exclaiming, “We were told they were gambling here! Where are the gamblers?”

Everybody in the boxes roared with laughter. The girls continued dancing, and the uniformed police threw off their disguise and appeared in evening dress, laughing. The floor began to move again, the girls still dancing, one of them making a gesture to a gentleman sitting alone, who sprang towards her, but failed to reach his vanishing charmer. The floor once more became the ceiling, the *petits chevaux* reappeared, and in the centre of the gaming-table sat the croupier once again.

“You see, ladies and gentlemen, we do give the police something for their pains—the nude girls! And if the case were really serious, they would soon have a scrap of clothing on. I have to announce that there is a change of programme every week.…” He continued for some time further in this way.

===Chinese===
他抬起手，又装模作样、郑重其事地压下去，食指按在身旁的黑色旋钮上。片刻之后，台面动了起来，开始下沉。它迅疾无声地移动，演说者也随之沉降下去。包厢纹丝不动，穹顶上的小马和彩色圆环却从高处降下来——从包厢旁边经过；穹顶也随之降落。几分钟后，只见一座新舞台上，四个十二岁的裸体孩童正随着提琴和竖琴的乐声翩翩起舞——乐声从看不见的什么地方响了起来。一小队身穿本市警察制服的人涌进包厢，嚷道：“有人报告说这里在赌博！赌徒们在哪儿？”

各包厢里的人哄堂大笑。女孩们继续跳舞，穿制服的“警察”甩掉伪装，露出晚礼服，笑个不停。地板再度移动，女孩们且舞且随，其中一个朝一位独坐的先生做了个手势，那人向她扑去，却够不着他那转瞬隐没的可人儿。地板重新变回天花板，「小马」再度现身，荷官又端坐在赌台中央了。

“女士们，先生们，您瞧，警察们的辛苦到底也不曾白费——这不有裸体姑娘们嘛！而倘若事态果真紧急，她们转眼就会披上一片衣衫。谨此奉告：本馆节目每周一换。……”他又如此这般讲了好一阵子。

===Original===
“This is only an ordinary cinema,” said Wenk, turning to Karstens, and whispering, “the most ordinary kind of cinema. If the police were to come, they would discover the whole trick in ten minutes.”

Karstens merely shrugged his shoulders.

Wenk wondered what the aim of such an establishment could be, for it was bound to be discovered and closed within a week’s time, and the outlay must have been considerable.

Hull was much struck, having nothing with which to compare what he saw and heard there.

“Ravishing! enchanting!” said Cara from time to time. “We live in ingenious times, don’t we? We must come here often, mustn’t we, Eddie? Which are you going to stake on? I am choosing the black Arab. Black for me, please Eddie, because you are so fair!”

===Chinese===
“这不过是个寻常的电影戏法，”文克转向卡斯滕斯，低声说，“最寻常不过的电影戏法。警察真要来了，十分钟之内就能识破全部机关。”

卡斯滕斯只是耸了耸肩。

文克暗自疑惑，这样一家场子图的是什么——不出一个礼拜，它注定要被查出来封掉，而开办的花销想必不菲。

赫尔却大受震动，此情此景、所闻所见，他平生无可比拟。

“销魂夺魄！迷人得很！”卡拉不时地赞叹，“我们活在一个多么灵巧的时代，是不是？咱们可得常来，对不对，埃迪？你打算押哪一匹？我挑那匹黑色的阿拉伯马。我要黑的，求你了埃迪——谁叫你生得这么白净！”

===Original===
Karstens cast an amused glance at Wenk. A supper of the most varied and recherché dainties was provided. Things which seemed to have vanished in the depreciation of the German currency were seen—pâté de foie gras, fresh truffles, caviar, fieldfares.… In front of a pile of truffles and foie gras, inhaling its pleasant odour, Karstens said suddenly:

“Our mark today stands at seven in Switzerland, but it is seven centimes, and here things which we have forgotten we ever ordered are provided for us.”

“Here a mark is worth less than seven centimes,” said Wenk, downcast and depressed. Whither was it all tending? His heart yearned for help in his enterprise, and he had no appetite for dainties.

===Chinese===
卡斯滕斯朝文克投去有趣的一瞥。夜宵端了上来，全是花样繁多、精致考究的珍馐。那些似乎已在德国货币贬值中绝迹的东西赫然在列——鹅肝酱、鲜松露、鱼子酱、田鸫……卡斯滕斯在一堆松露和鹅肝前，嗅着那股好闻的香气，忽然开口：

“如今我们的马克在瑞士的行情是七，可它本身只值七生丁；而在这里，连我们自己都忘了点过的东西，也照样给我们端上来。”

“在这里，一个马克还值不上七生丁。”文克说，神情沮丧抑郁。这一切究竟要走向何方？他一心盼着有人助成他的事业，对珍馐美味毫无胃口。

===Original===
Cara trilled a popular ditty, and Hull, in spite of the influence which she exercised over him, and his enjoyment of unwonted dainties, began secretly to be somewhat ashamed. He resolved to send her a parting present on the morrow, and it should be the parure of Australian opals she so ardently desired, which a Russian princess, anxious to get on the stage by Cara’s help, was willing to sell. “This should end it all,” said Hull to himself. He was disenchanted, and yet at the same time melancholy. What would become of her? For himself, he almost thought he would prefer the cloister to.…

Just then he savoured a delicious mouthful of truffle, and as he smacked his lips over it, Hull thought, “Well, there’s something to be said for this sort of thing, after all. I should not get any more aspic… and I’ve not broken with her yet, anyhow!…”

Suddenly Wenk got up to go.

===Chinese===
卡拉啭着嗓子哼起一支流行小调。赫尔呢，尽管受着她的摆布，也尽管享用着这些平日难得的美味，心底里却暗暗生出几分羞惭。他决意明日送她一件分手的赠礼，就送她朝思暮想的那套澳大利亚欧泊（Australian opals）首饰——那是一位俄国公主愿意出让的，她正巴望靠卡拉提携登上舞台。“就此一刀两断吧。”赫尔心里说。他幻梦已醒，却又同时心生惆怅。她将来会落到什么下场？至于他自己，他几乎觉得，自己情愿进修道院，也不愿……

正在此时，他美美地品尝了一口松露，咂着嘴回味，赫尔想道：“唔，话说回来，这种日子毕竟也有它的好处。一刀两断就再也吃不上肉冻了……何况眼下我跟她还没有掰呢！……”

突然，文克起身要走。

===Original===
“Where are you off to?” cried Cara, excited in a moment.

Karstens turned to her at this instant, separating her from Wenk, who left the hall undisturbed. He took his overcoat quickly from the vestibule and was conducted downstairs. The concierge opened the door for him, looking first through the peephole into the street. Then he exclaimed in great excitement: “Sir, there is a policeman standing there!” He opened the door, however, and Wenk went out. The policeman saluted. Wenk saw the uniformed official smiling, and looking back, found the concierge smiling too. The “policeman” belonged to the Go-Ahead Institute. If a real policeman were to enter the street, as the concierge hastily informed the departing guest, he would see that there was already someone on guard and move off.

Wenk soon reached the spot where he had ordered his chauffeur to wait. He was resolved to have this place closed, but he did not want the affair to get into the papers, and on his drive homeward he was considering how best to formulate the charge. If possible the place should not be described, but the cause should be given as that of disturbance of the peace, misleading of the public, swindling performances, or something of that kind. He worked the matter out fully, engaged in his conflict with the Go-Ahead Institute, and while still in his car, in his character of prosecuting counsel, he conducted an indictment which through his skill and stratagem should eliminate this plague-spot from public life without folks perceiving what it actually was.

===Chinese===
“你要上哪儿去？”卡拉叫道，霎时激动起来。

恰在此时，卡斯滕斯转身去招呼她，把她同文克隔开，文克遂得以从容不迫地离开大厅。他从门厅里迅速取过大衣，由人引着下楼。门房先从门镜里朝街上望了一眼，才替他开门。随即他激动万分地喊道：“先生，外头站着一个警察！”不过他还是开了门，文克走了出去。那警察敬了个礼。文克看见那名穿制服的官警面带微笑，回头一望，门房也在微笑。这位“警察”是前进馆的人。倘若真警察走进这条街——门房赶忙向离去的客人解释——他见已经有人值守，自会走开。

文克很快走到他吩咐司机等候的地方。他决意查封这家场子，却不想让这件事见报，归途车上，他一直在盘算起诉的措辞怎样最为妥帖。可能的话，地点一节不必细述，案由只写妨害治安、蒙蔽公众、欺诈性演出之类即可。他把整件事从头到尾推演周全，投入与前进馆的这场较量；人还在车里，便以公诉人的身份，默演了一篇起诉状——凭着他的手腕和计谋，这一状要既把这块毒瘤从公众生活中剔除，又不让世人看出它究竟是什么东西。

===Original===
Before he slept, his thoughts, without any apparent connection to guide them, reverted to Hull, who stood suddenly revealed to him as typical of the young men of the age. Bound by a liaison with a vulgar, good-for-nothing girl, whose only talent was to exhibit herself on the stage; elegantly dressed, without being elegant; spending his restless evenings between gaming-houses, nightclubs, and the arms of a courtesan—this was Hull’s life. Yet if he had taken the right turn he might have put his intelligence and all his available energies into administering an estate or pursuing a well-ordered peaceful life as an official of some kind; he might have been the head of a happy household and the father of legitimate children.

Many such men there were, strong in body and mind, living merely on their nerves, dedicating to a life of the senses powers which would have made them successful in the walk of life for which they were destined. Hull and his kind, feeble and enervated, represented the spirit of the age. What would the dawn of such a midnight yield?

Wenk went to the telephone and gave the address of the new gaming-house. The official whose duty it was to watch over Herr Hull was to get in touch with him at once, but do no more than keep him in sight when he left the house.

===Chinese===
临睡之前，他的思绪不经任何明显的因由，又回到了赫尔身上。此人忽然在他眼前显了形，成了这个时代年轻人的典型。被一个庸俗无用的姑娘拴住——她唯一的本事就是在台上展览自己；衣着考究，人却并不优雅；躁动不安的夜晚，消磨在赌场、夜总会和交际花的怀抱之间——这就是赫尔的生活。然而他若走上了正道，本可以把他的才智和全部可用的精力，拿来经营一份田产，或者当个什么官员，过一种井然安适的生活；他本可以做一户幸福人家的家主，做合法子女的父亲。

这样的人何止一二：身心俱强，却只靠神经活着，把本可令他们在命中行业里功成名就的才力，尽数供奉给声色犬马的生活。赫尔之流，孱弱委顿，正是这个时代精神的写照。这样一口深井似的午夜，又能产出怎样的黎明呢？

文克走到电话机旁，报出新赌场的地址。负责监视赫尔先生的那名警官，须立即与对方取得联系，但除此之外，只须在赫尔离开那所房子后盯住他的行踪。

---

===Original===
In the middle of a deep sleep the telephone at Wenk’s bedside began ringing. It was just two hours since he had returned home, and he was wide awake at once. “Wenk speaking!” said he, and he felt certain in some subconscious region of his mind, which was in tune with his last waking thoughts, that the news awaiting him on the telephone was in some dread, mysterious fashion concerned with Hull.

“Wenk speaking!” he called again, and his whole body was trembling with excitement.

“Here, sir; the police sergeant on duty.”

“Be quick!” said Wenk, his imagination running riot. What was there to report?

===Chinese===
酣睡正浓时，文克床头的电话铃响了起来。他到家不过两个钟头，此刻霎时全然清醒。“我是文克！”他说。在他心灵的某个潜意识角落——那里还与他入睡前最后的思绪同调——他已经断定，电话里等着他的消息，正以某种可怖而神秘的方式，与赫尔有关。

“我是文克！”他又喊了一遍，浑身因激动而发颤。

“是，先生；值班警长。”

“快讲！”文克说，他的想象已奔逸难收。有什么要报告的？

===Original===
The voice at the other end spoke hastily: “The gentleman named Edgar Hull, who was under police protection… has been murdered this night. In the open street, too, about 2 a.m. Another gentleman, name of Karstens, has been seriously wounded. The constable who was detailed to watch over him is also wounded, and both have been taken to the hospital. A lady who was with these gentlemen was arrested at the order of the wounded man. I have ordered the body to be left lying exactly as it was found until you have seen it yourself. The Service car is on its way to your honour. Please ring off!”

“Ring off!” echoed Wenk’s voice agitatedly.

He hastened to dress, for the car was already to be heard throbbing outside. He went down the dark staircase, forgetting to turn a light on. Then, when he perceived the car in the street, his profile revealed the jaws drawn firmly together, in the necessity of meeting calmly the tragic circumstances in which he was involved, and entering into every detail of this deed of blood perpetrated in the darkness of the night, so that he might be enabled to act to the best advantage.

During the drive, something within him compelled him to take himself to task. “I had no business to tremble,” he thought, “when this news reached me. I must be prepared to face even my own death unflinchingly. I must school myself further. I must develop all my tastes and interests and use them in the service of my life’s goal; then only shall I be equal to my task.…”

===Chinese===
对方的声音急促地说：“受警方保护的那位先生，埃德加·赫尔……今夜遇害身亡。而且是在大街上，凌晨两点钟左右。另一位先生，名叫卡斯滕斯，身负重伤。奉命监护他的那名警员也受了伤，两人均已送入医院。与这几位先生同行的一位女士，已奉伤者之命逮捕。我已下令尸体保持发现时的原状，一动不动，直到您亲自验看。勤务车已在开往阁下处的路上。请挂断！”

“挂断！”文克的声音激动地应了一遍。

他赶紧穿衣，汽车的马达声已在门外突突作响。他摸下黑暗的楼梯，忘了开灯。待到在街上望见汽车时，他的侧影显出紧紧咬合的牙关——他必须沉着应对自己卷入的这场惨剧，逐一查究这件于暗夜之中犯下的血案的每个细节，好使自己能采取最有利的行动。

行车途中，他心里有什么在责问他自己。“消息传来时，我不该发抖，”他想，“我必须有准备，连自己的死亡也能毫不畏缩地直面。我必须进一步磨砺自己。我必须发展自己全部的志趣，把它们用于我人生目标的役使；唯有如此，我才能胜任我的任务。……”

===Original===
Hull’s body lay in the darkness. Four men in sombre clothing were silhouetted around him, and they stepped back as their chief descended from the car. Wenk ordered them—they were constables—to watch the entrances to the street and allow no one to approach the scene of the murder, which was in a gloomy street-turning behind the Wittelsbach Palace. Not a soul was to be seen in any of the houses.

One of the constables said that none of the public had been near the place since the occurrence.

It was now three o’clock in the morning. By the light of an electric torch Wenk gazed upon the corpse. There was a gaping wound from the neck down the back, and the body lay with its face to the earth. Thus the police had found Hull when their colleague, blinded with pepper and bleeding from a wound, whistled for help. The body lay motionless, curled up like the gnarled root of a tree. The blood which had flowed from its wounds shone like black marble under the searching light. Wenk was convulsed with horror at the mental images he sought to overcome. He tried to photograph the details of the scene upon his memory, getting the exact position of the corpse. He wrote down the number of the house, tried to ascertain whether all the doors and windows in the neighbourhood were closed, whether any footprints could be seen, or any objects connected with the crime found in the immediate vicinity, but nothing was to be discovered. Its perpetrators had escaped into the palace grounds, one of the policemen had told him, and at one bound they had disappeared. Wenk examined the walls; there, too, there was nothing to be learnt.
