He sent a constable to fetch a car to remove the body, and ordered that nobody was to come into the street on any account. Those who tried to force their way in should be arrested, but people were to be treated with politeness, he said. He then drove to the hospital where the wounded men were lying.

===Chinese===
赫尔的尸体躺在黑暗中。四条身着深色衣服的人影围在四周，见长官下车，便退到一旁。文克命令他们——都是警员——把住街口，不许任何人靠近凶案现场。案发地点在维特尔斯巴赫宫（Wittelsbach Palace）背后一处阴沉沉的街角。四下房屋里不见一个人影。

一名警员说，事发之后，没有一个外人靠近过此地。

此刻是凌晨三点钟。文克借着一支手电的光审视尸体。从颈后直到背脊有一道豁开的伤口，尸体脸朝地面趴着。他们的同事被胡椒迷了眼、带着伤口流着血吹哨呼救时，警察们找到的赫尔正是这副样子。尸体一动不动地蜷缩着，像一截虬结盘曲的树根。伤口里流出的血，在逼视的光线下黑得发亮，宛如黑色大理石。文克竭力想压下心头翻腾的画面，还是惊骇得五脏抽紧。他设法把现场的种种细节摄进记忆，记下尸体的确切位置。他记下门牌号码，查究左近的门窗是否尽皆关闭，地上有无脚印，近旁有无与罪案相关的物件，结果一无所获。据一名警察说，凶手逃进了宫苑，一纵身便没了踪影。文克查验了围墙，那里同样无从究诘。

他派一名警员去调一辆车来移走尸体，并下令无论如何不许任何人进入这条街。硬闯者一律逮捕，但对众人须以礼相待，他说。随后他驱车前往伤者所在的医院。

===Original===
He found Karstens unconscious, and the doctor informed him that he had had a severe wound in the back from a narrow and apparently four-edged dagger, and a blow from some blunt object had probably been aimed at his head. The constable had not been so severely handled, and his were mainly flesh wounds. His shoulder and upper arm were bandaged, but he could scarcely open his eyes even yet.

He related his story thus:

“Just before 2 a.m. the deceased, with a lady and another gentleman, came out of the house which had been pointed out to me. In front of it a constable was standing, and that seemed odd, for I thought to myself, ‘Why is he standing there instead of being on his beat?’ He stood there for at least an hour; then I thought I would speak to him, but he said roughly, ‘What do *you* want? Go away,’ and came threateningly towards me. I was just going to show him my numberplate when the door opened, and although it was dark I could recognize Herr von Hull. The constable pushed me away, and as I did not want to be noticed I moved aside, but I saw that Herr von Hull had a lady and gentleman with him. They went off quickly in the direction of the Ludwigstrasse, and the policeman and I were about three houses away in the other direction. Then he turned to the house again, saying to me, ‘Now you had better be off!’ I didn’t bother any more about him, but followed, at some distance, the lady and the two gentlemen. They turned out of the Türkenstrasse into the Gabelsbergerstrasse and disappeared from my sight. I hurried after them, but could not see them anywhere. They could not have got any further than the Jägerstrasse. Suddenly I heard cries; they were shrill and then stifled. The war had taught me that that was how men in fear of death cry out. Before I could even see anybody I whistled for help, and ran to the street as hard as I could, drawing my revolver.

“I hadn’t gone far when I was suddenly seized from behind. My eyes smarted terribly, and I felt a thrust in my shoulder. I wanted to pull the trigger, but my revolver was no longer in my hand and my arm hung quite limp. Then I thought, ‘I had better do as our major used to advise us—fall down and lie as if I were dead.’ So I fell down and someone sat on me, and shoved something at me, holding my mouth. There may have been two of them; I can’t tell, for I closed my eyes. They must have rushed at me from a doorway, and I was half insensible by that time. What happened after that I do not distinctly remember, but I heard footsteps running, and I was lifted up. It was another constable, and I quickly told him what had happened and he ran on into the street. Then a second one came running up. ‘Police!’ I shouted to him. ‘Yes,’ he called back; ‘what is the matter?’ ‘Run round the corner, quick!’ I told him.

“I forced myself to rise, and then found I was not so badly wounded after all, though I couldn’t open my eyes. They had thrown pepper at them. I groped my way round the corner, but I could not see anything. It was the noise that guided me to the spot. I heard someone speaking, and a woman’s voice answering. ‘What is the matter?’ I said, and a voice answered, ‘He said we were to take the female into custody.’ ‘Who are you?’ I asked the woman, and she answered, ‘I am an actress, the friend of Herr Hull. What do you want with me?’ I said, ‘If the gentleman said so, arrest her!’ She protested, and said she wanted to speak to Dr. Wenk, the State agent, at once, but the constable said she could do that later. Then she tried to run away, and there was a good deal of confusion and bother, and finally the constable had to handcuff her, she was so defiant, and I heard her call out ‘George.’ So I told them to arrest her, and I don’t know what happened after that, for I fainted, and when I came to again I was in the ambulance. I am badly wounded. Will your honour please tell me the truth: am I going to die?”

===Chinese===
他见到卡斯滕斯时，人已不省人事。医生告知，他背上有一道重伤，系一柄窄刃、看去四棱的匕首所致，头部可能还挨过钝器一击。那名警员受的对待没有这般狠，伤的多是皮肉。他的肩头和上臂缠着绷带，可直到此刻还几乎睁不开眼。

他这样讲述了自己的经历：

“凌晨将近两点的时候，死者同一位女士和另一位先生，从那所指定给我的房子里出来。房前站着一个警察，这看着蹊跷，我心里想：‘他不好好巡逻，站在那儿做什么？’他足足站了一个钟头；后来我想上去同他搭话，他粗暴地说：‘你要干什么？滚开。’还气势汹汹地朝我逼近。我正要给他看我的警号，门开了，虽是黑夜，我还是认出了冯·赫尔先生。那警察把我推开；我不想惹人注意，便退到一边，但看见冯·赫尔先生身边带着一位女士和一位先生。他们快步朝路德维希大街方向去了，那警察和我在相反方向，隔着约莫三所房子远。随后他又转身朝房子走去，对我说：‘这回你最好滚吧！’我便不再理会他，远远缀着那位女士和两位先生。他们从图尔肯街（Türkenstrasse）拐进加布尔斯贝格街（Gabelsbergerstrasse），在我眼前消失了。我急忙追赶，哪里也不见人影。他们至多走到耶格街。突然我听见喊声，先是尖厉，随即被捂灭。大战教会过我：人怕死的时候，就是这么喊的。还没等看见人影，我就吹哨呼救，拔出左轮枪，拼尽全力朝那条街奔去。

“没走多远，我突然被人从背后揪住。两眼火辣辣地剧痛，肩头又觉着一记刺扎。我想扣扳机，可左轮枪已不在我手里，胳膊软软地垂着。这时我想：‘还是照咱们少校从前教的那样办——倒下，装死。’于是我倒下了，有人骑住我，捂住我的嘴，往我这里杵什么东西。他们可能是两个，也可能不止，我说不清，因为我闭上了眼。他们准是从某个门洞里扑出来的，那时我已经半昏迷了。后来的事我记不真切，只听得脚步声奔跑，接着有人把我扶了起来。是另一个警员，我很快把发生的事告诉了他，他便朝那条街跑去。接着又跑来第二个。‘警察！’我朝他喊。‘是，’他回喊，‘什么事？’‘绕过街角，快！’我对他说。

“我强撑着爬起来，这才发现自己伤得毕竟不算太重，虽然眼睛睁不开。他们是朝我们撒了胡椒。我摸索着绕过街角，什么也看不见。全凭声音把我引到那个地方。我听见有人说话，一个女人的声音在应答。‘出什么事了？’我问。一个声音答道：‘那位先生吩咐我们把这女人看押起来。’‘你是谁？’我问那女人，她答道：‘我是女演员，赫尔先生的朋友。你们要把我怎么样？’我说：‘既然那位先生这样吩咐了，就把她抓起来！’她抗议，说她要立刻见文克博士、见检察官，警员说这事以后再说也行。接着她想跑，一时人仰马翻、乱作一团，最后警员只好给她上了手铐，她实在太犟，我还听见她喊‘格奥尔格’。于是我吩咐他们把她抓起来，后来的事我就不知道了，因为我晕了过去，再醒过来时，已躺在救护车里。我伤得很重。求阁下对我说实话：我会死吗？”

===Original===
Then the doctor laughed in his face.

“No, please, I want his honour to tell me. It’s the doctor’s job to tell people they are not going to die.”

“But, my good Voss, how can you imagine you are going to die? You have some flesh wounds and some nasty bumps, but a man like you doesn’t die of those things!”

“Indeed, your honour, I have done my duty!” said the injured man. His voice began to falter; then the tension relaxed and he began to weep quietly and unrestrainedly. “I know… no more.… I have… done… my duty!” he stammered.

===Chinese===
医生当着他的面笑了。

“不，求您了，我要阁下亲口告诉我。叫人不死，本来就是医生的差事。”

“得了，我的好沃斯（Voss），你怎么会想到死上去？不过几处皮肉伤、几个难看的青肿块，像你这样的汉子，哪能就这么死了！”

“真的，阁下，我尽了职！”伤员说。他的声音开始发颤；随即那股绷紧的劲松了下来，他毫无顾忌地低声哭起来。“我知道……再没什么了。……我……尽了……职！”他讷讷地说。

===Original===
“You don’t need to tell me that,” said Wenk reassuringly. “He who stakes his life upon it certainly does his duty, for no one can offer anything he values more! But now, Voss, I want you to promise me something, and shake hands upon it. You won’t tell anyone else what you have seen or gone through this night… and I beg the same thing of you, doctor. A great deal depends upon it, for the public at large. I beg you to lay this very much to heart. It is not the pursuit of one crime, but of a generation of crime.”

From the constable who had been first on the spot Wenk learnt that he had seen several figures near the wall of the park, but darkness prevented his counting their number, nor could he describe them. He was stopped by one of the gentlemen, who tried to stand up and then clutched hold of him, saying two or three times over, “Arrest the woman—arrest the woman.”

“Then at last he fell back and let me go,” went on the man. “Then I could run a few steps and I saw those figures close to the wall going round the park, but when I reached it, there was no one there. They must have had accomplices on the other side of the wall. I wanted to go after them, but I couldn’t manage it; it was far too high to climb, so I came back to the spot.”

===Chinese===
“这个你不必对我说，”文克宽慰道，“肯拿性命作注的人，自然是尽了职，因为再没有人能拿出他更看重的东西了！不过，沃斯，现在我要你答应我一件事，咱们握手为定。今夜你的所见所历，不得再告诉任何人……医生，我也同样请求您。此事干系重大，为公众大局起见。我恳请二位牢牢记在心里：我们追查的不是一桩罪行，而是整整一代罪行。”

从最先赶到现场的那名警员口中，文克得知他看见几条人影在园林围墙近旁，但夜黑数不清人数，也描摹不出模样。其中一位先生拦住了他，挣扎着要站起来，随即一把抓住他，翻来覆去地说了两三遍：“逮捕那女人——逮捕那女人。”

“后来他终于倒了下去，松开了我，”那人接着说，“我这才跑得动几步，看见那几条人影贴着墙、绕着园子走，可等我赶到墙边，那里一个人也没有。墙那边必有他们的接应。我想追过去，可是办不到；墙太高，爬不过去，我只得回到原地。”

===Original===
“And the woman?” asked Wenk. “What about her?”

“I had the impression.…”

“Now, Stamm, I don’t want to hear your *impressions*—I only want to know what you saw with your eyes and heard with your ears. You will be scrupulously exact, won’t you?”

“Yes, indeed, your honour. When I came back, one of our men was holding the woman fast. I said to him, ‘Arrest her; the gentleman there said so. Arrest her at all costs! Hold her fast, don’t let her escape!’ We were all a bit excited, and she shouted out that she wanted to see Herr von Wenk, and no one was going to arrest *her*. She made a good deal of resistance, sir, and finally we had to tie her hands. There were only two of us, and we had to help the wounded and our own colleague. We did not know in the least what had happened, for we had only just.…”

===Chinese===
“那女人呢？”文克问，“她怎么回事？”

“我的印象是……”

“好了，施塔姆（Stamm），我不要听你的‘印象’——我只要知道你亲眼所见、亲耳所闻。你要做到分毫不爽，明白吗？”

“是，是，阁下。我回来的时候，我们的一个弟兄正把那女人死死抓住。我对他说：‘抓起她来；那边那位先生吩咐的。无论如何把她抓起来！抓紧了，别让她跑了！’我们都有点激动，她嚷着要见冯·文克先生，说谁也别想抓‘她’这种人。她拼命反抗，先生，最后我们只得把她的手捆上。我们只有两个人，还得救助伤员和我们自己的同事。究竟出了什么事，我们全然不知，因为我们才刚刚……”

===Original===
“*We?* Tell me only what you yourself have seen.”

“Then I began to try and find out what had happened. There was a man lying on the ground bathed in blood. He seemed to be dead, for he was quite still. The other was groaning. Then a third constable came up, and we sent him to telephone for the ambulance and make a report to the Criminal Division and let your honour know. That was what Voss had told us to do first of all.”

“What was the woman doing all this time?”

“The second of our men took her to the guardroom.”

===Chinese===
“‘我们’？只说你亲眼见到的事。”

“于是我着手查问出了什么事。地上躺着一个人，浑身浸在血泊里。看样子死了，一动不动。另一个在呻吟。这时第三名警员赶到，我们派他去打电话叫救护车，向刑事调查部报案，并禀报阁下。这些都是沃斯最先吩咐我们办的。”

“这工夫那女人在做什么？”

“我们的第二个弟兄把她送去了警卫室。”

===Original===
“Don’t go on with your story, Stamm, till I have spoken to him. What is his name? Keep yourself in readiness to report again; do you hear? And remember, not a word of this outside the official circle—not even to your wife. Give me your word of honour!”

“Yes, indeed, sir. The other man’s name is Wasserschmidt.”

Wasserschmidt duly appeared.

===Chinese===
“先别往下讲，施塔姆，等我问过他再说。他叫什么名字？你随时待命再来报告，听见没有？还有，记住，出了公门半字不得外传——连你老婆也不行。以名誉向我担保！”

“是，是，先生。另一个人叫瓦瑟施密特（Wasserschmidt）。”

瓦瑟施密特随即被传到。

===Original===
“You arrested a woman tonight who was present when the two gentlemen were attacked,” said Wenk. “Why did you do that?”

“I did it because constable Stamm said that one of the gentlemen, before he became insensible, called out to him to do so, and my colleague Voss gave me the order too.”

At this moment the telephone rang in the bureau of the Criminal Investigation Department, where Wenk was conducting these inquiries.

“Who is speaking?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“今夜两位先生遇袭时，场中有一位女士，是你把她逮捕的，”文克说，“你为什么这样做？”

“我这样做，是因为警员施塔姆说，其中一位先生昏死之前，高声吩咐过他这样做，我的同事沃斯也给我下了这个命令。”

就在这时，文克主持讯问的刑事调查部办公室里，电话铃响了。

“哪位在说话？”他问。

===Original===
“This is the night editor’s office of the Central News Agency. We have just been informed of a murder.…”

“One moment, please,” said Wenk angrily. “Who gave you that information?”

“I can tell you that without betraying any editorial secrets, for it was given anonymously, so to speak. Our night-bell rang, and as I went to the window I saw a man going away. When I opened it and asked what was the matter, he called out, ‘Look in the letter-box!’ Then I went down and found a letter in the box.”

“Can you read me what was in the letter? The State agent for prosecutions is speaking!”

“Yes, certainly, sir, one moment. The letter runs: ‘Edgar Hull, Esquire, was attacked and murdered in the Jägerstrasse in the early hours of this morning. The criminals have escaped. It appears to have been an act of revenge, for the murdered man frequented gambling circles.’ That’s all there is.”

===Chinese===
“这里是中央通讯社（Central News Agency）的夜班编辑部。我们刚刚收到一桩凶杀案的报信……”

“请稍等，”文克怒道，“这消息是谁给你们的？”

“这个我可以奉告，不算泄露编辑部的秘密，因为消息来路，可以说是不具名的。我们的夜铃响了，我走到窗前，看见一个男人正走开。我推开窗问有什么事，他喊道：‘去看看信箱！’我便下楼，在信箱里找到一封信。”

“能把信的内容念给我听吗？我是负责刑事公诉的检察官！”

“好的，当然，先生，请稍候。信上写道：‘埃德加·赫尔先生于今日凌晨在耶格街遇袭身亡。凶手已逃逸。此案似为复仇之举，因死者素来混迹赌界。’就这么多。”

===Original===
“Does anybody in the newspaper staff know about this letter?”

“No.”

“Can you bring this letter to me yourself immediately? I will send a Service car for you.”

“But, sir, that would be a very difficult matter. I am alone here, and I must complete the Press matter.”

“What is your name?”

===Chinese===
“报社里还有什么人知道这封信？”

“没有。”

“你能立刻把信亲自送到我这里来吗？我派一辆勤务车去接你。”

“可是，先生，这可太难办了。这里只有我一个人，我还得把报纸的稿子赶完。”

“你叫什么名字？”

===Original===
“Grube.”

“Well, Herr Grube, there’s no difficulty in the matter, when I tell you very decidedly that your coming here is of the utmost importance, far and away more important than that tomorrow morning every Tom, Dick and Harry should be able to discuss such a piece of news while he eats his breakfast.”

“But my duty is…” he began, but Wenk interrupted him.

“Don’t take it ill that my time won’t permit of my saying any more now, save that the police car is on its way to bring you here. The constable is furnished with the necessary authority. Arrange your Press matter so that the sheet can be printed without the information you have just given me about a murder. Au revoir, Herr Grube. Ring off, please.”

Wenk sent off the car immediately.

===Chinese===
“格鲁贝（Grube）。”

“那么，格鲁贝先生，此事毫无难处——我斩钉截铁地告诉你：你来这里至关重要，比明儿早上随便哪个张三李四边吃早饭边议论这么一条消息，不知要重要多少倍。”

“可是我的职责是……”他刚开口，文克便打断了他。

“恕我不再多说了，我的时间不允许——只告诉你，警车已在接你来的路上。随车警员带有必要的授权。把你的稿子安排好，让报纸不登你刚才向我通报的这条凶杀消息，也照样开印。再会，格鲁贝先生。请挂断。”

文克随即把车派了出去。

===Original===
“Well, now, Wasserschmidt, to continue. The lady offered resistance. How did she do that?”

“She ran a few paces from me towards the wall of the Wittelsbach Palace, to which the criminals had hurried, and then called out, ‘George.’”

“You heard that yourself?”

“Yes, quite distinctly, and she pronounced the name ‘Georsh.’ And as she began to run towards the wall too, I did not wait any longer, but I tied her hands together.”

“And what did she do then?”

===Chinese===
“好，瓦瑟施密特，接着说。那位女士进行了反抗。她是怎么反抗的？”

“她从我身边朝维特尔斯巴赫宫的围墙跑出几步——凶手们正是奔那面墙去的——然后喊了一声：‘格奥尔格。’”

“这是你亲耳听到的？”

“是，听得清清楚楚，她把那名字念成了‘格奥尔什’（Georsh）。见她也要朝墙那边跑，我便不再等候，把她的双手捆到了一起。”

“那她后来怎么样？”

===Original===
“Then she became quieter, and let us take her away. As we were going, she said again, ‘I shall certainly be able to speak to Herr von Wenk, shall I not?’ ‘Well, you will have to wait till after he has had his breakfast,’ I said. ‘Perhaps I could telephone to him?’ but I said that wasn’t very likely.”

“And then later? Where is the lady now?”

“Still at the guardroom. She spoke quite calmly and said, ‘You have made a very serious mistake, my good man; but I hope to be able to set you right with Herr Wenk, for, after all, you are only doing what you conceive to be your duty. I was with the gentleman who has been attacked, and the State Attorney was there too, but he went home a little earlier, or else he would have been in it as well.’ ‘Let us wait and see!’ was all I said to that.”

“Did you happen to tell her *why* you had arrested her?”

===Chinese===
“后来她安静了些，听凭我们带她走。路上她又问：‘我一定能同冯·文克先生谈一谈，对吧？’‘那您得等他吃完早饭再说，’我说。‘兴许我能给他打个电话？’我说这可不太可能。”

“那再后来呢？那位女士现在在哪儿？”

“还在警卫室。她说话十分镇定：‘你们犯了一个非常严重的错误，我的好心的先生；不过我希望能在文克先生面前替你们转圜，毕竟你们不过是各尽各人以为的本分。遇袭的那位先生当时同我在一起，检察官也在场，只是他早走了一步，不然他也难逃此劫。’我只回了一句：‘那就走着瞧吧！’”

“你可曾告诉她，你为什么逮捕她？”

===Original===
“No, not a word.”

“That’s right. Wait in the next room.”

Wenk interviewed others, and finally the assistant-editor arrived. He protested loudly against this high-handed action of the authorities, and said that his newspaper.…

“If it is the duty of your newspaper to serve its readers up the latest scandal, whether it be a murder or the unlucky ending of a love-affair, merely because it is a scandal, in as hasty and disconnected a fashion as it was reported to you… you would be right to protest. But you have no right to hinder the authorities whose duty it is to deal with infinitely more important matters so that you may satisfy fools with a thirst for gossip.”

===Chinese===
“没有，半个字也没说。”

“这就对了。到隔壁房间等着。”

文克又讯问了旁人。最后，那位助理编辑到了。他高声抗议当局这种专横的做法，说他的报纸……

“倘若贵报的职责，就是给读者端上最新的丑闻——不管是凶杀还是情场失意的下场——只因为它是丑闻，而且像人家报给你时那样仓促凌乱、不加核实照登不误……那你抗议得有理。可你无权因此妨碍当局——当局的职分是处理无比重大得多的事务——只为你好喂饱那些渴求八卦的蠢货。”

===Original===
“But,” stammered the editor, in an excited tone, “but you are trying to stifle the Press. We are not living under the old system, you know. The Republic will.…”

“I have no time to bother about what the Republic will do. Be so good as to give me the letter you telephoned me about!”

“I am sorry,” said the editor, with a confident and self-satisfied air. “These are Press secrets.”

“Pardon my saying so, editor, but you really are very foolish. I respect any Press secrets which protect the interests of the community, but your refusal to give me this letter only injures them. Before I take it from you by force (an action which would lay you open to a penalty for resisting the law), I will tell you that this letter is the only piece of evidence we have at present of an unusually serious crime. Perhaps then you will become more reasonable, and not entrench yourself behind the plea of your professional duty, which, as I have already stated, I do recognize, though I place it far below the interests which I represent.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”编辑激动地讷讷道，“可是你们这是想扼杀新闻界。要知道，我们可不是生活在旧制度底下了。共和国将会……”

“共和国将会如何，我没工夫操心。劳驾，把你在电话里说的那封信交给我！”

“很抱歉，”编辑说，一副自信而得意的神情，“这些是新闻秘密。”

“恕我直言，编辑先生，你实在愚蠢得很。凡保护公众利益的新闻秘密，我都尊重；可你拒绝交出这封信，恰恰损害了公众利益。在我动武夺信之前（那样一来，你便犯下抗拒执法之罪，要受罚的），我不妨告诉你：这封信是我们眼下掌握的唯一一件罪证，牵着一桩异乎寻常的重案。也许到那时你会清醒些，不再拿职业职责当挡箭牌——这个职责，我方才已声明过，我承认它，只是我把它排在我所代表的利益之下，远在其下。”

===Original===
Grube felt uncertain how to act. Finally he brought out the document, saying, “I deliver it under protest, and.…”

“Did you see anything of the man who brought it? Could you recognize him?”

“There was very little light on the street from my window. I could only see that he was well dressed, and he certainly wore an opera hat. A little while after he had disappeared from sight, I heard a car drive off in the direction he took on leaving our office, and I imagine it was his.”

“Herr Grube, you will be so kind as to leave this letter in my hands. You will be an important witness in one of the most notable criminal prosecutions of recent years. I beg you, upon your honour, to preserve absolute silence about this letter and everything connected with it.”

===Chinese===
格鲁贝一时不知如何是好。终于，他掏出那封信件，说：“我是在抗议之下交付的，而且……”

“送信的那个人，你看见什么了？你能认出他吗？”

“从我窗口望下去，街上灯光很暗。我只看得出他衣着讲究，头上肯定戴着一顶歌剧帽。他走后不大工夫，我听见一辆汽车朝着他离开报馆的方向开走了，我想那车就是他的。”

“格鲁贝先生，劳您大驾，就把这封信留在我手里吧。在近年最引人瞩目的刑事公诉之一里，你将是一位重要证人。我以名誉恳求你：对这封信及与之相关的一切，绝对保持缄默。”

===Original===
Grube, under the spell of the horror which had seized upon him, now became more pliable, and grew as eager about the affair as he had previously been obdurate. He handed over the document, exclaiming, “There it is then! I am quite at your service. That is a very different matter!”

“My car will take you back to your office again. Please leave word that I am anxious to see the editor-in-chief as soon as he is able to attend upon me.”

The assistant-editor withdrew.

===Chinese===
格鲁贝被攫住他的那阵骇然慑服，此刻变乖顺了，先前多么顽固，如今便多么起劲。他交出信件，喊道：“喏，拿去吧！我一切听凭您差遣。这可就是另一回事了！”

“我的车送你回报馆。请留句话：我急于会见贵报总编辑，请他一得便就到我这里来。”

助理编辑退了出去。

## VIII / 八

===Original===

Wenk remained alone, inwardly cool. He had been able to suppress the horror and dread which the crime had excited in his sensitive and sympathetic soul. He knew the reason underlying this murder. It was not revenge, but something far more dangerous and deadly. It was terrorization! That was revealed to him by the letter to the news agency, designed to give information about the murder other than the police reports. It was the terrorizing of all who felt themselves victims of that fair-bearded stranger who had appeared among them. How much this gambler must have at stake, he thought, that he could thus personally announce his crime, to give the affair the turn he wanted it to have! How many people were in his pay that he was able to carry out his criminal deeds in this fashion? What sort of people were they, and what was the example such conduct would afford to those who were still hovering undecidedly between good and evil? How many adherents might not the announcement of this deed yet secure for him?

Hull had met his fate because he had revealed to the authorities, in the person of Wenk, the history of the I.O.U., and because the pseudo Herr Balling desired thus to give an example of what would occur to those who stood in his way. Possibly, even probably, the attack had also been directed at himself, and he had only escaped because his indignation had driven him from the place.

===Chinese===

文克独自留在原处，内心冷静。他已经把这场罪案在他那敏感而富于同情的心里激起的惊怖与畏惧压了下去。他清楚这桩谋杀背后的缘由。那不是复仇，而是某种远为危险、远为致命的东西。那是恐吓！寄往通讯社的那封信向他揭示了这一点——那封信的用意，就是在警方通报之外另行散布凶案的消息。这是在恐吓所有自觉受过那个沙色胡须的陌生人坑害的人——此人曾在他们中间现身。他想，这个赌徒得押上多大的赌注，才敢这样亲自宣布自己的罪行，好让这桩案子朝着他希望的方向发展！他豢养着多少人，竟能以这种方式施行他的犯罪勾当？那都是些什么样的人？这样的行径，又会给那些还在善恶之间踌躇观望的人树立怎样的榜样？这桩罪行一经张扬，还会替他招来多少党羽？

赫尔之所以丧命，是因为他向当局——具体说，就是向文克其人——吐露了那张借据的来龙去脉，也因为那位假巴林先生有意借此立个榜样，让世人看看挡他路的人会是何等下场。那场袭击说不定也是冲着文克自己来的——甚至多半就是。他之所以逃过一劫，只因当时怒火中烧，先一步离开了那个地方。

===Original===

Now perhaps it would be impossible, for strategical reasons, to close down the Go-Ahead Institute.﻿ ﻿… Like so many similar places, it might serve as a trap.

“And what about Cara Carozza?” he said to himself. “Shall I be able to get her to confess for whom she was acting as a decoy? What can she confess, and whose name would she reveal? Even if a name and possibly an address be furnished me, do I know the man’s secrets, and what precautions he has taken against me? No, I will not go to see this girl. I will leave her in custody and let her wait.﻿ ﻿… Then she will realize that there’s trouble ahead of her. She is weak and vicious; perhaps she will give in of her own accord.”

Finally, however, Wenk decided otherwise. He would take the exactly opposite course. He would lull her suspicions by a friendly and sympathetic bearing. She was crafty, but she belonged to the theatrical world, and by his assumed friendliness and sympathy with her in the circumstances leading to her arrest he might make her more ready to confide in him. He therefore went at once to the guardroom, where he found her seated in a small compartment. Wenk hastened towards her.

===Chinese===

眼下出于策略上的考虑，恐怕还无法查封前进馆（Go-Ahead Institute）。……它同许多类似的地方一样，兴许可以充当一个陷阱。

"那么卡拉·卡罗扎呢？"他自忖道，"我能让她招供，说出她是在替谁当诱饵吗？她能招出什么，又会供出谁的名字？就算给了我一个名字，兴许还有一个地址，我可摸得清那人的秘密？摸得清他对我作了哪些防备？不，我不去见这个姑娘。我就把她关着，让她等。……到时候她自会明白，大祸临头了。她意志软弱，心肠歹毒；说不定她自己就会松口。"

然而最终，文克还是作出了相反的决定。他要走一条截然相反的路：以友善而同情的态度麻痹她的疑心。她狡诈，可她到底是戏剧界里混出来的人，只要装出友好体贴的样子，对招致她被捕的那些情由表示体恤，或许就能让她更乐于向他吐露内情。于是他当即去了警卫室，见她坐在一间小小的隔间里。文克快步朝她迎了上去。

===Original===

“But, my dear young lady,” he exclaimed, “how came you here? What have they been doing to you? They have just rung up to tell me what has happened. What a good thing you thought of me!”

“Oh, Herr Wenk, you come as an angel of light to me in my dungeon. Let us get away from this place at once! Don’t lose an instant! I am stifling here. I can’t breathe in these horrible surroundings.” She hastened towards the door.

“Ah, but now I must prepare you for a disappointment, which is unavoidable. You see, my dear young lady, we live under the State, and every State has supreme power. It appoints officials, each of whom carries on his own peculiar office, and they cannot encroach upon the domains of others. The State has appointed me one of its Attorneys, but I am only there to prosecute offenders, not to set innocent people free.”

“Then what’s to happen to me?” said Cara, suddenly hardening her attitude.

Her tone warned Wenk, and he came at once to the point:

===Chinese===

"哎呀，亲爱的小姐，"他扬声道，"您怎么会到这儿来的？他们把您怎么了？他们方才打电话，把事情一五一十告诉了我。多亏您想起了我！"

"哦，文克先生，您就像光明的天使，降临到我的牢房里。咱们马上离开这个地方吧！一分钟也别耽搁！我在这里快要憋死了。在这种可怕的环境里，我喘不上气来。"她急匆匆朝门口走去。

"唉，可现在我得让您对一场失望有个准备，这是免不了的。您瞧，亲爱的小姐，我们都生活在国家之下，而每一个国家都握有至高权力。它任命官员，各人执掌各人的职分，谁也不能越权插手旁人的地界。国家委任我做它的一名检察官，可我的职分只在起诉罪犯，不在开释无辜。"

"那我会有什么下场？"卡拉说道，态度忽然强硬起来。

她的口气给文克提了醒，他单刀直入：

===Original===

“Your case does not come under my jurisdiction first of all, but that of the court of inquiry, and you are bound to undergo an examination there. It is troublesome, no doubt, but you must blame the circumstances for that.”

“And what about your part in it?” asked the girl.

“Mine? I can do nothing but tell the examining counsel that we are old acquaintances, and that I do not think you capable of taking any part in such a crime.”

“Then why did you come here? You are not the examining counsel.”

Wenk realized then that she had seen through his ruse, and he knew, too, that she had escaped the snare, but at the same time he was convinced that she was guilty.

===Chinese===

"您的案子首先不归我管，而归预审法庭管，您免不了要在那里受一场讯问。麻烦是麻烦，可这您只能怪时势。"

"那您在这案子里担当什么角色？"姑娘问道。

"我？我顶多能告诉预审法官，我们是老相识，而且我不认为您会与这样的罪行有份。"

"那您来这儿做什么？您又不是预审法官。"

文克这才明白，她已看穿了他的把戏；他也知道，她逃脱了圈套，但与此同时，他更确信她有罪。

===Original===

“I came here on account of a minor circumstance in which I can help you,” he said quickly. “I understand that you resisted the constables?”

“What woman would allow herself to be attacked by coarse brutes of constables without resisting?”

“Yes, of course; it was the circumstances which were to blame for your behaving unreflectingly and forcing them to do their duty.”

“I am well known as an artiste. My name ought to have been enough for them!”

“Did you give the constables your name?”

===Chinese===

"我来是为了一桩小事，这件事上我能帮您，"他赶忙说道，"听说您抗拒了警员？"

"哪个女人肯任凭警员那班粗坯动手动脚而不反抗？"

"是，当然；都怪当时的情况，害得您不假思索就动了手，逼得他们只好履行职责。"

"我是个鼎鼎大名的艺人。光凭我的名字，就该让他们知趣！"

"您把名字报给警员了吗？"

===Original===

“Certainly I did, straight away!”

“It is strange that they should not have told me that. They mentioned another name that you had called out!”

Then Wenk observed that Cara threw a hasty and searching glance, full of hate, upon him. She looked away again at once, and drummed with her fingers on her knee.

“They said another name, did they? That’s curious, for my own name is well enough known, and thought enough of. What might this other strange name have been?”

“The constable said it was George.”

Her face showed no change when Wenk said that.

===Chinese===

"当然报了，马上就报了！"

"怪了，他们竟没有向我禀报这一节。他们提到的，是您喊出的另一个名字！"

这时文克注意到，卡拉朝他飞快地投来一道锐利的、充满憎恨的目光。她随即又望向别处，手指在膝盖上嗒嗒地敲着。

"他们说了另一个名字，是吗？真奇怪，我自己的名字够响亮的了，也够受人看重的了。那另一个古怪的名字会是什么呢？"

"警员说，是格奥尔格。"

文克说出这个名字时，她脸上没有一丝变化。

===Original===

“He couldn’t have heard properly, for my name, as you know, isn’t George,” she said, with an air of indifference.

“But a second constable says he heard you give the same name. It really *was* George!”

“How strange!” said Cara, after a pause for reflection. “My husband’s name was George. Could I, in my excitement, have called.﻿ ﻿…”

“Ah, now everything is perfectly clear. That is quite comprehensible, but, of course, nobody knew you had been married?”

“I *am* married!”

“You still are; oh, that’s something different. Shall I send word to your husband? But perhaps you no longer hold any intercourse with him?”

===Chinese===

"他准是听岔了，我的名字，您是知道的，不叫格奥尔格。"她说这话时，一副漠然的神气。

"可第二个警员说，他听见您报的也是这个名字。那*确实*是格奥尔格！"

"多奇怪呀！"卡拉沉吟片刻后说道，"我丈夫的名字就叫格奥尔格。莫非我一激动，喊出的是……"

"啊，这下一切都清楚了。这完全可以理解，不过，自然啦，没有人知道您结过婚吧？"

"我*是*结着婚的！"

"您如今还是；哦，那就是另一回事了。要我给您丈夫捎个信吗？不过，您也许早就不与他往来了？"

===Original===

“Indeed I do! His address is 234, Eschenheimerstrasse, Frankfurt-am-Main.﻿ ﻿… His name is George Strümpfli.”

“This will be painful news for him. Are you not afraid that there may be some difficulty when he hears your name connected with the circumstance of Hull’s murder?”

Then Cara spoke at last, falling back on her chair. “Hull murdered!﻿ ﻿…” she exclaimed, and she sank fainting from the chair to the ground.

For the moment Wenk was taken aback; then he decided that this fainting-fit was assumed. He raised her on to the couch, then went away without attending to her further. Going out, he ordered the constables to keep a sharp eye on the lady, and not let anyone at all go into the anteroom. They were to keep their weapons fixed.

He drove back to the central police-station and informed the divisional surgeon, requesting him to drive to the guardroom, and to search the girl’s clothing without exciting suspicion. He then wrote out the order for her arrest, and handed it over. He gave orders at the Police Information Bureau that any journalist who came seeking for news was to be sent to him direct.

===Chinese===

"往来着呢！他的地址是美因河畔法兰克福埃申海默街234号。……他的名字是格奥尔格·施特吕姆普夫利。"

"这对他来说会是痛苦的消息。听到您的名字同赫尔凶案的案情牵扯到一起，您不怕出什么乱子吗？"

这时卡拉终于开了口，身子朝椅背上一仰。"赫尔被谋杀了！……"她惊呼一声，随即从椅子上晕倒在地。

文克一时有些错愕；随即他断定这阵晕厥是装出来的。他把她抱上长榻，便不再照管她，径自走了出去。出门时，他吩咐警员们严密看住这位女士，任何人都不得进入前室，并要他们枪不离手、时刻戒备。

他驱车回到警察总局，通知了警区医生，请他驱车前往警卫室，设法不动声色地搜查姑娘的衣物。随后他开出了对她的逮捕令，交了下去。他又在警察新闻处下了命令：凡有记者前来打探消息，一律直接送到他那里去。

===Original===

By this time it was daylight. Wenk had a bath and then drove to the office of the Central News Agency, the editor-in-chief of which had rung him up on the telephone.

When Wenk had told him all that had occurred, he said: “The reason that emboldened me to lay claim to some of your time, was this. If it were an isolated murder I would, although unwillingly, let the reporting of it proceed in the usual manner. But behind this assault we are confronted by a gang having at their head a man of apparently enormous and comprehensive powers. He must have secured to himself an organized set of followers whose only aim is to guard him while he carries out his crimes. The letter, which he himself may have handed into your office, discloses the fact that he desires the affair to be made known in the way that suits his ends. He means it as a warning. The victim himself told me not long ago that he had come across him in very peculiar circumstances, and this he knew. It is his aim to surround his dark deeds by a wall of dread; folks are to realize that no one who makes any attempt against *him* can escape with his life. You can readily see how great a danger such a man is; at a time when the war has left folks weak and emotional on the one hand and more readily incited to evil on the other. We cannot altogether suppress such an occurrence as this, but I desire that it should be announced apart from the connecting circumstances known to me, so that the imagination may not make popular heroes out of murderers. In this I am counting on the assistance of yourself and your colleagues. May I beg you most earnestly not to make known *anything* concerning the Hull affair which has not first been seen by me? We are living in an age of mental and spiritual epidemics, and those who would help to bring healing must be prepared to sacrifice themselves.”

===Chinese===

这时天已经亮了。文克洗了个澡，随后驱车前往中央通讯社——总编辑刚才打电话找过他。

文克把事情原原本本讲给他听之后，说道："我之所以冒昧占用您一些时间，缘由就在这里。倘若这只是一桩孤立的凶案，那么尽管不情愿，我也会听任报道照常规进行。可在这起袭击背后，我们面对的是一个匪帮，为首的是个神通显然大得出奇、无所不包的人物。他必定已为自己网罗起一支有组织的党羽，这些人唯一的目标，就是在他为非作歹时为他护航。那封信——很可能就是他亲自递交到贵社的——表明了他的一桩心思：他要此事按合乎他目的的方式公之于众。他是拿它当警告用的。遇害者本人不久前对我说过，他曾在非常特殊的情形下与此人打过照面，这一点他心中有数。此人的图谋，就是给自己的黑暗勾当筑起一道恐惧之墙；要让世人明白，谁想对*他*下手，谁就休想保住性命。您不难看出这样一个人有多大的危险；更何况是在这样一个时代：战争一方面让人们变得虚弱而感伤，另一方面又让他们更容易被煽向作恶。这样一桩案子，我们无法完全压下，但我希望它与我已掌握的那些关联情节分开宣布，免得民众的想象力把杀人凶手捧成轰动一时的英雄。在这一点上，我要仰仗您和您的同僚鼎力相助。我可否恳切地请求您：凡有关赫尔一案的情况，未经我先过目，一律不得披露*任何*内容？我们生活在一个心灵与精神瘟疫流行的时代，凡愿带来疗救的人，都必须准备牺牲自己。"

===Original===

“I will certainly act as you desire,” said the editor-in-chief.

“At the same time,” Wenk went on, “I wouldn’t on any account allow the impression to get about that such a course is due to more complete knowledge of the circumstances, or the exercise of authority on the part of the law, you understand.”

“I quite follow you there,” said the sympathetic editor.

“Then I am grateful to you, and can only hope for good results from our combined efforts. Our nation is in evil case.”

===Chinese===

"我一定照您的意思办。"总编辑说。

"与此同时，"文克接着说，"我决不能让外界生出这样的印象，仿佛如此行事是因为对案情掌握得更全面，或是法律方面在行使职权——您明白我的意思。"

"这层我完全明白。"这位善解人意的编辑说。

"那我感激不尽，只能指望我们同心协力会有好结果。我们的民族病得不轻。"

===Original===

When he got home Wenk was anxious to go to bed and enjoy a few hours of much-needed rest. It was already ten o’clock, but just then his chauffeur, who acted as his personal attendant, brought him a visiting-card bearing the name of Countess Told.

“I am quite disengaged,” said Wenk immediately, and the Countess was ushered in.

“Is there any possibility of our being interrupted here by an anxious wife who is not au courant of the matter which is engaging our attention?” she asked, as she gave Wenk her slender hand cordially.

“The happiness of possessing a partner for life has never been mine!” answered Wenk, feeling a delicious sweetness in the proximity of this woman. And yet she stood before him as something dreamlike, connected with a life which he seemed to have led not long before. Between this hour and that lay the mysterious occurrences of the night, and he was unable to conceive that these feelings of love and longing could be actually real.

===Chinese===

回到家中，文克一心想上床去，享受几个钟头急需的休息。这时已经是十点钟了，可恰在此刻，他那兼任贴身听差的司机给他送来一张名片，上面印着托尔德伯爵夫人的名字。

"我完全有空。"文克应声说道，伯爵夫人随即被引了进来。

"在这儿，咱们会不会受到打搅——比如闯进来一位焦急的太太，对咱们正关注的事一无所知？"她一边亲热地把纤细的手伸给文克，一边问道。

"拥有终身伴侣的幸福，从来不曾归我所有！"文克答道，只觉这女人近在身旁，有一种甜滋滋的滋味。然而她站在他面前，又像一场梦，连着一段他仿佛不久前才刚刚经历过的人生。这一刻与那一刻之间，横亘着昨夜种种神秘的变故，他简直无法相信，这些爱与渴慕之情竟会是真实的。

===Original===

She stood before him, and he found no word to say to her, while she herself, insensibly influenced by the man’s force of character and lofty aims, felt embarrassed by this silence, because it seemed to be a confirmation of her own sensations. “Yes,” she confessed to herself, “the feeling I have for him is﻿ ﻿… ,” but she would not utter the word “love.” She blushed at the thought, a blush which Wenk saw. A tremor passed through him, and he struggled with himself as he bent low over her hand.

Then suddenly the vision of the murdered man rose before him, and he no longer felt bold enough to betray by word or gesture the infatuation which possessed him. He offered the Countess a chair, and while he fetched another for himself his imagination was fired by an idea which afforded a solution of the conflict waging within him. This woman, whom he loved and to whom he was evidently not wholly indifferent, should be associated with him in his undertaking, and their common endeavour might bring about their own harvest. Then he said to her seriously:

“During this last night an acquaintance known to both of us, Edgar von Hull, has been murdered. His friend Karstens is severely wounded, and I only escaped because I had happened to leave, two hours earlier, the locality into which we had been enticed. I believe I know the instigator of this crime. It is once more the sandy-bearded man and the old Professor. Its actual perpetrators have escaped, but we have made one arrest, of a person who is also known to you. I mean Cara Carozza, the dancer, whose liaison with Hull you are aware of. At present I have hardly more than a profound conviction that she has had some share in the crime, but I have thought of a way by which we might loosen her tongue. If you, Countess, would undertake the unpleasant enterprise of allowing yourself to be arrested, I would take care to arrange for your being put into the same cell as Carozza. She does not know you as Countess Told, but as a lady who frequents her own circles. Represent your offence as a very trifling one, and say that you will soon be set free, even if you are found guilty of taking part in an illicit game.﻿ ﻿… Promise to help her, perhaps by flight﻿ ﻿… and you must previously have informed her that her situation is a very serious one, and one never can tell what may happen to persons arrested in such circumstances as hers.﻿ ﻿… She will then probably tell you who would be able to arrange for her escape, and you understand the rest, Countess. Are you willing to play the part?”

===Chinese===

她站在他面前，他找不出一句话对她说；而她自己，受这个男人的性格力量与崇高志向潜移默化的影响，也为这份沉默感到窘迫，因为它似乎印证了她自己的心绪。"是的，"她暗自承认，"我对他的感情是……"可她不肯说出"爱"这个字。想到这里，她脸红了，这红晕被文克看在眼里。一阵战栗传遍他的全身，他俯身深深吻她的手，一边与自己苦苦争斗。

这时，遇害者的身影蓦地浮现在他眼前，他再没有胆量用言语或姿态，泄露攫住了自己的那份痴迷。他请伯爵夫人落座，趁去给自己搬另一把椅子的当口，一个念头点燃了他的想象，为他内心正酣的冲突指出了一条出路。这个他爱着的、而她对他显然也并非全然无动的女人，应当参与到他的事业中来，两人共同的奋斗，或许能结出属于他们自己的果实。于是他正色对她说：

"就在昨夜，咱们俩共同认识的一位熟人——埃德加·冯·赫尔——被谋杀了。他的朋友卡斯滕斯身负重伤，而我之所以幸免，只因碰巧提前两个小时，离开了那处引咱们上钩的地方。我相信我知道这桩罪案的幕后主使。又是那个沙色胡须的男子，还有那个老教授。动手行凶的已经逃脱，但我们抓到了一个人，这人您也认识。我指的是舞女卡拉·卡罗扎，她与赫尔的私情您是知道的。眼下我还只有一种深深的确信，认定她在这件案子里有份，但我想到了一个法子，兴许能撬开她的嘴。伯爵夫人，倘若您肯担下这桩不大愉快的差事——让自己被捕，我会设法安排您同卡罗扎关进同一间牢房。她不知道您是托尔德伯爵夫人，只当您是一位常出入她那个圈子的太太。您把自己的罪名说得非常轻微，就说即便被判参与了非法赌博，也很快就会获释。……答应帮她一把，也许是帮她逃走……而您得先让她明白，她的处境十分凶险，落在这种境地的人会有什么下场，谁也说不准。……这样她多半就会告诉您，谁有本事安排她逃走，剩下的您就都明白了，伯爵夫人。您愿意扮演这个角色吗？"

===Original===

“I will carry out your wishes,” said the Countess, without stopping an instant for reflection, and her voice sounded eager.

Wenk was sensibly touched by the haste, the ready zeal with which this gracious and beautiful woman accepted his suggestion.

“Up to now,” she said lightly, “there has never been a chance for me to do anything really useful, to engage in a bold enterprise with life at stake, to study life at first hand.”

“And that is what you have been seeking in the gambling-dens?” he asked.

“I do not rightly know. I felt at home in those places, because there seemed to be no barriers. In my own circle I could perceive the horizon everywhere, and I could not endure that. I feel I owe you much.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

"我愿照您的意思办。"伯爵夫人说，片刻的迟疑都没有，嗓音里透着热切。

这位娴雅美丽的女人如此爽快、如此热心地接受了他的提议，文克着实动容。

"直到如今，"她轻快地说，"我还从来没有机会做点真正有用的事，投身一桩性命攸关的大胆事业，亲身体察人生。"

"这就是您在赌窟里寻觅的东西？"他问道。

"我自己也说不清。在那些地方我觉得自在，因为那里仿佛没有藩篱。在我自己的圈子里，我在在处处都望得见地平线，这我受不了。我觉得我欠了您很多。……"

===Original===

There was a smarting in Wenk’s eyes. He was overcome with a sensation of longing; it took possession of him and tormented him, and he asked, almost roughly, “And your husband?”

She answered calmly, “In every marriage, although you cannot know it by experience, there is something of what the heart has sought left unfulfilled. I rob my husband of nothing, if I try to find what I am seeking without him.”

“I honour and esteem you,” cried Wenk, his voice trembling slightly.

“It is nothing but the natural law,” she countered; “and now tell me what I am to do.”

“On a certain day, which you shall appoint, I will take you in my car to the governor of the prison and we will arrange everything with him. When would it suit you?”

===Chinese===

文克的眼里一阵刺痛。他被一种渴慕之情攫住了；它占据了他，折磨着他，于是他近乎粗暴地问："那您的丈夫呢？"

她平静地答道："每一桩婚姻里——这话您虽无从亲身体会——总有心灵所求而未得的那一部分。倘若我尝试撇开他，去寻找我所寻求的东西，我并没有从我丈夫那里抢走什么。"

"我敬重您，看重您。"文克喊道，声音微微发颤。

"这不过是自然法则罢了。"她应道，"现在告诉我，我该怎么做。"

"日子由您来定。到了那天，我用汽车送您去见监狱长，咱们同他把一切都安排妥当。什么时候对您合适？"

===Original===

“Next Saturday at this time.” She rose.

“The grey prison walls will begin to shine!” said Wenk.

“Because of such odd proceedings,” laughed she.

“No, Countess, your beauty will light them up,” and Wenk suddenly felt as if he loved her with a passion which must be shining in his eyes. He bent so low over her hand in adieu that he concealed his face from her, and she yielded it to him in a gracious gesture that was almost like the confession of a mutual understanding between them, then hastened away.

Out in the street the blood mounted to her cheeks, and half unconsciously she murmured the word she had suppressed, “love﻿ ﻿… love,” while in Wenk’s room there remained a scent of her which he eagerly inhaled. Then pressing both hands to his face, and indulging his secret and mysterious presentiments, he whispered ardently into the darkness that concealed his vision, “Death and love﻿ ﻿… death and love!”

===Chinese===

"下星期六这个钟点。"她站起身来。

"灰色的监狱高墙就要熠熠生辉了！"文克说。

"托这项古怪差事的福。"她笑道。

"不，伯爵夫人，是您的美貌会把它们照亮。"文克忽然觉得，自己怀着一种炽烈的爱恋爱着她，这炽情一定正在他的眼中闪光。道别时，他把脸深深埋在她的手上，让她看不见自己的面孔；她优雅地任他捧着那只手，那姿态几乎等于默认了两人之间的一份默契，随即匆匆离去。

走到街上，热血涌上她的双颊，她半是不自觉地呢喃出那个一直被她按下的字眼："爱……爱。"而在文克的房间里，还留着她身上的芬芳，他贪婪地吸着。随后，他双手掩面，纵容着自己那隐秘而莫测的预感，朝着遮住他视线的黑暗热切地低语："死亡与爱……死亡与爱！"

---

===Original===

In the course of the day the report of the murder ran through the city. It arose from the dark quarter where Hull had yielded up his useless and trivial existence. A dark patch remained there, and the pavement was coloured with the blood that had been shed. The thaw had made the gutters moist and muddy, and they had sucked in the dark evidences of the crime, till from a mere patch it became a monster, reaching from its own narrow corner to spread throughout the town. Folks came to seek its source, drinking in on the spot the full horrors of the deed. They saw the monster rear its head, rush towards them and through them, leaving disorder, abuse and dread in its wake. Like a dragon it wound itself through the alleys to the broad Ludwigstrasse, crept through the squares to the very heart of the city, and began to overflow all quarters, to escape from the streets to the houses. Like an underground drain it ran all day long, its gloomy current and dismal stench striking terror into men’s hearts or drawing thence a force which could but find its outlet in evil.

Three days later a woman of the streets was murdered in the night, and the assassin was caught the very next day. He was an “out-of-work,” one of those relics of wartime, who had fallen into a state approaching savagery. He confessed that he did not know what he was doing when he pressed his fingers deep into the girl’s throat. Something seemed to seize upon him in the dark when he came round that corner by the Jägerstrasse, and drove him to do it.

The town was enveloped as in a misty fog, impressionable and passionate as the human heart, and the spring beyond it was obscured. The lights thrown on life became glaring, its shadows of a wild and overwhelming blackness. Men’s hearts were torn in two, and everywhere there was internal conflict.

===Chinese===

当天，凶案的传闻便传遍了全城。它发端于那个黑暗街区——赫尔正是在那里交出了他无用而无足轻重的性命。那里留着一摊黑渍，路面染着洒下的血。融雪把阴沟泡得又湿又泥泞，沟里吸饱了罪案的黑暗证据，于是那摊黑渍膨胀成一头怪物，从自己狭小的角落里伸展开来，蔓延全城。人们纷纷赶来寻它的源头，在现场把罪行的全部恐怖一饮而尽。他们眼见那怪物昂起头来，朝他们扑来、穿他们而过，身后留下混乱、辱骂与恐惧。它像一条巨龙，蜿蜒穿过小巷，游上宽阔的路德维希大街，爬过一个个广场，直抵城市的心脏，又开始漫过各个城区，从街道漫进千家万户。它像一条地下暗渠，整天流淌不息，那阴郁的水流与秽臭的气息把恐怖砸进人们心里，或者从心里引出一股力量——这力量只能在作恶中找到出口。

三天后，一个街头妓女在夜里遇害，凶手第二天就落了网。他是个"失业汉"，战争年月留下的残渣之一，已经堕入近乎野蛮的境地。他供认，把手指深深掐进那姑娘喉咙的时候，他不知道自己在干什么。他说，夜里拐过耶格街那个街角时，黑暗中仿佛有什么东西攫住了他，驱使他下了手。

整座城像裹在一团迷雾里，像人心一样易感而狂热，连城外的春色也为之黯淡。投在人生上的灯光变得刺眼，影子则黑得狂乱而逼人。人心被撕成两半，到处都是内在的交战。

## X / 十

===Original===
Next evening Dr. Mabuse was invited to spend the evening at the house of Privy Councillor Wendel, who was interested in hypnotism. After an early supper an interesting medium would appear. In her trances memories were awakened within her which referred to her very earliest days﻿ ﻿… to a time when the mind was not developed enough to be able to record or describe the physical existence of the moment.

Mabuse had made the Privy Councillor’s acquaintance through a patient of his, an aristocratic and wealthy dame, who had suffered from severe neurosis and whom Mabuse had very successfully treated by hypnotic suggestion. In the company were to be found not only professors, but also authors, artists and the reputed friends of art, such as frequent the society of the wealthy and fashionable nowadays.

Mabuse’s neighbour at the supper-table was a lady whom he recognized with astonishment and perplexity. In the gambling-dens she was known to his accomplices by the nickname of “the dummy.” The lady was Countess Told.

===Chinese===
第二天晚上，马布斯博士应邀到枢密顾问温德尔（Privy Councillor Wendel）府上消磨一个晚上，这位顾问对催眠术颇感兴趣。早早用过晚餐之后，会有一位有趣的灵媒到场。她在恍惚出神之中，体内会被唤醒一些记忆，那些记忆指向她生命最初的岁月……指向一个心智尚未发育到足以记录或描述当时肉体存在的年代。

马布斯与这位枢密顾问的相识，缘于他的一位病人——一位患严重神经症的贵族阔太太，马布斯用催眠暗示把她治得颇为成功。座中宾客不只有诸位教授，还有作家、艺术家以及那些所谓的艺术之友——如今这号人正是频频出入豪富时髦社交圈的常客。

晚餐桌上，马布斯身旁坐着的女士，让他认出来时不由得又惊又疑。在那些赌窝里，他的同伙都知道她的绰号——「木头人」（the dummy）。这位女士正是托尔德伯爵夫人。

===Original===
Throughout the meal he devoted himself to her, paying her every possible attention, and relating to her eager ears tales of strange and wonderful experiences in hazardous places, of the chase of wild animals or of human beings in parts of the world that are little frequented. He spoke with a grim earnestness, a savage unrestraint, enjoying once more in recollection the powers he had exercised in such circumstances. He realized what it was that drove this woman to the gambling-dens, and it seemed as if this sudden disclosure gave him a pang, as if there opened up within him a chasm and a gulf so deep that only a palpitating human heart could fill it. With his imagination and with his bold recital he was pursuing such a heart, as in the jungle he had pursued the tiger. The hope of conquest inflamed his blood; he felt he must make it his own.

It was this woman’s heart he wished to subjugate. He was consumed with passionate desire as he read in her eyes how his recital fired her blood. That was the kind of life she craved, and her nature understood and responded to it. He painted wild scenes for her; he showed himself struggling for conquest with body, soul and spirit pitted against unrestrained nature, and he desired her to believe that this wild and unrestrained nature was within her.

She trembled at his words, and, swayed by his ardour, a longing for support and tenderness overcame her. The recitals by which he sought to enchain her interest aroused so forceful an impression of human power that it seemed, in tearing herself away from them, she was actually tearing a fragment of living, bleeding flesh when she sought out her husband with an almost supplicating gesture, as if desirous of protection from a force too powerful to endure.

===Chinese===
席间他一心侍奉在她身边，对她殷勤备至，对她那双渴望的耳朵讲起种种奇异的经历：在凶险之地、在人迹罕至的世间角落，追猎野兽或追猎人的故事。他讲得冷峻而认真，狂放而无拘，在回忆中再度受用自己当年在那类境况里施展过的权力。他明白了是什么把这个女人驱向赌窝，而这层突然的洞悉仿佛叫他一阵刺痛，仿佛他体内豁然裂开一道深渊沟壑，深得只有一颗悸动的人心才填得满。他以想象、以大胆的讲述追逐着这样一颗心，一如当年在丛林中追逐猛虎。征服的希望灼热了他的血；他觉得非把它据为己有不可。

他想要降服的，正是这个女人的心。他从她的眼睛里读出，他的讲述怎样点燃了她的血液，顿时被炽烈的欲望吞噬。那正是她渴求的生活，她的天性懂得它、回应它。他为她描绘一幕幕狂野的图景；他展现自己以肉身、灵魂与精神，同不受羁勒的自然一决胜负、争夺征服，并要她相信，这狂野不羁的自然就存在于她自己的身上。

她在他话语下颤抖，被他的炽热所撼动，一股渴求倚靠与温柔的愿望压倒了她。他用以拴住她兴味的那些讲述，唤起的人力之感如此强悍，以致当她带着近乎哀求的姿态去寻找丈夫时，仿佛从那些讲述中挣脱出来，就是从活生生、血淋淋的肉身上撕下一块碎片，仿佛想从一股强大得难以承受的力量那里求得庇护。

===Original===
Mabuse saw her gesture, and the blood mounted to his forehead. He was flushed with passionate desire, and could no longer bear to see the glances of others rest upon her﻿ ﻿… other strangers address her﻿ ﻿… the lips of other men pressed to her hand﻿ ﻿… or the thought that any other will should impose itself on her. His was the call of blood that should reach her, and inflamed with passion and desire, he left the house and drove home.

All his thoughts were centred on her, however, and as he rapidly increased the distance between them, and as it were tore the bleeding flesh from his body, he called out to the image which filled the yearning gulf within him, “Death and desire! death and desire!”

At home he drank until all around him had dissolved in the mists of intoxication, and he no longer saw anything but her heart, her bleeding heart, snatched by his hand from her lovely body, held in his grasp, enticing and inflaming his passion.

===Chinese===
马布斯看见了她的手势，血直往额头上涌。他被炽烈的欲望烧得满面通红，再也无法忍受别人的目光落在她身上……别的陌生人同她攀谈……别的男人的嘴唇吻上她的手……更无法忍受任何别的意志强加于她的念头。理应抵达她的，是他的血的召唤。他满怀炽情与欲望，离开宅邸，驱车回家。

然而他所有的思绪都聚在她身上。他飞快地拉大着两人之间的距离，仿佛正把那块流血的肉从自己身上撕下来，一面朝着那个充塞着他体内渴念深渊的幻影呼喊：「死亡与欲望！死亡与欲望！」

回到家中，他一杯接一杯地喝，直喝得周遭一切都消融在醉意的迷雾里，眼前只剩她的心，她那颗流血的心，被他亲手从她可爱的身体里攫出，握在他的掌中，撩拨着他、煽燃着他的欲火。

---

===Original===
At length came the day when Countess Told should enter upon her prison experiences. She repaired to Wenk’s chambers, and he took her to the building, making the governor au courant of the whole story. Before being led to the cell, she asked, “How long am I to stay there?”

“As long as you like, Countess,” replied Wenk. “It all depends upon your skill, but of course you have but to say the word and you are free in an instant, even if you have not achieved your object.”

“I have plenty of time,” she answered, “but I should like to ask for leave next Monday, so that I can keep an appointment.”

“Most certainly, we can easily arrange that. With your permission, I will come and fetch you. Besides, you are sure to have something to report by then!”

“Finally, Dr. Wenk,” said the Countess, “I want you to know that my husband is in the secret, and you will go and see him, won’t you? Promise me!”

Wenk assented. A warder took possession of the Countess, and as she went with him she smiled back at Wenk. “Good luck!” cried he, ere she vanished along the corridor.

===Chinese===
托尔德伯爵夫人开始狱中体验的日子终于到了。她前往文克的办公处，由他带往那座大楼，并让监狱长对全部内情了然于胸。在被带往牢房之前，她问：「我要在那里待多久？」

「您愿意待多久就多久，伯爵夫人，」文克答道，「这全看您的本事。当然，只要您一句话，您立刻就能出去，哪怕还没达到目的。」

「我有的是时间，」她答道，「不过下周一我想请个假，好去赴一个约会。」

「完全可以，这好安排。如果您许可，到时候我来接您。再说，到那时您肯定有东西可汇报了吧！」

「最后还有一件事，文克博士，」伯爵夫人说，「我想让您知道，我丈夫已经知晓这个秘密，您会去见他的，对吗？答应我！」

文克应承下来。一名看守接管了伯爵夫人，她随他走去时，回头朝文克嫣然一笑。「祝您好运！」他在她消失于走廊尽头前喊道。

---

===Original===
The Countess had left the Privy Councillor’s house in a strange tumult of feeling. The stranger who had so impressed her had suddenly disappeared, but his forceful personality had left its mark, and she could not free herself of it. This mysterious and compelling power of his effaced the image of Wenk, and the latter receded into the background.

When the door of the cell opened before her, it seemed as if the time she had to spend in this narrow space, this strange, cold chamber, so far removed from the world, would be a period of probation, a time of testing for herself.

She was to see the stranger again on Monday. “I am asking your neighbour at the supper-table next Monday for another sitting with our medium,” the old Councillor had said to her with a mischievous smile. “He must make up for lost time, because he was called away unexpectedly. But if he did not see the medium asleep, at any rate he found Countess Told awake!”

“All right! I shall be pleased to meet him again,” she had answered in a friendly and noncommittal tone.

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人离开枢密顾问的宅邸时，心绪处于一种异样的翻腾之中。那位给她留下深刻印象的陌生人突然不见了，可他强悍的人格已经留下了印痕，她无法从中解脱。他那神秘而逼人的力量抹去了文克的身影，后者退到了背景之中。

牢房的门在她面前打开时，她觉得，她将在这狭窄的空间、这远离尘世的冰冷怪异的小屋里度过的时光，将成为一段磨砺期，一段检验自我的时日。

周一她将再见到那个陌生人。「下周一，我打算再请你晚餐桌旁的那位邻座，来同我们的灵媒办一次通灵会，」老枢密顾问曾带着狡黠的微笑对她说，「上回他临时有事被叫走，这回得把损失的光阴补回来。不过，他就算没见着睡着的灵媒，至少见到了醒着的托尔德伯爵夫人！」

「好啊！我很乐意再见他一面，」她当时用友好而不置可否的口吻答道。

===Original===
The door of the cell closed behind her, and she saw a figure seated on a stool, but it did not turn round. “Well?” it said growlingly.

“Good morning,” said the Countess.

The dancer turned round slowly. When she at length faced the Countess, the latter uttered a little cry, and with well-feigned astonishment hastened to Cara, exclaiming, “What, *you* here, my dear! But we know each other! What a strange coincidence!”

She began chattering at once, as if quite oblivious of Cara’s sullen mood. “Just imagine, they actually caught us all﻿—at Schramm’s﻿—the most noted resort of them all! I can tell you there was a fine to-do, my dear. One man sobbed, another tried to jump out of the window, and you know they are all shut up tight! Somebody sat down and wailed, ‘Oh my wife, my four children, I am disgraced forever!’ There was a tremendous fluttering in the dovecot. I could not slip away in time, and so they got me too! Tell me what is the best thing for me to do? There’s nothing wrong in entering a gaming-house, and I have never once played!”

===Chinese===
牢门在她身后关上，她看见一个身影坐在小凳上，却并没有回过头来。「怎么？」那身影粗声粗气地说。

「早上好。」伯爵夫人说。

那舞女缓缓转过身来。待她终于面对伯爵夫人时，后者发出一声轻呼，带着装得惟妙惟肖的惊讶，急忙迎向卡拉，喊道：「什么，*你*在这儿，亲爱的！我们可是认识的呀！多巧的缘分！」

她立刻滔滔不绝地说起来，仿佛全然没有察觉卡拉的阴沉脸色。「你想想看，他们竟然把我们都抓住了——就在施拉姆餐馆——那最有名的一家销金窟！我告诉你吧，亲爱的，那可真是一场好乱子。有人呜呜地哭，有人想从窗户往外跳，你知道的，他们全都被关得死死的！有个家伙一屁股坐到地上哀号：『哦，我的老婆，我的四个孩子，我这辈子算是完了！』整个鸽棚里顿时一片扑腾。我没来得及溜掉，也就被他们一并抓了来！你说说我该怎么办才好？进赌场又不犯法，我可是从来没有玩过一把！」

===Original===
But Cara only eyed her gloomily.

“Do say something. Is there anything the matter?” pleaded the Countess.

“The matter is that I want you to leave me alone,” answered the other. “Was the young gentleman with the fair sandy beard there?”

“The one who played against Basch, you mean? No, he wasn’t there. I have never seen him since that night.”

===Chinese===
可卡拉只阴郁地打量着她。

「倒是说句话呀。出什么事了吗？」伯爵夫人恳求道。

「事情就是，我要你别来烦我，」对方答道，「那个沙色胡须的年轻先生在场吗？」

「你是说跟巴施对阵的那位？不，他不在。那晚以后我再没见过他。」

===Original===
“Was the old Professor there?”

“No, I didn’t see him either.”

“Then you needn’t tell me any more about it; it doesn’t interest me. The whole world isn’t worth a pin. I am miserable, for I am forsaken and betrayed. There’s no interest left in life for me. I am lost and undone, and no one troubles any more about me than if I were a frozen field-mouse. What dirty dogs they are!”

Suddenly she sprang from her stool and seized the Countess by the shoulders. “You were with the rest of us. I want to drum it into your head,” she continued with increasing vehemence, “that there never was anybody so treacherously betrayed as I have been. And there was no reason for doing it, for I was an artiste, a well-known and admired artiste, and here I am now, forsaken and betrayed! Cast aside like a squeezed-out orange!”

===Chinese===
「老教授在场吗？」

「不，他也没见着。」

「那你就不用再往下讲了；我不感兴趣。整个世界连枚大头针都不值。我惨得很，因为我被人抛弃、被人出卖了。生活对我再也没有一点趣味。我完了，毁了，谁也不再为我操心，就像我不过是一只冻僵的田鼠。他们是多么肮脏的狗东西！」

她突然从小凳上跳起来，一把抓住伯爵夫人的肩膀。「你当时同我们大家在一起。我要把这话敲进你的脑袋，」她的口气越来越激烈，「从来没有谁像我这样被人背信弃义地出卖过。而且根本没有理由这样对我，因为我是个女艺人，一个赫赫有名、人人倾慕的女艺人，可如今落得这般下场，被抛弃、被出卖！像一只榨干了的橘子似的被扔在一边！」

===Original===
“Why did he forsake you?” asked the Countess shyly. In her own mind she seemed but a simple child in the presence of this wild and passionate personality. Yes, he had forsaken her, left her forever, she reflected, and she shuddered at the thought. And now he was dead. At the moment she felt doubtful of the enterprise she had undertaken. “He is dead,” she said in a low voice which vibrated.

“Who?” cried Cara.

“Your friend﻿ ﻿… Hull!” answered the Countess, preparing to enter sympathetically into the girl’s feelings, the image of Wenk growing yet fainter in her subconscious mind.

But the other exclaimed passionately, “What are you saying? The man I mean is not dead; he is alive, and yet I sit here in prison. Yonder in the town outside he stands, strong as a tower, firm as a rock, I tell you! How can a puny thing like you know what he was? All others were as dirt beneath his feet, and their faithlessness too small a trifle to consider! Hull is dead, but what does *that* matter? Who cares an atom about *him*? But that other, the master, the lord, he lives there in the free air, where there is light and love and life﻿ ﻿… where he might bear to have me lying at his feet, like a rug that only serves to warm his toes. He is the great man, the lord, the master! He is a bear, a lion, a royal Bengal tiger, do you hear? He does not belong to this cold and frosty land; he comes from Bengal, from paradise, from a place I shall never see again! And I﻿—I﻿—am left to linger in this dungeon!”

===Chinese===
「他为什么抛弃你？」伯爵夫人怯生生地问。在这狂野炽烈的个性面前，她自觉不过是个单纯的孩子。是的，他抛弃了她，永远地离开了她，她这样想着，为这个念头打了个寒噤。而如今他死了。此刻，她对自己承担的这桩使命产生了动摇。「他死了，」她低声说，声音微微震颤。

「谁？」卡拉喊道。

「你的朋友……赫尔呀！」伯爵夫人答道，准备满怀同情地走进这姑娘的感情里去，文克的身影在她潜意识中愈发淡薄。

可对方激昂地喊道：「你在说什么？我说的那个人没有死；他活着，而我却坐在这儿蹲监狱。在外面的城里，他就在那儿站着，塔一般强壮，岩石一般坚实，我告诉你！像你这样瘦弱的小东西，怎么会懂得他是什么人？其余所有人在他脚下都不过是尘土，他们的背信弃义也小得不值一顾！赫尔死了，可*那*又有什么要紧？谁肯为*他*费半个原子的心？可另外那一位，那主人，那君王，他活在自由的空气里，那里有光，有爱，有生命……在那里，他兴许还受得住我躺在他的脚边，像一条只配给他暖脚趾的地毯。他是伟人，是君王，是主人！他是一头熊，一头狮子，一头皇家孟加拉虎，你听见了吗？他不属于这片寒冷冰封的土地；他来自孟加拉，来自天堂，来自一个我再也见不着的地方！而我——我——却留在这儿的地牢里苦熬！」

===Original===
Suddenly she said, quite calmly and seriously, “Tell me, do you think there are men whose will is so strong that they can break down even these walls when they know how passionately I desire it?”

“There are no such men outside, but within us there are!” answered the Countess, carried away by the vehemence of that passionate storm of feeling which had so lately broken over her. How contemptible it was of her, she thought, to have desired to outwit a human being. She felt mean in her own estimation, and casting all projects and promises to the winds, she began to glow in the presence of this strange personality like the spark of an electric current. “Yes, they are to be found in us!” she repeated.

“He! he! the conqueror!” sang Cara, with a sound of passion in her tone, and in the Countess’s heart, too, there sprang up, like a marble image, the form of the man she had met a few evenings before. On her heart this image was sculptured, and she allowed its impress to recur again and again and remain there.

===Chinese===
她忽然说道，语气十分平静而郑重：「告诉我，你以为世上有那样的男人吗——意志坚强到只要知道我渴望得多么炽烈，就能连这些墙壁都推倒？」

「外面没有这样的人，我们内心却有！」伯爵夫人答道，被那场方才向她当头倾泻的感情暴风雨卷走了。她心想，自己竟存心去智取一个活人，多么可鄙啊。她在自己的估量中觉得卑劣起来，把一切计划和承诺都抛到了九霄云外，在这个奇异的人格面前，她像电流的火花一般开始发光。「是的，这样的人就在我们心里！」她重复道。

「嘿！嘿！那征服者！」卡拉吟唱般地说，音调里带着炽情。而在伯爵夫人的心里，也像一尊大理石像般，升起了几天前的那个晚上她遇见的那个男人的身影。这形象雕镌在她的心上，她任由它的印痕一次次浮现，滞留不去。

===Original===
“Do you love him?” she asked the dancer.

But the other answered, as if brushing away an unconsidered trifle, “I﻿ ﻿… love? I *adore* him!”

“I do not love him!” hastily asseverated the Countess, pursuing the mental image she had conjured up. “But yet he is great, superhuman. He is a world in himself. In the midst of this tame and quiet existence he is as a jungle and primeval forest. It seems to me as if he must have both the tiger and the serpent within him, as well as all that is boldest in Nature, its gigantic trees, its wild and impenetrable forests. Do you know, one can creep within them, never coming to an end, and yet be in him!”

She broke off suddenly. She dared not put into words the fancies evoked within her. For the husband whose eccentricities she tolerated was no more to her than a brother﻿—nay, a father. They were bound together by one voluptuous hour of which no human being knew or even suspected. It was such an hour as that in which two human personalities melted into one to create a new being that later on might emerge and begin a life bound by invisible ties to that mysterious hour. The threads might be torn from their place, snapped, distorted, yet they remained entwined. No other desire now possessed her than to yield her senses once more unrestrainedly to that consciousness of the depths of her being which enfolded her as in a dream, and which she nevertheless continually thrust aside.

The two women sat close together, the Countess on the ground. Both seemed alike to be struck down by an invisible and imperious fist, striking at these centres of abandonment and yearning and self-betrayal. After the hasty and intimate avowals forced from them, the shadow of silence fell upon them.

===Chinese===
「你爱他吗？」她问那舞女。

可对方答道，像掸开一件不值一想的小物件：「我……爱？我是*崇拜*他！」

「我不爱他！」伯爵夫人急忙申明，追逐着她心里唤起的那幅影像。「可他毕竟伟大，超乎凡人。他自身就是一个世界。在这温驯平静的生活中间，他就像一座丛林、一片原始森林。在我感觉里，他体内必定既有猛虎又有巨蛇，还有自然之中一切最大胆的东西：那些参天的巨树，那些狂野而人迹不至的密林。你知道吗，人可以一直往那密林深处爬呀爬，永远走不到尽头，却始终还在他的身体里！」

她蓦地住了口。她不敢把心里唤起的那些幻想说出口。因为那位怪癖被她一一宽容的丈夫，于她已无异于一位兄长——不，一位父亲。把他们系在一起的是那销魂的一个钟头，没有任何人知晓，甚至没有丝毫疑心。正是那样一个钟头，两个人格熔铸为一，造出一个新的生命，日后它会降生于世，开始一段被无形的纽带缚在那个神秘时刻上的生活。那些丝线或许会被从原处扯断、崩裂、扭歪，却依旧缠结在一起。此刻占据她的，只剩一个愿望：再一次不受拘束地把感官交付给那种对生命深处的意识——它如梦境般包裹着她，而她又不断地把它推到一旁。

两个女人紧挨着坐着，伯爵夫人坐在地上。两人仿佛都被一只看不见的、专横的拳头击中，拳头正砸在这些放纵、渴念与自我背叛的中心上。那些仓促间被逼出来的亲密自白吐露之后，沉默的暗影便笼罩了她们。

===Original===
“Say something!” pleaded the Countess timidly.

“Be silent, or I shall strangle you﻿ ﻿… with my own hands!” cried the dancer.

The Countess shrank back, feeling herself, beside the other, to resemble a hare in the claws of a mighty and powerful bird of prey.

Food was pushed into the cell, but neither of the women perceived it. It grew dark, and the dancer lay down, fully dressed, upon one of the plank beds. The Countess imitated her and stretched herself on the other straw pallet. The night passed by, and in the long sleepless hours their fancies flowed into a dark and turgid stream.

===Chinese===
「说句话呀！」伯爵夫人怯怯地恳求。

「闭嘴，不然我就掐死你……用我自己的这双手！」舞女喊道。

伯爵夫人缩了回去，觉得自己在对方身旁，就像一只落在猛禽利爪下的野兔。

饭食从门里递进牢房，两个女人谁也没有察觉。天色黑了下来，舞女和衣躺在一张木板床上。伯爵夫人学她的样，在另一张草垫上舒展开身子。黑夜过去，在那漫长的无眠时辰里，她们的幻想汇成一股幽暗浑浊的水流。

===Original===
Suddenly in the gloom Cara’s voice was heard: “Are you asleep?”

“No.”

“Why are you here?”

The Countess had not the courage to repeat her tissue of lies, and she remained silent. Cara, too, kept silent for a while, then she said suddenly:

“You were sent here to pump me! Have I told you anything?”

“Yes.”

“About *him*?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
黑暗中忽然响起卡拉的声音：「你睡着了吗？」

「没有。」

「你为什么会在这儿？」

伯爵夫人没有勇气把那套编好的谎话再重复一遍，便沉默了。卡拉也沉默了一阵，随后突然说：

「你是被打发来套我话的！我说过什么吗？」

「说过。」

「关于*他*？」

「说过。」

===Original===
“Did I tell you his name?”

“No.”

“That’s all right then, otherwise you would never leave this place alive. But if you are lying, and I had told it you, I tell you now, he *has* no name. He is a thousand men, a whole nation, a part of the universe!”

“Just like the man I have been thinking of,” reflected the Countess, but an instant later she did not know whether she had not spoken her thought aloud.

“When are you going away again?”

“When you want me to.”

===Chinese===
「我说过他的名字吗？」

「没有。」

「那还好。不然的话，你休想活着离开这个地方。不过你要是在撒谎，假如我真的告诉过你，那我现在告诉你：他*根本*没有名字。他是一千个人，是一整个民族，是宇宙的一部分！」

「跟我一直在想的那个男人一模一样，」伯爵夫人心里想着，可过了一瞬，她已经不知道自己方才究竟有没有把心里话说出声来。

「你什么时候走？」

「你想让我走，我就走。」

===Original===
“Then go at once, and tell everything I have told you!”

“No,” answered the Countess resolutely.

“Why don’t you, when that is what you came here for?”

“Things are different now.”

“Nothing is different,” asserted the dancer vehemently. “Everything is as it was and will ever be. He is out there, free as the air; I am here like a carcass rotting on the ground. Tell everything you know.”

===Chinese===
「那就马上走，把我告诉你的统统说出去！」

「不。」伯爵夫人斩钉截铁地答道。

「你为什么不？你来的不就是这个目的吗？」

「现在情形不一样了。」

「没有什么不一样的，」舞女激烈地断言，「一切都跟从前一样，也将永远如此。他在外头，自由得像空气；我在这儿，像一具在地上腐烂的尸首。把你知道的统统说出去。」

===Original===
“I shall say nothing!”

“Why not, you﻿—you cursed hussy!” she shrieked.

“Because you love him so!”

Then the dancer grew calm again, but a few moments later she burst into tears and sobbed wildly and unrestrainedly.

The Countess lay still on her pallet. She felt as if a naked soul with claws, whence the skin and tissues had been withdrawn, were clutching at her heart and holding it within its grasp. She felt her own blood shudder and leap up beneath the claws and mingle with that of the other. This naked soul that clutched at her was her sister. She was akin in blood to the criminal yonder, but neither of the women knew that he who had thus caused their hearts to beat in unison during this night in prison was one and the same mysterious being.

===Chinese===
「我一个字也不会说！」

「为什么不，你——你这个该死的贱货！」她尖声叫道。

「因为你这么爱他！」

舞女随即又平静下来，可没过片刻，她便泪如泉涌，狂乱而无拘无束地抽噎起来。

伯爵夫人一动不动地躺在草垫上。她觉得，仿佛有一个剥去了皮肉、只余利爪的赤裸灵魂，正抓住她的心，把它攥在爪中。她感到自己的血在爪子底下颤抖、跃起，同对方的血交融在一起。这个抓住她的赤裸的灵魂，是她的姐妹。她与那边那个罪犯血脉相连；可两个女人谁都不知道，这个让她们的心在狱中这一夜同律跳动的男人，正是同一个神秘的存在。

## XI / 十一

===Original===
The news Wenk received of Karstens’ state was very unsatisfactory. Since he had, apparently, offered strong resistance to his attackers, a second man seemed to have struck him violently on the head with a crowbar, and the blow had resulted in concussion of the brain. At intervals he became conscious, but for short periods only, and at present it was impossible to say what the outcome would be. His state was so critical, the doctor declared, that any sustained conversation with him could not be thought of for at least two or three weeks.

As for the dancer, about whose participation in the affair he would have something to say, as his shout to the constables to take her into custody proved, Wenk had for the present to content himself with any evidence the Countess might obtain. Today was Monday, and at four o’clock in any case he would hear whether any explanation might be looked for from Cara Carozza.

He did not leave the house that day. The two main centres of his activity could not be reached by him in person; one was the women’s prison, the other, and far more important, was the town of Constance. He was frequently called up by telephone from the latter place, for this Poldringer had to be kept constantly under surveillance.

While spending the waiting hours at home impatiently, he frequently walked backwards and forwards to the window. On one of these occasions he noticed a man whom he had first seen as early as eight o’clock, and again half an hour later, and then not again for some time. The man always happened to be passing the house rapidly, or else standing at a turning some distance off. Could it be that he was there to spy upon his movements? Wenk resolved to put the matter to the test.

===Chinese===
文克得到的关于卡斯滕斯伤情的消息很不乐观。看样子他对袭击者进行了顽强抵抗，于是似乎是另有第二个人用撬棍猛击了他的头部，这一击造成了脑震荡。他时不时恢复知觉，但每次都只短短一会儿，眼下还无法断定结果如何。医生宣称，他的状况危急至此，至少两三个星期之内，根本谈不上同他进行任何较长时间的谈话。

至于那名舞女——他当时朝警员们喊话、命他们把她收押起来，这足见关于她在本案中的牵连，他还有话要说——文克眼下只能满足于伯爵夫人可能取得的任何证词。今天是星期一，无论如何，到四点钟他总会知道，能否指望从卡拉·卡罗扎那里得到什么交代。

这一天他没有出门。他活动的两大重心，他都无法亲自前往：一处是女子监狱，另一处重要得多的，是康斯坦茨城。后者频频打电话来找他，因为这个波尔德林格必须时刻置于监视之下。

他在家中焦躁地打发着等候的时辰，一遍又一遍地踱到窗前又踱回去。其中一次，他注意到一个男人——此人他早在八点钟就见过头一回，半小时后又见过一回，此后好一阵子没有再露面。那男人总归是恰巧快步走过这所房子，要不然就站在稍远处的一个拐角上。莫非他是在那里侦察自己的行踪？文克决心试探个明白。

===Original===
He ordered one of the members of the Secret Police to disguise himself so that anyone at a hasty glance might mistake him for the State Attorney. Then Wenk’s chauffeur brought round the car to the door where the masquerader was waiting, and at a moment when the stranger was again visible at a corner this man got in quickly, settled himself down inside, and was rapidly driven away. “I shall be able to see how this simple trick succeeds,” said Wenk to himself.

At this moment there was an urgent call from Constance. “The man under observation brought the young fellows in his company to the station at 3:16 p.m. The Offenburg express is due to leave at 3:36. It is uncertain which of the party will travel by it; some have hand luggage, and the others none, and it is not yet ascertainable whether the suspected man will accompany them. One of them bought seven tickets for Offenburg, but the party consists of eight, and one among them looks different and has never been seen here before. It is possible that he may be the leader of the expedition, and in the service of the French. How are we to proceed?”

“Have three plainclothes police ready. If the eight go by train, let these three go too. If one or more stay behind, let one of the men be left too, so that those remaining are not allowed out of sight. They may be travelling by separate routes.”

The telephone official repeated the order given.

===Chinese===
他命令一名秘密警察（Secret Police）乔装改扮，教人匆匆一瞥之下会把他误认作检察官。随后文克的司机把汽车开到门口，那假扮者正在门旁候着；趁那陌生人又一次在拐角上露面的当口，此人迅速钻进车里，坐定身子，车子飞快地开走了。“这下可以看出这个小小的计谋灵不灵了。”文克自语道。

正在此时，康斯坦茨来了紧急电话：“受监视者已把他身边那伙年轻人带到车站，时间是下午3点16分。开往奥芬堡（Offenburg）的快车定于3点36分发车。这一伙人中哪些人会乘车尚不确定；有的带着手提行李，有的什么也没带，还无法查明那个嫌疑人是否会随行。其中一人买了七张去奥芬堡的车票，可这伙人共有八个，其中一个相貌迥异，以前从未在此地露过面。他可能就是这趟行动的头目，并且是在为法国人效力。我们该如何行事？”

“备好三名便衣警察。倘若那八个人都上火车，就让这三人也跟着上车。倘若有一个或几个人留下，也留下我们一个人，务必不叫留下的人脱离视线。他们可能分道而行。”

电话那头的官员复述了下达的命令。

===Original===
“Good. Arrange to speak to me immediately after the departure of the express. Ring off.”

Wenk asked to be connected with Offenburg, and in five minutes he was able to get on to the police there.

“Seven, or possibly eight, men are arriving by the express from Constance. Plainclothes men are in the same train. See that sixteen armed police are in readiness at the station. It is probable that the travellers will have passes to Alsace. They are forged.… When you arrest the men, be careful to avoid observation, and the only information to be given to the Press is that it was a case of Germans having been enticed into the Foreign Legion; and mind you state expressly that they will be at once set free and returned to their homes. You will know nothing about the forged passports. In case there is a man of the name of Poldringer or Hinrichsen among them, let him be separated from the rest and kept in close custody.”

===Chinese===
“好。快车一开出，立刻安排与我通话。挂断吧。”

文克要求接通奥芬堡，五分钟后他便接通了当地警方。

“将有七名、也可能八名男子乘康斯坦茨来的快车抵达。便衣与他们同在一列车上。务必安排十六名武装警察在车站待命。这些旅客多半持有前往阿尔萨斯（Alsace）的通行证。证件是伪造的。……逮捕这些人时，务必小心避开旁人耳目；向报界提供的唯一口径是：这是一起德国人被诱骗加入外籍军团的案子；切记要明确声明，他们将立即获释、遣返原籍。伪造护照一事，你们一概不知。倘若其中有个名叫波尔德林格或欣里希森的人，把他与其余的人隔离开来，严加看管。”

===Original===
Shortly after Constance telephoned again: “Seven men have left. It is Poldringer who stayed behind; he went to the Black Bull, and is under observation there.”

“Good. Thank you. Please ring up here again at seven o’clock, and should anything important occur in the meantime, notify the Criminal Investigation Department.”

Then Wenk had to hurry, so that he might call for the Countess at the prison at four o’clock. It was then half-past three, and he was alone in the house. He telephoned for his car, and just as he was going downstairs he heard a knock at the front door. He opened it.

===Chinese===
片刻之后康斯坦茨又来电话：“七人已经动身。留下的是波尔德林格；他去了「黑公牛」酒馆，在那里受到了监视。”

“好。谢谢。请七点钟再往这里来电话；此间若有要事发生，即报刑事调查部。”

接着文克便得抓紧了，以便四点钟到监狱去接伯爵夫人。这时已是三点半，家中只有他一人。他打电话叫车，正下楼时，听得前门有人敲门。他开了门。

===Original===
An elderly man was standing there. His figure was bent, and he had a bushy snow-white beard, red cheeks and blue eyes.

“Herr von Wenk?” he inquired courteously.

“Please come in,” answered the lawyer, “but I am sorry to say that I am just going out on urgent official business.”

“I will not detain you a moment,” said the other. “My name is Hull, and I am the father of the murdered man!”

Wenk bowed, and led the way to his office.

===Chinese===
门前站着一位老者。他身形佝偻，一部浓密的雪白胡须，红润的双颊，碧蓝的眼睛。

“是冯·文克先生吗？”他客客气气地问道。

“请进，”这位检察官答道，“只是抱歉得很，我正有紧急公务，马上要出门。”

“我一刻也不敢耽搁您，”来人说道，“我姓赫尔，是被害人的父亲！”

文克躬身还礼，引他走向自己的办公室。

===Original===
“Herr von Wenk, I have been told that you are conducting this inquiry. Edgar was my only son, and I brought him up badly, for my whole time was given to my business, and I had vast interests. My wife died when he was but a child. I think many sons in our days have had a similar experience.” He spoke evenly, almost harshly. “But that does not free me from blame. Our sons were our pleasure, our business our duty. It would have been better had it been the other way about. I cannot desire such a life as his to be restored, for what I have heard from various sides about the circumstances of the case is sufficient, and I do not wish to know more, but I have allowed myself the liberty of calling upon you for other reasons. My son used to receive an income of ten thousand marks from me each month, and the only wish left me in this unhappy affair is to be able to spend these ten thousand marks as if he were still living, and add another ten thousand to them. I want the money to be used to help men to make good, and how am I to set about this? Can you advise me, sir?”

Wenk answered in a hesitating tone, “I must first of all confess, Herr von Hull, that your words have taken me aback!”

This man’s bearing moved him deeply. Restrained force of character, suppressed paternal grief, unutterable sympathy… everything that had thus unexpectedly been laid bare to him, threw him for the moment somewhat off his balance. “Yes. I don’t know… Herr von Hull, why did you come to *me* above all men?”

===Chinese===
“冯·文克先生，听说这件案子是由您主持侦办的。埃德加是我唯一的儿子，而我把他教养得很坏，因为我全部的时间都投在了生意上，我的产业极其庞大。他还是个孩子的时候，我的妻子就去世了。我想，如今许多做儿子的都有过类似的境遇。”他说得四平八稳，近乎生硬。“可这并不能免去我的罪责。儿子是我们的乐趣，生意是我们的本分。倘若颠倒过来，本该更好。我不企望他那样的生活得以挽回，因为关于此案的情形，我从各方面听来的已经尽够，我也不想再知道更多；我冒昧登门，是另有缘故的。我儿子从前每月从我这里领取一万马克的进项；在这桩不幸的事件里，我剩下的唯一心愿，便是把这一万马克照旧花下去，只当他还在人间，并且再往上面添上一万。我想让这笔钱用来帮助世人改过向善，可我该从何处着手呢？您能给我出个主意吗，先生？”

文克迟疑地答道：“首先我必须坦白承认，冯·赫尔先生，您这番话使我大为错愕！”

这个人的气度深深打动了他。克制的性格力量、强抑的丧子之痛、难以言喻的悲悯……这一切如此出乎意料地袒露在他面前，一时竟使他微微失了方寸。“是啊。我不明白……冯·赫尔先生，天底下这么多人，您为什么偏偏来找*我*？”

===Original===
“I can tell you that at once, sir. It is your task to bring the murderers to justice, and I should like to replace with something that is beneficial the harm that has been done by one of my house. I should like the recollection of my son to bear good fruit. I have had nothing of his life, but perchance his death may yield something that may plead for me in eternity.” His voice remained firm until the last word had been uttered. “But I must not forget that you are in a hurry,” he continued. “Perhaps it is this same unhappy affair which prevents your giving me any more time now?”

“You are right,” said the lawyer.

“Can I see you tomorrow or some other day, when we can talk quietly, when you are free?”

“I shall be free tomorrow, my dear sir. Come when most convenient to you, preferably in the morning. You are not obliged to fix an exact time, for I shall be at home all day. I thank you for your suggestion; we shall be enabled to do a splendid piece of work together, I believe.”

“Nay, it is I who must thank you for being willing to help me raise a memorial to my unhappy boy that shall redeem his name among his fellow-men.”

===Chinese===
“这个我立刻就能回答您，先生。将凶手们缉拿归案，正是您的职分；而我家中一人闯下的祸害，我愿用某种有益之事来抵偿。我愿对我儿子的追念结出善果。他活着，我一无所得；可或许他的死能生出一点什么，在永恒面前替我申辩。”直到吐出最后一个字，他的声音始终坚稳。“不过我不可忘了您正忙着，”他接着说，“大约正是这桩不幸的案子，害得您此刻无法再多给我一点时间吧？”

“您说得不错，”检察官说。

“明天，或者改天，等您得空、我们能安安静静说话的时候，我能来见您吗？”

“我明天有空，亲爱的先生。您什么时候最方便就什么时候来，最好是上午。您不必约定确切的钟点，我一整天都在家。我感谢您的心意；我相信，我们将能一同成就一桩漂亮的事业。”

“不，该道谢的是我——您肯帮我为我不幸的孩子立起一座碑，让他的名声在世人中间得以洗雪。”

===Original===
They left the house together, and Wenk drove rapidly to the prison. “The lady left here long before four o’clock,” said the Governor.

“Indeed!” said Wenk, disappointed. “What did she leave for me?”

“Nothing!”

“And you yourself know nothing either? About the matter she had in hand, did she get any results?”

===Chinese===
两人一同出了宅门，文克驱车疾驰赶往监狱。“那位女士早在四点前许久就离开这里了。”监狱长说。

“是吗！”文克失望地说。“她给我留下了什么话没有？”

“什么也没有！”

“那么您自己也一无所知？她手头办的那件事，她得到什么结果了吗？”

===Original===
“I did not inquire.”

“Why not?” said Wenk, annoyed by his manner.

“I was not instructed to do so,” answered the Governor morosely.

“It is not a question of your exact instructions, but of attempting to track to earth one of the most dangerous bands of criminals Germany has ever known. You don’t seem to realize that. What you and your instructions may be counts for nothing.”

===Chinese===
“我没有过问。”

“为什么不过问？”文克说，被对方的态度惹恼了。

“没有人指示我这样做。”监狱长阴沉着脸答道。

“问题不在于您接到了什么确切的指示，而在于追踪查获德国有史以来最危险的犯罪团伙之一。您似乎没有意识到这一点。您是什么人、您的指示又是什么，全然无足轻重。”

===Original===
“So much the better. Perhaps another time I may be spared such innovations.…”

“You do not seem to feel yourself thoroughly comfortable in your post, Governor. I will say a word for you to the Home Secretary! Good morning.”

“What has happened?” said Wenk to himself. “What is up?” He felt disappointed and angry as he took his seat in the car again.

===Chinese===
“那就更好。也许下一次，这类破例之举就可以免了。……

“看来，监狱长，您在这个职位上坐得并不十分安稳。我会替您到内政部长（Home Secretary）面前进一言！再会。”

“这是怎么回事？”文克暗自思忖。“出了什么岔子？”他重新坐进汽车，心里又是失望又是恼火。

---

===Original===
At seven o’clock that evening the Countess drove to the Privy Councillor’s mansion. She found the same company assembled there as on the last occasion, and this time, too, she saw as little of them. Around her and Dr. Mabuse, her partner at the supper-table, the conversation rose and fell, isolating them from the rest. Her neighbour was more silent than on the previous occasion, but everything he said was spoken with an impressive intent, directed towards a goal which was unrecognizable.

The Countess was divided in her own mind as to whether she should relate her experience in the prison to him, should tell him that she had come in contact with the soul of a woman, strong and fearless as the figures in his own recitals; yea, even stronger, since it was a woman, experienced in renunciation, and carrying on her conflict in resistance and defence.

In imagination she had entered so thoroughly into the struggle, and her encounter with this criminal seemed to open up such unusual circumstances, that the power of the man at her side insensibly seemed to lessen, and this second meeting with him appeared to yield nothing that her passionate anticipation had longed for. The man seemed to decline before her.

She noticed that while he uttered his imperious sentences, both at their first meeting and on this occasion, he kept his eyes fixed on her with a compelling look. They were grey eyes, and their glance was a steely one. She grew somewhat frightened, and in her anxiety yearned for some human being who could warm her breast with his sympathy and afford her troubled spirit peace.

===Chinese===
当晚七点钟，伯爵夫人驱车前往枢密顾问（Privy Councillor）的宅邸。她发现聚在那里的还是上回那一批宾客，而这一回，她也照旧几乎见不着他们。晚餐桌上，谈话在她和她的同桌马布斯博士四周起伏涨落，把他们两人同其余的人隔绝开来。她的邻座比上一次更加沉默，但他说出的每一句话都带着一种慑人的用意，指向一个无从辨识的目标。

伯爵夫人心里七上八下，拿不定主意该不该把她在监狱里的经历讲给他听，该不该告诉他：她接触到了一个女人的灵魂，坚强无畏，一如他自己讲述里的人物；不，甚至更强——因为那是一个女人，深谙舍弃，在抵抗与防卫中打着她的仗。

在想象中她已经彻底投入了那场搏斗，她与这个女犯的邂逅又似乎展开出一番如此异乎寻常的境况，以至于身边这个男人的威力不知不觉间消退了下去，而这第二次会面，似乎再也给不了她炽烈期待所渴求的任何东西。这男人在她眼前仿佛黯淡了下去。

她注意到，无论初见还是这一回，他说出那些专横的句子时，总以一种慑人的目光牢牢盯住她。那是一双灰眼睛，目光冷硬如钢。她生出几分惧意，惶惶间渴念着一个人，一个能用他的体恤温暖她的胸怀、给她纷乱的心灵以安宁的人。

===Original===
She looked across at her husband. He was sitting near the medium, engaging her in talk, and it seemed as if his words were the mere play of his graceful fingers, on one of which the ring was flashing, as if dominating the whole. Then the woman’s heart was overcome with a strange sad feeling, stilling the fever in her breast—a feeling of lofty womanly sympathy. He seemed such a child, she said to herself. “Without me he would be defenceless. He is like a hoop rolling down the street, its course determined by the obstacles and unevennesses in its path.”

With this feeling upon her, she experienced a renewed glow as she thought of her encounter with the dancer; she was lifted out of her everyday existence, borne onward as in a mighty rush of passion, then again becoming cool and collected as at the contact with something cold and forbidding. It seemed to her then that she was struggling to reach her husband and ever as she approached him she was driven back, encountering the inflexible and steely glance of the man beside her.

Mabuse grew more and more silent. He ate nothing, and he took no pains to conceal his taciturnity. On the contrary, he seemed, as it were, to strive to impress it upon the whole company, just as a mighty African potentate might exercise his tyranny on his patient and long-suffering followers, and the very actions of the others served to accentuate this attitude of adoration of a superior force.

===Chinese===
她朝丈夫那边望去。他坐在灵媒（medium）近旁同她攀谈，他的言语仿佛不过是他那双优雅手指的游戏——其中一根手指上，戒指正闪着光，仿佛统御着一切。顿时，这女人的心被一种奇异的哀伤感征服了，平息了她胸中的燥热——一种高贵的、女子气的怜惜。他多么像个孩子啊，她自忖道。“没有我，他便全无防卫。他就像一只顺街道滚下去的铁环，滚向何方，全由路上的障碍与坎坷决定。”

怀着这份心绪，一想到与那舞女的相遇，她便重新感到一阵炽热；她被托出了日常的生存，仿佛被一道汹涌的激情之流裹挟着向前，随即又冷却下来、凝定下来，如同触到某种冰冷而狰狞的东西。随后她只觉得，自己正在奋力奔向丈夫，可每每一挨近，又被逼退回来——迎头撞上的，是身旁那人绝不通融的钢铁般的目光。

马布斯越来越沉默。他什么也不吃，也毫不费神去掩饰这份缄默。相反，他仿佛存心要把这缄默强加给满座宾客，正像一位威势赫赫的非洲君主，在他隐忍久苦的随从们身上施行他的暴虐；而旁人的一举一动，反倒把这副对强势的膜拜姿态衬托得愈发分明。

===Original===
Count Told alone seemed to trifle with graceful gestures about the medium, who, black-haired and deadly pale, kept her unwieldy form pressed close to his side, seeming to have eyes for no other. Then the Countess felt that she hated the man who sat beside her in his sullen mood while her husband’s attitude was thus bordering on the ridiculous. And yet it was not hate she felt, but the inward conflict between the desire to yield herself to the domination of a self-sufficing and stronger heart and brain and resistance to the impulse of subjugation.

The supper-table was cleared and the company stood around talking for a while. Mabuse had left his table-companion and sought the society of Count Told. He engaged him in a discourse on the psychological aspect of gambling.

“I am a born gambler,” said the Count. “When I am losing, I remain as cold as ice, but when I am winning my brain lights up and my fantasies are redoubled.”

===Chinese===
唯独托尔德伯爵，仿佛还在灵媒身旁做出种种优雅的姿态消遣；那灵媒一头黑发，脸色死白，把她那笨重的身躯紧紧贴在他身侧，眼里似乎再也容不下第二个人。这时伯爵夫人感到，自己恨透了身旁这个阴沉着脸的男人，而丈夫的做派已然近乎荒唐。然而她感到的又不是恨，而是一场内心的交战：一边是渴望委身于一颗自足而更强大的心灵头脑的支配，一边是抵抗这股驯服的冲动。

餐桌撤了下去，宾客们站着闲谈了一阵。马布斯撇下同桌的伴侣，去寻托尔德伯爵作伴，同他谈起赌博的心理学一面。

“我是个天生的赌徒，”伯爵说。“输的时候，我冷得像冰；可赢的时候，我的脑子便亮堂起来，幻想也成倍翻涌。”

===Original===
