Then Mabuse said: “Games of chance are the oldest form, the strongest and most widespread form, in which a man who is not gifted with artistic expression may yet feel himself an artist.”

“That is an interesting idea,” said the Count; “pray follow it up a little further.”

“It is because in a game of chance every man feels that he can force himself to a creative act. Creation, through the principle which underlies all life, draws its force from the parallel powers of volition and accident. By accident we must understand all that is untried, immeasurable, strange, and impossible of expression in itself. This is, too, the mental process of creative work, to which nature has lent a portion of primal force, the work of the artist! Between the poles of volition and accident this power is wielded as in a state of trance. Goethe confessed that to be the case with himself when he was composing his poems. In games of chance there is a like synthesis. Accident gives the player his material—it may be trifling and insignificant, or it may be of dominating power. The player sets his will to work to accomplish a creation of his own from his material.”

===Chinese===
于是马布斯说道：“碰运气的赌戏是最古老的一种形式，也是最强烈、最普及的一种形式——一个没有艺术表达天赋的人，凭它能感到自己是个艺术家。”

“这想法很有意思，”伯爵说，“劳驾再往下讲讲。”

“这是因为，在碰运气的赌戏里，每个人都觉得自己能逼出一桩创造的行为。创造，凭着那贯通一切生命的根本原则，从意志与偶然这两股并行的力量里汲取伟力。所谓偶然，我们必须理解为一切未经尝试的、不可度量的、陌生的、自身无从表达的东西。这也正是创造性工作的心理过程——大自然把一缕原始之力借给了它，艺术家的工作！这股力量在意志与偶然的两极之间被挥使着，宛如处于迷醉之中。歌德就自承，他作诗时正是这般情形。在碰运气的赌戏里，存在着一种近似的综合。偶然把材料交到赌客手里——那材料也许琐屑微末，也许具有主宰一切的伟力。赌客则发动自己的意志，要从这材料里完成一件属于他自己的创造。”

===Original===
“You are a poet yourself, Doctor?”

“Oh, no, I am a physician practising psychotherapy.”

“Such people are our most modern poets. For they give our knowledge of the unconscious, or rather the subconscious, its perceptible form, and the subconscious world, which is now firmly established, produces our psychic existence. We will have a game of baccarat afterwards, shall we not?”

“Agreed!”

===Chinese===
“您自己就是位诗人吧，博士？”

“哦不，我是个行心理治疗的医生。”

“这样的人正是我们最现代的诗人。因为他们把我们对无意识——不如说潜意识——的认识赋予了可感知的形式；而如今已然牢牢确立的潜意识世界，正生产着我们的心灵存在。待会儿我们来一局巴卡拉，好不好？”

“好极了！”

===Original===
The hypnotic subject was about to begin her test. A doctor led her forward and threw her into a hypnosis in which she would recall her wonderful recollections. On the first evening, as Count Told informed Mabuse in an awestruck whisper, she had related her mental experiences during her first attempts to walk.

While the Count was speaking he felt an unnatural warmth stealing over the back of his head. He turned round, but there was nothing behind him save the tapestried wall, upon which pictures of the old school, to which he was quite indifferent, were hanging.

The patient did not respond to the hypnotist’s suggestions. She did indeed fall into a state of trance, but all the spectators could see that gradually the expression of her eyes indicated that she was returning from a far-off view, until suddenly they looked straight ahead and were wide awake again, awake and indignant.

“Someone is tormenting me,” she said.

===Chinese===
受催眠者就要开始她的试验了。一位医生把她领上前来，使她进入催眠状态，好让她召回那些奇妙的记忆。托尔德伯爵心怀敬畏、悄声告诉马布斯：头一晚，她讲述了自己最初学步时的心理体验。

伯爵正说着，忽觉一股异样的暖意悄悄爬上自己的后脑。他回过头去，身后却什么也没有，只有挂着壁毯的墙，墙上悬着几幅旧派画作——他对这些画全然无动于衷。

病人对催眠师的暗示毫无反应。她确实陷入了迷睡状态，但所有旁观者都看得出，她眼中的神情渐渐表明，她正从一片遥远的景象中归返，直到突然间，双眼直视前方，整个人完完全全醒了——醒了，而且怒气冲冲。

“有人在折磨我。”她说。

===Original===
“No one is tormenting you,” said the hypnotist in a monotonous and measured tone. “We are guiding you to the early home of your youth—one, two, three… you are sleeping—one, two… you are sleeping!”

He passed his hand slowly and lightly over her forehead, continuing to count, “Three… one… two… where are you now?—how old are you?”

“I am ten months and three days old.”

“What did your mother do this morning when she took you out of the cradle?”

“She unwrapped me and hurt me and…and…” She breathed a deep sigh, then awoke suddenly and said, “There is someone here who ought to go away. Who is tormenting me?”

===Chinese===
“没有人在折磨你，”催眠师用单调而有板有眼的声调说。“我们正引着你回到你少年时光的家——一，二，三……你睡吧——一，二……你睡吧！”

他的手缓慢而轻柔地拂过她的额头，继续数着：“三……一……二……你现在在哪儿？——你几岁了？”

“我十个月零三天大。”

“今天早晨你母亲把你从摇篮里抱出来的时候，她做了什么？”

“她解开我的襁褓，弄疼了我，还有……还有……”她深深叹了一口气，随即猛然醒来，说道：“这儿有个不该在这儿的人。到底是谁在折磨我？”

===Original===
“We can obtain no results today. There are some disturbing influences which I do not recognize and therefore cannot remove,” said the hypnotist.

The Privy Councillor approached Mabuse. “How would it be, Doctor, if *you* were to make an attempt? After the tests of your power which I have already seen, I think we can promise to get rid of these disturbing influences,” he said.

Mabuse declared himself willing to try, at any rate, though he could not vouch for the result, as he was suffering from a slight chill which affected his head. He at once took a short step towards the medium, however, and they saw that she moved slightly in his direction as if attracted by a magnet. Mabuse did not utter a word, but he let his glance wander over part of her body. The girl became even paler than before, if possible, and although she made no movement, it was easy to see that she struggled against something invisible, that her resistance grew quickly weaker and that her eyes fell before him.

===Chinese===
“今天我们得不出结果了。有某种干扰性的影响，我辨认不出，因而也无法排除。”催眠师说。

枢密顾问走到马布斯跟前。“博士，要是*您*来试一试，您看如何？凭我亲眼见过的那些对您神力的检验，我想我们可以担保，那些干扰一定排除得掉。”他说。

马布斯表示愿意一试，至少可以试试，只是不敢担保结果，因为他头部受了点轻微的风寒。然而他当即朝灵媒跨出一小步，众人便看见她微微朝他那边挪动，仿佛被磁石吸引。马布斯一言不发，只让目光在她身上缓缓逡巡。那姑娘的脸色，倘若还能更白的话，比先前更白了；她虽一动不动，却很容易看出，她正在同某种看不见的东西搏斗，她的抵抗迅速衰竭，双眼在他面前垂了下去。

===Original===
Then Mabuse said in a rapid and violent tone: “You are lying in swaddling clothes. Your arms are bound fast to your side. You are six months old. It is evening, and you are crying. Why are you crying?”

And from the heavy body of this girl, sleeping with wide-open eyes, there came a piping, fretful voice: “I have a pain in my stomach.”

“That is only wind. You’ve had too much to drink. Who gave it you?”

“I got it from the breast of a woman,” answered the baby voice.

“Do you love that breast?”

===Chinese===
随后马布斯用急促而暴烈的声调说道：“你躺在襁褓里。你的两条胳膊被紧紧捆在身侧。你六个月大。天色是傍晚，你在哭。你为什么哭？”

从这姑娘沉重的躯体里——她睁着双眼沉睡着——发出了一个尖细、烦躁的声音：“我肚子疼。”

“那只是胀气。你喝得太多了。谁给你喝的？”

“我是从一个女人的乳房里喝来的。”奶孩的声音答道。

“你爱那乳房吗？”

===Original===
Then the girl grew deathly white, and into the childish voice there crept a piercing and angry note, “No.”

“What did you want to do?”

“I wanted to bite it with my gums!”

“Why?”

Then the girl was seized with trembling, which passed over her whole body, and Mabuse said, “Every minute that prolongs this endangers her life. I must bring the experiment to an end!”

===Chinese===
这时姑娘的脸色变得死人一般惨白，稚气的嗓音里钻进一缕尖利而愤怒的调子：“不爱。”

“你想干什么？”

“我想用牙床咬它！”

“为什么？”

于是姑娘浑身颤抖起来，颤栗传遍她整个身体；马布斯说：“这样拖下去，每一分钟都在危及她的生命。我必须结束这个实验！”

===Original===
He laid the girl down on a sofa, and with reassuring movements he released her from sleep and bathed her face, and when she came to herself again recommended her being put to bed.

The conversation now turned upon Mabuse’s experiment, and everyone was asking questions, speculating on what she would have said.

“That was a fairytale,” said Told; “a fable of the preconscious existence! Doctor, you are a genius. But what did she want to say that made her tremble so?”

A lady came forward with the same question on her lips, but Mabuse’s eyes sought the Countess, and she, too, came forward to ask. Then Mabuse answered, “She wanted to say, ‘Because I hated her so!’”

===Chinese===
他把姑娘放倒在一张沙发上，以抚慰的动作把她从睡眠中唤醒，替她擦拭脸面；待她重又清醒过来，他便建议送她上床歇息。

谈话此刻转到了马布斯的实验上，人人都在提问，揣摩她方才本来还会说出什么来。

“那简直是一篇童话，”托尔德说，“一则前意识存在的寓言！博士，您是个天才。可她想说什么话，竟叫她抖成那样？”

一位女士走上前来，唇边挂着同样的问题；但马布斯的目光在寻觅伯爵夫人，于是她也上前发问。马布斯这才答道：“她想说的是：‘因为我恨透了她！’”

===Original===
The Countess shrank back and the others were silent, painfully affected. Then the Countess leaned forward, saying coldly, “A baby cannot hate!”

“How do you know that?” asked Mabuse roughly.

“I know it… of myself,” she replied.

“Then you can rejoice over yourself, for you are not only a genius at recollection, but also an angel in disposition!” retorted Mabuse sarcastically.

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人瑟缩了一下，其余的人都沉默了，个个痛苦地受了触动。随后伯爵夫人俯身向前，冷冷地说：“婴儿不会恨！”

“这你怎么知道？”马布斯粗暴地问。

“我知道……凭我自己。”她答道。

“那你尽可以为自己庆幸了，因为你不仅是个追忆的天才，还是个性情中的天使！”马布斯讥诮地反唇相讥。

===Original===
Conversation broke the company up into little groups. Count Told alone remained silent. There was still that unnatural warmth at the back of his head. He looked behind him, and he felt his head; there was nothing there. He went to a mirror, but nothing was to be seen. He sat down again and it seemed as if he were falling asleep, yet he saw them all and heard everything. He wanted to say something, but it seemed as if the words were plucked from his mouth like ripened fruit ready to fall.

After a short time had passed thus, he rose and went to the group wherein Dr. Mabuse was standing, saying, “We were going to play baccarat!”

“So we were!” answered Mabuse. “Shall we be likely to find enough players?”

===Chinese===
谈话把宾客们分成了三三两两的小圈子。唯独托尔德伯爵始终沉默。他后脑上那股异样的暖意还在。他朝身后看看，又摸摸自己的头；那里什么也没有。他走到一面镜子前，也照不出什么名堂。他重新坐下，仿佛就要睡去，然而他又看得见所有的人，听得见每一句话。他想说点什么，可那些话仿佛正被人从他嘴里摘走，像熟透欲坠的果实。

这样过了一小会儿，他站起身，走到马布斯博士所立的那一圈人里，说道：“我们本来要玩巴卡拉的！”

“正是！”马布斯答道。“人手凑得齐吗？”

===Original===
Then Told grew wide awake and eager. “It will be fine, playing baccarat with you. Herr Wendel, will you join us, eh?”

“I must attend to my social duties among the ladies,” answered the Privy Councillor, “but you will soon be able to find partners!”

Six gentlemen quickly gathered round the card-table which stood in a part of the room leading to the conservatory. The lamp with its enormous shade hung low over the table, leaving the rest of the room in the half-light. In the conservatory, to which a glass door led, the ghostly branches of foreign palms could be seen outlined against the glass, and in the moonlight they looked like stiff forms stretching their dark limbs heavenwards.

They cut the cards to see who should be the first to hold the stakes. The visitors crowded round the card-table and Countess Told stood in the dim light, looking down upon it. Mabuse saw her smooth white skin gleaming from the rich dark red dress she wore. His bearing was cold and gloomy, and scarcely a word escaped his lips. The feelings that arose within him were sternly suppressed, and his thoughts were busy with Count Told alone. When anyone addressed him, he answered abruptly. He seemed to pay great attention to the game, but he played by leaps and bounds.

===Chinese===
托尔德顿时全然清醒过来，兴致勃发。“能同您一起玩巴卡拉，定是妙事。温德尔（Wendel）先生，您也来一份，嗯？”

“我还得到女士们那里去尽我的社交义务，”枢密顾问答道，“不过你们很快就能找齐牌伴的！”

六位绅士很快聚拢到牌桌四周，牌桌立在通向暖房的那一侧屋里。那盏罩着巨大灯罩的吊灯低低悬在桌面上方，把房间的其余部分留在半明半暗之中。隔着一道玻璃门望进暖房，异域棕榈鬼影似的枝柯映在玻璃上，月光下，它们宛如一具具僵直的形体，正向天空伸展着黝黑的肢臂。

他们切牌，定夺谁第一个坐庄。宾客们围拢在牌桌边，托尔德伯爵夫人立在幽暗的光里，俯视着桌面。马布斯望见她那光洁白皙的肌肤，从她那袭华贵的深红裙装里透出光泽。他的举止冷漠阴沉，唇边几乎吐不出一个字。胸中涌起的种种情感被严严压制下去，他的心思全在托尔德伯爵一人身上。有人同他说话，他便唐突地应一声。他似乎对牌局极其专注，押起注来却一跳一个台阶。

===Original===
Soon the gentlemen who had begun their game with modest stakes began to imitate his example, and there was no unanimity in the value of the stakes. Beside a stake of a mark or two there stood a fifty-mark note, and then one for two hundred. The small stake seemed to feel ashamed; it rapidly became twenty, and still faster it grew to a hundred, to two hundred.… Very soon there was no player who ventured less than a hundred marks. When they began they found time for conversation between the end of the hand and the fresh deal, but after a time the talk grew less, and then ceased. The onlookers, too, became silent. The contest between the players grew more pronounced, the game feverish, and this excitement spread to the spectators.

The Countess noted the high stakes her husband wagered. “He has never played before,” she thought. “What is the matter with him?”

The Count was winning. He let his winnings accumulate. It seemed as if he were a horse, urged and threatened onward by an eager rider. He threw his money down. It was now his turn to hold the stakes. It seemed to him as if the moment in which he should deal the cards and undertake the manifold risks of gain or loss would be a supreme experience for him, yielding rich secrets of wonderful joy. He grew excited, and his fantasies played about the room.

===Chinese===
不久，开局时押注尚且节制的那几位绅士便开始效法他，赌注的数额便参差不一了。一两个马克的注旁边立着一张五十马克的钞票，接着又是一张两百的。小额的赌注仿佛自惭形秽，迅速涨成二十，又以更快的速度窜上一百、窜上两百。……很快就再没有一个赌客押少于一百马克的了。开局时，一局终了到重新发牌之间，他们还有工夫说说话，可过了一阵，话便少了，随后干脆没了。看客们也沉默下来。赌客之间的较量愈见分明，牌局渐趋狂热，这股亢奋又蔓延到看客身上。

伯爵夫人注意到丈夫押下的赌注之高。“他从来不赌钱的，”她想。“他这是怎么了？”

伯爵一直在赢。他任凭赢金积攒。他像一匹马，被一名急切的骑手又催又吓地逼着向前。他把钱掷下去。现在轮到他坐庄了。在他看来，发牌的那一刻——连同盈亏的种种风险一并担起来的那一刻——将成为他一次至高无上的经历，向他泄露奇妙欢愉的丰盛奥秘。他兴奋起来，他的幻想在房间里四处游走。

===Original===
The Countess turned aside in the half-light, constrained at her husband’s incomprehensible actions. Suddenly the full light of the lamp fell upon her, revealing where her slender breast rose white and stately from the enclosing circle of her gown.

“North and south!” said Mabuse, as he contemplated her lovely figure, “north and south, your turn is coming,” and his tone was sinister and threatening. Then he turned his glance away, and it fell upon Count Told’s hands as he took over the bank at this moment. He dealt the cards out, and hesitated a moment as if perplexed at some strange occurrence. He was relieved when he had distributed the pack. He won considerable sums, and it was singular that the same feeling of perplexity recurred. He won a second time, and now this seemed to happen continually. Players and spectators alike were astonished at the run of luck the Count’s game exhibited.

“Look at your husband,” said someone, turning to the Countess; “he is winning every hand.”

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人在半明半暗里侧过身去，为丈夫不可理喻的举动感到窘迫。忽然，灯光的全部光辉落在她身上，映出她纤细的胸脯从裙装的合围中白皙端庄地隆起的地方。

“北与南！”马布斯打量着她可爱的身姿，说道，“北与南，就快轮到你们了。”他的语调阴森而带着威胁。随后他移开目光，目光恰落在此时接掌庄家的托尔德伯爵那双手上。他把牌发了出去，迟疑了片刻，仿佛对某种怪异的变故感到困惑。一副牌发完，他才如释重负。他赢了相当可观的一注，可奇怪的是，那同样的困惑感又冒了出来。他第二次又赢了，如今这情形仿佛没完没了。赌客和看客无不惊异于伯爵牌运之顺。

“瞧瞧您丈夫，”有人转向伯爵夫人说道，“他局局都赢。”

===Original===
They all cast a glance at the Countess and then quickly returned to their cards. The Count dealt the cards once more. He disclosed his cards; he had two picture cards and was about to buy another.

“Halt!” cried a voice suddenly, like the voice of a drill sergeant, and a hand was laid roughly on the table, reaching the white and delicate hand of the Count, on which the jewelled ring was sparkling, and turning it over. Then all the company saw that the Count had been about to take a card from underneath the pack instead of the one that lay on the top. The card was a nine.

“Aha, a nine! *Now* I understand your luck, you gudgeon! You are a common cheat!”

===Chinese===
众人都朝伯爵夫人瞥了一眼，随即又赶紧回到各自的牌上。伯爵再次发牌。他亮开手中的牌：两张花牌，正要再买一张。

“住手！”忽然一声断喝，像操练军士的嗓音；一只手重重按在桌上，一把捉住伯爵那只白皙秀气的手——戒指上的宝石还在闪闪发光——把它翻转过来。于是满座宾客都看见了：伯爵方才要拿的，不是牌堆顶上的那张，而是牌堆底下的牌。那张牌是九点。

“啊哈，一张九点！*现在*我明白你的手气了，你这好上钩的呆子！你是个不折不扣的骗子！”

===Original===
They all sprang up in confusion. Count Told sat still in his chair, in a state of utter collapse. He seemed absolutely crushed, finding no word to say.

“Give the money here!” cried the harsh voice again. “All of it!” The tone was threatening.

The spectators and the players were crowding together, and a cry rang through the obscurity. Through the hasty movements of the powerful man who had seized the Count, one man had fallen to the ground, dragging another down with him. The latter clutched at the tablecloth, and it was pulled off, money and cards being strewn over the floor, people flinging themselves upon it. Suddenly the electric lights went out, but Dr. Mabuse, who had waited for the cry from the dark corner, rushed to the fainting Countess, lifted her in his arms and with one spring bore her under the palms and out into the garden under the moonlight, through the shrubbery and to the wall leading to the street. He lifted her over, and from the other side someone helped him with his burden. An instant later a car was stealing swiftly down the street.

“The northern and southern hemispheres,” he shouted aloud furiously during the drive. “Now I hold you both!”

The Xenienstrasse was empty. The car came to a sudden standstill. He carried the Countess, still unconscious, into his house.

===Chinese===
众人乱作一团，纷纷跳了起来。托尔德伯爵却僵坐在椅子里，整个人彻底垮了。他仿佛被碾得粉碎，一句话也找不出来。

“把钱拿来！”那刺耳的嗓音又喊道。“统统拿来！”口吻咄咄逼人。

看客和赌客挤作一团，一声喊叫穿透了昏暗。擒住伯爵的那个壮汉动作迅猛，撞倒了一个男人，那人又带倒了另一个。后者伸手去抓桌布，整块桌布被扯了下来，钱钞和纸牌撒了一地，人们纷纷扑上去。电灯骤然熄灭；而马布斯博士——他早已守在黑暗的角落里专候这声喊叫——直扑向晕倒的伯爵夫人，把她抱进怀里，一个箭步穿过棕榈之下，冲进月光里的花园，穿过灌木丛，直奔临街的院墙。他把她托过墙头，墙那边有人帮着接住了他怀里的负担。片刻之后，一辆汽车正悄然疾驰，顺街而下。

“北半球与南半球，”驱车疾驰途中，他狂怒地高声喊道。“如今两个都在我手里了！”

克塞尼恩大街空无一人。汽车蓦地停住。他抱起仍然不省人事的伯爵夫人，走进了自己的宅邸。

## XII / 十二

===Original===
Scarcely heeding the abuse and scorn heaped upon him by the crowd, out of the chaos and confusion of the contemptuous glances of others and his own feeling of perplexity, Count Told stole, as if in a dream, towards the vestibule. He thought of his wife, but he had not the courage to look round for or inquire about her. His car stood before the door, and the chauffeur was about to start the engine when the Count made a gesture of denial, saying, “Wait for the Countess!”

He went into the town and hired the first taxi he saw to drive him home. “What has happened to me?” was the question that he perpetually put to himself. “What was it that overcame me? Who moved my hand? … What is it that has happened? I know nothing about it. Can it be merely a bad dream?”

But it was no dream. He reached his house and had to descend. He went down the length of the garden and into the house. The footman took his coat, and the Count went to the room where he and his wife, whenever they had been out together, were wont to spend a short time before going to bed, in exchanging the experiences the evening had afforded. He always looked forward eagerly to these moments.

Tonight he was alone there. “Where can my wife be?” he asked himself, astonished and yet unconscious. So many tender memories clung to this room, and he felt disappointed that in this dreadful hour she was not by his side. It was the first painful experience of his existence.

But all at once it became clear to him that she must have sundered herself from him, and he realized that by that inexpressibly strange occurrence at the gaming-table in the Wendel mansion he had covered himself with mire. It clung fast to him, and he thought, “Lucy must leave me. She must remain away until I have purified myself.” But how was he to accomplish the task?

===Chinese===
托尔德伯爵几乎顾不上众人劈头盖脸砸来的辱骂与讥嘲，从旁人轻蔑目光交织成的混乱纷扰和自己满心的惶惑迷乱里，恍如在梦中一般，悄悄溜向门厅。他想到了妻子，却没有勇气回头找她、打听她。他的汽车还停在门前，司机正要发动引擎，伯爵摆手止住，说道：“等伯爵夫人！”

他进了城，雇下看见的头一辆出租汽车送自己回家。“我这是怎么了？”他一遍又一遍地问自己。“是什么东西制服了我？是谁在操纵我的手？……到底出了什么事？我一无所知。难道只是一场噩梦吗？”

可这不是梦。他到了家门口，不得不下车。他沿着花园走到尽头，进了宅子。男仆接过他的外套，伯爵走向那个房间——每逢夫妻俩一同外出，他们总习惯在就寝前到那里小坐片刻，交换这一晚的种种见闻。这样的时刻他一向是热切盼望的。

今夜他独自一人在那里。“我的妻子会在哪儿呢？”他问自己，既惊愕又恍惚失神。这个房间里萦绕着多少温柔的回忆，他感到失望：在这可怕的时辰，她竟不在自己身边。这是他平生头一遭痛苦的遭际。

可他蓦然明白过来：她必定是同他断然分开了。他意识到，温德尔府邸赌台边那桩难以言喻的怪事，已经使他浑身蒙上污泥。那污点紧紧粘在他身上，他想：“露西必须离开我。她必须远远避开，直到我把自己洗净为止。”可这件事他又该怎么办成呢？

===Original===
And suddenly there came over him, like an icy blast in all its pitiless severity, the full meaning of what he had done. He had done it, he really had put cards at the bottom of the pack and then drawn them when he wanted them, and with these he had won money. Yet he had not desired to win money! What could have happened? Was there no help anywhere? He had done something against his will. His act had thrust him out of decent society, and to the end of his days he would be known as a cheat. Was there no help to be found?

“I know now,” he said to himself, “what it is I have done, but I do not know how I came to do it, neither the why nor the wherefore. I am growing crazy, losing my self-confidence, and I shall henceforth be unable to feel safe, whatever I do. Horrible, monstrous thought! I am absolutely afraid of myself. How can I ever have reached such a point? Yonder is a sculpture by Archipenko and the picture hanging there is one of Kokoschka’s; I am quite certain of that; but what proceeds from my own brain, and is my own creation, of that I can never more feel certain again. I retain my sight, hearing and feelings, but my brain is rotting! … I shall end in a lunatic asylum! My body moves in the light of day while my mental powers are wrapped in a dim twilight. Is there no one that can help me?”

He struggled with his tears, but he could not even allow himself to weep, for he thought, “Perhaps I shall lose all consciousness of what I am doing. If I weep, may I not possibly destroy a picture that I have hitherto loved and worshipped, or abuse my man, or act improperly to Lucy’s maid?”

And suddenly, at the utterance of his wife’s name, he collapsed entirely. “Ah, Lucy, light of my life, can *you* not help me?” he cried. “Will you not come? Have you no longer faith in me? Why am I left alone?”

===Chinese===
突然间，仿佛一阵凛冽无情、寒彻骨髓的狂风向他袭来，他所做之事的全部含义压了下来。他做了，他确实把牌塞到了牌堆底下，又在需要的时候把它们抽了出来，靠这些牌赢了钱。可他根本无意赢钱！到底出了什么事？哪里都找不到救助吗？他做了一件违背自己意志的事。这一行为已把他逐出体面的社会，直到生命尽头，他都要顶着骗子的名头。难道就没有任何办法可想了么？

“我如今明白了，”他对自己说，“我做了什么，可我不知道自己是怎么做出来的，既不知为何，也不知所以然。我在一步步发疯，在失去自信，从今往后，无论做什么，我都无法再感到安稳。可怕、可怖的念头！我实实在在地害怕自己。我怎么竟会落到这般田地？那边是一座阿契彭科（Archipenko）的雕塑，挂在那儿的那幅画出自科柯施卡（Kokoschka）之手；这两点我完全确信；可是凡出自我自己脑髓、属于我自己创造的东西，我却永远无法再确信了。我的视觉、听觉和感觉都还在，脑子却在腐烂！……我终究是要进疯人院的！我的身体在日光下走动，我的心智却裹在一层昏沉的暮色里。就没有一个人能帮帮我吗？”

他强忍着眼泪，可他连哭都不敢让自己哭，因为他想：“说不定我会完全失去对自己行为的意识。如果我哭起来，岂不是可能毁掉一幅我至今热爱、敬奉的画，或是辱骂我的仆人，或是对露西的女仆举止不端？”

而一说出妻子的名字，他顿时彻底崩溃了。“啊，露西，我生命里的光，*你*就不能帮帮我吗？”他喊道。“你不肯来吗？你不再信任我了吗？为什么单单把我一个人丢下？”

===Original===
He rang, and then, hastening to meet the footman, inquired for the Countess.

“The Countess has not yet returned,” he was told.

“Nor telephoned? Has she not …”

“No, my lord, but an hour ago Herr Dr. von Wenk rang up, asking if he might have the honour of waiting on her ladyship tomorrow morning. His telephone number has been written down.”

“Go!” said the Count. “I will go to Dr. Wenk … yes, to Dr. Wenk,” he thought, and then, a prey to a thousand nameless fears, he cried aloud, “Or else I shall hang myself! I must be able to tell some human being what I feel. …”

===Chinese===
他按了铃，随即迎着走过来的男仆，打听伯爵夫人。

“伯爵夫人还没有回来。”男仆答道。

“也没来过电话？她没有……”

“没有，老爷。不过一小时前冯·文克博士先生来过电话，问明天上午可否有幸拜见夫人。他的电话号码已经记下来了。”

“去吧！”伯爵说。“我要去找文克博士……对，去找文克博士。”他心想，随即又被千百种无名的恐惧攫住，高声喊道：“要不然我就上吊！我总得能把心里的感受讲给一个人听。……”

===Original===
He hurried to the telephone, giving the number written down. “Yes, this is the State Attorney, Dr. Wenk!” answered a strange voice in the distance, and Told began to tremble. But he rallied all his energy and self-control, saying, “Can I speak to you at once?”

He was terribly afraid that the fever of his desire might melt the connecting wire and that he might get no answer. He breathed freely again when he heard the words, “With pleasure! I shall expect you!”

“Fritz!” he shouted; “get the two-seater ready,” and he drove back to Munich.

Wenk believed he had come on the Countess’s errand, and that something had happened in the prison to put an end to the enterprise they had in hand.

===Chinese===
他急忙奔向电话，拨了记下的号码。“对，我就是检察官文克博士！”远处一个陌生的声音答道，托尔德开始发抖。但他鼓起全部的精力和自制力，说道：“我能马上同您谈谈吗？”

他怕得要命，生怕自己热望的炽度会把通话的电线熔断，从此听不到回音。待听到那句“非常乐意！我恭候您！”他才重新畅快地呼吸了。

“弗里茨！”他喊道，“把双座车备好。”于是他乘车回了慕尼黑。

文克只当他此行是受伯爵夫人差遣，以为是监狱里出了什么事，要了结他们手头正在办的那桩事。

===Original===
“I think, Count Told, that after all it was too risky an experiment. The Countess. …”

“No, no,” cried Told, interrupting him. “I … I … it is on my own account that I’ve come here,” and then he began his story. He told, too, what an extraordinary sensation of heat he had felt at the back of his head, and this must have been the forerunner of misfortune. “Do not be vexed, Dr. Wenk, that I, a stranger, should come to you thus, but I should have had to put an end to myself if I had not been able to confide in someone tonight. May I go on? Well, these powerful rays, that were like red-hot iron at the back of my head, changed gradually to a feeling of well-being throughout my whole body. They seemed to bathe me in pleasant warmth, and I had a feeling that I was somehow saved from something that lay before me, and in this very moment of relief … it happened! In the first half-hour afterwards I denied that it could have done, but when I reached home I realized that the dreadful story was true, and this thing had really happened. There is no getting away from it, either for others or for myself.”

Wenk at once recalled his experience with the old Professor. He was startled. Could it be possible that here too … and he thought of the Countess and of Cara Carozza. He asked Told, “Have you any suspicion at all?”

===Chinese===
“托尔德伯爵，我如今觉得，这终究是一场太过冒险的试验。伯爵夫人她……”

“不，不，”托尔德打断他，喊道。“我……我……我是为我自己的一件事来的。”接着他讲起了自己的经历。他还讲到，自己后脑曾感到一阵异样的灼热，那必定是灾祸的前兆。“文克博士，我这个陌生人这样深夜来找您，请您不要见怪，可今夜我若不能向一个人倾诉，我就只得一死了之了。我可以讲下去吗？好。起初是那一道道强劲的射线，像烧红的烙铁贴在我后脑上，渐渐化作一种通体的舒畅。它们仿佛把我浸在宜人的暖流里，我觉得自己不知怎么躲开了横在眼前的某种东西，而恰恰在这个如释重负的时刻……那件事就发生了！事后头半个钟头里，我一直不肯相信会有这种事，可回到家里，我明白这可怕的事情是真的，它确确实实发生了。无论是别人还是我自己，谁都绕不开它。”

文克立刻回想起自己同那位老教授的那番经历。他吃了一惊。莫非这里头也……他想到了伯爵夫人，又想到了卡拉·卡罗扎。他问托尔德：“您心里有什么怀疑吗？”

===Original===
The Count did not understand the question.

“Any suspicion? What do you mean? That I have been like this before? Ill in this way? No, never!”

“No, a suspicion of any special person who was there?”

“The idea never occurred to me. I can’t understand how anybody else could. … No … I don’t suspect anyone!”

“Was there nobody in the company who did not seem to belong there, who was not quite like the other guests?”

“It was a company of the Privy Councillor’s intimate friends. No, there was nobody!”

===Chinese===
伯爵没有听懂这个问题。

“怀疑？您是什么意思？说我从前也这样过？生过这样的病？不，从来没有！”

“不，是怀疑在场有什么特别的人？”

“我从来没有起过这种念头。我不明白别人怎么会有。……不……我不怀疑任何人！”

“宾客当中就没有一个看着不像属于那里、同其他客人不大一样的人吗？”

“在座的都是枢密顾问的至交好友。不，没有这样的人！”

===Original===
Wenk rejected the idea. Besides, how could there be any connection between the criminal he was seeking and the Count’s act of cheating? It was apparently a momentary mental aberration, a loss of willpower. A subconscious process in a strange and elusive personality which bordered upon morbidity, which thus strove to register a mental impression upon its fellow-players. The Count ought to consult a psychiatrist. It was extraordinary that he should appeal to him, a criminal prosecutor, but he did not put any question to him on this head.

Told became silent, and the lawyer respected his mood. Then suddenly he seemed to pull himself together and said, “I realize that I am keeping you from your night’s rest; I beg you not to be vexed with me. In misfortune it seems as if the mind sinks into a gulf, and the consciousness grasps at the nearest support. You had rung up, and there was some connection between you and … my house, and so. …” He broke off. “But tell me, am I really saying what I want to, or am I talking nonsense? You see, that is the horror of such an experience as mine. It seems as if I shall always require a neurologist to guide my future life.”

===Chinese===
文克打消了这个念头。再说，他要缉拿的那个罪犯，同伯爵的作弊行为之间又能有什么瓜葛呢？这显然是一时的心智失常，是意志力的丧失。是一个近乎病态、奇特而难以捉摸的人格之中的某种潜意识过程，它就这样竭力要把自己心灵的印记刻到同桌牌友身上。伯爵应当去看精神科医生。他竟求到自己这个刑事检察官头上，实在是异乎寻常，但在这一点上，文克没有向他提出任何盘问。

托尔德沉默下来，这位检察官也体谅他的心境。忽然他像是重新振作起来，说道：“我知道我耽误了您夜间的休息；求您不要生我的气。人身处不幸之中，心智仿佛沉入深渊，意识便抓住离得最近的支持。您来过电话，而您和……我的宅子之间又有某种关联，于是……”他顿住了。“可是请告诉我，我讲的真是我心里想讲的吗，还是我在胡言乱语？您瞧，这就是我这种遭遇的可怕之处。往后的日子，我似乎永远需要一位神经科医生来指引了。”

===Original===
“Reassure yourself, Count; you are speaking quite clearly and saying exactly what you want to express. I beg you to make use of me if you can. My calling in some respects borders upon the sphere of the specialist in nerve-disorders; perhaps it goes even further, and at any rate it is bound up with the most mysterious and most speculative part of man’s being. I am very sorry that the occasion that brings you to me is such an unfortunate one, else I should be only too pleased by your visit.”

While Wenk was speaking, desiring to convey that anything out of the common which was mentally or spiritually of an unusual and critical nature was really his concern, the idea occurred to him to enlist the Count’s sympathy in his own aims. Count Told was a man of the world. He belonged to a sphere through which Wenk hoped to be able to endow the life of the nation with nobler qualities and loftier ideals. In the practical necessities which the last few months had forced upon him he had almost neglected the ideal side of the task before him. The events of this night had brought him into unexpected relations with a human being, and he could best serve him by not leaving him alone. He explained his views to the Count.

“They talk of ours as the ‘upper’ class. This description, which certainly has a substratum of truth, must be made a living reality once more. Our class, free of the struggle to obtain a better social status, is more than ever called upon to foster intellectual development and mental gifts. It must cherish these noble qualities in itself and turn them to account for others. Our sphere of politics must be the spiritual one!”

Count Told’s life hitherto had been irreproachable. Both in sentiment and in the externals of life he had shown himself superior, but for lack of serious pursuits to which he could devote himself he had thrown his energies into following up his hobbies, such as the collection of Futurist works of art, for which there was as yet no standard to judge by. He patronized young poets who were at present but a minority and a novelty. They were brought into the light, and the discovery of their powers engaged the serious attention of himself and his like. The struggle to get possession of something new and striking was carried on in this respect just as it was by the profiteers of ordinary wares. … It was not the uneducated rich who devoted themselves to it, but those who sought for their wealth a channel which should return their gold stamped with the impress of beauty and of intellectual superiority. But these fell victims to the age, and their ideas dissolved in hysteria akin to that of a weeping woman whose whole consciousness can hold but one idea. The value of money declined, and in so doing its power over men became all the greater; it seized upon them with ever-growing force, till at last it was like a disease.

===Chinese===
“您放心，伯爵；您讲得十分清楚，说的正是您想表达的意思。若用得上我，请尽管吩咐。就某些方面而言，我的职业同神经科专家的领域本是接壤的；或许还更深一层，无论如何，它总与人性中最神秘、最须臆测的那一部分息息相关。很遗憾，您到我这儿来竟是这样一件不幸的事，要不然，您的来访只会让我喜出望外。”

文克说着这些话，想传达的意思是：凡是心智上、精神上异乎寻常、关乎紧要的不平常之事，本就是他分内的关切——这时他忽然起了一个念头：要争取伯爵同情他自己的抱负。托尔德伯爵是个见过世面的人。他属于这样一个圈子，文克正希望借这个圈子，赋予国民生活更高贵的品质和更崇高的理想。过去几个月迫在眉睫的实际事务，几乎使他忽略了自己使命中理想的一面。今夜的事把他同一个活人带到了意想不到的关联之中，而他能帮此人最好的忙，就是不让他独处。他把自己的见解向伯爵陈述了。

“人们把我们这个阶层称作‘上层’阶级。这个说法固然有其真实的底子，却必须让它重新成为活生生的现实。我们这个阶级，既然免除了为谋取更好社会地位而进行的挣扎，就比以往任何时候都更有义务促进心智的发展与精神的禀赋。它必须在自身中珍视这些高贵的品质，并将其转化为他人之用。我们从事政治的领域，必须是精神的领域！”

托尔德伯爵此前的生平无可指摘。无论在情操上还是在生活的外表上，他都显得高人一等，只是苦于没有可以全身心投入的正经事业，他便把精力尽数倾注在自己的癖好上，比如收藏未来派的艺术品——对这类作品，当时尚无任何评判的标准。他扶持年轻的诗人们，这些人在当时不过是少数，是新鲜事物。他们被带到光亮之下，发掘他们的才具，占据了他和他这一类人郑重其事的关注。在占有某种新奇夺目之物的争斗上，这一行的角力同寻常货物的暴利贩子并无二致。……投身于此的并非不学无术的富人，而是那些要为自己的财富寻一条去路的人，好让流出去的金子带着美与心智优越的印记流回自己手中。可这些人统统成了时代的牺牲品，他们的理念溶化在一种歇斯底里里，就像一个啼哭的女人，整个意识只装得下一个念头。货币贬值了，而它对人的支配力反倒因此愈发强大；它以不断增长的蛮力攫住人们，到末了竟像是一场疾病。

===Original===
Such was the connection between the hobbies of the Count and his like and the age they lived in. The age made use of what was valuable in them. The propagandists of the “new art” were merely stockjobbers, uniting their intellectual ambitions with their speculations. The celebrated “Blue Horses” were to be had for a couple of hundred marks at first. X bought them for eight hundred, and now it was impossible to obtain them for two hundred thousand. It was such anecdotes as these that spurred them on.

For a long time Wenk and Count Told discussed these things. The Count opposed Wenk’s view, having learnt some of the terminology of the artists whose pictures he bought.

“Folks even begin to say,” said Wenk to him on one occasion, “that he speaks as well as a Futurist! And this school begins to affiliate itself with another intellectual movement of our day, which stands on much the same foundations—with the so-called theosophy. You will notice that the Futurist *eo ipso* is also a theosophist or an anthropologist. But it is not because these ideas are really inwardly connected, but because the pursuance of them is united. You will always find nowadays that those who most freely deplore the materialism of our age are those who in private life are most devoted to it. Moreover, in the one case as in the other, it is not always a question of money. Mental and spiritual greed is also an aspect of this age, which exchanges the dominion of one for that of another. Everywhere folks are seeking, seeking eagerly to escape from the misery of the present, and for us mortals there remains but warfare—war against those near us, against those among us, and against ourselves, and it is our class especially which must wage war against ourselves!”

===Chinese===
伯爵和他这一类人的癖好，同他们身处的时代之间的关系便是如此。时代利用了他们身上有价值的东西。“新艺术”的鼓吹者不过是些证券投机贩子，把心智上的抱负同投机买卖结为一体。那幅大名鼎鼎的《蓝马》（“Blue Horses”），起初几百马克便可到手。某君以八百马克买下，如今二十万马克也求之不得。正是这类轶闻刺激着他们。

文克和托尔德伯爵就这些事情谈了很久。伯爵不赞成文克的见解——他买画那些画家的行话，他已学得几分。

“人们甚至开始说，”文克有一回对他说，“他讲起话来活像个未来主义者！而这一派又开始同当今另一场思想运动攀亲结缘——两者立足的地基大同小异——那就是所谓神智学（theosophy）。您会注意到，未来主义者本身（eo ipso）同时也是神智学者或人类学者。但这并非因为这些观念真有什么内在的勾连，而是因为奉行它们的人是同一批。如今您总会发现：最起劲地哀叹时代唯物主义的，恰恰是私生活里最沉溺于唯物主义的人。况且，无论这一种还是那一种，问题并不总在金钱。心智与精神上的贪欲，同样是这个时代的一个侧面，它以一种支配换取另一种支配。到处都有人在寻求，热切地寻求逃避当下的苦难，而我们这些凡人剩下的唯有战争——同亲近之人的战争，同身边之人的战争，同我们自己的战争，而尤其必须对自己开战的，正是我们这个阶级！”

===Original===
Wenk then asked the Count whether he would not spend the night with him, as it was now so late.

The Count answered involuntarily, “Yes, but my wife. …” Then he stopped, looking at Wenk, and his face showed the return of his tormenting thoughts. After a time he began again: “You had caused me to forget my trouble, Dr. Wenk! For this night I have robbed you of, which you have devoted to me so sympathetically, I shall eternally be in your debt. I cannot think how I should have lived through it—alone! Now it seems to be behind me, and I gratefully accept your offer of a bed.”

“How would you like,” said Wenk to Count Told next morning, “for me to see the Privy Councillor and relate your story to him?”

“I should be extremely glad if you would.”

He hesitated as if he wanted to say something more. Wenk noticed it and waited. Then he said, anticipating the other, “I am absolutely at your service. If you have any other wish. …”

===Chinese===
随后文克问伯爵，天已这么晚，何不就在他这里过夜。

伯爵不假思索地答道：“好，可是我的妻子……”随即他打住了，望着文克，脸上又浮起那些折磨人的念头。过了一阵他才重新开口：“您方才让我忘掉了自己的苦恼，文克博士！我夺去了您这一夜，而您如此体谅地把它用在我身上，这份情我永远报答不完。独自一人，真不知我怎么熬得过这一夜！现在它似乎已落在身后了，我感激地接受您留宿的美意。”

第二天早晨，文克对托尔德伯爵说：“您看这样好不好：我去见一见枢密顾问，把您的事讲给他听？”

“您肯去，我高兴极了。”

他迟疑着，似乎还有话要说。文克察觉了，便等着。于是文克抢先说道：“我完全听候您的差遣。您若还有别的什么心愿……”

===Original===
The Count answered quickly, reddening as he spoke, “Yes, I want to speak to my wife. When I think of her I feel … so ashamed!”

“You need not be ashamed!”

“My wife has such a strong and forceful idea of life. It always seemed as if she found our life together a somewhat feeble thing. … I wonder whether it will be possible for her to go on living with a husband who henceforth is but an invalid.”

“I will see her, too,” said Wenk.

===Chinese===
伯爵飞快地答道，说话间涨红了脸：“是的，我想同妻子说话。一想到她，我就觉得……那么羞愧！”

“您用不着羞愧！”

“我的妻子对生活的观念是那样坚强有力。她似乎一向觉得，我们共同的生活有几分孱弱。……我不知道，往后的日子她还能不能同一个从此形同废人的丈夫过下去。”

“我也会去见她。”文克说。

---

===Original===
The Privy Councillor received Wenk at once. As amiably as he could, and in the pleasantly sarcastic tone which distinguished him on all occasions, he told Wenk that his opinion was that the Count had been anxious to adventure something that might raise him in his wife’s esteem. The force of her personality stood far above his own, and he hoped to attain to it by undertaking so hazardous a scheme as to “pack” the cards and win the game. It was not on account of the money, he was convinced of that. He merely wanted to exercise his imagination in adventure as his wife did, but her strength of character always ensured a safe way of escape. For the more feeble personality the first attempt had ended in misfortune. His fantasies had been excited by the current stories of the thieving band of gambling cheats, and the whole affair was mainly due to his neighbour at the table, whose own desire for gain influenced a weaker character and thus paved the way to a society scandal.

“May I inquire, sir, who this neighbour was?”

“Ah, now that I have been so unamiable as to speak of him thus, I cannot possibly betray him. Moreover, he is the blameless head of a household, a professor of physiology.”

“The matter is a great deal more serious than you can have any idea of, sir. The Count spent last night with me, driven to get away from himself. He told me the story, down to the most trifling detail, and I have no reason whatever to suspect that he was misrepresenting the facts. He was absolutely confounded and crushed by the affair. It seemed as if it had been a failure of intellectual force, a sudden inhibition of brain-control. May there not have been someone among your guests who exercised some special influence on the Count?”

===Chinese===
枢密顾问立刻接待了文克。他尽量和颜悦色，带着他每逢场合都自成一格的那种含着愉悦讥诮的口吻，告诉文克：依他之见，伯爵是一心想冒险做成一件能在妻子眼中抬高自己身价的事。她人格的力量远在他之上，他便指望靠“做牌”赢下牌局这样一桩铤而走险的计谋来企及那份力量。这不是为了钱，这一点他深信不疑。他不过是想像妻子那样在冒险中施展自己的想象力，只是她性格的力量总能保得一条安全的退路，而对这个较为孱弱的人格来说，头一回尝试就以不幸告终。近来流传的赌场骗匪盗窃团伙的种种传闻刺激了他的幻想，而整桩事情，主要得归咎于他在牌桌旁的邻座——那人自己的贪欲左右了一个较弱的性格，就此铺开了一条通往社交丑闻的路。

“请问先生，那位邻座是谁？”

“啊，我已经这样不厚道地把他议论了一通，就再不能出卖他了。何况他是个清白无瑕的一家之主，一位生理学教授。”

“事情要比您所能想象的程度严重得多，先生。伯爵昨夜是在我那里过的，他被逼得要逃离自己。他把事情原原本本讲给我听，连最微不足道的枝节都没有漏，我毫无理由怀疑他陈述失实。他叫这件事弄得彻底昏乱、彻底垮掉了。那情形仿佛是心智力量的一次失灵，是大脑控制力的一次猝然抑制。您的客人当中，难道就没有一个人对伯爵施加过什么特殊的影响吗？”

===Original===
“No, there was no Futurist poet or painter among them,” laughed the Privy Councillor.

“I beg you not to consider my questions importunate, Councillor. You really are convinced that no such person was present?”

“No, I do not believe there could have been any. All my guests have been personally known to me for some time. You know what the occasion of our meeting was, don’t you? We were studying the effect of hypnosis on a medium. There were experts, professors, artists of repute, and some personal friends in the company. Then there was a Dr. Mabuse, whom I have not known very long, but whose extraordinary success as a practitioner I respect very highly. He practises psychotherapy. And that reminds me. If Count Told’s state is such as you describe it to be, we might see what he can do for the Count, who is the son of one of my very oldest friends, for I feel a great deal of sympathy for him in his present position. Tell him from me that I strongly advise his seeing Dr. Mabuse, to whom I will give him a letter, for I know his telephone number only.”

Wenk said farewell, and drove from the house to Count Told’s villa at Tutzing, hoping that he might find the Countess there, but he was told by the footman that neither his master nor his mistress had spent the night at home. Then he returned to his own chambers, where the Count, pale and haggard, waited eagerly for him.

“I felt sure of it,” he said disconsolately, when Wenk told him that the Countess had not returned home, “but one always hopes for the impossible. And what about the Privy Councillor?”

===Chinese===
“没有，座中可没有什么未来主义诗人或画家。”枢密顾问笑道。

“请枢密顾问别嫌我的问题纠缠不休。您真的确信没有这样一个人在场？”

“不，我不认为会有。我所有的客人，个个都是我相识已久的。您知道我们那晚聚会的缘由，不是吗？我们是在研究催眠对灵媒的作用。在座的有专家、教授、有声望的艺术家，还有几位私交。另外还有一位马布斯博士，我认识他不久，但他开业行医的非凡成就，我是极为敬重的。他从事心理治疗。这倒提醒了我。如果托尔德伯爵的境况真像您讲的那样，我们不妨看看他能替伯爵做些什么——伯爵是我一位至老之交的儿子，他眼下的处境我很同情。请代我转告他，我力劝他去见一见马布斯博士，我会给他写一封引荐信，因为我知道的只有博士的电话号码。”

文克告辞出来，驱车从枢密顾问府前往图青托尔德伯爵的别墅，指望能在那里找到伯爵夫人，可男仆告诉他，主人和夫人都没有回家过夜。于是他回到自己的寓所，伯爵正等在那里，面色苍白，形容枯槁，望眼欲穿。

“我早就料到了。”听文克说伯爵夫人没有回家，他凄然道，“可人总是盼着不可能的事。枢密顾问那边怎么说？”

===Original===
“I told him exactly what you told me; he had regarded the matter in another light, but not a very serious one. He advises you to consult a neurologist whom he knows, and has given me this letter to him for you.”

“Dr. Mabuse,” read the Count. “Why, he was at the party last night.”

“Shall I go to him?” suggested Wenk.

“No, Doctor, I really must not rely on your kindness any longer. I must pull myself together and deal with this crisis in my life. I will call up Dr. Mabuse on the telephone, as we have his number there. I will do it from here, if I may.”

===Chinese===
“我把您对我讲的，一五一十讲给了他；他对这件事另有看法，不过没看得太重。他劝您去请教一位他认识的神经科医生，还托我把这封写给医生的信带给您。”

“马布斯博士。”伯爵念道。“咦，昨天夜里的聚会，他不是也在场吗。”

“要不要我替您去一趟？”文克提议道。

“不必了，博士，我实在不能再仰仗您的好意了。我必须振作起来，亲自料理我人生中的这场危机。我来给马布斯博士打电话——他的号码我们有。如果可以，我就在您这儿打。”

===Original===
“Dr. Mabuse,” said the Count at the telephone, “you were present at Privy Councillor Wendel’s party last night when I had the misfortune to. …”

“That is so.”

“I want your professional help. The Councillor gave me a letter of introduction to you. Can I bring it at once?”

The other voice answered harshly, “No. I do not see patients except in their own homes. What is your address? Expect me there tomorrow morning at 11 a.m. Repeat the appointment; what time is fixed?”

“Eleven a.m.,” said the Count, thoroughly terrified, and then he left Wenk’s house.

===Chinese===
“马布斯博士，”伯爵在电话里说，“昨天晚上枢密顾问温德尔的聚会上您也在场，当时我不幸……”

“正是。”

“我需要您的专业救助。枢密顾问给了我引荐信来见您。我能立刻把信给您送去吗？”

对方的声音生硬地答道：“不行。我只在病人自己家中看病。您的住址是哪里？明天上午 11 点我到您那儿去。把约定复述一遍；定的是几点？”

“上午 11 点。”伯爵说，吓得魂飞魄散，随即离开了文克的寓所。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
The Countess opened her eyes on something black, intersected with red circles and rays. All around her was dark and strange. Somewhere on high a faint light was glimmering in the room in which she lay. She was on a sofa, fully dressed. She had never seen the room before, and all its contents were unfamiliar. She lay there, trying to recall what had happened, but she found it impossible. One moment alone stood out in her memory: the recollection of the grey eyes of that Dr. Mabuse who had told her of tigers﻿—eyes which had held her as with the clutch of a beast whose claws ran blood. She recalled something like a spring in the air, a hold that left her breathless, feeling as if the very heart were being torn from her body and she was sinking, sinking down into a gulf.

Suddenly a door opened; where, exactly, she did not know, for she felt rather than perceived it. She was expecting something, but her imagination flowed back upon herself and she waited.

After a time a voice spoke out of the semidarkness: “You are awake. Would you like the light?”

It was a voice which seemed to the Countess at the first moment like the trump of doom, but in an instant this sensation left her and she felt incredulous. How came that voice into this mysterious obscurity? It was the very last she could have expected to hear. She shrank terrified within herself, and it seemed as if her whole body gradually stiffened. There was a sound in her throat, but she was not conscious of it. She stretched her hands in front of her as if warding off a danger. Then suddenly the room was flooded with light.
===Chinese===
伯爵夫人睁开眼睛，看见一片黑色，上面交错着红色的圆圈与辐射线。四周一片昏暗而陌生。高处的什么地方，一点微光在她躺卧的房间里闪烁。她躺在一张沙发上，和衣而卧。这房间她从未见过，屋里的一切陈设她都认不得。她躺在那里，竭力回想发生过的事，却发现无从想起。记忆里只有一瞬间独自清晰地留存下来：那个对她讲过老虎的马布斯博士，他那双灰色的眼睛——那双眼睛攫住了她，如同一只利爪见血的野兽死死抓着不放。她依稀记起空气里仿佛有一记弹跃，一个令她透不过气的擒抱，只觉得心仿佛要从躯体里被生生撕走，她往下沉、往下沉，沉进一道深渊。

忽然一扇门开了；究竟开在哪里，她并不知道，因为她与其说是察觉到，不如说是感觉到了。她在期待着什么，可她的想象又倒退回她自己身上，于是她等着。

过了一阵，半暗中有一个声音说话了：“您醒了。要灯吗？”

这声音听在伯爵夫人耳中，第一刻宛如末日审判的号角；可转瞬间这感觉便离她而去，她只觉得难以置信。这声音怎么会进入这神秘的幽暗之中？这是她最不可能料到会听见的声音。她惊恐地在心里缩作一团，仿佛整个身体渐渐僵直。她喉咙里发出一点声响，自己却没有察觉。她把双手伸在身前，像要挡开什么危险。随后，房间骤然被灯光淹没。

===Original===
Dr. Mabuse closed the door and approached the sofa. He said: “The situation is exactly what I desired. I have brought you home!”

At these words the Countess regained control of herself. She rose from the sofa, though she felt faintness stealing over her. What did this man want with her?﻿—but indeed she knew what he wanted. He was a tiger, intent on his prey. Nevertheless, she asked him, “What do you want?”

“I have just told you,” he answered curtly.
===Chinese===
马布斯博士关上门，走向沙发。他说：“局势正合我意。我把您带回家来了！”

听了这话，伯爵夫人重新控制住了自己。她从沙发上站起身，尽管一阵晕眩正悄悄向她袭来。这个男人想拿她怎么样？——可其实她知道他想要什么。他是一头老虎，一心扑在猎物上。尽管如此，她还是问道：“你想要什么？”

“我方才已经告诉过您了，”他简慢地答道。

===Original===
“And now?”

“You will remain with me.”

“I will not!” cried the Countess. “I will go and help my husband!” And at that moment she recollected clearly what had happened. Her husband had cheated at cards. Oh, merciful Heaven, she thought, how could such a thing have happened? She knew so well how utterly foreign to his nature such a thing would be. What misery, what despair, what depths of misfortune! And she herself had been with the woman who was an accomplice in Hull’s murder, and had succumbed to her power. Everything seemed to swim before her eyes, and she saw her husband’s unconscious act through a mist of blood.

She heard the voice of the man beside her, stern and threatening: “You will not? Have I asked you whether you will?”

He had not asked the tiger or the buffalo. Was he to ask a weak woman? Was he to ask *her*? She, too, was his prey. This idea filled her with a sort of voluptuous dread. She was the prey of the strongest man whom she had ever known. How could she defend herself? He had simply taken her. Were there men whose will was strong enough to give them possession of a woman if they never even touched her?
===Chinese===
“那现在呢？”

“您要留在我身边。”

“我不留下！”伯爵夫人喊道。“我要去帮我的丈夫！”就在这一刻，她清清楚楚地回想起了发生过的一切。她的丈夫在牌局中作了弊。哦，仁慈的上天，她想，这种事怎么可能发生？她深知这种事与他的天性何其格格不入。何等的悲惨，何等的绝望，何等深不见底的不幸！而她自己，先前竟一直同那个在赫尔谋杀案中充当从犯的女人在一起，还被她的魔力所降服。一切都在她眼前浮动起来，她隔着一层血雾，看见了丈夫那无意识中犯下的举动。

她听见身旁那个男人的声音，冷峻而带着威胁：“您不留下？我可曾问过您愿不愿意？”

他可不曾问过老虎或野牛。难道还要问一个弱女子？难道要问*她*？她也一样，是他的猎物。这个念头让她充满了一种近乎沉醉的恐惧。她成了她此生所知的最强大的男人的猎物。她怎能自卫？他索性就把她劫走了。世上难道真有那样的男人，意志强大到甚至从不碰一个女人一根指头，便能占有她？

===Original===
“How did I come here?” she asked.

“We have something more important than that to talk about,” he answered in a cold, harsh voice that made her tremble. “How are you going to adapt yourself to the situation?”

“I will never adapt myself to it!” she cried; and it seemed as if instruments of torture were engraven on her brain.

“That is not the question!” answered the voice, falling like a stone, falling, lying, lying for thousands of years. “The question is, are you going to remain with me of your own free will or as my prisoner?”

The Countess, now fully alive to the force and compulsion which threatened her, strove to collect her wits. She looked, listened, considered, and slowly began to ask herself, “Shall it be cunning or resistance?” After a time she answered, “You cannot keep me as your prisoner in Munich.”
===Chinese===
“我是怎么到这儿来的？”她问。

“我们还有比这更要紧的事要谈，”他用一种令她战栗的、冷硬的嗓音答道。“您打算如何让自己适应眼下的处境？”

“我永远也不会适应它！”她喊道；仿佛有种种刑具镌刻进了她的脑海。

“问题不在那儿！”那声音答道，像一块石头坠落，坠着，横陈，一横陈便是千万年。“问题是：您是自愿留在我身边，还是做我的囚徒？”

伯爵夫人此刻完全看清了那威胁着她的强力与胁迫，竭力镇定心神。她看，她听，她思量，慢慢开始问自己：“是使巧计，还是硬抗？”过了一阵她答道：“在慕尼黑，你没法把我当囚徒关着。”

===Original===
Mabuse replied roughly, “How do you know that you are in Munich?”

“Have you run away with me?” she cried.

“I am not a gorilla.”

“Who *are* you? What is your name?”

“Whatever you like to call me!”

“Then I shall call you a gorilla,” she was about to retort angrily, but it seemed as if her tongue refused to utter the hateful name. It would not be expressed, and something within her appeared to change and soften the situation, to promise allurement in the distance and play around her fancy like busy little elves of night. Yet something in her conscience seemed to tell her that there could be no ease for her while her husband was cast down by misfortune and her own future was so uncertain, and she spoke defiantly, “What do you want with me?”
===Chinese===
马布斯粗暴地答道：“您怎么知道您这是在慕尼黑？”

“你把我拐跑了？”她喊道。

“我又不是大猩猩。”

“你*究竟*是谁？你叫什么名字？”

“随你想叫我什么！”

“那我就叫你大猩猩，”她正想愤然反唇相讥，可她的舌头仿佛拒绝说出这个可憎的名字。它就是吐不出来，而且她内心深处似乎有什么东西开始改变这局面、软化这局面，在远处许下诱惑，像一群忙碌的小夜精灵绕着她的遐想嬉戏。然而她的良知似乎又在告诉她：只要丈夫被厄运击倒、自己的前途又这般未卜，她便休想安宁。于是她抗声道：“你想拿我怎么样？”

===Original===
But the man looked at her long and steadily, and she felt as if her question floated away, minute and unconsidered as a trifle on the mighty ocean. The ocean was the breast of the man before her. There was no breast more mighty or powerful; it represented what her inmost being and her secret desires had yearned after. To rest upon it, to rest﻿ ﻿… as in the jungle.﻿ ﻿…

Then, after he had looked at her in a silence fraught with meaning, the man spoke. “The human race is too contemptible and inferior to give its men and women such force as nature has provided for its other creations; that the one sex should see, know and belong to the other as naturally and inevitably as light belongs to day!”

“You mean to say,” said the Countess hesitatingly, “that you love me, and that﻿—is why you have brought me here!”
===Chinese===
可这男人久久地、目不转睛地注视着她，她感到自己的问题漂走了，渺小而不值一顾，像浩瀚大洋上的一点屑末。大洋就是眼前这个男人的胸膛。再没有比这更雄浑、更强大的胸膛了；它体现着她内心深处、她那些隐秘欲望所渴慕的一切。倚在上面休憩吧，休憩……如在丛林之中。……

随后，在一场意味深长的沉默中注视过她之后，这男人开口了。“人类太可鄙、太低劣了，竟不肯给它的男人和女人这样一种力量——大自然已把这种力量赐予了自己的其他造物——好让一个性别看见另一个性别、认识另一个性别、属于另一个性别，就像光属于白昼一样自然而必然！”

“你是想说，”伯爵夫人迟疑地说道，“你爱慕我，而这——就是你把我带到这里来的缘故！”

===Original===
“I desire you, and that﻿—for me﻿—is stronger than love! You are here because there is no resisting my desires. You may reign as a queen, in this breast, and in my kingdom of Citopomar in Southern Brazil. A queen ruling the virgin forest, its savage beasts, savage and civilized human beings, valleys, rocks and heights. Who in this miserable continent can offer you more?”

“No one!” said the Countess, under the secret dominion of the dream which had so rapidly begun its twofold play in her spirit.

“You have decided, then, to remain of your own free will?” asked Mabuse.

The Countess once more realized her position. She shrank from him, and tried to shelter herself behind the ottoman. She closed her lips firmly, but at the same time she was torn by a conflict within; she desired to go, and at the same time she felt a yearning in some part of her being to remain and to submit.
===Chinese===
“我渴望你，而这——对我而言——比爱更强烈！你之所以在这里，是因为我的欲望无从抗拒。你可以像女王一般君临，君临这副胸膛，也君临我在巴西南部的锡托波马尔王国。一位女王，统治处女林、林中的猛兽、野蛮与文明的人类、山谷、岩石与峰巅。在这悲惨的大陆上，还有谁能给你更多？”

“没有谁！”伯爵夫人说——那场梦已在她精神里如此迅速地开始了它双重的戏耍，她此刻正处在它隐秘的支配之下。

“这么说，您已经决定自愿留下了？”马布斯问。

伯爵夫人又一次认清了自己的处境。她从他面前退缩，想躲到那张软榻背后去。她紧紧闭住双唇，可与此同时，她又为内心的交战所撕裂：她想要走，却又同时感到生命的某一处萌生渴望，想留下，想屈从。

===Original===
He continued: “If it were like this: a man and a woman see each other for the first time, and in the first glance that they exchange they say to themselves, ‘There is nothing left to me of what I was. Everything has vanished like a dissolving view, and thou, the only one, thou alone remainest. It is inconceivable that there should be a single heartbeat that does not belong to thee.’ It is as if all the races in all the ages had united their powers in these two beings, instead of giving each individual a beggarly portion of it. What a puny creature is man, but if it were the other way with the race he would be the image of God and of creation!”

The Countess felt as if a sudden force was stretching her between two poles. She knew that she herself resembled both of them, and yet they were unlike each other. “Must I proceed from the one extreme to the other?” she asked herself, feeling very weary, “or can I remain hovering between them, calm and comfortable, in the warm rays of a sunshine that steals over me so pleasantly?”

There was always the inclination to follow the extraordinary and unusual, that she might feel wherein she was most akin to humanity, and yet most herself when surrounded by what did not belong to or affect her. And over her spirit there stole again a feeling as of Paradise, the scent of the Elysian Fields, the songs of enchanting sirens, and it seemed as if the limits of her physical nature were dissolved and, leaving her narrow horizon behind her, she floated as if in ether. “What is happening to me?” she thought, as, struggling with herself, she advanced yet nearer to the vision of Paradise which swam before her eyes.
===Chinese===
他继续说道：“倘若是这样的：一个男人和一个女人初次相见，在他们交换的第一瞥里，他们对自己说：‘我从前的一切都不复属于我了。一切都像一帧正在消融的幻灯景象般散尽，而你，唯一的人，唯有你留存。难以想象，竟还会有一下心跳不属于你。’这就仿佛历世历代所有的种族把他们的一切力量联合起来，倾注在这两个人身上，而不是给每个个体分派一份寒伧的零头。人是一件多么孱弱的造物啊，可倘若人类换作这般光景，他便会是上帝与创世的肖像！”

伯爵夫人感到，仿佛有一股骤至的力量正把她往两极之间拉开。她知道自己与这两极都相像，可这两极又彼此不像。“我必须从这一个极端走到那一个极端吗？”她问自己，只觉十分倦怠，“还是我能悬停在这两极之间，平静而安逸，浸在那一缕缕如此惬意地悄悄漫上我全身的温暖阳光里？”

她心里始终有一种追随超凡越常之物的倾向，好让她感到自己究竟在何处与人类最相亲近，却又在被与她无关、不触及她的事物环绕时，最成为她自己。一种仿佛乐园的感觉又悄悄漫上她的精神——极乐原野的芬芳、迷人塞壬的歌声——她肉身的界限仿佛消融了，她把狭窄的地平线抛在身后，像漂浮在以太之中。“我这是怎么了？”她想道，一面与自己挣扎，一面又向那浮现在眼前的乐园幻景更趋近了一步。

===Original===
The eyes of this strange, compelling being flooded her like a spring season of sunshine. He stood high as the clouds above her. The sunshine overpowered the earth, but the earth yielded itself gladly to its rays. Was that the secret of her nature too? she herself asked. The season, now wild and stormy, advanced like a monster endued with power, from beyond the horizon, over the forests, rivers, cities, mountains, looking neither to right nor left and penetrating to the very heart of things. “If this man overcomes me in such a way, fills my whole being, is that indeed Paradise? Is it for me completion, redemption, deliverance? Is this my second nature which I have never yet dared to follow?”

She desired to resist, but a subtle and enchanting feebleness stole over her, and she felt herself like a March field, dark and yielding. A jackdaw was screeching in it, but somewhere or other a thrush was singing behind her. And the screeching jackdaw and the singing thrush were snatching at a maggot, a living maggot in the bark of the tree, and even the bark of the tree seemed to be awaiting and expectant, and there was a murmuring sound in its cells. And the thrush mounted high into the air, singing triolets born of the spirit of the soil.﻿ ﻿…

Woman was the thrush, and at the same time she was the maggot. She yielded herself to the destroying force, and knew it not for the tumult in her blood. She was stirred in her inmost being, plunged into the depths and soared again, intangible as an air-bubble.﻿ ﻿… Above her rose the call of the man like the rustling sound of the summer, calling the sap to rise, to push forward the growth which should end in a glorious harvest.
===Chinese===
这个奇特而慑人的存在，那双眼睛如一季春日的阳光，将她淹没。他高高地伫立在她头顶之上，高及云霄。阳光制服了大地，而大地也欣然把自己交付给它的光芒。这也是她天性的秘密吗？她自己问自己。这个如今狂野而暴烈的季节，像一头被赋予了伟力的怪物，从地平线之外压将过来，越过森林、河流、城市、群山，左右不顾，直抵万物的至深内核。“倘若这个男人这样降伏了我，充盈了我的整个存在，那果真是乐园吗？对我而言，那是圆满，是救赎，是解脱吗？这是我那从未敢追随的第二重天性吗？”

她想抗拒，可一种微妙而迷人的软弱悄悄爬遍她的全身，她觉得自己像一片三月的田野，黝黑而顺从。一只寒鸦在田间聒噪，可不知在什么地方，一只歌鸫正在她身后啼啭。聒噪的寒鸦和歌唱的歌鸫都在争抢同一条蛆，一条活的蛆，藏在树皮里；连那树皮似乎也在等待、也在期待，它的细胞里响起一片窸窣的低语。歌鸫高高飞上天际，唱着一支支从泥土之魂中诞生的三韵句。……

女人是那只歌鸫，同时她又是那条蛆。她把自己交付给那毁灭之力，却因血液中的喧嚣而无从知晓。她最内在的深处被搅动了，坠入渊底，又再度高翔，虚缈得像一粒气泡。……男人的呼唤在她头顶升起，像夏日里的一片萧萧之声，唤着树液上升，催着那终将以辉煌丰收告终的生长。

## XIV / 十四

===Original===
Mabuse’s visit to Count Told duly took place. “Your neurosis is not by any means an unusual one,” said the doctor. “It will be cured when you regain control of yourself, but it will become worse and finally be incurable if you don’t succeed in doing that. It is a precursor of *dementia praecox*. For professional reasons I shall treat you in your own home, as I do all my patients. I make one condition, however. As long as you are undergoing treatment you must not leave the house or see anyone who recalls your former life.”

Told was stupefied by the power and authority which this doctor assumed towards him. Timid and shrinking by nature, downcast by what had occurred, he did not venture to make any objection, and from the very first moment he stood in absolute awe of him.

When Mabuse left the villa, in which he had seen many things which revealed the life the Count and his wife had led, he said to himself, “He must be got rid of if she even mentions him again.”

The doctor was in a highly excitable and savage state. The meeting with this man, who had so long called her his own, had fired his blood and inflamed him as if he had been a bull in the arena transfixed by a javelin. He unconsciously lowered his head as if for attack, and his imagination ran riot, thirsting to satisfy his hate and revenge. It seemed to him as if a tumour had suddenly burst within him, scattering its evil and offensive discharge everywhere, and he allowed himself to bathe in its stream.

===Chinese===
马布斯博士对托尔德伯爵的造访如期进行。“您这种神经官能症绝不是什么罕见的病症，”医生说道。“等您重新控制住自己，它就会痊愈；可要是您做不到这一点，它就会日益加重，最终不可救药。它是*早发性痴呆*（dementia praecox）的先兆。出于职业上的考虑，我将像对待我所有病人那样，在您自己家中为您治疗。不过我有一个条件：只要治疗还在进行，您就不得离开这所宅子，也不得见任何会让您想起往日生活的人。”

这位医生在他面前摆出的那种权势与威严，把托尔德震慑住了。他生性怯懦退缩，又因遭遇变故而消沉，不敢提出任何异议，而且从最初一刻起，他就对他怀着彻头彻尾的敬畏。

马布斯离开别墅时——在这座宅子里，他已看到许多足以揭示伯爵夫妇昔日生活的东西——暗自说道：“只要她再提起他一次，这个人就必须被除掉。”

医生正处于一种极度亢奋、凶蛮暴戾的状态。同这个长久以来把她称作自己所有的男人会面，点燃了他的血液，把他刺激得像斗牛场上被标枪刺穿的公牛一样。他不知不觉地低下头，仿佛就要发起冲击，他的想象横冲直撞，渴望一偿仇恨与报复。在他看来，仿佛体内有一颗肿瘤骤然迸裂，把邪恶刺鼻的秽物溅得到处都是，而他竟任凭自己浸浴在这股浊流之中。

===Original===
When he reentered his house he went straight to the room in which the Countess was confined. It was in a secluded corner of the villa. The only light there was came from a round window in its arched and richly decorated dome.

The Countess arose as he came in. She was white as the sheets upon her bed. She went towards him, saying, “Something happened to me in the night—something of which I was wholly unconscious. What have you been doing to me?”

“Nothing but what you allowed me to do!”

Then the woman trembled so that she sank down to the ground, raising her glance to his like an animal that has been shot down, and crying in horror, “You devil! oh, you devil!”

===Chinese===
他重新回到自己的宅邸，径直走进囚禁着伯爵夫人的那个房间。房间在别墅一个僻静的角落里，屋里仅有的一点光亮，来自装饰华丽的拱顶上的一扇圆窗。

他进来时，伯爵夫人站起身来。她苍白得像她床上的被单。她朝他走去，说道：“昨夜在我身上发生了一件事——一件我全然没有知觉的事。你对我做了什么？”

“无非是你容许我做的事！”

于是那女人抖个不停，瘫倒在地上，抬眼望着他，像一头被击倒的牲畜，惊恐万状地喊道：“你这个魔鬼！哦，你这个魔鬼！”

===Original===
“That name pleases me,” said Mabuse. “I consider it flattering. It is, without your realizing it, a caress. Next time you will call me Lucifer, for I shall bring you light!”

The Countess, lying in a heap on the floor, broke into passionate sobs, crying in the midst of her anguish, “Where is my husband?”

Then she saw that at the question Mabuse made a gesture, so indifferent and trivial that she felt her painful anguished appeal was no more than a drop of dew vanishing in the sand, and as hopeless to look for. And her downcast broken heart asked itself whether this man could indeed be so powerful that everything went down before his will—that what she and others before her had been must be brought to nought?

===Chinese===
“这个名字让我中意，”马布斯说。“我认为这是一种恭维。你自己没有意识到，它其实是一次爱抚。下一次你会叫我路西法（Lucifer），因为我要带给你光明！”

瘫作一团躺在地板上的伯爵夫人迸发出激烈的抽泣，在极度的痛苦中哭喊：“我的丈夫在哪儿？”

随即她看见，听到这个问题，马布斯做了个手势，那般冷漠、那般轻慢，让她觉得自己这声痛楚哀切的恳求，不过是消逝在沙中的一滴露水，再寻也寻不回来。她那低垂破碎的心暗自问道：这个人难道当真强大到一切都得在他的意志面前低头——强大到她本人、以及她之前的那些人曾经是过的一切，都必须被化为乌有？

===Original===
Once again she must yield herself to the twofold stream within. It bore the most secret and hitherto unsuspected currents along with it, and her tortured imagination gave them full play. Must not that which her blood sought to reveal to her be true? She could not separate herself from this new world of feeling. Resist and inveigh against it as she might, she could yet not tear it from her.

The man stood silent before her, and his silence seemed to threaten her. She thought that by a word of her own she could destroy this threatening attitude of his, but she found no power to say anything more than to repeat helplessly, “Where is my husband?” Then Mabuse, silently and roughly, turned away.

When he had left her, leaving behind nothing but the impression of his dominating will, she felt as if she missed something in the room. She would have preferred him to stand there still, and her sense of isolation passed all bounds, overwhelming her. A bottomless abyss opened before her, and phantom figures made appealing gestures. But she could not cast herself down; she hung on to one slender rootlet; she knew it to be the tiny remnant that remained to her of her former life. She wished too, that even this rootlet might be torn adrift, for she would rather have faced death in its entirety than hover over the void.

===Chinese===
她不得不再一次听凭体内那股双重激流摆布。激流裹挟着最隐秘、迄今从未被察觉的潜流，她备受折磨的想象力任其充分施展。她的血液极力要向她昭示的，难道会不是真的？她已无法把自己从这个新的感情世界里分离出来。任凭她怎样抗拒它、痛斥它，她终究不能把它从自己身上撕下来。

那男人默默站在她面前，他的沉默仿佛带着威胁。她想，只消自己说一句话，就能摧毁他这咄咄逼人的姿态，可她再也找不出别的话，只能无可奈何地重复：“我的丈夫在哪儿？”于是马布斯默不作声、粗鲁地转过身去。

他走后，除了他那统辖一切的意志留下的印迹，什么也没留下；她却觉得房间里像缺了点什么。她倒宁愿他仍旧站在那里，她的孤寂之感漫无边际，把她整个人淹没了。无底的深渊在她面前张开，幽灵般的身影朝她做着哀告的手势。可她不能纵身跳下去；她攀着一根纤细的须根；她知道，那是往日生活残留给她的最后一点残余。她甚至巴望这根须根也被扯断，因为她宁可直面完整的死亡，也不愿悬在虚空之上。

---

===Original===
Mabuse went backwards and forwards in his room. He was like a caged beast, caught between his rage for vengeance and lust of domination on the one hand and the resistance raised to the attainment of his goal on the other. That which baffled him was such a trifle, merely the memories binding a wife to the hours she has passed with her husband, either alone or in company, and because it was so slight an obstacle, the desire to remove and destroy it utterly possessed him with fury such as he had not known till now.

Spoerri entered. He was dressed as a soldier. “What is that for?” asked Mabuse morosely, but he did not wait for an answer, and asked about George’s movements.

“He is at the villa in Schachen. He is very cautious, and does not go out.”

“What is he doing there?”

“At night he helps to bring the store of cocaine under the summerhouse into Switzerland. I have found something fresh which they are ready to take there. Ether.”

===Chinese===
马布斯在自己的房间里来回踱步。他像一头笼中困兽，一边是复仇的狂怒与统治的贪欲，一边是横在目的面前的抗拒，他被夹在当中。叫他一筹莫展的不过是件小事——无非是把一个妻子拴在她同丈夫共度的那些时辰上的回忆，无论那是两人独处还是有旁人在场；可正因为这障碍如此微末，将它拔除净尽的欲望才攫住了他，激起他至今从未体验过的狂怒。

施佩里走了进来。他一身士兵装束。“这是干什么用的？”马布斯阴沉地问，但他不等回答，便问起乔治的动向。

“他在沙兴的别墅里。他十分谨慎，不出门。”

“他在那儿干什么？”

“夜里他帮忙把藏在凉亭底下的一批可卡因运进瑞士。我又发现了一样新东西，他们正乐意往那边运。乙醚。”

===Original===
“What is the ether for?”

“Folks are beginning to take it.”

“Who? What folks? Where?”

“Our folks, in Switzerland!”

“*Your* folks; how many have you?”

“We can get it to the others!”

===Chinese===
“乙醚是做什么用的？”

“有人开始服用它了。”

“谁？什么人？在哪儿？”

“咱们自己人，在瑞士！”

“*你*的人；你手底下有多少？”

“咱们可以把它弄到其他人手里去！”

===Original===
“That reminds me of the girls you were sending to Switzerland, to speed up the smuggling of salvarsan. I don’t want to hear anything about business matters. You understand, nothing.”

“I won’t say any more about it.”

“Perhaps, Spoerri, there’ll be no need for that sort of thing any more!”

Then a hoarse cry was uttered by Spoerri. “Oh, Doctor, Citopomar! Is it to be soon now?”

===Chinese===
“这倒叫我想起你先前往瑞士送的那些姑娘，为的是加快砷凡纳明的走私。生意上的事我什么也不要听。你明白，什么都不要讲。”

“这事我不再多说一个字。”

“也许，施佩里，往后再也用不着干这种营生了！”

这时施佩里发出一声嘶哑的喊叫。“哦，博士，锡托波马尔！如今就快了吗？”

===Original===
“We’ll drink to it, Spoerri, we’ll drink to it. I don’t know. Let’s drink to the shepherd boy with eighty-six thousand marks yearly income!”

“Oh, what have I out of it? Do I not always invest it again in one or other of your enterprises, Doctor?”

“Because it brings you in ten percent more there than it would in an insurance society. Shall I have to use force, shepherd? Drink, I say!”

Spoerri was the first to fall from his chair. He lay on the floor, disorder all around him, gazing sadly at his master. He lay there like a dog about to die, knowing that he could no longer protect his master’s life.

===Chinese===
“为它干杯吧，施佩里，为它干杯。我不知道。为那个年收入八万六千马克的牧童干杯吧！”

“哦，我能从中落着什么好处？博士，我不是总把它又投进您的这项或那项营生里去吗？”

“因为钱投在那儿，比放进保险公司能多给你生百分之十的利。要我来硬的吗，牧童？喝，我叫你喝！”

施佩里第一个从椅子上栽倒下来。他躺在地板上，四下杯盘狼藉，悲伤地望着自己的主人。他躺在那儿，像一条行将死去的狗，知道自己再也护卫不了主人的性命。

===Original===
Mabuse, tottering so that he was obliged to hold on to the edge of the table to save himself from falling, stuttered: “Spoerri, do you think there is anyone whose will is strong enough for him to kill someone else without even touching him?”

But Spoerri did not understand him. He looked up at his master with glassy eyes, stupid yet faithful, troubled and sick.

“*I* can! and I shall do it, too! … Sleep,” he said suddenly, and rising, he spurned the other with his foot. He took a few steps forward, having to seek support. Then he pulled himself together, and his willpower was held as it were within an iron vice. Rigidly upright, without a sign of swaying, inflamed with drink and in a state of exaltation, he went into the room the Countess occupied and remained with her without saying a word. And from that hour of humiliation this woman, too, acknowledged his supremacy. She forgot her past, forgot her very self, and submitted willingly to her master.

===Chinese===
马布斯也摇摇晃晃，只得抓住桌沿才没有摔倒。他结结巴巴地说：“施佩里，你可相信世上有这样的人——意志强大到不必碰对方一下，就能置人于死地？”

可施佩里听不懂他的话。他抬起头望着主人，眼神呆滞，愚蠢却忠诚，惶惑而病态。

“*我*就行！而且我一定要做到！……睡！”他忽然说道，随即站起身，一脚把对方踹开。他朝前走了几步，不得不寻个支撑。然后他打起精神，意志力仿佛被夹在一只铁的老虎钳里。他挺得笔直，纹丝不晃，酒意烧身、亢奋已极，走进伯爵夫人住的那个房间，一言不发地留在她那里。而从那屈辱的时刻起，这个女人也承认了他的至高地位。她忘掉了自己的过去，忘掉了自身，心甘情愿地顺从了自己的主人。

---

===Original===
During the night Mabuse started for Lake Constance. Just as he was approaching the villa at Schachen, having extinguished his lights, he narrowly missed a collision with the engine of the steamroller which was standing in the road a few yards from the garden entrance. It was directly in front of him when he applied his brakes, and he therefore did not drive up to the house, but continued along the road for another kilometre, then left the car standing and went back to the house by the shore-path.

“Why did you not tell me the steamroller was here?” he asked George imperiously. “Even a matchbox lying out in the street might betray us. Go and fetch the car, quickly! It is on the highroad near Wasserburg. Put it away and come straight back here.”

===Chinese===
夜里，马布斯动身前往博登湖。他熄了车灯，正驶近沙兴的别墅时，险些撞上那台停在路上、离花园入口只有几码远的蒸汽压路机的机身。他踩下刹车时，它正好横在眼前，于是他没有把车开到宅子跟前，而是沿路又驶出一公里，把车撂下，再从湖岸小径走回宅子。

“你怎么不告诉我压路机在这儿？”他专横地质问乔治。“哪怕一只火柴盒撂在街上，都可能出卖我们。去把汽车取回来，快！它停在瓦瑟堡（Wasserburg）附近的公路上。把它藏好，立刻回到这儿来。”

---

===Original===
Next morning the telephone bell woke Wenk from his sleep. “News from the steamroller,” he heard, and was at once wide awake.

“Yes, yes; please go on.”

“Last night about two o’clock a car arrived, and pulled up directly in front of our engine, then drove on again. As it was driving without lights, I ordered Schmied to follow on a bicycle. He found it about a kilometre further on, left alone by the roadside, and came back at once to report. I stole into the garden of the villa, but the dog began barking and I went outside round the shore. I saw a man come from the direction of the lake and go into the house. When Schmied and I went back to find the car it had vanished. There is nothing to be noticed this morning!”

“Thanks. You can expect me there today.”

===Chinese===
第二天清晨，电话铃声把文克从睡梦中唤醒。“压路机方面来的消息，”他听见这一句，顿时睡意全消。

“好，好；请讲下去。”

“昨夜两点钟左右来了一辆汽车，正停在我们压路机跟前，随后又开走了。因为它没开车灯，我派施米德（Schmied）骑自行车跟了上去。他在约莫一公里外发现了它，孤零零撂在路边，便立刻回来报告。我潜进别墅的花园，可是狗叫了起来，我只得到外头绕着湖岸走。我看见一个男人从湖那边走来，进了宅子。等我和施米德回去找那辆车，它已经无影无踪。今天早上什么异常也没有！”

“谢谢。今天你们就能见到我到场。”

---

===Original===
An hour before this conversation took place on the telephone, while still dark, Mabuse left the villa. He was wearing women’s clothes and was rowed across to Nonnenhorn. A motorboat approached, and in it was a fisherman returning from a smuggling expedition. Mabuse accosted him, but the man said he was in a hurry, for he must take his fish home. Then Mabuse at one bound sprang into his boat, overpowered him, threw him down and gagged him, and then transferred him to the rowing-boat. He took off his female garments, beneath which he was dressed as a fisherman, and making a wide detour, he returned to shore and went to the farm where in a barn the car was concealed. George was lying in it asleep.

After a long conversation with George, Mabuse turned and drove back into Würtemberg, while George returned to Schachen.

Mabuse wanted to get to Stuttgart. His agents there had telephoned the previous day that a patient wanted to consult him. That meant that they had got hold of a rich man worth plucking.

===Chinese===
这场电话交谈进行之前一小时，天色未明，马布斯离开了别墅。他穿着女人的衣裳，由人划船把他渡到对岸的农嫩霍恩（Nonnenhorn）。一艘摩托艇驶近，艇上是个跑完一趟走私营生的渔夫。马布斯招呼他，可那人说自己赶时间，必须把鱼送回家。于是马布斯一个箭步蹿进他的艇里，把他制服、撂倒、堵上嘴，然后把人挪到那条划艇上。他脱下女人的衣裳——底下早穿好了渔夫的行头——绕了个大弯回到岸边，走向那座农庄，汽车就藏在庄里的谷仓中。乔治正躺在车里睡觉。

同乔治长谈之后，马布斯调头驱车返回符腾堡，乔治则回到沙兴。

马布斯要去斯图加特。他在那里的爪牙前一天来过电话，说有位病人想找他求诊。这就是说，他们又网住了一个值得宰上一宰的有钱人。

===Original===
While Mabuse was sitting at the gaming-table that evening, he had a sudden vision of the steamroller as it appeared directly in front of him when he applied his brakes. The huge machine was outlined in the darkness, and it seemed as if it were about to fall upon him, and to his fancy it took on a strange shape, finally revealing the features of the State Attorney. As he recalled it, it seemed to stand forth like some antediluvian monster, bearing Wenk’s face, about to fall upon and crush him. Mabuse felt vaguely uneasy, and he suddenly left the gaming-table, where he was losing, and drove back in the night to Munich. On the way this action of his seemed ridiculous, and he felt as if his impulse had been unwarranted. “My desire for that woman will conquer any fear of that accursed lawyer,” he thought, but yet Wenk seemed to stand in his way, more powerful than ever. Why was he still there? Had Mabuse’s order not been distinct enough? If not, he would repeat it!

When once again in his house at Munich he went straight to bed. He controlled his desire to go to the Countess, and fell fast asleep at once.

===Chinese===
当晚，马布斯正坐在赌台前，眼前忽然浮现出那台蒸汽压路机——就是他踩下刹车时赫然横在面前的那个庞然大物。那台巨大的机器在黑暗中显出轮廓，仿佛正要朝他压下来；在他的幻想里，它渐渐变成一副奇形怪状，最后露出了检察官的五官。他一回想起它，它便像某种洪荒巨兽般矗立在眼前，长着文克的脸，眼看就要扑上来把他碾碎。马布斯感到一阵莫名的不安，突然离开了正输着钱的赌台，连夜驱车赶回慕尼黑。一路上，他觉得自己这一举动可笑，仿佛这阵冲动全无道理。“我对那个女人的欲望，会压倒对那个该死律师的任何恐惧，”他想，然而文克似乎仍旧挡在他的路上，比以往任何时候更加强大。他怎么还在？难道马布斯下的命令还不够分明吗？若是不够，他会再下一道！

重新回到慕尼黑的宅邸后，他径直上床。他克制住去伯爵夫人那里的欲望，转眼便沉沉睡去。

---

===Original===
When the road-menders in Schachen returned to work after their midday rest, a man who had come out of the inn attached himself to their party, saying that he wanted to speak to the overseer. Was it likely he could find a job? he asked them.

“You can have mine this minute, if you’ll pay for it well,” said one jokingly, but the man said that he only wanted the work so that he could get some pay himself. “That’s another matter,” laughed the navvy. “There’s the overseer standing there.”

The man went towards him, speaking in a low tone, and unobtrusively drew him somewhat away from the rest. Yes, he could possibly get a job, said the overseer, who was really a police inspector; let him show his papers.

These the man brought out, saying, “Do not show yourself surprised, inspector. Look as if you were reading the papers through, and take me on to help the stoker on the engine. He is Sergeant Schmied, isn’t he?”

===Chinese===
沙兴的筑路工人们午休过后回到工地时，一个从酒馆里出来的男人凑到他们中间，说他想同监工说话。他问他们：能不能找份活计？

“你这会儿就能接我的活儿，只要你肯出大价钱，”有个工人打趣道，可那男人说，他要活儿是为了自己挣点工钱。“那就是另一回事了，”筑路工笑道。“监工就站在那边。”

那人朝监工走去，低声说着话，不动声色地把他从人群里引开几步。行，兴许能给他找个活儿，监工说——此人其实是个警察督察——叫他出示证件。

那人掏出证件，说道：“督察，别露出惊讶的神色。您只管装作把证件从头到尾看一遍，然后雇下我，让我到压路机上给司炉打下手。他是施米德警长，对吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir. … Well, all right, I’ll take you on,” said the inspector aloud. “We can give you some work. Come this way. Schmied,” he called out. He explained to Schmied in an undertone that the State Attorney was going to spend the day on the engine as stoker’s assistant.

“What have you noticed now?” asked Wenk of Schmied, as the road-engine moved backwards and forwards.

“While you were on the way, the inspector telephoned to you, but you had already started. Things seem very strange here. We saw the man go to the villa that night, and we thought he must be the one who had left the car standing in the road, but yet it doesn’t seem to tally with the rest, for when we came back to the car it had disappeared. Early this morning there was a woman in a rowing-boat on the lake near the villa, but we could not be sure whether she actually came from there. An hour later, Poldringer, the man we are watching, came from the highroad and went into the house; but we had never seen him leave it, and that is very curious.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生。……好，行，我雇下你。”督察高声说道。“我们可以给你点活儿干。这边来。施米德！”他喊了一声。他又压低嗓音向施米德解释：检察官今天要以司炉助手的身份，在压路机上待上一整天。

“现在你们有什么发现？”压路机来回驶动时，文克问施米德。

“您还在路上的时候，督察曾打电话给您，可您已经动身了。这儿的事情透着蹊跷。那天夜里我们看见那个男人走进别墅，心想他必定就是把汽车撂在路上的那人，可这又同其余的情形对不上，因为我们回头去找那辆车，它已经不见了。今天一大早，有个女人坐在划艇里，在别墅附近的湖面上，可我们拿不准她是不是当真从那儿出来的。一小时后，我们盯梢的波尔德林格从公路那边走来，进了宅子；可我们从来没见他出去过，这一点非常古怪。”

===Original===
“You have no idea whether the villa has some unknown exit?”

“No, for hitherto our observations of Poldringer all tally. He used to return the same way he went out. He scarcely ever leaves the place, not once in three days.”

“Is there no way of getting into the villa?”

“Not without exciting attention. I see that by the way tramps are turned away. They have a well-trained bloodhound there. … It would not be possible to effect a secret entrance.”

===Chinese===
“你们不清楚别墅是否另有什么不为人知的出口？”

“不清楚，因为迄今为止，我们对波尔德林格的观察全都能对上号。他向来从原路出去，又从原路回来。他几乎从不离开那地方，三天里也出不了一回门。”

“没有办法进到别墅里去吗？”

“想不惹人注意就进不去。从流浪汉一律被轰开这一点，我就看出来了。他们那儿养着一条训练有素的寻血猎犬。……秘密潜入是办不到的。”

===Original===
“Is Poldringer still there?”

“Yes; I saw him at a window just now.”

“Had the car a numberplate?”

“Yes, the Constance district; here is the number.”

===Chinese===
“波尔德林格还在那儿吗？”

“在；方才我还在一扇窗户里看见他来着。”

“那辆车有车牌吗？”

“有，康斯坦茨地区的；号码在这儿。”

===Original===
“That, of course, is a false one. It came from the Lindau direction, I think you said?”

“Yes, sir. I telephoned the number to Friedrichshafen, Ravensburg, Lindau, Wangen and Constance. From Constance they told me that the number I gave belonged to a car in use by the Sanitary Commissioners which never left Constance.”

“Isn’t it possible that the car had been expected at the villa, but did not stop at it, either because they wanted to use it again shortly or because something had made them a bit suspicious—the steamroller, for example? … and therefore Poldringer was told to wait for the car in the street and take it to some place of concealment? During that time the man who had brought it here arrived at the villa. He is either still there with Poldringer or else he was the woman in the rowing-boat, and he has driven to the place where the car is. We must find out where they keep it hidden.”

“We often hear the sound of a motorboat at night not far from the shore, but we are not able to keep an eye on it.”

===Chinese===
“那当然是假的了。我记得你们说，它是从林道方向开来的？”

“是的，先生。我把号码用电话通知了腓特烈港（Friedrichshafen）、拉文斯堡、林道、旺根（Wangen）和康斯坦茨。康斯坦茨那边答复说，我报的号码属于卫生专员（Sanitary Commissioners）使用的一辆汽车，而那辆车从不离开康斯坦茨。”

“会不会是这样：别墅里本来就在等这辆车，只是它没有停在宅前——或是因为他们过不多久还要用它，或是因为有什么东西叫他们起了几分疑心，比如那台压路机？……于是他们吩咐波尔德林格到街上去等车，把它开到什么藏匿的地方去？在这段时间里，把车开到这儿来的那个人到了别墅。他要么还同波尔德林格一起待在里面，要么就是划艇里的那个女人，他已经开着车去了藏车的地方。我们必须查明他们把车藏在哪儿。”

“夜里我们常常听见摩托艇的响声，就在离岸不远的地方，可我们盯不住它。”

===Original===
“I shall sleep in the trolly with you tonight, and we will stop the roller half a kilometre further away from the house. Is there any suitable place to hide in near the house?”

“Yes.”

“Then we’ll go together. Is that settled? All right, then; now I’m going to learn how to lay out the stones. Hitherto, I’ve only laid out criminals!” laughed Wenk.

“Yes, your honour,” said Schmied cheerily, as he released the throttle and started the engine. “Will your honour please to stoke up!” And Wenk heaped more coal into its glowing maw.

===Chinese===
“今晚我就跟你一起睡在拖车里，咱们把压路机停到离宅子再远半公里的地方去。宅子附近有没有合适的藏身之处？”

“有。”

“那咱们就一起去。就这么说定了？好，那么；现在我要来学学怎么铺石块了。到现在为止，我只‘铺’过罪犯！”文克笑道。

“是，阁下，”施米德痛痛快快地松开汽阀，发动了压路机。“请阁下添煤！”于是文克往那灼热发光的炉口里又铲进几锹煤。

===Original===
“Up to now your honour has never fired an engine, only criminals!” he continued, carrying on Wenk’s joke.

“Yes, but not enough of those, as you see at the villa, my good Schmied,” answered the lawyer. “However, I hope with your help. …”

“We shall catch them all right,” said Schmied eagerly.

“If we don’t overreach ourselves, for I think we are dealing at the moment with the most dangerous and daring gang in Europe. You know that we have ascertained so far that it is a case of card-sharping, murder, terrorization, and all of it done by the help of a gang.”

===Chinese===
“阁下至今还从来没给压路机生过火，只‘生火办’过罪犯！”他接着文克的玩笑往下说。

“是啊，可那样的还不够多，你在别墅这儿就看得出来，我的好施米德，”这位律师答道。“不过，我希望有你帮忙。……”

“咱们准能把他们统统抓住，”施米德急切地说。

“只要别弄巧成拙。因为我认为，我们此刻对付的是欧洲最危险、最大胆的匪帮。你知道，到目前为止我们已经查明，这是一桩牌局行骗、谋杀加上恐怖胁迫的案子，而这一切都是靠一个匪帮的助力干成的。”

===Original===
“Yes, indeed,” said Schmied.

As they were leaving the trolly that evening Schmied whispered: “I should like to draw your attention to something, sir. Every evening I go by as if I were taking a little rest after the day’s work, and I light up my pipe. Just at the side there, you see, we are getting to a little door. Whenever anyone goes by, the dog begins barking, and I couldn’t help thinking there was some reason for it, but one can’t find it out from the street. You see now, I am just close to it, and while I am going by I fasten… (just listen to the dog now!) a thread across the door. Anybody who opens it would break the thread, but he would not notice it when going through. In this way I can keep watch over the door, even when it is not actually in view. Then I can tell whether anyone has gone through the gate in the dark. In the morning I go and look at it first thing, and take the thread away.”

“Is it there already?”

“I have just fastened it there.”

“Then you did it very smartly, for I did not notice anything,” said Wenk, praising him.

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，”施米德说。

傍晚他们离开拖车时，施米德低声说：“有件事我想请您留意，先生。每天傍晚我都从那儿经过，装作收工后歇一歇的样子，点起烟斗。就在旁边那儿，您瞧，我们挨近了一扇小门。只要有人经过，狗就叫起来，我不由得琢磨这里头总有缘故，可从街上又查不出来。您现在看，我正好走到它跟前，一边走一边把一根线……（您听，狗这会儿就叫了！）横着绷在门上。谁开门都会碰断这根线，可他走过时不会察觉。这样，即便那扇门实际不在视线之内，我也能守着它。这样我就知道夜里有没有人穿过那道门。早上我头一件事就是去查看，再把线取走。”

“已经绷上了吗？”

“我刚刚绷上去的。”

“那你干得十分利落，我竟一点没有察觉，”文克称赞道。

===Original===
“Let us go back. It really is a side-entrance to the other villa.”

“Do you know who is living there?”

“For the last thirty years an old maid has been living there. There certainly is no connection between the two villas.”

They strolled back along the road.

===Chinese===
“咱们回去吧。那确实是另一座别墅的侧门。”

“你知道住在那儿的是谁吗？”

“三十年来，那儿一直住着一位老小姐。两座别墅之间肯定没有连通之处。”

两人沿着大路缓步走了回去。

===Original===
“If you would like to go to sleep, Schmied, I have no objection. I know what I’ve to look out for now.”

“Well, I really should be glad to, sir, for last night I got no sleep, and I must be out there again before four o’clock.”

“I understand. Well then, good night. …”

Wenk continued his patrol throughout the whole of the spring night, but nothing happened, and he noticed nothing out of the common. Next morning he repaired to the hotel at Lindau, the address of which he had notified before leaving Munich. The director told him he had been rung up from Munich, and his man wanted him to know that Count Told most earnestly desired to speak to him as soon as possible. The call had come from his home at Munich. He seemed to be greatly agitated, and begged the man to telephone the message on.

===Chinese===
“你要是想睡觉，施米德，我没有意见。现在我知道该留神什么了。”

“那我真巴不得呢，先生，因为昨夜我没合过眼，而且四点钟以前我还得再赶出去。”

“我明白。那么，晚安。……”

整个春夜，文克巡逻不辍，但什么也没有发生，他也没有察觉任何异常。第二天早晨，他去了林道的旅馆——离开慕尼黑之前，他已把那边的地址通知过人。经理告诉他，慕尼黑来过电话，他手下的人要他知晓：托尔德伯爵极其恳切地希望尽快同他谈话。电话是从伯爵慕尼黑的家中打来的。他显得大为激动，恳求那人把口信转达下去。

===Original===
Wenk returned to Munich and rang up the Count, but an unfamiliar voice informed him that the Count had started on a journey.

“Did he leave no message for me?” said Wenk.

“No.”

===Chinese===
文克回到慕尼黑，拨了伯爵的电话，可一个陌生的嗓音告诉他，伯爵已经动身出门旅行去了。

“他没有给我留什么话吗？”文克说。

“没有。”

===Original===
“Where has he gone?”

“He left no address. Please ring off.”

Wenk was thoroughly perplexed.

===Chinese===
“他去哪儿了？”

“他没留地址。请挂断吧。”

文克全然摸不着头脑了。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
Mabuse hastily carried the insensible woman from the bank of the Rhine channel to the nearest house. It was that of an osier-binder.

“We have had an accident,” said Mabuse, and then seated himself at the window to watch the approach.

When an hour had gone by thus, and the Countess opened her eyes again, Mabuse noticed that she started on recognizing him and turned away, overcome with dread. He went hastily towards her and, stooping down, he whispered, “We are saved! We are irrevocably bound together!”
