The whispered words impressed her with a certain sense of comfort and security. She no longer withstood him, and soon sat up, the peasant’s wife promising to look after her.

Mabuse sought for the nearest village on the map. Then he went thither, in security, knowing that he was not being followed. George had remained as the victim of the pursuer’s vengeance, and he was saved. The other’s fate was due to the little trick of the police uniform.

===Chinese===
马布斯急忙把不省人事的女人从莱茵河水道的岸边抱到最近的一户人家。那是一个编柳匠的家。

“我们出了点事故。”马布斯说，随后便在窗前坐下，监视着来路。

就这样过了一个钟头，伯爵夫人再次睁开眼睛。马布斯注意到，她一认出他便惊得一颤，满面惧色地转过脸去。他快步走到她跟前，俯下身来低语道：“我们得救了！我们已经无可挽回地联结在一起了！”

这几句耳语给了她某种安慰与安全之感。她不再抗拒他，不一会儿便坐起身来，农妇一口应承照看她。

马布斯在地图上找到最近的村子，随后放心前往，他知道没有人在跟踪自己。乔治留在了那里，成了追兵泄愤的牺牲品，而他本人得救了。那一个人的命运，就栽在那件警服的小小诡计上。


===Original===
The village was not more than twenty minutes’ distance, and in an inn he found a telephone. He ordered coffee, and then rang up Zürich. In half an hour’s time the call came through, and asking who was there, he was answered, “Dr. Ebenhügel, Zürich.”

“Has Spoerri arrived?” he inquired.

“Spoerri has just come: he is still here;” and Spoerri rushed to the telephone.

“Spoerri, I’ve had a misfortune. George is taken, but we have escaped. Bring the car here at once, and put in a travelling dress and coat for my wife. I shall expect you at 2 p.m. at the Au railway-station in the Rhine Valley.”

“Very good, sir,” replied Spoerri.

“I called her my wife, and said it quite coolly and intentionally,” mused Mabuse, dallying with the thought, which yet seemed to imply something like a fetter; but he dismissed the idea, saying, “She *is* my wife, my own property!﻿ ﻿… It is true, she *is* mine.”

===Chinese===
村子离此地不过二十分钟的路程，他在一家客栈里找到了电话。他点了咖啡，然后给苏黎世打了电话。半小时后电话接通了，他问对方是哪位，得到的回答是：“苏黎世，埃本许格尔博士。”

“施佩里到了吗？”他问。

“施佩里刚到：他还在这里；”施佩里应声冲到了电话机旁。

“施佩里，我遭了一场变故。乔治被抓走了，可我们逃脱了。马上把车开到这儿来，再往车里放一套给我妻子用的旅行连衣裙和大衣。下午两点，我在莱茵河谷的奥厄（Au）火车站等您。”

“好的，先生。”施佩里答道。

“我管她叫妻子，叫得那么平静，又是故意的。”马布斯暗自思忖，把玩着这个念头；它却似乎隐含着某种近似枷锁的东西。但他把这个念头撇开了，说道：“她就是我的妻子，我私有的财产！……没错，她就是我的。”

---


===Original===
Spoerri arrived punctually. “I shall drive you through the Engadine direct to the Italian frontier,” he said, when Mabuse had told him all that had occurred. But to that proposal Mabuse merely uttered one word: “No!”

“But, Doctor,” Spoerri pleaded, “you can’t remain in Switzerland. The Munich police have informed the authorities here of your movements. We shouldn’t get even as far as Toggeburg. It would be almost better to return to Germany.”

“And that’s exactly what I mean to do! Spoerri, from this day forward the State Attorney’s life stands under my protection. You are to revoke my earlier orders to the Removal Committee at once.”

“You are going in for a remarkable friendship, Doctor,” tittered Spoerri.

“He is to remain absolutely under my protection!” repeated Mabuse, and they drove through the flat marshland back to the peasant’s hut.

The Countess got into the car, and they were soon hastening to the Austrian frontier. “What sort of passports have you for us?” asked Mabuse.

===Chinese===
施佩里准时到达。等马布斯把事情的经过原原本本讲完，他说：“我送您取道恩加丁，直驶意大利边境。”对这一提议，马布斯只回了一个字：“不！”

“可是，博士，”施佩里恳求道，“您不能留在瑞士。慕尼黑警方已经把您的行踪通报给了这里的当局。我们连托根堡都过不去。差不多还不如回德国去。”

“我打的正是这个主意！施佩里，从今往后，检察官的性命处于我的保护之下。你立刻去撤销我先前给清除委员会下的命令。”

“您这是要结一段非同寻常的友谊啊，博士。”施佩里嗤嗤窃笑。

“他必须绝对处于我的保护之下！”马布斯重复道。随后他们乘车穿过平坦的沼泽地，回到那间农舍。

伯爵夫人上了车，他们很快便朝奥地利边境疾驰而去。“给我们备的是什么样的护照？”马布斯问。


===Original===
“Swiss ones: please take them,” answered Spoerri, handing over documents with many visas, calculated to arouse a confidence which was constantly abused yet remained unconscious of the fact.

Three hours later the car was driving along the highroad leading from Bregenz to Kempten. It drove past a house from which, the night before, a message had been flashed through to Munich telling of its passing, and went towards Würtemberg. The travellers spent the night in a village south of Stuttgart.

In the evening Mabuse went to Spoerri’s room, and said to him: “There is just one thing left for me to do in Germany, in Europe﻿ ﻿… and that is to get hold of that lawyer, the State Attorney, Wenk, alive. I want him *alive*, mark you! as much alive as a fly under a glass. The Countess and I are staying here tomorrow. You will go to Stuttgart and buy, whatever the price may be, a two-seater aeroplane. We are quite safe here. The landlord did not even register us, so if the police appear he is bound to hold his tongue, or else he will be fined. Have you any brandy?”

Spoerri shrank back in dismay; his martyrdom was about to begin again. Nevertheless, he had smuggled three bottles out of Switzerland.

“Of course you have some brandy!” said Mabuse, before he could even answer.

Mabuse drank from the travelling cup which he always carried in his pocket, and Spoerri had to fill the toothglass on the washhandstand.

===Chinese===
“瑞士的：请收下。”施佩里答道，一面递上签证盖得满满的证件——这些签证精心算计，为的就是唤起一种信任，一种屡遭盗用却始终浑然不觉的信任。

三个小时后，汽车行驶在从布雷根茨通往肯普滕的公路上。它驶过一座房屋——头天夜里，一封报告它经过的电讯正是从那里发往慕尼黑的——随后朝符腾堡方向开去。这几位旅人在斯图加特以南的一个村子里过了夜。

晚上，马布斯走进施佩里的房间，对他说：“在德国，在欧洲……我只剩一件事要办：把那个法律家、检察官文克活着弄到手。我要他活着，你记住！要像玻璃杯底下扣着的苍蝇那样活生生。明天我和伯爵夫人留在这里。你去斯图加特，不管价钱多高，买一架双座飞机。我们在这里万无一失。店主连登记都没给我们登记，所以警察若是找上门来，他必定闭紧嘴巴，不然他自己要吃罚金。你这儿有白兰地吗？”

施佩里惶恐地缩了回去；他的殉难又要开始了。不过，他还是从瑞士私运出来了三瓶。

“你当然有白兰地！”他还没来得及答话，马布斯便说道。

马布斯用他总揣在口袋里的旅行杯喝，施佩里只好往洗脸架上的漱口杯里斟。


===Original===
Mabuse was longing for a carouse, a really heavy carouse which should seize him by the throat and press him under the water, as if he were being given a millstone for a swimming belt. When he had emptied the second bottle, he saw that he was not likely to get his wish.

“Haven’t you any more?” he asked.

“That’s all there is. I couldn’t venture to bring any more across the frontier.”

Mabuse laughed satirically. “That’s fine. Here is Spoerri, who has brought three railway vans full of salvarsan, two of cocaine, enough prostitutes to fill three brothels across the frontier, yet he hasn’t enough courage to bring more than three bottles of brandy! Empty your glass into mine. Don’t your wages include the getting of brandy?”

When the third bottle had been emptied Mabuse, clearheaded as ever, but more hot-blooded, went back to the room next his own, occupied by the Countess. He was out of sorts, and resembled an engine that had been run too fast, so that the heat had covered the glowing cylinders with vapour, and they could not be set in motion.

He approached the Countess’s bed. “You and I had come to an understanding together. You have broken through it: you were ready to betray me!”

===Chinese===
马布斯渴望一场豪饮，一场真正沉醉的豪饮，好揪住他的咽喉、把他按进水里，仿佛给他套上一块磨石当游泳圈。喝空第二瓶时，他看出自己的心愿多半要落空了。

“你再也没有了？”他问。

“就这些了。我不敢再多带过边境。”

马布斯讥诮地笑了。“好极了。瞧瞧这位施佩里，运过边境三整车皮的砷凡纳明、两车皮可卡因，还有塞得满三家妓院的娼妓，却没有胆量带三瓶以上的白兰地！把你杯里的倒进我的杯里。难道置办白兰地不在你的工钱里吗？”

第三瓶喝空之后，马布斯头脑一如既往地清明，只是血气更加燥热，他回到自己隔壁那间伯爵夫人住的房间。他心绪败坏，像一台开得过快的发动机，热气把烧红的汽缸裹进了蒸雾，再也无法开动。

他走到伯爵夫人的床前。“你我之间有过一个默契。你破坏了它：你已经准备出卖我了！”


===Original===
“I was!” said the Countess in a low voice.

Then ungovernable fury seemed to possess the man. He snatched her from the bed, and as he seized her, lifted her high in the air as if he were going to dash her in pieces against the wall like rotting timber. At that moment he hated her; she was the embodiment of all his weaknesses. For ten long minutes, when the patrol-boat was on their track, the power of his will over her had ceased, and now, when he wanted to destroy her and would have dashed against the wall the head that defied him, he could not do it.

With a low cry the woman found herself held on high, and realized the strength of arm and indomitable willpower of the being to whom she was secretly﻿—and yet irrevocably﻿—bound. She longed for death. Softly she repeated a fragment or two of a prayer learnt in her childhood’s days, and she knew that if she were to die now she would draw this man also to his death.

But Mabuse, conscious of his power over the woman he held aloft in his grasp, suddenly came to himself again. Once more he realized that he was alive, was safe, and felt a fierce joy in the knowledge and in his possession of her. Almost gently he laid her down, and the poor woman, condemned afresh to a life of humiliation and degradation, was at the mercy of the tyrant who dominated her, and from whose power there was now no escape. She lay wide-eyed and tearless till the dawn, her only desire for floods and floods of tears wherein to drown forever the misery of her existence.

===Chinese===
“我是的！”伯爵夫人低声说。

随后，狂怒似乎不可遏制地攫住了这个男人。他把她从床上猛地拽起，抓住她的同时把她高高举到半空，仿佛要把她当作一段朽木，摔到墙上砸个粉碎。那一刻他恨她；她是他一切弱点的化身。巡逻艇盯上他们的那漫长的十分钟里，他的意志支配她的权柄曾中断过；而此刻，当他想毁掉她、想把那颗公然违逆他的头颅撞到墙上时，他却下不了手。

女人低呼一声，发觉自己已被高高举起，认清了这臂力、这不可屈服的意志力属于怎样一个存在——她已秘密地、而且无可挽回地与此人缚在了一起。她渴望一死。她轻声念诵起一两句童年学会的祷文；她还知道，倘若自己此刻死去，也会把这个男人一并拖向死亡。

可马布斯意识到自己掌控着这个被他高举在掌中的女人，突然又清醒过来。他再度认清自己活着、安全了，并为此、为自己占有她而感到一阵凶狠的狂喜。他几乎是轻柔地把她放下。这可怜的女人被重新判处了屈辱卑贱的一生，任凭那个支配她的暴君摆布——如今再无法从他的掌心逃脱了。她睁着双眼、无泪地躺到天明，唯一渴求的是滔滔不尽的泪水，好把她一生的苦难永远淹没其中。

---


===Original===
On the morning of the following day Mabuse flew with her from Stuttgart to Berlin.

There, caught in the toils of the mighty city, among those whose instincts he developed and used to his own ends, he lived, bent on one aim alone. One idea presented itself with ever-increasing intensity, one vision swam ever before his eyes, intoxicating him with a fury of desire. His fantasies, his strivings, and the goal before him gained their force because born of the strongest impulse within him, his lust for power!

There was one man in the world who had set himself to follow his path, had discovered him in his own territory, and dislodged him from his fortress. There was one alone who had dared to disturb his plans, to oblige him to undertake a flight in which his life had been in danger. It was due to this man’s efforts that the State had interfered with his schemes for getting rid of those whom his imperious will desired to remove from his path.

From the woman who had first moved him to the very depths of his being he had wrested all the power of will with which her personality resisted him. It was his pride to know that. He had taken her being, her beauty, her independence, her exclusiveness, and grappled them to himself, and this work of his was the very highest spiritual expression of his powers and capabilities. But between him and her there was a period of ten minutes in which she had escaped his domination, in which he had to renounce his claim to this symbol of his superhuman force. And that period of time, that barren, useless part of his life, he owed to the power of this one man.

His flight from Germany with this woman and his journey across the Atlantic had been so minutely prepared in all its details that only death could intervene. His empire of Citopomar, with its virgin forests, tigers, rattlesnakes, where death lay in wait at every moment, its mountains and its waterfalls and its rare exotic growths, was waiting for him, waiting to set him free from Europe, to offer him a new life. Any day might see him crowned as emperor.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，马布斯带她从斯图加特飞往柏林。

在那里，他陷在这座巨大都市的罗网之中，置身于那些被他开发出本能、又被他驱策着为自身目的所用的人们中间，活着，一心只抱定一个目标。一个念头以不断增强的烈度反复浮现，一个幻象始终在他眼前游动，以欲望的狂热将他灌醉。他的种种幻想、种种奋斗，以及他面前的目标，之所以有那份力量，正因为它们都生于他心中最强烈的冲动——他的权力欲！

世上只有一个人，立定心志要循着他的踪迹穷追不舍，在他自己的地盘上把他识破，把他从堡垒里逐了出去。唯有这一个人，胆敢搅乱他的计划，迫他踏上一次性命堪忧的逃亡。也正是由于此人的努力，国家才出面干预，坏掉了他的图谋——他想借此除掉那些他的专横意志必欲从自己道路上清除的人。

从这个头一个把他存在的最深处都撼动了的女人身上，他已经夺尽了她人格中用来抵抗他的全部意志力。明了这一点是他的骄傲。他夺走了她的存在、她的美貌、她的独立、她的孤高，把它们牢牢扣在自己身上，而这番功业，正是他的力量与才干在精神上的极致表现。可在他与她之间，横着一段十分钟的时光，在那段时光里她逃脱了他的支配，他不得不放弃对这一象征——他那超人力量的象征——的一切权利。而那段时光，他生命中那段荒芜无用的部分，正是拜此人的力量所赐。

他带着这个女人逃出德国、远渡大西洋的旅程，每个细节都筹措得万分周密，唯有死亡才能横加阻挠。他的锡托波马尔帝国——原始森林、猛虎、响尾蛇，死神无时无刻不在暗中窥伺，还有群山、瀑布与珍奇的异域草木——正在等他，等着把他从欧洲解脱出来，赐他一种新生活。随便哪一天，都可能见他加冕称帝。


===Original===
But he would eat of Dead Sea fruit for the rest of his life, did he take possession of his realm before he had seized upon this man with all the full force of his lust for power and his deadly hatred, had held him within his grasp and annihilated him. Between him and Wenk it was a struggle for existence, and he could know no peace while the other lived.

Once, when the thoughts surging within him would no longer be controlled, he replied to the Countess’s inquiry as to when they would leave Germany, “I shall catch him alive. I shall catch him like a bird in the snare. He will flutter helpless into my hands. Not till then do I go.”

She turned away afraid, guessing the man he meant. Since that moment of her resistance and hope of escape she seemed to have become more subdued than ever, falling deeper under his demon spell. She did not venture to oppose or question more.

Mabuse’s enterprise with regard to Wenk developed slowly. But steadily and surely the net around him was tightening.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
可是，倘若他不先倾尽权力欲与致死仇恨的全部伟力抓住此人、把他攥进掌中加以毁灭，便先行登上自己的王国，那么他余生所尝的，将尽是死海之果。在他与文克之间，是一场生死存亡的搏斗；只要对方还活着，他就休想安宁。

有一次，胸中翻腾的思绪再也压制不住，伯爵夫人问起他们何时离开德国，他答道：“我要活捉他。我要像捉罗网中的鸟一样捉住他。他会扑腾着翅膀、无可奈何地落进我手里。不到那时，我不走。”

她害怕地转过脸去，猜到了他指的是哪个人。自从那次反抗与那场逃跑的希望以来，她似乎变得比以往任何时候都更加驯顺，更深地坠入他的魔咒。她再不敢违逆，也不敢多问。

马布斯针对文克的图谋进展缓慢。然而，他周身的那张网，却在稳稳地、确然地收紧。……

---


===Original===
Wenk was in Munich again. George had been imprisoned there, and he played the role of a deaf mute. No one had heard a word from him since his arrest. He was confronted with the constables and tradespeople from Schachen who had seen him for many weeks, with the young fellows whom he had tried to hand over to the Foreign Legion, all of whom instantly recognized him, but he did not utter a word.

One morning they found he had hanged himself with his braces. He had written one word on the wall of his cell, the word that one of Napoleon’s generals had made renowned after he had lost the battle of Waterloo.

An exhaustive search in the Villa Elise brought little to light. It merely revealed proofs that Mabuse employed the money obtained by gambling or theft to carry on smuggling and profiteering on a gigantic scale. The police worked side by side with the Swiss authorities, for it was believed that Mabuse must be in Switzerland, or at any rate that he had passed through. Wenk went once a fortnight to Zürich. Now and then one of Mabuse’s gang was caught, but all were so thoroughly schooled that no word of betrayal escaped them.

News reached Wenk from Frankfurt that a gambler was at work there, whose description so closely resembled Mabuse that Wenk travelled thither at once, but when he arrived there was no trace of the man to be found. Three days later there was a report of a similar kind from Cologne, then from Düsseldorf, and later both from Essen and Hanover.

===Chinese===
文克又回到了慕尼黑。乔治已被囚在那里，他扮演着一个聋哑人的角色。自被捕以来，没有一个人听他说过一个字。人们让他与沙兴来的警员和商贩对质——这些人一连许多个星期见过他——又让他与那几个他曾企图送去外籍军团的年轻人对质；所有这些人当即认出了他，可他一个字也不吐。

一天早晨，人们发现他用背带自缢了。他在囚室墙上写了一个词——那个词，是拿破仑麾下的一位将军在输掉滑铁卢战役之后弄得尽人皆知的。

对埃莉泽别墅（Villa Elise）的彻查几乎一无所获，只是查明了一些证据，证明马布斯把赌博或偷盗所得的钱财用于经营规模庞大的走私与投机倒把。警方与瑞士当局携手办案，因为人们断定马布斯必定在瑞士，或者至少从那里经过。文克每两周去一次苏黎世。马布斯的党羽不时有人落网，但个个都受过严密的调教，没有一个人吐露过半句出卖的话。

消息传到文克那里：法兰克福有一个赌客在活动，相貌描述与马布斯如此相像，文克当即赶去；可等他抵达，那人已无影无踪。三天后，科隆传来一份类似的报告，随后是杜塞尔多夫，再后来埃森和汉诺威也接连传来。


===Original===
Wenk went hither and thither, not doubting in his own mind that he was indeed on the track of Mabuse. The latter must have spies in Munich who watched and reported Wenk’s movements. Knowing that he was followed, he took every possible precaution, and employed all the cunning at his command. On his journeys he made use of trains, cars, aeroplanes indiscriminately. Since he could not help suspecting that Mabuse had accomplices among his own subordinates, Wenk watched these very closely. He changed his chauffeur and his housekeeper, altered his address and his telephone number, took rooms in a hotel, or lodged with friends in the suburbs. But as soon as he arrived at the town where the gambler had been seen, he found he had vanished without trace of any kind, only to reappear a few days later in some other part. The whole country already rang with reports of the existence and operations of the robber-king. Dr. Mabuse, the gambler! It was like a ballad, expressing the devilry and defiance of all who offered resistance to existing law and order, and it spread from place to place.

In all the towns the police arrested men in gangs, but when the criminals were sorted out, this man, whose capture was worth more to them than all the rest, was never to be found. Suddenly it struck Wenk that Mabuse must be making his way by a circuitous route to Berlin. From his superior officers Wenk obtained permission to leave Bavaria, and got in touch with the Prussian courts of justice, and these appointed him to Berlin on special duty.

He at once travelled thither and took lodgings in the Central district. Mabuse saw him arrive at the railway-station, and an hour later he knew where he was staying. At last he had him within reach, in the place where he desired to accomplish his scheme of revenge and towards which he had been working, for Mabuse in reality had never left Berlin. In all the towns to which Wenk had travelled in search of the gambler, Mabuse had doubles, persons of his own gang, instructed by him. Munich was too small for the scheme Mabuse had in hand. The abysses of Berlin would be a safer hunting-ground, and the hunt began on the very next day.

===Chinese===
文克四处奔波，心里毫不怀疑自己确确实实咬住了马布斯的踪迹。马布斯在慕尼黑必定安有密探，监视着文克的行动并随时上报。文克知道有人盯梢，便处处设防，把手里的一切狡计都用了出来。旅途上，火车、汽车、飞机他无所不用。由于免不了怀疑马布斯连他自己的下属里也安插了同党，文克把他们盯得极紧。他换掉了司机和女管家，更改了住址和电话号码，一时在旅馆开房，一时借宿在郊外朋友家中。可每当赶到那赌客现身过的城市，他总发现对方已消失得无影无踪，不出几天又在别处现身。全国上下已经响遍了关于这位盗贼之王的存在与行藏的种种传闻。赌徒马布斯博士！这简直像一支民谣，唱尽了所有反抗现行法律与秩序之徒的凶顽与肆无忌惮，一处一处传扬开去。

在所有那些城市，警方成伙成伙地抓人，可等把罪犯们一一分拣出来，这个比其余所有人加起来还金贵的要犯，却始终不见踪影。文克猛然醒悟：马布斯必定正取道迂回的路线前往柏林。文克从上级那里获准离开巴伐利亚，同普鲁士法院接上了头，后者委派他赴柏林执行特别任务。

他随即动身前往，在中区落了脚。马布斯看着他抵达车站，一个钟头后便知道了他下榻的地点。他终于把此人够到了手边，就在这座他蓄意完成复仇计划的城市——他一直朝之经营的城市，因为马布斯实际上从未离开柏林。在文克为搜寻那赌客而赶去的所有城市里，出没的都是马布斯的替身，都是他党羽中的人物，由他亲自面授机宜。慕尼黑对于马布斯手中的计划而言太小了。柏林的深渊会是更稳妥的猎场，而狩猎第二天便开了场。

---


===Original===
That day Wenk had been describing to a junior colleague in the Berlin police his course of action in “the Mabuse case.” They had talked about it together, discussing a plan of operation, but the only conclusion they had come to was that the gambler should be allowed to show his own hand first. To aim at him in the dark would be likely to reveal to him prematurely the whereabouts of his pursuers.

In the evening, when Wenk had taken a meal in the Traube restaurant, he visited a café, and then, tired out by his long discussion, he sought his lodgings. There a man accosted him, standing in a doorway removed from the light.

“If you please, sir﻿ ﻿…” said he.

“What do you want?” asked Wenk reluctantly.

“Would some cocaine be useful to you, sir?”

Wenk went on without vouchsafing a reply, and he noticed that the man followed him, but when he came to the busy Friedrichstrasse he lost sight of him.

===Chinese===
那天，文克正向柏林警局一位年轻的同僚讲述自己在“马布斯案”中的行动方针。两人一起商量过，讨论了一套行动方案，但得出的唯一结论是：应当先让那赌客自己亮出牌来。摸着黑朝他下手，反倒可能过早暴露追捕者的行藏。

晚上，文克在特劳贝（Traube）餐馆用了餐，又去了一家咖啡馆，随后，长谈的困倦压上身来，他便往住处走去。有个男人上来搭话，那人站在一道背光的门洞里。

“劳驾，先生……”他说。

“你要什么？”文克不情愿地问。

“来点可卡因对您有用吗，先生？”

文克没有赏他一句答话，径自往前走。他注意到那人跟了上来，但走到人烟熙攘的弗里德里希大街，便把他跟丢了。


===Original===
Wenk soon took himself to task for having let the man escape him thus. He ought to have got into touch with this pedlar of illicit wares, for he belonged to the same stock as Mabuse. He was half inclined to go back, but the feeling of weariness was too strong for him and he went home.

The next night he took the same way home from the restaurant, but the man was not there. Wenk lingered here and there, and then, as he approached his lodgings near the Police Market, a man came out of an entry towards him, saying in a whisper, “Do you want to see some nude dances?”

Wenk stopped still, saying, “You have come just at the right time. I don’t belong to Berlin, and I should like to see the real nightlife of this city just for once. Where are your dancers? Go ahead!”

“Follow me, then. I’ll go in front, and when you see me go in somewhere, you come quick, guv’nor, ’cos of the peelers!”

Wenk promised to follow his lead. The man went round the corner, listened to see if he were following, and then went on again. Suddenly he disappeared. Wenk went a few steps straight on. The man must have gone into one of the entries near, and he walked slowly, expecting to find him, and looking round about. Suddenly he heard the man’s voice behind him, speaking low and reproachfully: “I don’t call that quick, guv’nor. You’ll have the bobbies after you if you can’t be more spry. Come on here, then!” and the man pulled him into a house standing far back. The door opened on to a dark corridor, and silently and unawares it closed behind him, while the corridor was lighted up in the same instant. This corridor led into a little living-room, and that again into a hall crowded with people. Two gentlemen sitting near the door made room for Wenk beside them. His guide had disappeared.

===Chinese===
文克很快便责备起自己，不该就这样放那人溜走。他本该同这个私货贩子搭上线的，因为此人与马布斯本是同一种货色。他有些想折回去，可倦意太重，压倒了他，他还是回了家。

第二天夜里，他从餐馆循原路回家，那人却不在。文克在各处流连了一会儿，随后，走近警察市场附近的住处时，一个男人从一条门道里朝他走出来，低声问道：“想不想看看裸舞？”

文克站定了，说：“你来得正是时候。我不是柏林人，这座城市的地道夜生活，我倒真想见识一回。你的舞女在哪儿？带路吧！”

“那就跟上。我在头里走，您瞧见我进了哪家，就赶紧跟进来，老板，免得撞上条子！”

文克答应听他引领。那人拐过街角，侧耳听了听他有没有跟上，然后又往前走。突然他不见了。文克径直朝前走了几步。那人准是进了附近哪条门道，于是他放慢脚步，指望寻到他，一边四下张望。忽然，他听见那人的声音在背后响起，低低的，带着责备：“这可算不得快，老板。您再不麻利点，条子可就要来盯您了。跟我上这边来！”那人说着，把他拽进一座深深缩在后面的宅子。门开处是一条黑黢黢的走廊，门在他身后无声无息、不知不觉地阖上了，同一瞬间，走廊里亮起了灯。走廊通向一间小小的起居室，起居室又通向一个挤满了人的大厅。坐在门边的两位绅士给文克腾出了身边的位子。他的引路人已经不知去向。


===Original===
What Wenk saw was a simple performance, deriving its interest only from the secrecy with which it was performed.

He heard the conversation of the two men at his table. One of them said, “The only thing that interests me is how this entertainer manages to get a hundred or more persons here, year in and year out, without the police finding it out. Now, as an expert, you just tell me that!”

The other answered in German that sounded unfamiliar, “Well, you can’t really tell whether it is known to the police or not. There are such places winked at by the police because they are traps for criminals﻿—yes, really traps set for them. Now in Budapest.﻿ ﻿…”

Wenk listened eagerly. The gentlemen went on talking, drawing him naturally into their conversation. They disclosed their calling, and then gave their names. One of the gentlemen was, as Wenk had conjectured, a highly placed police official. They frequently met each other. The Hungarian told of various interesting and complicated cases occurring during the practice of his profession. He described the Budapest haunts of crime, touched on the many secret gaming-houses which had sprung up so quickly everywhere since the war, and waxed eloquent against the ever-increasing boldness displayed by criminals and the mob generally.

===Chinese===
文克看到的是一场平平无奇的演出，它的引人之处，全在演出时的那份隐秘。

他听见同桌那两个人的交谈。其中一个说：“唯一让我感兴趣的是，这位班主怎么能年复一年把一百来号人弄到这里来，警察却始终察觉不出。来，你是行家，倒给我说说这是怎么回事！”

另一个用听来生硬的德语答道：“这个嘛，你实在说不准警察究竟知不知道。有些地方警察是睁一只眼闭一只眼的，因为它们是给罪犯设下的圈套——对，是真正设来捕他们的陷阱。就说布达佩斯吧。……”

文克听得兴致勃勃。两位绅士接着谈下去，自然而然地把他带进了他们的话题。他们亮明了各自的职业，随后通了姓名。其中一位，正如文克所料，是个位高权重的警官。两人时常会面。那位匈牙利人讲起他执业生涯中种种有趣而错综的案件，描绘布达佩斯的犯罪巢穴，谈到战后各地如此迅速冒出来的大批秘密赌窟，又对罪犯和流氓群伙日益嚣张的气焰慷慨陈词，大加挞伐。


===Original===
Wenk, with a certain unconfessed distrust, talked very warily, saying that he was only on leave in Berlin, for the scene of his activities lay in Munich. But Berlin, as the hotbed of crime, afforded a good field of study for a Munich criminal prosecutor. He touched lightly on the existence of Mabuse, though without naming him, and related some of his bold and shameless crimes.

“Just lately,” said the man from Budapest, interrupting him, “we took into custody a similar adventurer, and we did it by curious and not exactly legal methods, but we got no further in any other way. With us in Hungary, as it is with you here, the assistance of hypnotism in the detection of crime is forbidden. We had the man of whom we were almost certain﻿—but, my dear sir, you won’t betray me, I am sure, for the professional interest you feel in putting an end to such aberrations is just as strong in me﻿—well, we were practically certain that he was the leader of a gang which had several murders to their account. He was in prison, as I have told you. He made himself out a deaf mute, and we could glean nothing from his papers. No one knew him, yet we felt almost sure of our man, and that kind of thing is almost unbearable to an expert, isn’t it?﻿—for when he appeared before their worships, there was the risk of his being acquitted for lack of sufficient evidence. That was a most disagreeable idea to me, for I had spent about six months in tracking him down, and if he were discharged the mistake would be due to me. I therefore took a very bold step. A friend of mine had hypnotic powers. He was a barrister, and had often displayed these gifts of his in private. I wanted him to go to the jail with me, but he said, ‘I can operate on him from outside,’ and, indeed, a quarter of an hour later I knew that we had the leader of the gang at last, and various disclosures were made which shortly after sent him to the gallows.”

While the Hungarian was telling this story Wenk experienced an aversion to him. He had a sensation of profound mental resistance to the man, although he could not explain what had caused such a reversal of feeling.

===Chinese===
文克心里怀着一种未曾言明的猜疑，说话极为小心，只说自己是来柏林休假，因为他供职的地方在慕尼黑。不过，柏林作为罪恶的温床，对一个慕尼黑的刑事检察官倒是一片上好的考察园地。他轻轻带过马布斯其人，虽未点出姓名，却讲述了此人的几桩胆大包天、寡廉鲜耻的罪行。

“就在不久前，”布达佩斯来客打断了他，“我们也收押过一个类似的冒险家，用的是些古怪的、不大合法的法子，可换别的路子我们一步也迈不动。在我们匈牙利，同你们这里一样，借助催眠术侦查罪行是被禁止的。我们抓住了那个几乎可以断定的人——不过，亲爱的先生，您一定不会出卖我，这我信得过，因为您念兹在兹要根除这类狂悖行径的职业关切，在我心里同样强烈——是这样，我们几乎可以断定，他是一个手下背着好几条人命的匪帮首领。他关在牢里，这我已经告诉过您。他装成聋哑人，从他的证件里我们什么也榨不出来。没有人认得他，可我们对自己的判断几乎十拿九稳，而这种滋味对一个行家来说几乎无法忍受，不是吗？——因为一旦他站到法官大人们面前受审，就有因罪证不足而无罪开释的危险。这念头让我极不痛快：我花了约莫半年工夫才把他追查到手，倘若放跑了他，这个过失就得记在我的账上。于是我走了非常大胆的一步。我有一位朋友身怀催眠的本事。他是个律师，常在私下里显露他这份天赋。我本想让他陪我一道进监狱去，可他说：‘我可以隔着外面对他施术。’果然，一刻钟之后我就知道，我们总算把那个匪帮首领攥在了手里，随后揭出的种种罪行，不久便把他送上了绞架。”

那匈牙利人讲述这段故事的时候，文克对他生出了一种反感。他感到自己对这个人有一种深入心底的抵触，尽管他说不清，是什么造成了这种情感的逆转。


===Original===
“Are you also interested in persons who possess this gift of suggestion?” asked the police superintendent.

“Uncommonly so!” answered Wenk.

“Perhaps you would like to meet my friend, and see something of his gifts?”

“Is he in Berlin, then? Yes, that I certainly should!”

===Chinese===
“您对具有这种暗示天赋的人也感兴趣吗？”警察总监问道。

“非常感兴趣！”文克答道。

“也许您愿意见见我那位朋友，领教领教他的天赋？”

“那么他人在柏林？好啊，这个我当然愿意！”


===Original===
“Yes, he’s here now. He has given up practising law, and now exercises his gifts openly. He has very quickly become celebrated. You must have heard the name of Weltmann?”

Wenk did not like to say No, so he answered with a subdued “Certainly!”

“Well, he is the celebrated Weltmann. You know he is noticeable on account of his having only one hand. He lost the other in the Carpathians in 1915. Well, we’ll arrange a meeting, then. I will see him in the morning. Are you on the telephone by any chance?”

Wenk mentioned his telephone number. Both the gentlemen then left, to go to a house where ether, cocaine and opium were procurable, and other more obvious vices were pandered to.

On the very next day Wenk was summoned to the telephone. “Police Superintendent Vörös speaking! Things have fallen out most favourably for you, my dear sir! In the home of one of our countrymen, about whom I will tell you a few things in confidence, Weltmann is giving an entertainment this very evening. It is quite enough for you to have expressed the wish; you may consider yourself invited, without any further formality. It is a most hospitable house, and you won’t feel yourself in any way a stranger. There are between sixty and seventy people invited. I’ll undertake all the arrangements, and if it suits you I’ll come in a car for you at nine o’clock. The villa is some distance out behind Nicholas Lake.”

===Chinese===
“是的，他此刻就在这里。他已经弃律不做，如今公开施展他的天赋。他很快就名满天下了。韦尔特曼（Weltmann）这个名字，您总该听说过吧？”

文克不愿说没有，于是压低了声音答道：“当然！”

“正是，他就是那位大名鼎鼎的韦尔特曼。您知道，他只有一只手，格外惹眼。另一只手，他是 1915 年在喀尔巴阡山丢的。好，那我们就安排一次会面。我明天上午去见他。您府上恰好有电话吗？”

文克报出了自己的电话号码。随后两位绅士起身离去，要去一处能弄到乙醚、可卡因和鸦片的宅邸，那里还兜揽着其他更加露骨的声色罪恶。

第二天一早，文克就被唤到了电话机旁。“我是警察总监沃勒什（Vörös）！亲爱的先生，事情为您安排得再顺利不过了！就在今天晚上，韦尔特曼要在我们一位同胞的宅邸里献艺助兴——那位同胞，回头我再私下跟您透露几句。您只消流露过这个愿望就够了；您只管把自己当作已蒙邀请，不必再讲任何客套。那是一座极其好客的宅邸，您绝不会感到自己是外人。受邀的客人有六七十位。一切安排都包在我身上；若是合您的时间，我九点钟开车来接您。别墅在城外相当远的地方，就在尼古拉斯湖（Nicholas Lake）后面。”


===Original===
“Thank you very much. Your kindness overwhelms me,” answered Wenk, “and I do not know how to requite it.”

“Oh, *that’s* all right,” answered the other heartily. “We Hungarians are only too pleased to have such a chance. Then we can regard that as settled?”

“Quite, thank you!”

“How very amiable the Hungarians are,” thought Wenk, as he hung up the receiver. He felt quite ashamed of himself for having had a doubt of the police superintendent’s good faith.

He spent the afternoon among the archives of the Criminal Investigation Department, where he and the colleague with whom he had talked concerning the Mabuse crimes looked through the collection of photographs of criminals. Face after face drew his attention. He would not give up until he had seen them all, and when he came back to his lodgings, tired out with his protracted labours, he had only just time to don his evening clothes in readiness for the function he was to attend.

===Chinese===
“太感谢了。您的好意真让我承受不起，”文克答道，“我不知道该怎样报答才好。”

“哦，那个呀，不算什么。”对方由衷地答道。“我们匈牙利人能有这样的机会，高兴还来不及呢。那么，这事就算说定了？”

“说定了，多谢！”

“匈牙利人真是殷勤可亲。”挂上听筒时，文克想道。先前竟怀疑过这位警察总监的诚意，他为此颇感惭愧。

下午，他在刑事调查部的档案室里度过，同那位与他商讨过马布斯罪案的同僚一起，翻检那套罪犯照片集。一张又一张面孔牵住了他的注意。他不把照片全部过目绝不罢休；等他拖着久坐劳顿的身子回到住处，距离他要去出席的那个盛会，只剩下刚刚够换上晚礼服的时间了。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
That same morning Mabuse had visited Told. “You are not so well, I can see,” said he to him. “Your pupils are very much dilated.”

“Is that a sign﻿ ﻿… ?” said Told hesitatingly.

“Yes. Don’t talk about your state; put it entirely out of your head. Where is your wife?”

The startled Count could not venture on an answer.

“Your wife did not want to live with you any more﻿—never any more!” went on the Doctor harshly. “That is so, isn’t it? You must destroy the past, break off all relation to it. Call your man here!”

===Chinese===
就在同一天上午，马布斯博士来探望托尔德。“我看得出，您身子不大好。”他对他说，“您的瞳孔放大得很厉害。”

“这是不是一种征兆……？”托尔德迟疑地说。

“是的。别谈论您的病况；把它从脑子里彻底赶出去。您的妻子在哪里？”

惊惶的伯爵不敢贸然作答。

“您的妻子再也不愿同您一起生活了——永远不愿了！”医生生硬地继续说道，“是这样，对不对？您必须毁掉过去，同过去一刀两断。把您的仆人叫来！”

===Original===
Told rang, and the man came. The Count, with a gesture, referred him to the doctor.

“Has anybody telephoned?”

“No, Doctor.”

“Has anyone rung up from here?”

===Chinese===
托尔德按铃，仆人进来。伯爵做了个手势，示意他听医生吩咐。

“有人来过电话吗？”

“没有，医生。”

“从这里往外打过电话吗？”

===Original===
“I did,” answered Told.

“Whom?”

“Dr. von Wenk.”

“Why?”

“I wanted to speak to him.”

“What did you want to say?”

===Chinese===
“我打过。”托尔德答道。

“打给谁？”

“冯·文克博士。”

“为什么？”

“我想同他说话。”

“您想说什么？”

===Original===
The embarrassed Count answered, “Only﻿ ﻿… to speak﻿ ﻿… to speak to some human being or other!”

“Is your servant a bullock, then, or am I one?” asked Mabuse harshly. “You can talk to me if you want to. What crazy idea has got into your head?”

The Count turned his head away; he no longer had the courage to face his doctor.﻿ ﻿… “Is he going to cure me?” he asked himself. Then he looked up at him timidly and irresolutely. “You are no human being: you are a devil!” was the secret cry of his heart, but these fierce thoughts soon left him, and he felt suddenly sleepy. “I am always so tired!” he exclaimed.

“Tell your man now, in my presence, to refuse all visitors or anyone who telephones. He must say, ‘The Count has gone on a journey. He left no address. Please ring off.’ ”

Slowly and mechanically Told repeated the order, and the man bowed and withdrew.

“I am really not sure whether I shall go on with your case,” said Mabuse. But Told hardly heeded him; he seemed to feel a slow poison stealing into his veins.

===Chinese===
窘迫的伯爵答道：“只是……想说话……想随便找个人说说话！”

“这么说，您的仆人是头蠢牛呢，还是我是？”马布斯生硬地问，“您想说话，尽可以同我谈。您脑子里又钻进什么疯狂的念头了？”

伯爵把头扭向一边；他再也没有勇气直视自己的医生了。……“他到底能不能治好我？”他自问。随后他怯生生、犹犹豫豫地抬眼望他。“你不是人：你是魔鬼！”这是他心底暗暗的呐喊。可这些凶狠的念头很快便离开了他，他忽然觉得困倦起来。“我总是这么疲乏！”他脱口喊道。

“现在就当着我的面吩咐您的仆人：一切来访都挡驾，一切来电话都不接。他只消说：‘伯爵出门旅行去了。没留地址。请挂断。’”

托尔德缓慢而机械地复述了这道吩咐，仆人躬身退出。

“我实在拿不准，您的病案还要不要接下去。”马布斯说。可托尔德几乎没听进他的话；他仿佛觉得有一种缓慢的毒液，正悄悄渗进自己的血管。

===Original===
“You are thirsty!” said Mabuse, suddenly.

“Yes, I am,” whispered the Count.

“You are to drink a mixture of brandy and Tokay, as much as you like. Take good long draughts﻿—the brandy will do you good. You must forget everything in your past, your wife as well. When you are convinced that you have succeeded in doing that, you are on the road to recovery. You must destroy the past, you understand. The alcohol will help you there.”

“Destroy the past,” stammered the Count, as if sinking into a bog that threatened to engulf him, “destroy﻿ ﻿… the﻿ ﻿… past.﻿ ﻿…”

“In two years’ time you can think about resuming your ordinary life again. In what time?” he broke off suddenly. “What time did I say?” he thundered.

The Count aroused himself from his lethargy. Horrified at the length of time involved, he answered in a low tone, “Two years.”

===Chinese===
“您口渴了！”马布斯忽然说。

“是的，我渴。”伯爵低声说。

“您要喝白兰地兑托考伊酒的合剂，想喝多少喝多少。大口大口地喝——白兰地于您有益。您必须忘掉过去的一切，您的妻子也在其列。等您确信自己做到了这一点，您便已走上康复之路。您必须毁掉过去，您明白吗。酒会在这上头帮您的忙。”

“毁掉过去。”伯爵讷讷地说，仿佛正沉进一片眼看要把他吞没的泥沼，“毁掉……这……过去。……”

“两年之后，您可以考虑重新恢复您的正常生活。多长时间来着？”他突然刹住话头。“我说的是多长时间？”他咆哮如雷。

伯爵从昏沉麻木中惊醒过来。一想到要熬过这么漫长的岁月，他惶骇不已，低声答道：“两年。”

===Original===
“Do you know that your wife wants to put you into a lunatic asylum? She is getting the State Attorney, Wenk, to help her. Was not that the man who rang you up?﻿ ﻿… I am coming again tomorrow.”

The Count remained alone, dejected and humiliated. It seemed as if elephants were trampling out his brains, that his spirit was a prey to crocodiles and he was covered with mud and slime. “The whole world has forsaken me,” he murmured. The pictures he had collected around him seemed to be celebrating orgies on the walls. He could no longer understand how it was they could ever have pleased him, nor why he had endured them so long. He took a hunting-knife and slit every one of them from top to bottom, hacking at their frames. When he had done it, he sprang back in horror. He held his head in his hands, groaning, “Oh God, am I really mad?”

He began drinking brandy, and he drank it out of a claret tumbler. When he had had three glasses he was intoxicated. Then it seemed as if the doctor had left something behind him and that this lay in front of him. He did not know what it was, but he tried to grasp it, and then suddenly it had jumped to his head. It seemed like a wedge fastened there, fitting tightly between the two halves of the brain. Fear seized upon him and tore his courage to shreds. “Doctor, Doctor,” he shouted, and he heard his voice reecho in the empty rooms. The world was so wide, yet he was alone. And then he became unconscious.

===Chinese===
“您可知道，您的妻子想把您送进疯人院？她正在请检察官文克帮忙。方才给您打电话的，不正是这个人吗？……我明天再来。”

伯爵独自留下了，沮丧而屈辱。他恍然觉得，仿佛有大象在踩烂他的脑髓，仿佛他的灵魂成了鳄鱼的猎物，而他浑身糊满泥浆和黏液。“全世界都把我抛弃了。”他喃喃自语。他聚拢在身边的那一屋子藏画，此刻仿佛正在墙上狂欢纵欲。他再也想不明白，它们当初怎么会讨他喜欢，也想不通自己何以容忍了它们这么久。他取来一柄猎刀，把每一幅都从头到尾划破，对着画框乱劈乱砍。砍完之后，他自己吓得猛地朝后跳开。他双手抱头，呻吟着：“哦，上帝，难道我真的疯了吗？”

他开始喝白兰地，用的是喝红葡萄酒的大杯。三杯下肚，他已经醉了。这时他仿佛觉得，医生落下了一件什么东西，那东西就摆在他面前。他不知道那是什么，伸手想去抓，它却猛地蹿进了他的脑袋。它像一枚楔子钉在那里，紧紧嵌在脑子的两大半球之间。恐惧攫住了他，把他的勇气撕成碎片。“医生！医生！”他喊叫起来，听见自己的声音在空荡荡的房间里回响。世界这样广大，他却孑然一身。随后，他便失去了知觉。

---

===Original===
Karstens succumbed to his wounds, and again the public imagination busied itself with the death of a second victim. Wenk found himself in a difficulty and decided one day to make a final appeal to the dancer. He went to her cell.

“I am not going to speak to you,” said Cara when she perceived him.

Wenk took no notice, and said in a troubled tone, his hopes disappearing: “Do you know that the beautiful lady who was always looking on at the play at Schramm’s has disappeared?”

“Not the one you sent to me in prison?” answered the dancer instantly.

===Chinese===
卡斯滕斯终因伤重不治，公众的想象力又一次围着这第二名遇害者的死打转。文克陷入了窘境，一天，他决定对那舞女作最后一次争取，便到她的牢房里去了。

“我没什么要对你说的。”卡拉一眼瞧见他便说。

文克并不理会，用发愁的语调说，心里的希望正在消散：“你可知道，那位总在施拉姆餐馆牌局旁观战的美貌夫人，失踪了？”

“不就是那个你打发到监狱里来见我的女人吗？”舞女立刻答道。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Wenk, and it was not till he had uttered the word that he perceived the significance of this admission. It was all very mysterious. Had the Countess revealed her errand to Cara, and was she in league with the gamblers? It seemed incredible, but yet how strange it was that Cara, who would not at first speak to him, at once gave him her attention when he mentioned the Countess. Wenk did not want Cara to think that he was astonished at this, and went on talking, while he was trying to consider how he could best arrive at the secret; but he did not stop to reflect upon the ideas that came uppermost. In the course of the conversation he hazarded a conjecture that had often occurred to him when he thought of Cara’s connection with the criminal, but which he had never mentioned till now. He said, “You are sacrificing yourself for this criminal because you could not make up your mind to part from him.”

Then Cara sprang up, staring at Wenk as if convulsed. He looked her right in the eyes, and noticed that an expression of overwhelming horror stood in them, and was clearly written upon her distorted features.

“Well?” he asked, encouraged and hopeful.

But Cara remained as if frozen in her stony attitude.

Then he ventured further. “If we came to some agreement, I could make proposals that would be to your advantage.”

===Chinese===
“是的。”文克说。话出了口他才咂摸出这句承认的分量。这一切实在蹊跷。莫非是伯爵夫人向卡拉泄露了自己的使命，她本就与那伙赌徒串通一气？这似乎难以置信；可是何等奇怪，卡拉起先不肯同他说话，一提到伯爵夫人，却立刻侧耳听起来了。文克不愿让卡拉看出他为此惊异，便接着说下去，一面盘算怎样才能最好地摸到这个秘密；可那些最先冒上来的念头，他却无暇细想。谈话之间，他斗胆抛出了一个揣测——每逢想起卡拉与那罪犯的干系，这揣测便常在他脑中浮现，只是直到今天从未出口。他说：“你为这罪犯牺牲自己，是因为你下不了决心同他分手。”

卡拉霍地跳了起来，直勾勾地瞪着文克，浑身像抽搐了似的。他直视她的双眼，看见那惊骇排山倒海，凝在她眼中，也清清楚楚写在她扭曲的面容上。

“怎么样？”他问，备受鼓舞，满怀希望。

可卡拉僵在那里，仿佛冻成了一尊石像。

他又向前逼了一步。“倘若我们能达成某种协议，我可以提出于你有利的方案。”

===Original===
Slowly the dancer recovered from the horror that had seized upon her. For the last three years, ever since Mabuse had repulsed her, her life had been a story of self-sacrificing martyrdom and devoted adherence to the man who had wrought her ruin and driven her to crime. Not for a single instant had she thought of betraying him, of refusing her allegiance. Indelibly stamped upon her whole nature like the brand of a slave was the feeling that mastery and might such as his could never be contested. And now, through Wenk’s words, she beheld this man whom she adored threatened with danger. What did the State Attorney know, and how had he obtained his knowledge? Had the Countess betrayed her after all? Slowly she evolved a plan by which to discover how much the lawyer knew. She might possibly convey a warning to Dr. Mabuse, and at the thought her blood was fired and the delicious sensation of feeling herself his deliverer, and perhaps, too, regaining the ascendancy she had lost, stole over her. No, it could not be, she dared not even conceive of it; to save him from danger would be enough for her, to know him secure would be bliss. Finally she said, “Since you seem to be better informed than I imagined, I will speak, but you must give me two days to think it over.”

===Chinese===
舞女缓缓从那攫住她的惊骇里回过神来。三年来，自打马布斯把她拒之门外，她的日子便成了一部自我牺牲的殉道史，死心塌地追随着这个毁了她、又把她逼上犯罪道路的男人。她片刻也不曾想过背叛他，不曾想过收回自己的忠诚。有一种感觉，如奴隶的烙印一般深镌在她的整个天性里：他那样的主宰与威势，永远不容抗争。而此刻，借着文克的这些话，她眼睁睁看着自己崇拜的男人大难临头。检察官知道些什么？他又从何得知？到底还是伯爵夫人出卖了她？她慢慢琢磨出一个主意，好探一探这位检察官究竟知道多少。她兴许可以给马布斯博士递个警信；一念及此，她的血便烧了起来，一种甜丝丝的感觉悄悄爬遍全身——她要做他的救星，说不定还能重新赢回失掉的支配地位。不，不能这样，她连想也不敢往深处想；能救他脱险，于她便足够了；能知道他安然无恙，便是莫大的幸福。终于她开口：“既然你知道得比我料想的更多，我愿意开口，但你必须给我两天，容我好好想想。”

===Original===
The dancer had learnt from the warder that someone had been inquiring about her, and from the description given she believed it to be Spoerri. She would therefore have an opportunity of telling him about her interview with Wenk and warning him of what might occur.

“Very well,” said Wenk, relieved. Then he thought he would clinch the matter, and as his previous supposition seemed to have hit the mark, he imagined it a favourable opportunity to inflame her imagination still further, so he said, “I am trying to get on the track of the Countess; she seems to be in hiding with your friend.”

He was so ashamed of these words, however, that he blushed as he uttered them, recalling with painful intensity his few meetings with the missing lady﻿—meetings which had bound him so closely to her. But the effect of his words on the dancer was wholly unexpected. She fell back on her pallet, sobbed aloud, tried to speak, but could utter no word, and then she clenched her fists and raised them despairingly to her brow.

Wenk went off quickly, thinking it best not to disturb this attitude of mind but to let her yield wholly to its influence. As he opened the door a man stumbled against it, but it was only the warder, who had come, as he said, to look at the prisoner as his duty was just at this time. “All right,” said Wenk, and he made his way out.

===Chinese===
舞女已从看守口中得知，有人来打听过她，就那人所描述的模样，她断定是施佩里。这样，她便有机会把同文克的会面告诉他，提醒他可能发生的事。

“很好。”文克说，如释重负。转念他想把这事敲定；既然先前的猜测看来已经中的，他觉着这正是进一步撩拨她想象的好时机，便说：“我正设法追查伯爵夫人的下落；她似乎就藏在你那位朋友那里。”

然而这些话却让他羞愧难当，说出口时脸都红了；他带着钻心的痛楚，回想起同那位失踪的夫人屈指可数的几次会面——正是那几次会面，把他同她如此紧密地缚在了一起。可他的话在舞女身上激起的反应全然出人意料。她仰面倒回铺板，放声抽泣，想说话，却一个字也吐不出来，随即攥紧双拳，绝望地举到额前。

文克快步离开，心想最好不要搅扰她这种心绪，索性由她整个人沉湎进去。他开门时，一个人影撞在门上，却原来是看守——照他自己的说法，此刻正是他查看囚犯的当班时辰。“好。”文克说着，径自出去了。

---

===Original===
Shortly afterwards the following things occurred. Near Hengnau, on the borders of Würtemberg, a man was detained and arrested as he was about to drive cattle to Würtemberg. At first he pretended to be dumb, but afterwards he raged furiously at his capture. The examining counsel, in order to intimidate him, said one day, “You had better confess before the new law is passed. If you are tried before then you may get off lightly, but later on it may cost you your head.”

“What new law is that?” asked the man.

“The crime of endangering the food distribution is punishable with death.”

“What sort of death?”

“Probably hanging!”

“And if I am convicted before that is passed?”

===Chinese===
此后不久，接连发生了下面这些事情。在符腾堡边境的亨格瑙附近，一名男子正要把牲口赶进符腾堡去，被截住逮捕了。起初他装哑巴，一言不发，后来则为了自己被捕而暴跳如雷。预审法官为了震住他，有一天说：“您最好趁新法令还没通过就招供。若赶在那之前受审，兴许还能从轻发落；再往后拖，可就要拿您的脑袋抵账了。”

“什么新法令？”那人问。

“危害粮食配给罪，可判处死刑。”

“怎么个死法？”

“多半是绞刑！”

“那要是我赶在法令通过之前就被定罪呢？”

===Original===
“You won’t get more than a year’s imprisonment at the most.”

Then he suddenly confessed, and his confession opened many doors. He confessed all that he had been doing for years and gave the names of all the profiteers known to him. Many arrests were the result. Every day afforded fresh opportunities, and finally one day the name of the man whom Mabuse had dismissed on the highroad to Lindau﻿—Pesch﻿—was mentioned.

Pesch was arrested, and his first night in prison was spent at Wangen, which was his native place. When the warder entered his cell next morning, the prisoner had disappeared. A few hours later a telephone message came to the Wangen police. In a wood on the highroad to Lindau a man was lying dead. It was undoubtedly a case of murder.

An inquiry took place on the spot. The dead man was Pesch. He had been stabbed, and as they raised his body they saw on the large white stone on which it had rested certain signs which had been written in blood. The very same day experts deciphered these signs. They stood for “Villa Elise.”

The mayors in the neighbouring districts were asked whether they knew a villa bearing this name, and thus it was soon ascertained that at Schachen there was a villa so called, and it was under police surveillance.

===Chinese===
“至多不过一年监禁。”

于是他突然招供了，他的供词打开了许多扇门。他把自己多年来的勾当一股脑儿招认出来，还供出了他知道的所有奸商的名字。随之而来的是一连串逮捕。一天天追查下去，每天都有新的收获，终于有一天，提到了那个被马布斯在通往林道的大路上打发掉的男人——佩施。

佩施落网了，在押的头一夜是在他的家乡旺根的监狱里过的。第二天早晨看守走进牢房，犯人已经无影无踪。几小时后，旺根警方接到电话：通往林道的大路旁的一片树林里，躺着一具男尸。这无疑是一桩谋杀。

现场随即展开勘查。死者正是佩施。他身中刀伤；人们抬起尸体时，看见他躺过的那块白色大石上，有一些用血写下的记号。就在当天，专家们破译了这些记号。它们是“埃莉泽别墅”（Villa Elise）。

人们询问邻近各乡的乡长，是否知道有叫这个名字的别墅，于是很快查明：沙兴有一座这么称呼的别墅，已置于警方监视之下。

===Original===
Wenk was at once informed, and he drove to Lindau. The two detectives who were in charge of the steamroller had ascertained that Poldringer had left Schachen on a bicycle the very day that Pesch was imprisoned, and had not returned until three o’clock the next morning.

Then Wenk arranged that two motorboats should be stationed on the lake. They were made to appear as if they were Customs’ official boats, and were provided with searchlights.

Another human life had been sacrificed, but this fresh murder had revealed something more far-reaching and dangerous than had yet been suspected. It was certain that the gang was taking part in this profiteering movement also, and it became clear that its leader had created an entire yet invisible State to carry on his purposes and give effect to the deeds his will imposed on his fellows.

Pesch left a wife and five children, and since the family breadwinner was gone, they were in absolute danger of starving. Then Wenk sought out Edgar Hull’s father, to obtain help for them, and the idea suddenly occurred to him, “Why not establish an educational institution, a real home-school for the children of criminals, taking them in under an assumed name? Perhaps that would be a good way to lay out your money. The children, who so often inherit the parents’ characteristics, could be watched over and perhaps influenced for good in their early years. If it were not possible to eradicate their vices, at least they could be kept apart from their fellows and removed before they have a chance of harming them. In this way a large proportion of the criminal class might be rendered harmless and many people would be saved.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
文克立刻得到报告，驱车赶往林道。负责那台蒸汽压路机的两名侦探查明：就在佩施入狱的当天，波尔德林格骑自行车离开了沙兴，直到第二天凌晨三点才回来。

随后文克作出部署：调两艘摩托艇驻泊湖上。艇只装扮成海关公务艇的模样，并配了探照灯。

又一条人命被牺牲了；可这起新的凶案所暴露的东西，比迄今怀疑到的更为深远、更为凶险。可以断定，这个匪帮也插手了这场投机倒把的风潮；而且事情已然清楚：它的首领缔造了一个完整却无形的国度，借以推行他的图谋，使他凭意志强加给党羽的种种勾当一一落到实处。

佩施撇下了妻子和五个孩子；一家之主一倒，他们陷入了饿死的绝境。文克于是去寻埃德加·赫尔的父亲，为这一家老小求援。这时他忽然灵机一动：“何不创办一所教育机构，一所名副其实的罪犯子弟寄宿学校，用化名把孩子们收容进来？这或许是您把钱财用在正道上的一个好法子。孩子往往继承父母的禀性，趁幼年加以看护，或许还能导人向善。即便恶习无从根除，至少也能把他们同别的孩子隔开，趁他们还来不及为害，就把祸患消解在未成之时。这样一来，为非作歹之辈便有相当一部分可望变得无害，许多人也能得到保全。……”

===Original===
“I will do it,” said Hull, “and I am grateful to you for the suggestion.”

===Chinese===
“我一定照办。”赫尔说，“多亏您出了这个主意。”

---

===Original===
The next evening Wenk was walking from the Marstall to the Maximilianstrasse, and as he passed the Four Seasons Hall he thought he saw someone he knew in the crowd in front of him struggling to gain an entrance; but he could not recall who it was, and went straight ahead. As he walked on he strove to remember whose back and shoulders it was that had seemed so familiar, but he could not place the individual. Soon afterwards he came to an advertisement window in which the scheme of a popular lottery was displayed. The large letters could be seen through the dusty pane, and the words “Lucky Chance” stood out. These words at once gave Wenk the clue he had been seeking. The back he had noticed belonged to the sandy-bearded gambler.

He was astounded at the discovery. He had been seeking this man for many days and nights all over Germany, and here he was, and he had passed so close by him that he could have touched him on the shoulder. He turned round at once, went back to the hall and at the entrance he read a notice stating that Dr. Mabuse was giving a lecture, with experiments, there that evening.

He immediately ordered one of the constables standing outside to fetch six plainclothes men and tell them to close all the exits without exciting any attention, and when the detectives were placed, he entered the hall. It was an easy one to search, and he went from row to row, while the lecturer was engaged in preparing his experiments. Wenk took up a position here and there, and looked at the folks one after another. But nowhere did he find the owner of the back which was so impressed on his mind.

===Chinese===
第二天傍晚，文克从马施塔尔步行往马克西米利安大街去。路过四季厅时，他恍惚觉得，前面挤着往里进的人群里有个熟识的身影；可他想不起那是谁，便照直朝前走。一路走，一路竭力回想：那似曾相识的背影和双肩究竟是谁的，却怎么也对不上号。不多时，他经过一处广告橱窗，里面陈列着一种大众彩票的玩法图样。隔着蒙尘的玻璃，大号字样看得清清楚楚，“幸运机会”几个词格外扎眼。这几个词立刻给了文克苦寻已久的线索。他方才留意到的那个背影，属于那个沙色胡须的赌徒。

这一发现令他大为骇然。为了寻这个人，他已在德国各地奔忙了多少个日日夜夜；而他竟然就在此处，方才两人挨得那样近，近得他一抬手就能拍到那人肩上。他当即转身，回到大厅，在入口处看到一则告示：马布斯博士当晚将在那里作带实验演示的讲演。

他立刻命令站在外面的一名警员去叫六名便衣来，让他们不引人注意地封住所有出口。侦探们各就各位之后，他走进大厅。这厅不难搜，讲演人正忙着准备实验，他便一排排看过去。文克东站站、西站站，把人们挨个端详。可到处都找不到那个背影的主人——它已深深烙进他的脑海。

===Original===
He noticed some of his acquaintance. There was Privy Councillor Wendel sitting in the front row, and a legal colleague of his was there with his wife and grown-up daughter, but he behaved as if he saw nobody and continued his eager search. It was all in vain, however. Then he took a sudden resolve, went outside again, and gave the detectives the following orders. All the exits were to be locked except one. Two detectives were to enter the hall, and one of them was to go on to the platform at once and request the audience to leave the hall quietly, one by one. Both were to see that there was no one left behind. The four others were to stand at the folding doors and let the people pass through singly, only one half of the door being opened.

Wenk himself would stand by the door, and if he gave any order for arrest, two of the detectives would at once take the man aside and handcuff him. The two others would then only have to take care that no one got near the man arrested. All were to have their service revolvers ready for use. There was great excitement in the hall when the announcement was made, and several cries of disapproval of the order were heard. The detective strove to pacify the disappointed audience.

===Chinese===
他认出几张熟面孔。枢密顾问温德尔坐在第一排，他的一位司法界同僚也带着妻子和成年的女儿在场；可他装作谁也没看见，继续热切地搜寻。然而全是白费力气。于是他当机立断，再到外头，给侦探们下了如下的命令：所有出口一律锁上，只留一个。两名侦探进大厅去，其中一人立刻上台，请听众安静地、一个一个地离场；两人都得负责查看，不许漏掉一个人。另外四人站在折门旁，门只开一半，放人挨个通过。

文克本人守在门边。他一发逮捕的指令，两名侦探便立即把那人拉到一旁，铐上手铐；另外两人则只须留神，别让任何人靠近被捕的人。人人都要备好制式左轮手枪，随时可用。命令宣布后，大厅里一片哗然，好几处响起抗议这道命令的喊声。那侦探竭力安抚失望的听众。

===Original===
Mabuse’s first thought, when he heard the Secret Service agent’s announcement, was a doubt whether he should have ventured on this public appearance, but he soon dismissed the troublesome idea. Yes, he had been right, for it provided him, in concentrated form, with the nourishment upon which his mind battened. With such hypnotic powers as he possessed he must always be in relation with a larger and unknown public. To feel his power over the narrow circle to which his professional duties bound him, the members of which were known to him, was not enough for his insatiable ambition. His sphere must know no limits, and with these weird and mysterious gifts of his he could exploit the triviality and credulity of his fellows and at the same time give full play to his hatred and his lust for domination.

Upon such a stage as this he felt as if born anew. It was here that he had inaugurated his reign of power, when the war sent him from his South Sea plantations back to his home, a ruined man, and this domination of his he could not renounce. While these thoughts were passing through his mind he went to the detective and asked what had happened. “You must inquire of the State Attorney, sir,” said the man. “Dr. von Wenk is just outside.”

===Chinese===
马布斯听到密探宣布命令，第一个念头是怀疑自己是否不该贸然作这次公开露面；但他很快便把这恼人的想法抛开了。是的，他来对了，因为这场合以浓缩的形式，供给他那颗大脑赖以饕餮的养料。以他这般催眠的神通，他必须永远同一个更广大、不相识的公众保持接触。只在职业本分所囿的狭小圈子里——里头的人他都认识——感觉自己的权力，对他那贪得无厌的野心来说，远远不够。他的疆域必须不设边界；凭着他这些诡秘莫测的天赋，他既能榨取同类的庸碌与轻信，又能同时纵情宣泄他的仇恨和统治欲。

站在这样的讲台上，他自觉如获新生。当年大战把他从南洋种植园遣返回乡，那时他已是个破了产的人；正是在这讲台上，他开创了自己权力的统治，而这份统治，他再也割舍不下。这些念头掠过脑际的当儿，他已走到那侦探跟前，问出了什么事。“这您得问检察官先生。”那人说，“冯·文克博士就在门外。”

===Original===
Mabuse turned pale and walked away, going rapidly towards the Privy Councillor, whom he saw still sitting in the front row. As he went, he felt in his pocket to make sure that his revolver was safe, and sensations of hatred and defiance went through his whole body, fastening as it were like a brand upon his mental image of Wenk.

“First of all you, and then﻿ ﻿…” he said to himself, but he was already smiling in the Councillor’s face.

“Your hypnotic powers,” said the latter, “seem to be giving the State Attorney some trouble!”

“Is that Dr. Wenk?” said Mabuse, drawing back as if astonished.

“I saw him just now going from seat to seat and fixing an eagle eye on everybody here, as if to pierce through coat, waistcoat and shirt to reach a guilt-burdened conscience. He does not seem to have found his man, however.”

===Chinese===
马布斯脸色发白，转身走开，快步朝枢密顾问走去——他还看见后者仍坐在第一排。一路走，他探手入袋，确信左轮手枪安然无恙；仇恨与抗争的激流滚过他全身，仿佛烙印一般，灼在他心中文克的影像上。

“先是你，然后……”他对自己说；脸上却已堆起笑，迎向枢密顾问。

“您那套催眠的神通，”后者说，“似乎给检察官添了不少麻烦！”

“那位是文克博士？”马布斯说，做出不胜惊讶的样子直往后缩。

“我方才看见他从这张座位走到那张座位，把鹰隼似的目光盯在场的每一个人身上，仿佛要穿透外套、背心和衬衫，直抵那负罪的良心。不过，他似乎没有找到他要找的人。”

===Original===
Mabuse’s breast heaved, inflated at the thought of his success. He felt like a horse in sight of its manger after a long and weary road. Although he clearly understood what the words implied, he nevertheless asked the Councillor, “How do you know that?”

“It is quite simple, for if he had found his man, he would have let one of the detectives take him out without disturbing your lecture.”

“That is true,” said Mabuse. “Let us go.”

He pressed towards the door, taking the Councillor with him. He was thoroughly on the alert, looking behind him to see that he did not lose touch with Wendel, and also ahead, where lay the danger he wished to avoid. Whenever any movement threatened to separate him from the elderly savant, he used all the cunning at his command to get near him again. It was above all essential not to leave the hall exposed to Wenk’s gaze as a solitary individual. The Councillor, who was old and well known, must help him to throw the hound off the scent. He was aged, however, and could not hurry; but Mabuse dared not be the last one to leave, closely eyed as he would be by a disappointed man who had not found the quarry he sought. There were still some others behind him, to whose party he might attach himself, so that he need not be the last.

===Chinese===
想到自己的得手，马布斯胸口起伏，洋洋自得。他像一匹长途跋涉、疲惫已极之后望见食槽的马。尽管他清清楚楚懂得这话的言外之意，却仍旧问枢密顾问：“您怎么知道的？”

“再简单不过：他要是找到了那个人，自会派一名侦探把人带出去，决不至于搅扰您的讲演。”

“这话不假。”马布斯说，“我们走吧。”

他朝门口挤去，携着枢密顾问同行。他时刻全神戒备：一面回头照看，惟恐同温德尔走散，一面又要顾着前方——那正是他要躲避的危险所在。每当人流涌动，眼看要把他同这位年迈的学者隔开，他便使出浑身解数的狡黠，重新贴到人家身边。最要紧的是：绝不能独自一人、孤零零地暴露在文克的目光下走出大厅。枢密顾问年纪大、名气响，正可帮他甩开那条猎犬的嗅迹。怎奈老先生上了年纪，快不起来；而马布斯又不敢殿后——一个没寻着猎物、大失所望的人，一双眼睛准会死死盯住他不放。好在他身后还有另外一些人，他可以凑进那一伙，这样便不至于落到最后一个。

===Original===
One thing was certain. It was he, and none other, whom the State Attorney was seeking, but Wenk did not know that Mabuse was his quarry, or he would have had him arrested on the platform. How had he got upon the track? Was it a mere guess that had started him off? Was there treachery in it? No; *he* would never be betrayed. Could Wenk have recognized him, one of those evenings at the gaming-table? No; it was impossible, his disguises were too perfect for that, so.﻿ ﻿…

Then a hand touched his, and Mabuse looked into Spoerri’s inquiring eyes, and saw beside him another man of his bodyguard, and he immediately looked away again unconcernedly. Spoerri and his accomplice were pressing towards the exit in front of him. Mabuse went on thinking, and came to the conclusion that mere chance had put Wenk on this track, some faint resemblance or recollection, some movement or action.﻿ ﻿… In any case, Wenk must see as little of him as possible, and since his back would be exposed to him longer than any other part, he put his arms through the sleeves of his overcoat and thus altered his appearance.

And now he had reached the exit with the Privy Councillor. He quickly pushed him in front, following closely on his heels. At the moment when Wendel stepped to the door, Wenk was ordering a detective to tell two men who were lingering on the stairs to move on. Mabuse heard the man say, “Shall I arrest them?” Then he looked ahead and saw that the order referred to Spoerri and his subordinate. Mabuse sought to catch his eye; he took his pocket-handkerchief out with a flourish and blew his nose loudly. Spoerri saw it and understood, and at once withdrew with his companion.

===Chinese===
有一点是确定无疑的：检察官要找的正是他，别无他人；可文克并不知道马布斯就是他的猎物，否则早在讲台上就把他逮捕了。他是怎么追到这条线索的？莫非全凭一个猜测起了兴？其中可有告密的背叛？不；*他*是永远不会被人出卖的。难道是哪个夜晚，文克在赌台边认出了他？不；不可能，他的化装天衣无缝，无懈可击，所以……

这时一只手碰了碰他的手。马布斯抬眼，正对上施佩里探询的目光，又看见旁边站着卫士中的另一个，便立刻若无其事地把目光挪开。施佩里和他的同伙正走在他前头，朝出口挤去。马布斯继续盘算，得出结论：把文克引上这条线索的，纯属偶然——某种依稀的相像或回忆，某个动作或举止。……无论如何，要尽量少让文克看见他；既然背部暴露在他眼里的时间最长，他便把双臂穿进大衣袖筒，就此改换了形貌。

此刻他已同枢密顾问走到出口。他赶紧把老人推在前头，自己紧紧跟在后面。温德尔跨到门边的那一瞬，文克正吩咐一名侦探，去叫两个在楼梯上磨蹭的人走开。马布斯听见那人问：“要逮捕他们吗？”他往前一望，这命令指的正是施佩里和他的下属。马布斯想法子去引他的目光；他潇洒地抽出帕子，大声擤起鼻涕。施佩里看见了，心领神会，当即带着同伴退了出去。

===Original===
Mabuse saw Wenk shaking hands with the Councillor. Then it was his turn to come forward, and Wendel introduced “Dr. Mabuse.” Without taking his eyes off the door, through which the light from the hall was now streaming, Wenk shook hands with Mabuse, saying courteously, “You won’t be annoyed with me for carrying out my duty, I hope, Doctor?”

Mabuse answered with affected friendliness, his hand on the revolver in his pocket, “Certainly not; I must naturally take the second place when it is a question of the good of the community, whom you are endeavouring to rid of a criminal.”

He had already passed on. Wenk nodded to him, but did not look round again, as his gaze was still fastened on the door.

The Privy Councillor took Mabuse’s arm going downstairs. Mabuse accompanied him to the gentlemen’s cloakroom and then took his leave. One of his cars was waiting in the Maximilianstrasse, and right and left of him at the entrance to the foyer his people were standing in readiness for anything that might happen. Spoerri had taken up his position at the main door of the hall, to keep watch upon the stairs; then he went out behind Mabuse, and the others, who were in small detached groups, always ready to close up at a word, followed them. It was not until Mabuse had taken his seat in the car and driven off that they dispersed, each going a separate way.

===Chinese===
马布斯看见文克同枢密顾问握手。接着便轮到他上前，温德尔引见：“这位是马布斯博士。”文克目不转睛地盯着那扇门——大厅的灯光此刻正从门里涌出来——一面同马布斯握手，客气地说：“我想，您不至于为我的执行公务而见怪吧，博士？”

马布斯装出一副亲热的样子答话，一只手按着口袋里的左轮手枪：“当然不会；事关公众的福祉——您正竭力替它除掉一个罪犯——我自然甘居其次。”

他已经走过去了。文克朝他点点头，却没有再回头，目光依旧钉在那扇门上。

下楼时，枢密顾问挽住马布斯的手臂。马布斯陪他走到男士衣帽间，随后告辞。他的一辆汽车正候在马克西米利安大街上；在休息厅入口处，他的人左右侍立，随时准备应对可能发生的任何变故。施佩里守在大厅正门，监视着楼梯；随后他跟在马布斯身后出去，其余的人三三两两散作小股，一呼即合，尾随而去。直到马布斯坐进汽车、驶离而去，他们才四散分开，各走各的路。

===Original===
Driving homeward, Mabuse reflected that he had committed one act of folly. He ought at any rate to have asked when he would be allowed to give his experiments. This fact depressed him, and he felt that he had failed in some way. He would never have done anything so foolish formerly, and the idea occurred to him that perhaps his power was on the decline, and that it was now time for Citopomar.

Then suddenly he shouted aloud, “No! this is due to that woman! Wenk wants to hang me, the woman makes me feel old, and she is delivering me over to the gallows.” Why should this woman, young and beautiful as she was, who had abandoned herself to her lot with despairing fatalism, make him feel old? Her abandonment of herself was like wine to him, and this idea started another train of thought. He was in conflict with himself. There was no enjoyment in the thought that he had escaped a great danger, and in the midst of his uneasy reflections he had a sudden breathless conviction that she made him feel old because he loved her. Then he felt a hatred of himself, gathering into one mighty heap all the fierce and bitter hatred he had cherished for others and pouring it out on himself. So strongly did he suffer from the burden of these chaotic feelings that his brain grew giddy. But now he had reached his house.

===Chinese===
归途中，马布斯反省过来：自己干了一件蠢事。他至少也该问一声，什么时候准许他把实验做下去。这件事叫他丧气，他觉得自己在什么地方失手了。从前他可绝不会干出这等蠢事。一个念头浮上心头：莫非他的权力正在衰落，莫非动身去锡托波马尔的时辰已经到了。

忽然他高声喊了出来：“不！这全怪那个女人！文克想绞死我，这女人叫我觉得自己老了，是她把我往绞架上送。”这女人年轻美貌，抱着绝望的宿命论听凭命运摆布，为什么偏偏叫他觉得自己老？她的自暴自弃于他如醇酒，这念头又引出一串新的思绪。他同自己交起战来。脱过大险的念头竟不能给他一丝快意；在纷乱不安的沉思中，他忽然憋住了呼吸似地确信：她之所以叫他觉得老，是因为他爱她。随即他生出一种对自己的憎恨，把多年来对旁人怀有的一切狂暴刻毒的仇恨拢作一大堆，尽数倾倒在自己身上。这团乱麻般的情感压得他苦不堪言，连脑子都晕眩起来。不过，此刻他已到了自家门前。

===Original===
All the wrinkles in his face were deepened and intensified, but it was his eyes that looked most dreadful, and the Countess trembled as he entered her room. No longer were they of the steely grey of an agate, but rather seemed shot with rays of copper colour.

“What has happened?” she asked.

Then he told her something quite different from that which he had meant to tell her.

“Do you know who I am?” he asked, and his tone was one of frenzied delirium. “I am a werwolf; I suck man’s blood. Every day my hatred burns up all the blood in my veins, and every night I fill them again by sucking the blood of some human being. If men caught me, they would tear me into little bits. I will bite through your white throat, you tormenting witch!”

The Countess started as if stung, and, mad with pain and torture, cried aloud, “Kill me then! What could be better than death?”

“But I love you!” cried the voice of the man beside her, who seemed to be possessed by devils.

===Chinese===
他脸上的皱纹道道加深、分外触目，可最骇人的还是那双眼睛。他走进她的房间时，伯爵夫人打了个寒噤。那双眼不再是玛瑙似的钢灰色，倒像是迸射出一道道铜色的光芒。

“出什么事了？”她问。

于是他讲给她听的，同他本来打算讲的完全是另一回事。

“你知道我是谁吗？”他问，语调狂乱如谵语。“我是一头狼人；我吸人血。白天，我的仇恨烧干我血管里的血；夜里，我靠吸某个活人的血，再把血管重新灌满。人若是捉住我，会把我撕成碎块。我要咬穿你那白皙的喉咙，你这折磨人的妖女！”

伯爵夫人像被蜇了似的惊跳起来，痛楚与煎熬逼得她发疯，高声喊道：“那就杀了我吧！还有什么比死更好的呢？”

“可我爱你！”她身边那男人喊道，那声音仿佛鬼魔附体。

===Original===
The woman hid her face in her hands. It was the first time she had heard such a confession from that imperious mouth, and it stirred her to the depths of her nature. Her free spirit had been snatched from the world and confined in a fortress whence there was no escape. Her life was a dead thing, but the blood within her raged in dread and mysterious tumult, inflamed and excited by the power of this man. Her dead soul was afire, and there was nothing left to consume: whence then came this flame?

Mabuse left the Countess without saying another word. “I have told her enough,” he said to himself. He threw himself down on his bed, but could not sleep. He felt as if something new had come into his life, till then so steady and changeless, as if the danger which he had always been able to grasp and bring to nought had eluded him and were sinking into the icy black gulf in whose depths his life and actions were grounded. For hours he tried to grapple with this new force and subordinate it to his will, but evermore it seemed to evade him.

===Chinese===
女人双手掩面。这是她头一回从那张专横的嘴里听到这样的表白，它把她天性深处整个搅动了。她那自由不羁的灵魂，被人从这世上掳走，囚进了一座插翅难逃的堡垒。她的生命早已是死物一具，可她体内的血液却在骇人而神秘的激荡中奔涌，被这个男人的权力点燃、煽炽。她死寂的灵魂烧起来了，而它本已无可燃烧：这火焰又是从哪儿来的？

马布斯再没说一句话，离开了伯爵夫人。“我对她说得够多了。”他自语道。他一头栽到床上，却睡不着。他仿佛觉得，有什么新东西闯进了他此前一直平稳无变的生活；仿佛那危险——从前他总能一把攥住、将它化为乌有的危险——竟从他手里溜掉了，正沉入一片冰冷漆黑的深渊，而他的生命与行止，本是以这深渊之底为根基的。一连几个钟头，他试图制服这股新的力量，把它压到自己的意志之下；可它似乎总在他指间溜走。

===Original===
Then he returned to the Countess, lying fully dressed and sleepless on her bed, and he said, “We must talk matters out. Our fates are entwined, and we must go through life together. From some source or other of my existence my blood has received something which revolts against a peaceful and well-ordered life, and will not permit to others a power above its own. Thus it is that I have become, as it were, the chief of a robber horde. I have known but two states: the desire to dominate and the necessity to hate! But now you have come upon the scene. At first I thought that your spirit would be consumed in the twin flames that inspire mine, but it is not so. Hundreds have been consumed by them, but you seem to feed upon them, and they nourish you. When I am intoxicated, not forgetting my hatred, but putting it on one side for the time being, because there are more beautiful things, I often name to you one name﻿—Citopomar. Citopomar is not the outcome of a disordered fancy, the result of a fit of intoxication. It is a virgin forest in Brazil, far in the interior. It is being cultivated for me. All the money I can wring from this petty community of miserable wretches on this side of the world is being employed there. There is my country, the land in which I shall end my days. First of all, I thought of myself there with my harem. Now I know it is there I shall be with *you*. It is a forty days’ journey to the nearest human dwelling, and the human beings there could not endure life here, but they cannot be reached, for the Botocudos would not let anyone pass. It is even possible that my agents, who have been carrying out my plans, may have deceived me, and that when we arrive there we may find there is no kingdom of Citopomar. But no one can deceive me about *you*!

===Chinese===
后来他又回到伯爵夫人那里；她和衣躺在床上，毫无睡意。他说：“我们必须把话说透。我们的命运已经缠在一起，必须携手走完这一生。我这生命，不知打哪个源头起，血液里便浸了一种东西；它反叛安宁有序的生活，也容不得任何人握有比它更高的权力。正因如此，我才落到今天这般地步，成了一伙匪帮的头领。我一生只认得两种状态：统治的欲望，和仇恨的必需！可如今你登场了。起初我以为，你的魂灵会被点燃我心智的那双重火焰烧成灰烬；事实却不然。数百人已葬身其中，你却似乎拿它们当食粮，它们反倒滋养着你。每当我醺然沉醉——并没有忘掉我的仇恨，只是暂时把它搁到一边，因为世上还有更美的东西——我便常向你提起一个名字：锡托波马尔。锡托波马尔不是错乱的幻想生出的产物，也不是一阵醉狂的结果。它是巴西腹地深处的一片原始森林。它正替我开垦经营。我在世界这一端、从这伙可怜虫的小小社会里榨出来的每一分钱，都投在了那里。那是我的国度，是我了却余生的土地。起初，我想的是自己带着后宫住在那里。如今我知道，在那里与我同处的将是*你*。从那里到最近的人烟，要走四十天的路；那边的人受不了这里的日子，可谁也到不了他们那儿，因为博托库多人不放任何人过去。甚至可能，替我执行计划的那些代理人早已骗了我；等我们到了那儿，也许根本没有什么锡托波马尔王国。可是在*你*身上，谁也骗不了我！”

===Original===
“My professional life here has extended to ever-widening circles, and I could live a good deal longer under the protection of the State and in well-ordered society. Today, however, I had proof that folks are on my track, and henceforth I must act cautiously. A ship is being built for me in Genoa. I do not travel by strange ships, but sail under my own flag. The ship is to be ready on the 1st of June, and on that night we will embark. Between this and then, however, there is nearly two months to pass. I cannot rest, and until the very night of our departure I shall still be a robber chief.

“We will be wary. You must go to another house. It is quite as well guarded as this one, but if they should discover this one, they will catch you. I am probably about to leave the place, and at midnight tomorrow you will depart. Spoerri will take you to the new home.”

As incapable of resistance as of mental participation in his schemes, consumed in the devouring flames of this man’s all-powerful domination, the Countess endured his conversation and took his orders. Her fate lay in his hands.

===Chinese===
“我在此地的营生，圈子已越扩越大；凭着国家的庇护，在秩序井然的社会里，我本来还可以再活上好一阵子。然而今天我拿到了证据：有人已经盯上我的踪迹，从今往后我必须步步谨慎。热那亚正在替我造一艘船。我从不搭别人的船，航行只挂我自己的旗。船定于6月1日完工，当晚我们就登船。可在那之前，还有将近两个月要挨过去。我闲不住；离我们动身的那一夜之前，我依然要当我的匪首。”

“我们须得处处提防。你得搬到另一处宅子去。那里的戒备同这里一样森严；可万一这里被他们发现，你便会被逮住。我多半就要离开此地了；明天午夜，你动身。施佩里会送你去新居。”

伯爵夫人既无力反抗，也无力在精神上参与他的图谋，被这个男人那无所不在的支配所吐出的噬人烈焰吞没，只默默听他说话，领受他的吩咐。她的命运，攥在他手里。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===
At nine o’clock next morning Mabuse was at Count Told’s villa. As he was now endeavouring to hold himself ready for flight at any moment, he wanted to bring this matter of the Count to an end.

He had desired him to drink, and for some days now Told had been drinking, in passionate abandonment. Mabuse looked at him in silence. When Told was intoxicated he said to him, “You are a person without the slightest power of resistance. Where is your razor?”

In a thick voice Told answered that it was on the washstand.

“Is it sharp?” said Mabuse with a peculiar intonation. “Sharp enough?” he repeated with an emphasis so marked that it seemed as if he wanted to hammer an idea into the Count’s head.

===Chinese===
第二天上午九点，马布斯到了托尔德伯爵的别墅。他如今正力求让自己随时准备出逃，所以想把伯爵这桩事做个了结。

他早有意图让他纵饮，这几天托尔德也确实沉溺于狂饮，喝得不能自拔。马布斯默然打量着他。等托尔德醉了，他才开口：“你这个人，没有丝毫抗拒之力。你的剃刀在哪儿？”

托尔德声音含混地答道，剃刀在盥洗台上。

“它锋利吗？”马布斯用一种异样的声调问。“够锋利吗？”他又问了一遍，语气那么重，仿佛要把一个念头硬锤进伯爵的脑袋。

===Original===
Mabuse took it up, seized a sheet of paper and made a sharp clean cut in it. Then he said threateningly, “Yes, it is sharp enough.” Thereupon he laid the razor aside, but did not return it to its case. He called the servant in, saying to him, “The Count’s condition is not so good as it was. He is drinking brandy with his Tokay. I have no objection to a little light Burgundy, but these strong spirits are not to be allowed. You must take away what is left in the bottle. Your master will … now … go … to sleep!” He uttered the last words in a long-drawn-out, menacing tone. Then he went out of the room in front of the footman, and left the house.

Half an hour later, Count Told, not knowing what he was doing, cut his throat from ear to ear. He had a feeling as if something in his throat were preventing him from enjoying some great happiness, and he wanted to remove the hindrance.

At two o’clock a message came from Mabuse to ask how the Count was getting on. The footman said he was asleep, but he would go and look at him to make sure. Then he found him bathed in blood, where he had fallen from his armchair to the ground, his body now cold in death. The doctor’s messenger came into the room, looked at the corpse, and went back to report to his master.

===Chinese===
马布斯拿起剃刀，抓过一张纸，干净利落地划开一道口子。随后他威吓似的说：“是的，它够锋利了。”说完他把剃刀搁在一边，却没有放回刀匣。他把仆人叫了进来，对他说：“伯爵的病情不如从前了。他往托考伊酒里兑白兰地喝。淡一点的勃艮第酒我不反对，可这些烈性酒绝不能允许。你必须把瓶里剩下的酒拿走。你的主人要……现在……去……睡了！”最后几个字，他拖得又长又充满威胁。然后他走在男仆前面出了房门，离开了这所宅子。

半小时后，托尔德伯爵在浑然不知自己所作所为的状态下，割开了自己的喉咙，从这只耳朵到那只耳朵。他只觉得喉咙里有什么东西堵着，不让他享受某种莫大的幸福，而他想除掉这个障碍。

两点钟，马布斯那边来了口信，问候伯爵的近况。男仆说他睡着呢，不过他去瞧瞧也好放心。随后他发现伯爵浸在血泊之中——他从扶手椅跌倒在地，躯体已经冰冷死透。医生的信使走进房间，看了看尸体，回去向主人复命了。

===Original===
The manservant did not know what to do. Since none of the Count’s relatives were in the neighbourhood and he did not know the Countess’s address, he felt he must inform the police first of all. But then, again, he was not sure which was the right office to go to to give such information, and it occurred to him that the State Attorney, Herr von Wenk, was an acquaintance of his master’s and had asked after him recently, so he drove to Munich, sought out the lawyer, and told his story.

“Was the Count at home then all the time?” asked Wenk.

“Yes, sir, all the time.”

“Then why did you tell me on the telephone that the Count had gone on a journey?”

“The doctor told me that on account of my master’s state no one was to be allowed to see him, and I must tell anybody who inquired that he had gone away. My master saw nobody but his doctor.”

===Chinese===
男仆没了主意。伯爵的亲眷没有一个住在近处，他又不知道伯爵夫人的地址，便觉得首先必须报警。可转念一想，又拿不准报这种案该进哪个衙门，这时他想起检察官冯·文克先生是主人的熟人，近来还打听过他，于是他驱车前往慕尼黑，寻到这位司法官，把事情讲了一遍。

“这么说，伯爵当时一直在家？”文克问。

“是的，先生，一直在家。”

“那你为什么在电话里对我说，伯爵出门旅行去了？”

“医生吩咐我，鉴于主人的身体状况，任何人都不许见他，凡是来打听的，我都必须说他走了。主人除了他的医生，谁也不见。”

===Original===
“What was the doctor’s name?”

“I never heard his name, sir. I don’t know it.”

Then Wenk remembered that Privy Councillor Wendel had given him a letter to Dr. Mabuse, and that the Count had used Wenk’s own telephone to make an appointment with this doctor.

===Chinese===
“那位医生姓什么？”

“我从没听人叫过他的名字，先生。我不知道。”

这时文克想起来了：枢密顾问温德尔曾交给他一封写给马布斯博士的信，而伯爵当时正是用文克自己的电话，同这位医生约定了就诊。

===Original===
Wenk trembled as, struck by the horror of a strange suspicion, he described to the footman the figure of Dr. Mabuse as he had seen it recently at the Four Seasons Hall. He spoke of him as a tall man, stooping slightly, without beard or moustache, with a broad face and big nose and large grey eyes. When the man said, “Yes, he looked exactly like that,” Wenk grew pale as death. In a moment all the disconnected impressions, hazy ideas, vague recollections, half-defined thoughts and images which had been partially obliterated, but not altogether lost, gathered together in his mind. When Wenk had the hall emptied, why had Dr. Mabuse not asked the reason for this measure? Why had he not inquired whether he could continue his experiments at another time? Why had Wenk, who had seen a man whose back he had recognized go into the hall, not found him again inside? Why had the two men who would not obey the detective’s order to move on, suddenly done as they were told immediately Mabuse appeared? Why had Mabuse’s eyes, in the brief moment he had looked into them, affected him so powerfully, as if they sought to read something that lay hidden in his very soul and was now almost forgotten?

He dismissed Count Told’s servant, and then tried to find Dr. Mabuse’s number in the telephone book, but it was not given there. Yet Mabuse had a telephone, for the Count had rung him up from this very house. The Privy Councillor knew the number.

===Chinese===
文克打了个寒噤——一个离奇的猜疑带来的惊怖攫住了他——他向男仆描述起马布斯博士的模样，正是他不久前在四季厅（Four Seasons Hall）见到的那副身形。他说那是个高个子男人，微微佝偻，没有蓄胡子，脸盘宽阔，鼻子肥大，一双灰色的大眼睛。当那人说出“是的，他看上去正是那样”时，文克的脸白得像死人。刹那间，那些支离破碎的印象、模糊的想法、隐约的回忆、半成形的思绪与图景——虽已部分磨灭、却并未全然丧失——一齐汇聚到他心头。当文克下令清空大厅时，马布斯博士为什么没有问一问此举的缘由？他为什么没有询问能否改日继续他的实验？文克亲眼看见一个自己认得背影的人走进大厅，为什么进去之后就再也找不到了？那两个不肯听从便衣侦探命令走开的人，为什么马布斯一露面就立刻乖乖照办了？还有马布斯的那双眼睛——就在他望进那双眼睛的短短一瞬，为什么对他产生了那样强大的作用，仿佛它们要读取某种深藏在他灵魂最深处、如今已几乎被忘却的东西？

他打发走托尔德伯爵的仆人，随后想在电话簿里找马布斯博士的号码，可簿子上没有登载。然而马布斯是有电话的，因为伯爵恰恰是在这所宅子里给他打的电话。枢密顾问知道号码。

===Original===
When Wenk, having obtained the telephone number from Herr Wendel, gave it, there was no reply. Ringing up the exchange, he was told that the telephone had been disconnected. He asked who had had it three weeks before, but this could not be ascertained at once.

Again Wenk rang up the Councillor. Dr. Mabuse had changed his number; did he happen to know his address? Wendel could give no information. He only knew the telephone number, and spoke to him on the phone. Wenk then asked at the Police Registry Office for Dr. Mabuse’s address, but the name was not to be found anywhere among the arrivals in Munich, and when, at the Municipal Registry, all the old telephone books were searched to find Mabuse, he was again unsuccessful.

Thereupon Wenk repaired to the manager of the telephone exchange in order to make a more thorough search. The manager took him to the inquiry-room, where two young women were employed, and he asked them to look again for the number he had telephoned about.

“What were you wanting?” asked the elder of the two, and Wenk explained that he was seeking the address of a Dr. Mabuse, who three weeks before had a telephone number that did not appear in the directory.

===Chinese===
文克从温德尔先生那里拿到电话号码后拨了过去，却无人应答。他又打给电话局，被告知那部电话已经拆机。他问三个星期前这个号码在谁名下，可这一点无法立刻查清。

文克又给枢密顾问挂了电话。马布斯博士换了号码；他可巧知道他的住址吗？温德尔提供不出任何信息。他只知道电话号码，平素也是在电话里同他交谈。文克于是到警察登记处去打听马布斯博士的地址，可这名字在慕尼黑的来客登记里根本无处可寻；等再到市政登记处，翻遍了所有旧电话簿查找马布斯，仍旧一无所获。

于是文克去找电话局局长，要把这件事查个底朝天。局长把他带到查号室，那里雇着两名年轻女子，他请她们再查一查他来电话问过的那个号码。

“您要查什么？”两人中年长的那位问。文克说明来意：他在找一位马布斯博士的地址，此人三个星期前名下有一个电话号码，却没有登在电话簿上。

===Original===
The girl said she could not find it anywhere, whereupon Wenk returned to the manager with this information. He declared this was something quite unheard of, and himself accompanied Wenk to the inquiry office. He, too, made a search with the clerks, but could find nothing. While the manager was looking through the lists without success, an idea occurred to Wenk, and when he was informed that no one of the name of Mabuse had been entered on the list at all for the last year, he asked the manager for the telephone number and address of a man named Poldringer. As he uttered this name he saw the elder girl start and then immediately recover herself, but an instant later she told him rudely that there were ever so many Poldringers in Munich, and without the Christian name and the exact address she could not furnish any information.

Then Wenk turned to the manager, saying politely, “I am sorry to have to put you to some inconvenience, but I must take both these ladies into custody!”

He at once took up a position between the girls and the telephone. “Be so good as to sit down on these chairs till the detectives arrive; you here, and your companion there!” The elder of the girls turned as white as a sheet. The other blushed, and then began to cry. Wenk said, turning to her, “It is only a formality. If you behave properly, this matter can be carried through without exciting notice, and it is probable that it will not be long before the mystery is cleared up.” Then he rang up the Criminal Investigation Department and asked for three detectives.

===Chinese===
那姑娘说哪儿都查不到，文克便带着这个消息回去见局长。局长声称这是闻所未闻的事，亲自陪文克到查号处。他也同职员们一起查了，却什么也没找到。趁局长翻检名册一无所获的当口，文克灵机一动；在得知整整一年里名单上压根没有登记过任何姓马布斯的人之后，他向局长要一个名叫波尔德林格的人的电话号码和地址。说出这个名字时，他瞥见那年长的姑娘猛地一怔，随即立刻定住神，可下一瞬她就粗鲁地告诉他：慕尼黑姓波尔德林格的多得很，没有教名和确切住址，她无法提供任何情况。

于是文克转向局长，客气地说：“很抱歉要给您添些麻烦，可我必须把这两位女士一并拘押起来！”

他随即在两个姑娘和电话机之间站定。“劳驾，请在这几把椅子上坐等侦探到来；你坐这儿，你的同伴坐那儿！”年长的姑娘脸白得像一张纸。另一个涨红了脸，接着哭了起来。文克转头对她说：“这只是例行手续。只要你们规矩配合，这件事可以办得不惊动外人，而且谜底很可能不久就会揭开。”说完他给刑事调查部挂了电话，要派三名侦探来。

===Original===
The manager looked through the list of Poldringers, for there were many entries under the name, most of them being tradespeople. One, of whom no further information was given, was living in the Xenienstrasse, and another, without any professional status, in the Ludwigstrasse.

The girls were given in charge, and Wenk went to the Ludwigstrasse. He came to a lodging-house, looked at the surroundings and inspected the inside, and then went to the Xenienstrasse. Then suddenly his heart stood still, for in the Xenienstrasse, at the address given under the name of Poldringer in the telephone list, he saw on a professional plate the words

> **Dr. Mabuse**, 

 Neurologist.

He hastened away, merely noting the numbers of the houses standing near. The street consisted of detached villas. A mist swam before his eyes, and the blood pounded in his pulses; there was a sound as of pistols in his ears. He had his man. No, he had not got him, but at last he knew who he was!

===Chinese===
局长翻检波尔德林格这个姓氏的名单，名下条目众多，大多是商贩之流。其中一条没有任何进一步的说明，住在克塞尼恩大街；另一条没有职业身份，住在路德维希大街。

两个姑娘被交人看押，文克前往路德维希大街。他到了一处出租公寓，看了看四邻环境，察看了内部，然后又转去克塞尼恩大街。突然间他的心脏骤然停跳——在克塞尼恩大街，在电话名单上以波尔德林格之名登记的那个地址，他看见一块职业铭牌上写着

> **马布斯博士**，

 神经科医生。

他匆匆离去，只把近旁几所宅子的门牌号记了下来。这条街上全是独栋别墅。一片薄雾在他眼前浮动，血液在脉搏里擂击，耳中响起一阵手枪击发似的声响。他逮住那个人了。不，他还没有逮住他，但他终于知道他是谁了！

===Original===
Before doing anything else he drove to the prison, for the time Cara Carozza had demanded had now expired, and what she might tell him would probably set the seal upon the success of his enterprise.

===Chinese===
在做任何别的事情之前，他先驱车去了监狱，因为卡拉·卡罗扎要求的期限现在已经届满，而她可能吐露的话，多半会给他这桩行动的成功盖上最后的印章。

---
===Original===
Early that morning, when it was time for the warder of the women’s prison to make his first round, the door of Cara’s cell was opened. The dancer was still asleep. She was shaken by the shoulder and, awaking quickly, found the warder bending over her, yet it was not the warder, it was Spoerri. Surely she was dreaming? But no, she was still in prison. How came Spoerri to her bedside? She put her hand to her eyes to shut out the vision, and yet she knew in her heart it was reality. Spoerri was standing there. He said to her:

“Surely you know that I am in league with the warder?” She nodded. “Then you know, too, that he told me what happened yesterday when the State Attorney came to see you?”

“What did he tell you?” the girl asked breathlessly.

“That you are going to betray the master!”

===Chinese===
那天清早，到了女子监狱看守查第一遍牢的时候，卡拉的牢门开了。舞女还在酣睡。有人摇她的肩膀，她很快醒了过来，发现看守正俯身对着自己——然而那不是看守，是施佩里。莫非她是在做梦？不，她分明还在牢里。施佩里怎么会到她的床边来？她抬手遮住眼睛，想把幻影挡在外面，可她心里明白，这是现实。施佩里就站在那里。他对她说：

“你总该如何道，我同那看守是串通一气的吧？”她点了点头。“那么你也该如何道，昨天检察官来见你时发生的事，他已经讲给我听了？”

“他都对你们说了些什么？”姑娘屏着气问。

“说你要出卖主人！”

===Original===
The dancer sprang out of bed. “Who says so?” she shouted.

“Please don’t talk so loudly. The warder says so.”

“It is a lie.”

“The warder would have no interest in lying.”

===Chinese===
舞女从床上一跃而起。“这是谁说的？”她嚷道。

“请别这么大声嚷。是看守说的。”

“那是谎话。”

“看守撒谎可捞不到半点好处。”

===Original===
“Did he tell the doctor so?” she asked anxiously, and Spoerri lied in answer:

“Yes, of course he did, and the doctor sent me to you.”

“It is a lie,” cried Cara again, on the verge of tears; “I was going to save him!”

“How can you prove that?”

===Chinese===
“这话他对医生也说了？”她焦急地问。施佩里撒谎答道：

“说了，当然说了，医生还就是为这个派我来的。”

“这是谎话，”卡拉又喊起来，已到落泪的边缘；“我是要救他！”

“这你怎么证明？”

===Original===
“I was going to save him, I tell you. Spoerri, danger is threatening him.”

“Danger is always threatening him. That’s mere nonsense. Can you prove what you say?”
