Someone stepped on to the stage.

“Do you know this gentleman, Princess?”

“Yes, it is Baron Prewitz!”

“Is the Baron’s being known to the Princess sufficient for the company to rule out the idea of any private understanding between him and myself?”

There were cries of “Certainly!”

===Chinese===

“这一套就到此为止吧。”韦尔特曼说。“现在，如果诸位允许，我们来演示几例意志的转移。我想请一位先生上台来。”

有人走上了台。

“这位先生您认识吗，亲王夫人？”

“认识，这是普雷维茨男爵！”

“男爵既为亲王夫人所识，是否足以让诸位排除他与我二人之间存有任何私下默契的可能？”

四下响起“当然！”的喊声。

===Original===

Meanwhile Weltmann was writing on a table something which it was impossible for the Baron to read. Then he threw the small writing-block down to the company below. He looked at Prewitz, quite quietly, for a short time. Then Prewitz, with stealthy movements, left the platform and went slowly and cautiously from chair to chair, looking everyone in the face. Weltmann called out, “I should like four ladies or gentlemen to come up here quickly. Be quick, please!”

Several started up. Three gentlemen and one lady remained on the platform, the others returning to their seats. Weltmann placed them round the table, pointing at a pack of cards lying there.

“Are this lady and these gentlemen known to the company?”

The Princess nodded, and there was a chorus of “Yes!” Meanwhile Prewitz was advancing towards Wenk. Again Weltmann wrote for some time upon a memorandum, casting from time to time his glance upon the four sitting at the table. Suddenly one of them said, “Shall it be vingt-et-un or poker?” Weltmann went on writing.

===Chinese===

与此同时，韦尔特曼在一张桌子上写着什么，男爵是绝无可能看到的。写罢，他把那本小小的写字板抛给了台下众人。他静静地、直直地看了普雷维茨一小会儿。随后普雷维茨蹑手蹑脚地挪动身子离开了平台，从一把椅子挪到另一把椅子，缓慢而小心地端详每个人的脸。韦尔特曼高声道：“我想请四位女士或先生快些上台来。请快点！”

好几个人站了起来。三位先生和一位女士留在台上，其余人各自归座。韦尔特曼安排他们围桌而坐，指着桌上摆着的一副纸牌。

“这位女士和这几位先生，在座诸君都认识吗？”

亲王夫人点点头，四座齐声应道“认识！”这时普雷维茨正朝文克走来。韦尔特曼又在记事本上写了好一阵，不时抬眼瞥一瞥围桌而坐的四人。忽然其中一人开口：“玩二十一点还是扑克？”韦尔特曼继续写着。

===Original===

They decided upon vingt-et-un, and at once began to play.

“We want one more,” said the lady.

“I am just coming, dear lady,” said Weltmann. “You take the bank!”

By this time Prewitz had come to Wenk. He looked at him steadfastly for a while, then suddenly seized his left breast-pocket and drew out the revolver, placing himself at Wenk’s side, the weapon in his hand.

Weltmann said from the stage, “That is because you are so incautious as to carry a loaded revolver in your pocket! Please”﻿—he turned to the audience﻿—“be so good as to read what I have written there!”

===Chinese===

他们选定了二十一点，当即开局。

“还缺一个人。”那位女士说。

“我这就来，亲爱的女士。”韦尔特曼说。“您先坐庄！”

这时普雷维茨已经走到文克跟前。他直勾勾地盯了他好一会儿，然后突然抓住他的左胸口袋，掏出那把左轮手枪，随即握着武器在文克身旁坐了下来。

韦尔特曼在台上说：“这是因为您太不小心，居然在口袋里揣一把上了膛的左轮手枪！请——”他转向观众，“——哪位行个好，把我写在那上面的念一念！”

===Original===

Someone read out: “The Baron is to go along the first row, chair by chair, and where he finds someone with a loaded revolver in his pocket, he is to take it out and sit beside him with it.”

They all clapped their hands, a proceeding which Weltmann, by a brief gesture, stopped. He left off writing, handed the block down to the Princess, and sat down with the cardplayers.

“Page one!” he said to his hostess. She read it to herself, then handed it to her right-hand neighbour and looked anxiously towards the stage, where the following incidents were taking place. Weltmann won game after game. Sometimes he looked away from the table, and then it seemed to Wenk as if he were beckoning him to come up. Wenk knew it must be a delusion, due to some effect of the light striking Weltmann’s eye, but none the less he felt uneasy. The idea occurred to him to yield, and go up so that he might face this man at close quarters and make sure that those lightning glances had no reference to him. “Yet that would be very foolish!” he said to himself, striving to get rid of the impulse.

Suddenly, without a single word having been spoken, one of the players leaned back, saying in a clear ringing voice, as if speaking aloud in a dream, “What have I just done? I had twenty-one, and then someone spoke with my voice, and said, ‘I have lost again.’ ”

He seized the cards he had thrown aside, and showed an ace, a knave and a ten.

===Chinese===

有人念道：“男爵须沿第一排座椅逐一走过去，在谁的口袋里发现上了膛的左轮手枪，就把枪掏出来，持枪坐到那人身边。”

众人齐齐鼓掌，韦尔特曼做了个简短的手势止住了他们。他写完了，把写字板递下去给亲王夫人，随即在打牌的人中间坐下了。

“第一页！”他对女主人说。她默读了一遍，然后传给右手边的邻座，一面不安地朝台上张望，那里正上演着如下一幕。韦尔特曼一局接一局地赢。有时他的目光离开牌桌，文克便觉得他仿佛在召唤自己上台。文克知道这必定是错觉，是灯光照进韦尔特曼眼里折出的某种光影所致，但他仍觉不安。他一度起了顺从的念头，想走上去，与这个男人近距离对峙，弄清那些闪电似的目光究竟与他有没有干系。“可那样就太蠢了！”他对自己说，竭力摆脱这股冲动。

突然，谁也不曾开口说一句话，一名牌手向后一仰，用清越的嗓音说了起来，仿佛在梦中大声呓语：“我刚才做了什么？我手里是二十一点，可接着有人借了我的嗓子，说：‘我又输了。’”

他抓起方才扔掉的牌，摊开：一张爱司、一张杰克、一张十点。

===Original===

“Too late!” said Weltmann, who was holding the bank. Wenk put his hands to his head. He had already lived through such a scene once before. When was it, where, and to whom did it occur? He cudgelled his brains to remember. The image of it stood out distinctly, but it stood apart from any suggestion of the time and place and person.

From the recesses of his mind a form seemed to emerge simultaneously with his groping efforts to recover the recollections he sought. A form﻿—was it a human being, a lifeless column, a monster? He could not say which﻿ ﻿… but then the form was bleeding somewhere, and now Wenk saw, through the misty fantasies of these recent occurrences, that it had a mouth, and that this mouth suddenly uttered, in clear staccato tones, the name “Tsi﻿—nan﻿—fu!”

Wenk now distinctly recollected having heard this name from the lips of the old Professor who was none other than the Dr. Mabuse on whose account he had come to Berlin. “Dr. Mab﻿ ﻿… , Dr. Mab﻿ ﻿… ,” whispered the secret voices. Wenk tried to call to mind the features of the old Professor, but he could not recollect them clearly. Only the mouth which had uttered the name of the Chinese town with such strange impressiveness was distinct to his vision.

===Chinese===

“太迟了！”正坐庄的韦尔特曼说。文克双手抱住头。这样的一幕他从前经历过一回。那是什么时候？在哪里？落在谁身上？他绞尽脑汁去回忆。那一幕的影像清晰凸出，却与任何时间、地点、人物的线索都隔绝着。

就在他摸索着寻回记忆的同时，一个形体仿佛从他心底深处浮现出来。一个形体——它是人？是无生命的立柱？还是怪物？他说不上来……可接着那形体在某处流着血，而现在，透过近事织成的迷雾般的幻象，文克看见它有一张嘴，这张嘴突然用清晰顿挫的音节，吐出了那个名字：“济——南——府！”

文克此刻清清楚楚地记起来了：他曾从那位老教授口中听过这个名字，而那老教授不是别人，正是害他专程来柏林的马布斯博士。“马布斯博士……马布斯博士……”那些隐秘的声音低语着。文克努力回想老教授的容貌，却怎么也想不真切。只有那张嘴，那张以何等奇异的力量道出这座中国城市名字的嘴，还清晰地浮现在他眼前。

===Original===

“Now why,” said Wenk to himself in the midst of the images raised in him by these recollections, “why should I think at this moment of the pseudo-Professor? Why do I think of the Professor, and not of Mabuse under another form, his real form, such as I saw him that evening in reality in the Four Seasons Hall? Mabuse as a hypnotist? What audacity! As a hypnotist appearing in public? Had Mabuse the same disconcerting capability as Weltmann, and had Weltmann the same dark background of crime as Mabuse?” he asked himself. His thoughts grew ever more remote, more indistinct and unreal. They were no longer thoughts﻿—they were misty images which had arisen in his fantasy under the compelling power of those eyes yonder. He sought to fix his eyes on Weltmann, striving to picture him with a reddish beard, such as Mabuse had appeared possessed of when he first encountered him.

And then suddenly Wenk realized how it was he felt some unmistakable connection with the player there who threw away his cards although he held twenty-one and must undoubtedly have won the game. These words were familiar to him from the story told by the murdered Hull. They were written upon the first page of the notebook stolen from him by Mabuse’s chauffeur when he left him that night in the Schleissheim Park, and he had copied them down word for word after his first talk with Hull. Yes, the bleeding form was that of Hull, and it drooped like a weeping-willow over Wenk’s spirit. The blood-besprinkled leaves whispered ever “It is I, Hull! It is I, Hull!”

Then it seemed as if in the mists which continued to gather in ever-varying shapes in Wenk’s brain there grew and stood out, as the bone stands out from its tissues in the Röntgen-ray photographs, a dark nucleus, a central, death-endowed essence, something stony﻿ ﻿… something black﻿ ﻿… a man.

===Chinese===

“那么为什么，”文克在这些回忆激起的重重幻影中间暗自思忖，“为什么我此刻偏偏想起那个假教授？为什么想的是教授，而不是换了另一副模样——他真实的模样——的马布斯，就像那天晚上我在四季厅亲眼见到的那样？马布斯当催眠师？好大的胆子！以催眠师的身份公开登台？难道马布斯有着和韦尔特曼一样令人方寸大乱的本领，而韦尔特曼也有着和马布斯一样黑暗的犯罪底细？”他自问着。他的思绪越飘越远，越来越模糊、失真。那已不再是思绪——而是在那边那双眼睛的逼视之下，从他幻想里升起的重重雾影。他竭力把目光钉在韦尔特曼身上，努力想象他蓄着一把红胡子的模样——他初次遭遇马布斯时，那人正是那样一部胡须。

接着文克猛然醒悟，自己何以与台上那个牌手之间感到一种割不断的联系——那人明明握着二十一点、必胜无疑，却把牌扔了。这些话他耳熟能详，出自惨遭谋杀的赫尔生前的讲述。它们写在那本笔记本的首页上——那晚在施莱斯海姆公园，马布斯的司机把他撂在那里时偷走的本子；他第一次与赫尔谈话之后，曾把那些话一字不差地抄录下来。是的，那个流血的形体就是赫尔，他像一株垂柳，凄然垂覆在文克的心神上。溅血的柳叶一遍遍低语：“是我，赫尔！是我，赫尔！”

这时，在文克脑中不断聚拢、变幻出千形万状的雾气深处，仿佛有什么东西生长着、凸现出来，如同伦琴射线照片里从血肉组织中透出的骨头：一个黑暗的内核，一种居中的、被死亡浸透的本质，某种石质的……某种漆黑的……一个人。

===Original===

The Princess handed him Weltmann’s block, and he thrust these ideas away somewhat, though he had to struggle to see the words. Then he read: “The banker wins every game. If one of the players has a better card than he who holds the bank, he is incapable of holding them against him.”

He had hardly read this when Weltmann, speaking from the midst of his game in a voice which seemed to strike Wenk to earth, said, “Read the second page!” Wenk turned the page in affright. He read, “Under the hypnotist’s influence one of the players tries to cheat, by dealing himself an ace. He is caught in the act!”

Then the blood rushed to Wenk’s heart, and like molten lava it coursed along his veins. His eyes were fixed and glassy, and his trembling fingers let fall the block. A horrible certainty burst upon him. That was the secret of Count Told’s fall! Mabuse had subconsciously forced him to cheat, to ruin him in the eyes of the wife whom Mabuse desired to possess! That was why he had seen the Countess leaving Mabuse’s house that night. Mabuse it was who had killed her husband.

What Mabuse had written down occurred on the stage. The lady, who had in the meantime taken over the bank, dealt the cards so as to cheat and was caught in the act. Thereupon Weltmann brought the experiment to an end. He released the four subjects from their hypnotic state, and, disturbed and still dreamy-eyed, they sought their seats once more.

===Chinese===

亲王夫人把韦尔特曼的写字板递给了他，他把那些念头勉强推开，尽管他得费些劲才看得清上面的字。他读到：“庄家局局皆赢。若哪家手中的牌好过坐庄者，他便无力持牌与之相抗。”

他刚读完，韦尔特曼便从牌局正中开口，那嗓音仿佛一击把文克打翻在地：“读第二页！”文克惊惶地翻过一页。他读到：“在催眠师的影响下，一名牌手试图作弊，给自己发了一张爱司。他当场被抓获！”

热血猛地涌上文克的心头，像熔化的岩浆顺着血管奔流。他双目发直、呆滞如玻璃，颤抖的手指松开了写字板。一个可怕的确定性轰然砸中他。这就是托尔德伯爵身败名裂的秘密！马布斯在潜意识里强迫他作弊，让他在马布斯欲据为己有的那个妻子眼中身败名裂！所以他那晚才看见伯爵夫人从马布斯家里出来。杀害她丈夫的，正是马布斯。

马布斯写下的情形在台上应验了。方才接手坐庄的那位女士发牌作弊，当场被抓住。于是韦尔特曼结束了实验。他把四名受试者从催眠状态中解脱出来，他们心神不定、眼中犹带迷离，又摸索着回到了各自的座位上。

===Original===

Weltmann looked down at Wenk, and the latter knew without a doubt that he was Mabuse. The suddenness of the discovery paralysed him for the moment, and he struggled to regain calm and self-control. Had he been enticed into a snare? Was the Hungarian police superintendent appointed as a decoy? Was this whole place, so far removed from other dwellings, and this assembly merely an ambush arranged on *his* account?

Slowly he fought the matter out. He stood between two poles. Either all around were in league with Mabuse, and in that case there was no hope of escape, and what he was now going through was merely the preparation of a revenge which could only end with his death, or else it was merely by chance that he found himself in a company in which Mabuse also appeared accidentally. It might well be that Mabuse was a Hungarian. He might also have been a barrister in Budapest formerly, for his relations with the Privy Councillor Wendel proved that he had had a twofold career. It was not, therefore, to be assumed straight away that he and this criminal could not have met by accident. The next question Wenk asked himself was whether Mabuse recognized him, and he told himself that it must be so, for Mabuse had seen him both at Schramm’s and at the Four Seasons Hall; that was certain. But could this man be so foolhardy and so certain of himself that in spite of that he could represent before Wenk’s eyes, with a devilish mockery, what he had just seen occurring on the tiny stage? If so, it provided the solution to all the enigmatic acts with which he had concealed his crimes.

The help of the police was quite out of the question, for Wenk did not even know where he was. But how would it be to let the Prince into the secret, and get help in the company itself to secure the murderer? He could only do it if he were quite sure of the company, otherwise it was doomed to failure from the start. He knew from experience that this master criminal was always surrounded by a bodyguard of accomplices, and that they were people who shrank from no devilish deed. Around him there must be many of Mabuse’s confederates. Should Wenk, as if accidentally, make his way to some door and escape under cover of the darkness, leaving Mabuse to be dealt with at a later time, when he was better prepared to accomplish his overthrow﻿ ﻿… or should he try unobserved to find a telephone in the house and summon the police? But then again, where were they to come to?

===Chinese===

韦尔特曼低头望向文克，而后者已毫不怀疑地知道：他就是马布斯。这发现来得猝不及防，一时间他浑身瘫软，只能奋力找回镇定与自持。自己是不是被诱进了圈套？那个匈牙利警察总监是不是被派来当诱饵的？这处远离其他人家的地方、这群宾客，会不会恰恰是专为*他*一人布下的埋伏？

他慢慢地把这件事想了个透。他站在两个极端之间。要么，四周的人全与马布斯串通一气——那样的话逃生无望，他此刻经历的一切不过是复仇的开场，而那复仇只能以他的死告终；要么，他只是碰巧落进一个马布斯同样偶然现身的宾客圈子里。马布斯完全可能是个匈牙利人。他从前也完全可能在布达佩斯当过律师，因为他与枢密顾问温德尔的往来证明他有过双重生涯。因此，并不能立刻断言他与这个罪犯就不可能是偶然相遇。文克问自己的下一个问题是：马布斯认出他没有？他自答：必定认出来了，因为马布斯在施拉姆餐馆和四季厅都见过他；这一点确凿无疑。可是，这个人能这般有恃无恐、这般自信，竟不顾这一层，当着文克的眼睛、带着魔鬼般的嘲弄，把他方才在小台上目睹的情形重演一遍吗？倘若如此，那些用来遮掩罪行的费解之举便全部有了解答。

求助于警察是万万不能了，因为文克连自己身在何处都不知道。可不可以把秘密透露给亲王，在这群宾客内部寻求助力、把凶手拿住？只有当他确信满座宾客都靠得住时才能这么干，否则从一开始就注定失败。他从经验中深知，这个罪犯首脑身边总有一帮党羽护卫，而且都是些无所不敢为的狠角色。他四周必定埋伏着不少马布斯的同党。文克是该装作无意间挪到哪扇门边，趁着夜色脱身，把马布斯留待日后收拾——等他准备得更充分、更有把握将他扳倒的时候……还是该设法不被察觉地在宅子里找一部电话、把警察召来？可是再说，警察又该往哪里赶呢？

===Original===

“Isn’t it remarkable, Herr von Wenk? Have you ever seen anything like it before?” said Vörös.

Wenk had heard the question, but he was so preoccupied with his own train of thought as to forget to answer it enthusiastically, as he had intended to do. In the torrent of ideas and possibilities which rushed through his mind he forgot his resolution. Vörös gave him a hasty glance, and just at that moment Weltmann asked for fresh assistants.

Wenk, coming to a sudden resolve, pulled himself together and calmly and boldly ascended the stage, the very first to respond. Far better to look the wild beast in the face than be behind him! Then he noticed that Baron Prewitz, whom everybody had forgotten, followed him up. Stepping forward as before, automatically, he came after him, still holding the revolver.

“You don’t venture into my domain without protection, I see, Herr von Wenk,” smiled the hypnotist.

“That is just sarcasm,” said Wenk to himself; “he knows who I am!”

===Chinese===

“真是不可思议吧，冯·文克先生？您从前见过这样的场面吗？”沃勒什说。

文克听见了这句问话，但他满脑子都在转自己的心事，竟忘了按原先的打算热情作答。在心头奔涌的种种念头与可能性里，他把自己的决心忘了个干净。沃勒什匆匆瞥了他一眼，而恰在此刻，韦尔特曼在招呼新的助手了。

文克蓦地打定主意，振作精神，沉着而大胆地登上了台——他是第一个响应的。直面猛兽，总比把后背亮给猛兽强！随即他发觉，早被众人忘掉的普雷维茨男爵跟了上来。他和先前一样，不由自主地亦步亦趋，手里仍握着那把左轮手枪。

“看来您不敢不带护卫就闯进我的领地啊，冯·文克先生。”催眠师含笑道。

“这就是讥讽，”文克暗想；“他知道我是谁！”

===Original===

Wenk merely bowed, as much as to say that in Rome he did as Rome does. He was now standing next the hypnotist, and each took the other’s measure. Wenk had pursued this werwolf with vindictive fury because in him he saw the enemy of all that could heal and restore the nation. When he stood there on the platform with him, isolated for a moment from all the rest, he felt as if they were two great powers going in opposite directions. To his mind it was no longer the conflict of good and evil; it was the struggle of man to man; and, oppressed as he felt, he still had something almost like confidence in the chivalry of his opponent﻿ ﻿… a confidence that rested upon an impelling yet hardly perceptible instinct: both were staking their lives on the result of the struggle. Each was directing a fierce attack upon the other, yet both must make allowances in this last supreme moment.

“If only I could sleep!” thought Wenk with an inward yearning.

He looked closely into Weltmann’s eyes, taking in all his features. His was a powerful, muscular figure, and in imagination Wenk divested the face of its false moustache, eyebrows and wig, seeing beneath it the smooth-shaven, well-formed cranium of Dr. Mabuse. Wenk would have recognized him now beneath all his disguises. He gazed at him calmly, and the other’s glance flickered. The large grey eyes seemed to withdraw into their own fiery depths.

===Chinese===

文克只躬了躬身，那意思无非是入乡随俗。此刻他站在催眠师身旁，两人彼此掂着对方的斤两。文克之所以怀着不共戴天的怒火追猎这个狼人，是因为他在他身上看到了一切能疗救、复兴民族之物的仇敌。当他与他一同站在台上、一时间与其余众人隔绝开来时，他觉得自己仿佛面对着两股背道而驰的强大力量。在他心目中，这已不再是善与恶的冲突，而是人与人之间的搏斗；尽管胸中郁结，他对对手的骑士风度竟仍怀有一种近乎信心的东西……一种建立在某种强烈却几乎无从察觉的本能之上的信心：两人都把性命押在了这场搏斗的结果上。双方正向对方发起凶猛的攻势，然而在这最后的决胜时刻，彼此都必须留有余地。

“要是能睡着就好了！”文克心里涌起一阵渴望。

他凑近端详韦尔特曼的眼睛，把他五官的每一处都收进眼底。那是一副强健多力的身板，文克在想象中剥去那张脸上的假髭、假眉和假发，看见底下马布斯博士那颗剃得精光、头形端正的头颅。此刻无论他披着怎样的伪装，文克都能把他认出来。他平静地凝视着他，对方的目光闪动了。那双灰色的大眼睛仿佛正缩回它们自身火热的深处。

===Original===

For a time the performer paid no attention to Wenk. He concentrated on those who were advancing. No sooner had one of them set foot upon the stage than he unexpectedly turned right round again and hurried back into the hall. One after another did this; a dozen, and even more. Those below were laughing heartily, and the little hall reechoed with their merriment. More and more pressed forward, but the effect was the same on all. With one hand Wenk grasped the wrist of the other, anxious to see whether he retained consciousness of nerves and muscles. He meant to resist. The welling-up of generous and magnanimous feelings had rapidly cooled. He hated, menaced and execrated his enemy now, and prepared himself for the final conflict. His eager blood was inflamed against his foe, and he watched him warily, while still upon his defence. Somewhere in his being a stringed instrument like a guitar seemed to be playing a melody, and he began to listen to this mysterious music. It was so tender and yet so distant, but then he fell back again upon his position of guard and defence. Suddenly a strange idea occurred to him. How would it be if he too did like the rest, and ran as if impelled down there along the gangway past the chairs﻿—that gangway of safety and escape﻿—to where the big door stood encouragingly open﻿ ﻿… to escape and at the same time to do his duty﻿ ﻿… to go to the nearest telephone and summon the police﻿ ﻿… to carry out a daring trick﻿ ﻿… and then do like the others, return, still in the same dreamlike state﻿ ﻿… return to the hall and wait﻿—wait for the police, the rescuers?﻿ ﻿… It would be a daring trick!

===Chinese===

有一阵子，这位表演者根本不朝文克看。他全神贯注于那些走上前来的人。其中一个刚一脚踏上台，就出人意料地猛地转身，又匆匆跑回厅里去了。一个接一个都是这样；足有一打，甚至更多。台下的人开怀大笑，小厅里回荡着他们的欢腾。人越围越多，可在每个人身上的效果一模一样。文克用一只手扣住另一只手的腕子，急于弄清自己神经与肌肉的知觉是否还在。他决意抵抗。方才涌起的那些宽厚豪迈的情愫已迅速冷却。此刻他恨他的敌人、威吓他的敌人、诅咒他的敌人，为最后的决战作好准备。他滚烫的热血烧向仇敌，他警戒地盯着他，同时严守着自己的防线。在他身体的某个深处，仿佛有一件吉他似的弦乐器正奏着一支旋律，他开始侧耳倾听这神秘的音乐。它那么温柔，又那么遥远，可转瞬他又退回到守卫防御的姿态上。突然，一个奇怪的念头攫住了他：要是他也像其余人那样，身不由己地顺着那条通道跑下去，跑过一排排座椅——那条通向安全与逃脱的通道——跑向那扇令人鼓舞地敞开着的大门……逃出去，同时又尽了职责……跑到最近的电话机前把警察召来……耍一出大胆的花招……然后像其他人一样，仍带着那副如梦似幻的神情返回……回到厅里，等待——等待警察，等待救援者？……那可是一出大胆的花招！

===Original===

Already some of the muscles in his legs were twitching.﻿ ﻿… Then Weltmann called out harshly to Prewitz, “Why don’t you pay attention? Cock your revolver! Don’t you see that this criminal is trying to escape?” He pointed at Wenk, and Prewitz cocked the revolver with a dreamy nonchalance, an indifference that excited horror and dread. He raised the revolver to Wenk’s face, and Wenk saw in its little orifice the dark hell of danger before him, for he knew that the weapon was loaded. “The first step that he takes, without my orders, you will shoot,” said Weltmann with an ambiguous smile.

In this dread moment Wenk heard once again the clear sweet musical tones, now in another direction. They sounded soft, sad, and familiar, as if it might have been his father whistling a lullaby beside his cradle. He listened, and in the few heartbeats in which he was lapped in the sound of those wonderful tones, he lost the sense of reality he had had when he felt with one hand for the pulse of the other. The flute became the magic flute, and round this fantasy there rose up an enchanted garden. A high, thick hedge circumscribed the area of his uneasy wanderings, but there was a gap in the hedge, a wide, unguarded gap, and in it he perceived the free light of heaven beckoning and enticing him to tear away and escape.

And then he ran, defying the Baron’s revolver. He sprang with leaps and bounds across the stage﻿ ﻿… the weapon dropping from the Baron’s hands.﻿ ﻿… He sprang down its steps at one bound, rushed along the gangway, leaping like a young colt that feels the approach of summer. The entire hall was animated over this crowning stroke of the hypnotist, but Mabuse sent after him a ferocious laugh that resounded from the walls which had witnessed it.

===Chinese===

他腿上的肌肉已经有一些在抽动了。……这时韦尔特曼厉声呵斥普雷维茨：“你怎么不留神？扳开你手枪的击锤！你没看见这个罪犯想逃跑吗？”他指着文克，普雷维茨便带着梦游般的漫不经心扳开了击锤，那份漠然令人毛骨悚然、心生恐惧。他把左轮手枪举到文克的脸前，文克从那小小的枪眼里看见了自己面前黑暗地狱般的危险，因为他知道枪里上着子弹。“他若不经我吩咐迈出第一步，你就开枪。”韦尔特曼带着莫测的微笑说。

就在这惊恐的一刻，文克又一次听见了那清越甜美的乐音，如今换了方向。它轻柔、哀伤而熟稔，仿佛是他的父亲在摇篮边吹着摇篮曲。他侧耳听着，在被那奇妙乐音包裹的几次心跳之间，他失去了方才用一只手去探另一只手脉搏时的那点现实感。笛声化作了魔笛，环绕着这支曲子，升起了一座魔法花园。一道又高又密的树篱圈住了他惴惴不安的漫游之地，可树篱上有一道豁口，一道宽阔而无人把守的豁口，豁口里他望见自由的天光，正向他招手，诱他挣脱出去、逃之夭夭。

于是他跑了起来，全然不顾男爵的左轮手枪。他连蹦带跳地窜过舞台……手枪从男爵手里跌落。……他一个箭步跃下台阶，沿着通道狂奔，像一头感到夏日将临的小马驹般欢腾跳跃。整个厅堂为催眠师这压轴的绝技群情鼎沸，而马布斯在他身后送出一阵狞笑，笑声撞在那目睹了这一切的四壁上，久久回荡。

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===

Wenk ran full tilt past the servants at the door, who stood with serious faces, laughing behind their hands. He ran through the hall and the open door on to the steps, clattered down them, and flung open the door of the waiting car. It sprang forward, and in a few moments had disappeared in the dark avenue. In the hall Mabuse stopped laughing to say, “He is going to the Hell Café to fetch you some of the devil’s white bread!”

The jerk with which the car started threw Wenk back on to the seat, but scarcely had he touched it than the cushions seemed to open and he sank quickly into a hole in them. Something closed together over him, and it creaked like iron. Then he awoke from his hypnotic state. He lay there in misery, unconscious how he had got into that position, with his head hanging back, apparently in some gap in the back seat. He tried to rise, seeking painfully for ease and consciousness, but he could not raise himself from the depths. Something seemed to press him down again, and hard unyielding fetters were crossed over him many times. The motor went at furious speed, and shook him against an iron grating which he soon discovered to be the fetters that made an upright position impossible. They pressed closely down upon him. He made a furious effort to throw them off, but soon found it was quite impossible. He would only have his trouble for his pains. He was absolutely done for. He was himself the bird which had stepped on to the limed twig!

With angry defiance he turned upon himself saying, “That is as it should be! The stronger one conquers, and you were the weaker!” But why was he the weaker? Because he had undertaken a task that from the very first exceeded his powers. Each one knows his own capabilities. But what had tempted him to undertake something beyond him? Why, in the most forlorn and miserable situation of his whole life﻿—a situation that seemed so incredible that he still had a faint hope it might prove only a dream﻿—why was he able to guide and reason out his thoughts like the solution of an arithmetical problem? What was it that had enticed him? He knew the answer. It was the good in him, the outcome of his feeling of responsibility towards his fellow-countrymen. He wanted to help them, and because his conscience was stronger than his powers, he had come to grief. If this experience were to end in his death, at least he would die in a good cause, and the soul-sparks which at his death would flame up again in some other existence would form a beacon to light others upward.﻿ ﻿… He would live again in spirit among men.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

文克从门口的仆人们身边疾奔而过，那些人板着面孔站在那里，却把手掩在嘴后窃笑。他跑过大厅，出了敞开的大门奔下台阶，鞋声橐橐，一把拉开候命汽车的车门。汽车猛地蹿出，转眼便消失在幽暗的林荫道里。大厅里，马布斯收住笑声，说道：“他这是上地狱咖啡馆去，给你们取些魔鬼的白面包来！”

汽车起步的冲力把文克向后甩到座位上，可他刚一沾座，座垫仿佛豁然张开，他迅速陷进垫子里的一个洞中。有什么东西在他头顶合拢，发出铁器般的轧轧声。他这才从催眠状态中醒转。他痛苦地躺在那里，不知道自己怎么落到了这步田地，脑袋向后仰着，看样子是卡在后座的一道缝隙里。他挣扎着想撑起来，痛苦地寻回舒缓与清醒，却怎么也无法从深处脱身。有什么东西似乎又把他压了回去，一副坚硬无情的镣铐在他身上纵横交错地缠了一道又一道。马达狂吼着疾驰，把他颠得撞在一面铁栅上，他很快发现那正是叫人无法直起身来的镣铐。它们紧紧压在他身上。他狂怒地奋力想甩脱，旋即发觉全然无望，只会白白徒劳。他是彻底完了。他自己就是那只踏上了黏鸟枝的鸟！

他怀着愤怒的倔强转而质问自己：“理当如此！强者胜，而你本是弱者！”可他为什么是弱者？因为他揽下了一桩从一开始就超出他力量的任务。各人都有自己的分寸。可是什么诱使他去承担力所不及之事？为什么，在他一生中最凄惶、最悲惨的境地——那境地离奇得让他至今还存着一丝侥幸，指望它不过是一场梦——他仍能像解一道算术题那样，条理分明地引导、推究自己的思绪？是什么引诱了他？他知道答案。是他心中的善，是他对自己同胞那份责任感的结晶。他想救助他们，而因为他的良知强过了他的力量，他遭了殃。倘若这番经历以死亡告终，至少他是为一件正当的事业而死，他死时迸起的灵魂火花将在另一种存在里重新燃亮，化作一座灯塔，照亮后人向上攀登。……他将以精神的形式活在人们中间。……

===Original===

The sound of the motor echoed through the forest, and Wenk heard it. What was the enemy’s plan regarding him? The car raced on through the night like a ship driven by the typhoon. Where was it going? Whither were they taking him? Was it to Munich? But, if so, why? If they wanted to put him to death for having disturbed the powers of evil and undermined their efforts, why did they not take their revenge at once, instead of delaying it for hours?

He noticed that the windows of the car had no blinds, and he saw stars gleaming fitfully through the panes. They would not arrive in Munich till the morning, and it would be impossible to drive a fettered man by daylight over half Germany in a car with the inside exposed. They were carrying him off somewhere or other, but where? Where could it be?

It must have been midnight when he left the villa, but even that he did not know for certain, for of all that had happened to him since the moment when he had tested his pulse, he had only a dim and hazy idea. They must certainly be taking him to the place of execution now.

===Chinese===

马达声在林间回荡，文克听见了。敌人对他打的是什么主意？汽车在黑夜里飞驰，如同一艘被台风驱赶的船。它开向哪里？他们要把他带到何处去？是去慕尼黑吗？可若是那样，又是为什么？倘若他们要处死他，以惩罚他搅扰了邪恶势力、破坏了他们的勾当，为什么不当即复仇，却要拖延上几个钟头？

他注意到车窗上没有窗帘，看见星星透过玻璃忽明忽暗地闪烁。他们要到早晨才能抵达慕尼黑，而大白天用一辆里面一览无余的车，载着一个戴镣铐的人横穿半个德国，那是办不到的。他们正把他劫往某个地方，可是哪里？又能是哪里呢？

他离开别墅时想必已是午夜，可就连这一点他也不敢肯定，因为自他试脉的那一刻起所遭遇的一切，在他脑中只剩一片模糊混沌的印象。现在，他们必定正把他押往刑场。

===Original===

He recalled, with an endless yearning which seemed to encompass him like the sea, his long-dead father, and with all his energy he clung to these recollections, melancholy as their associations were. The jolting hither and thither of his body in the car and the mental excitement under which he was labouring made him sick, and in his helpless state he could not even turn his head aside. His brain lost the power of thinking in clear outlines. Spectres arose around him and devils played ball with him. They tossed him backwards and forwards between the Carse of Gowrie and Aconcagua, let him fall, and snatched at him again, just as he was about to be dashed to pieces on the Cape of Good Hope.

Then it seemed as if a gigantic black band had stuffed him down in a cave as if he were a sack. The walls of this cave were so close that he could not lie down, but suddenly, slowly, and yet without ceasing, they began to grow. They did not grow apart, however, but proceeded, always at the same pace, towards him, and the moment was already close at hand when they would crush his bones together and burst his brains. Consciousness forsook him, and he fell into a dreamlike condition, dominated by a dull sense of impending death.

When he awoke he found himself stretched out on a leather seat, the iron fetters no longer binding him. But his arms were tied behind him, and his legs were crossed on each other and fastened together. A large handkerchief had been bound over his face, so tightly as to be painful. It covered his mouth altogether and made breathing difficult.

===Chinese===

他以一种如大海般将他环抱的无尽眷恋，回想起自己亡故多年的父亲，竭尽全力抓住这些回忆不放，尽管它们牵惹的都是忧伤。车身把他颠得东摇西晃，加上他所承受的精神激荡，令他阵阵作呕，而在那无助的境地里，他连把头偏到一边都办不到。他的大脑失去了轮廓清晰的思考能力。鬼影在他四周升起，群魔拿他当球耍。他们把他扔来扔去，一会儿扔到戈里低地，一会儿扔到阿空加瓜峰，让他坠落，又一把将他攫住——就在他即将摔碎在好望角上的时候。

随后，仿佛有一条巨大的黑带把他当作一只口袋，塞进了一个洞穴。洞壁四面逼近，他连躺都躺不下，可忽然间，那洞壁又缓缓地、却一刻不停地开始生长。它们不是向四外扩展，而是始终迈着同样的步子朝他逼来，把他挤得骨断脑裂的那一刻已近在眼前。意识离他而去，他坠入一种梦魇般的状态，其中弥漫着一股死期将至的钝重预感。

待他醒来，他发现自己直挺挺躺在一张皮座上，铁镣已不再缚他。但他的双臂被反绑在背后，两腿交叠捆在一处。一块大手帕紧紧蒙在他脸上，勒得生疼。手帕把他的嘴整个捂住，令他呼吸困难。

===Original===

It was now day, and he heard a rushing sound that rose and fell at intervals. He soon recognized it﻿—it was the sea! A man looked down upon him. The handkerchief covered one eye only, and with the other he saw, over the edge of the bandage, half the objects on his eye-level. He did not know the man, who just then called to another, “Come here! he is awake.” Then the other came to look at him, and he too was a complete stranger to Wenk. He heard them talking, and one said to the other, “It is nearly five o’clock. The Doctor must be here soon!”

The other answered, “If he said soon after five, he will come then. We must be ready for him!”

“Can’t you see anything yet?”

The two men went off. Wenk tried to raise his head, but could not see beyond the frame of the window. The country must be flat﻿—there was nothing but sky discernible.

“Give me the glass! There he is!” Wenk heard suddenly.

“Now comes the decisive moment,” he thought, and summoned all his powers to help him dispel the dread ideas which crowded upon him.

===Chinese===

天已大亮，他听见一阵涛声时起时伏。他很快听出来了——那是海！一个男人低头打量他。手帕只蒙住了一只眼睛，他用另一只眼睛越过蒙布的边缘，看见与他视线齐平的半边景物。他不认识那人，那人正朝另一个喊：“过来！他醒了。”于是另一人走过来看他，这人对文克同样是个十足的生面孔。他听见他们交谈，一个对另一个说：“快五点了。博士想必就快到了！”

另一个答道：“他说的是五点刚过，那他就准来。咱们得给他备好！”

“你还什么都没看见吗？”

那两人走开了。文克试着抬起头，可视线越不过车窗框。这一带想必是平地——望得见的除了天什么都没有。

“望远镜给我！他在那儿！”文克猛然听见。

“决定性的时刻到了。”他想，于是唤起全部心力，帮自己驱散那些蜂拥而至的可怖念头。

===Original===

The events that followed occurred in rapid succession. The door of the car was flung open, and hands gripped him by the shoulders, which lay nearest the door. They dragged him out, his feet striking painfully on the step and then on the ground. The second man took his legs, and they carried him a short distance. Then Wenk saw sand-dunes in front of him, and a few steps further the men had climbed with him to the top.

“Faster!” cried the man behind, as he turned round and looked back over the landscape.

Wenk heard a motorcar, and said to himself, “That is Mabuse coming!” Suddenly a light awning appeared above him, and after a time he recognized it for the wing of an aeroplane.

The two men arranged everything with hasty movements. Wenk was laid on the sand, and two cords tied together made a noose under his chest and arms. One man raised his legs and these were fastened by two cords which had been attached somewhere to a pole rather high up. A third leash was then slung round his hips. It was not long before Wenk realized that he was hanging bound to the outer wall of the car of a flying-machine. He lay closely fastened there like a package that was to be taken on a journey. With his uncovered right eye he saw beyond the edge of the bandage that the aeroplane stood on a prepared landing-stage over a course which sloped down to the sea. Beyond it stretched the shore. It was ebb-tide.

“I am going to have a sea voyage,” cried a despairing voice within Wenk sadly. “How long it is since my last voyage. All the years of war lie between, and yet now, for me, comes the war﻿—the bombshell is prepared.”

===Chinese===

接下来的一幕接一幕，急遽地发生。车门被猛地拉开，几只手攥住他最靠近车门的双肩。他们把他拖出去，他的脚先是磕在踏板上，随即磕在地上，疼得钻心。第二个人抬起他的双腿，他们抬着他走了一小段。随后文克看见面前出现沙丘，再走几步，两人已抬着他登上了丘顶。

“快点！”后面那人喊道，一边回头张望整片原野。

文克听见一辆汽车驶来，暗自说道：“是马布斯来了！”忽然，一方浅色的翼篷在他头顶上方展开，过了一阵他才认出那是一架飞机的机翼。

那两人手忙脚乱地张罗停当。文克被放倒在沙地上，两条系在一起的绳索在他胸口和手臂下面挽成一个套。一人抬起他的双腿，用两根绳子捆牢——绳子的另一头拴在高处的什么杆子上。第三根束带又兜住他的腰臀。不多时文克便意识到：他正被绳索缚着，吊挂在一架飞行器吊舱的外壁上。他紧紧贴在那里，像一件要随行上路的包裹。他用露在外面的右眼越过蒙布边缘望见，飞机停在一处预先整备好的起飞场上，正对一条向大海倾斜而下的跑道。跑道之外便是海岸。正值退潮。

“我要来一次海上旅行了。”文克心中一个绝望的声音悲戚地喊道。“离我上一次航行有多久了啊。中间隔着整场战争的那些年月，可现在，对我来说，战争来了——炮弹已经备下。”

===Original===

From the depths of his muscular being there came an answer to this sad voice of despair. He exerted his muscles against his bonds. His body moved and wriggled in the nooses, and the wing of the machine quivered beneath the shock, and swayed above him.

Then a broad face and a high, well-formed head bent over him, and two fiery eyes seemed to pierce him through and through.

“Aha!” said the voice of the man who stood above him.

“Yes, there is the foe, there is Mabuse,” thought Wenk.

“Get in!” he heard him say, and there was the rustling of a woman’s dress, and out of the rustling a voice﻿ ﻿… a voice that made his knees tremble in their bonds. He knew that voice! The rustling was louder and closer, and the woman’s voice cried, “What is that?” Wenk heard the horror, trouble and anxiety that spoke in the voice as she put the question.

“Get in!” said Mabuse again. Then the voice, the well-known, low, sweet voice of the Countess Told, said in a tone of anxious entreaty, “What are you doing with this man?”

===Chinese===

从肌肉的深处，升起对这个绝望悲声的回答。他鼓动周身肌肉对抗绳缚。他的身体在绳套里扭动挣揣，机翼被这冲力震得发颤，在他头顶摇晃。

随后，一张宽阔的脸和一颗线条端正的高颅朝他俯下来，两只烈火般的眼睛仿佛要把他前后洞穿。

“啊哈！”站在他上方的那人开口道。

“对，这就是仇敌，这就是马布斯。”文克心想。

“进去！”他听见那人说。接着响起女人衣裙的窸窣声，窸窣声里透出一个嗓音……一个让他被缚的双膝都为之战栗的嗓音。他认得这个声音！窸窣声越来越响、越来越近，那女人的声音喊道：“那是什么？”文克听出了她发问时话音里的惊骇、惶乱与焦灼。

“进去！”马布斯又说。于是那声音——托尔德伯爵夫人那人所熟知的、低柔甜美的嗓音——以一种焦灼恳求的语气说道：“你们要把这个人怎么样？”

===Original===

Wenk said to himself, “She does not know who I am.”

“Get in! He’s going to make the trip with us, and we haven’t a third seat. Come along quickly, now!” cried Mabuse.

Wenk saw Mabuse’s arm seize the woman and lift her into the gondola, then he himself got in, making use of Wenk’s body as a step, and when he was settled in the pilot’s seat, not two fingers’ breadth above Wenk, he bent down to him and said in a harsh tone, “The gentleman is going to accompany us on our journey﻿—but whither? Good luck!﻿—All ready?” he called out to the men.

“All quite ready, sir!”

===Chinese===

文克暗想：“她还不知道我是谁。”

“进去！他要跟我们同行，可我们腾不出第三个座位。快着点，别磨蹭！”马布斯喊道。

文克看见马布斯伸手攫住那女人，把她举进吊舱，随后他自己也跨了进去，踩着文克的身子当踏级。等他在驾驶座上坐定——离文克头顶不过两指宽——便朝他俯下身来，用刺耳的声音说：“这位先生要陪我们走一程——去哪儿呢？祝他好运！”接着他朝那两人喊道：“都备好了吗？”

“全都备好了，先生！”

===Original===

The propeller hummed and the aeroplane glided along the course so swiftly that the very moment Wenk felt the throbbing of the engine its wheels were already clear of the ground and the earth vanished from his sight. The machine soared upwards steeply, and it seemed to Wenk as if his body were standing upright. No word was spoken in the car. The air beat so heavily upon him that it seemed like flying wood, and he soon began to feel bitterly cold. The cold seemed to cut through the wide opening of his evening suit and strike at his very heart. He felt that it pressed ever deeper and deeper within him, like revolving knives. His hair was stiff and stood on end, and it seemed as if needles were pricking him all over. He had lost all capability of thought, save for one idea. It dimly occurred to him that he was enduring martyrdom, and that this martyrdom was on account of the Countess Told, whom he had once loved, at a time when such love was not lawful.

Then he felt the blow of a fist on his head, and a harsh voice asked, “Is twelve thousand feet high enough for you?” A few moments later he heard, “Or are you already dead﻿—of fright?”

The voice died away and Wenk felt that the aeroplane was being righted. When it was flying level, a hand touched his head, hastily tearing away his bonds. Then Wenk saw the face of Mabuse bending over him. He was silent, but his features were distorted with a malicious joy which aroused horror. His grey eyes had neither shape nor pupils; they were like old weather-beaten stones, and, as Wenk recognized with a shudder, they were glowering death at him. Then the capacious mouth opened like the yawning chasm in a rocky gorge, and the harsh voice said, “You have dared to oppose your will against mine. You are now facing your last moment, and I have taken the gag from your mouth so that my ears may enjoy the shriek with which you fall twelve thousand feet down to your own world!”

===Chinese===

螺旋桨嗡嗡作响，飞机沿着跑道疾滑，快得文克刚觉出引擎的震颤，机轮已离了地，大地从他眼前消失了。机体陡直地向高空窜升，文克只觉自己的身体仿佛直立了起来。机舱里没人说一句话。气流沉沉拍打在他身上，仿佛一片片飞掠的木板，他很快便觉出刺骨的严寒。寒气仿佛透过晚礼服的宽大领口直切进来，直捣他的心口。他感到那寒气在他体内越钻越深，像一柄柄旋转的尖刀。他的头发僵直倒竖，仿佛周身有万千钢针在扎。他已失去一切思维能力，只剩下一个念头。他恍惚意识到自己正在受难，而这受难是为了托尔德伯爵夫人——他曾经爱过她，在那样一种爱尚属非法的年月。

随即他感到一拳砸在头上，一个刺耳的声音问：“一万二千英尺，对你来说够高了吗？”片刻之后他又听见：“还是你已经死了——吓死的？”

那声音远去，文克觉出飞机正在改平。等它飞得平稳了，一只手碰到他的头，急急扯掉他脸上的蒙布。随后文克看见马布斯的脸俯在他上方。他一言不发，可五官因一种令人胆寒的恶毒快意而扭曲着。他灰色的眼睛既无形状也没有瞳仁；它们像两块久经风蚀的顽石，而文克战栗地认出，它们正冲他瞪视着死亡。随即那张阔嘴张开，像岩壑间豁然洞开的深渊，刺耳的声音说道：“你竟敢把你的意志同我的意志相对抗。你现在已面临最后的时刻，我摘掉了你嘴上的堵口布，好让我的耳朵享用那声尖叫——听你惨嚎着一万二千英尺直坠而下，跌回你自己的世界去！”

===Original===

Wenk heard his voice, and it sounded like thunder rolling along after the lightning flash. He saw that Mabuse was loosening the bonds that held his legs. He tugged and tore at them. Suddenly his legs were free. For a moment they fell, then the leash that was bound round his hips held them again, and the hands were now busy with this. In a few seconds it was untied.

In his further fall Wenk’s body regained an upright position, held only by the noose which bound his chest to the wall of the car. He suddenly felt that his hands were free, and at this feeling he was fired with a sudden hope. In the midst of his fantasies there surged upwards like a fairy story the recollection of the Countess’s beauty and sympathy. He had never forgotten her, and now in the last moment of his life, when she herself was so close to him, his feeling for her, exalted to an undying and compassionate brotherhood, was wafted as a cloud beyond the savage and brutal murderer, to envelop the frail human being beside him with indomitable pride and courage.

Wenk saw her eyes, fluttering like birds shot down in the clear blue ether, glance for a moment beyond and above Mabuse’s eager bent head.﻿ ﻿… He saw her hands, tearing off their fur gloves, cling white and trembling to Mabuse’s shoulder as she strove to drag him back from his deadly intent.

===Chinese===

文克听见他的声音，那声音像闪电过后滚滚而来的雷鸣。他看见马布斯正在解开缚住他双腿的绳索。他又扯又拽。刹那间他的双腿自由了，向下坠了一坠，随即兜住他腰臀的束带又把它们吊住，那双手此刻正来对付这条带子。不过几秒钟，它也被解开了。

再往下坠时，文克的身子又立了起来，全靠那只把他胸口拴在舱壁上的绳套吊着。他忽然发觉双手自由了，这一感觉令他心中蓦地燃起希望。在纷乱的幻象中间，伯爵夫人的美丽与慈悲像一则童话，从记忆深处涌了上来。他从未忘怀过她，而现在，在他生命的最后一刻，当她本人近在咫尺，他对她的情感——已然升华为一种不朽而悲悯的兄妹之情——化作一片云，飘过那个凶残暴戾的凶手，去庇护他身旁那个柔弱的人，带着不屈的骄傲与勇气。

文克看见她的眼睛，像明净碧空中中弹的鸟儿般扑闪着，有一瞬越过马布斯那颗热切俯就的头，朝上朝后望来。……他看见她的双手扯下毛皮手套，白皙而颤抖地攀住马布斯的肩头，竭力想把他从那致命的意图里拽回来。

===Original===

But Mabuse shook the woman off, and raised his hands with mad rage to untie the last noose. He tore undone the first of its fastenings, making Wenk’s body sink deeper, and beat away Wenk’s hands, which were seeking to maintain a grip on the edge of the car, with his closed fists.

Then one last defiance of fate, arising from the will to live, lent strength to Wenk’s voice as he shouted in the air, “He is the murderer of Count Told. He made him cheat at cards! He put the razor into his hands that he might cut his throat!”

A fist struck at his mouth, and blood spurted from it, yet at this last moment of his life it seemed as if his very blood were tasting the sweetness of a noble spirit. Then a final effort was made to release him from the bond that held him. A fearful weight pressed on his head, rolled over his body to press him downward. The weight of it was immeasurable, black, imbued with the swiftness of a raging storm. But all at once the iron weight was removed. A part of it became detached from the aeroplane, unrecognizable, and sank. Wenk’s hands held the edge of the car as in a vice. The aeroplane hovered and swayed as if drunken with the high clear air.

===Chinese===

可马布斯把那女人甩开，狂怒地举起双手去解最后那个绳套。他扯开了第一道扣结，文克的身子随之向下沉去；他又抡起握拢的双拳，把文克正想抓住舱沿的双手一一打开。

于是一股源自求生意志的、对命运的最后抗争，给文克的嗓音注入了力量，他朝着天空高喊：“他就是杀害托尔德伯爵的凶手。是他逼他在牌局上作弊！是他把剃刀塞进他手里，让他割断了自己的喉咙！”

一拳砸在他嘴上，血涌了出来，然而在他生命的这最后时刻，他仿佛觉得连自己的血都尝到了一种高贵灵魂的甘甜。随后，为了把他从最后那道束缚中解脱出来，又作了一番最后的努力。一个骇人的重量压上他的头顶，滚过他的身体，把他往下压去。那重量无边无际、黑沉沉的，挟着狂飙般的迅疾。可突然间，那铁一般的重压被卸掉了。它的一部分从飞机上脱落下来，辨不出形状，向下坠去。文克的双手像老虎钳一样死死抠住舱沿。飞机在高阔澄澈的空气里飘悬摇晃，仿佛醉了一般。

---

===Original===

This is what had happened:

When Count Told’s name rang through the air, as if thrown from measureless space, it seemed to the Countess as if she were awaking from a dream at the bottom of a swamp. Since the night when she had been torn from her husband and chained to Mabuse’s wicked will, she had never spoken his name, nor even thought of it. The memory had crept into her inmost being and hidden itself away, deep in the welter in which her life was inextricably bound. It had been forced there by the diabolic power of Mabuse’s lust for domination, and the wife had suffered it in a kind of subconscious self-defence. Were it not so, she would have been absolutely and entirely without escape from the werwolf.

There within her the name had lain and waited and watched until now it arose again to provide her with a way of escape.

===Chinese===

事情的经过是这样的：

当托尔德伯爵的名字响彻空际，仿佛从无边无际的远处抛来时，伯爵夫人只觉得自己像从沼泽底的一场梦里醒了过来。自那夜被人从丈夫身边夺走、锁进马布斯的邪恶意志之日起，她从未再说过那个名字，甚至没有想过它。那段记忆潜入了她生命的最深处，把自己藏了起来，藏在她的生命纠纠缠缠、无法脱解的那团乱麻之中。是马布斯那统治欲的魔性力量把它强压到那里去的，而做妻子的以一种潜意识的自卫默默承受了它。若非如此，她就完完全全、彻头彻尾地无从逃离那个狼人了。

那名字就在她体内潜藏、等待、守望着，直到此刻重新跃起，为她指出一条逃生之路。

===Original===

Wenk’s last words had brought it forth from the subconscious recesses once more. The Countess had received it as a direct weapon against the secret power of this man who had so long taken forcible possession of her will and her entire person. She suddenly came to herself, and all that was frozen within her melted. The gloom and darkness in which she lay bound grew lighter, and it was day within.

Then, too, she regained all the proud youthful force of her disposition. She fell into a God-given fury, and her muscles were endowed with unconquerable strength and vigour. Her hands and her heart were like iron, and she seized the first weapon to hand, the heavy screw-wrench, striking the murderer from behind, and dealing a terrific blow upon his skull.

Mabuse, judged and condemned, lost his balance, and fell over Wenk into the depths below, which instantly swallowed him up.

===Chinese===

文克最后的喊声又一次把它从潜意识的深处唤了出来。伯爵夫人把它接在手里，当作一件直刺要害的武器，去对付这个人的隐秘力量——这个人长久以来强占着她的意志和她整个的人。她猛然回过神来，体内一切冻结的东西都消融了。缚住她的那团幽暗与昏黑渐渐亮起来，她心里已是白昼。

与此同时，她也寻回了天性中全部骄傲的青春力量。她陷入一阵天赐的狂怒，她的肌肉被赋予了不可战胜的膂力与锐气。她的双手和她的心都成了铁，她抓起手边第一件武器——那把沉重的扳手，从背后朝那杀人犯抡去，狠狠砸在他的颅骨上。

马布斯——已受审判、已定罪——失了平衡，从文克身上方一头栽进下面的深渊，深渊顿时把他吞没了。

---

===Original===

Wenk reached a thwart with his legs, raised himself up at lightning speed, the knots at his breast breaking of themselves. He fell into the car. The aeroplane was already swaying in space, but Wenk seized the throttle and righted it. It flew on, and after he had found his whereabouts he shut off the engines and allowed it to descend to earth and glide along the shore.

He landed on the sand-dunes of the East Frisian coast. He helped the Countess out of the machine. She was pale, but fully conscious. She fell down before him, pressing her hands to her face.

He raised her, saying, “We have saved each other’s lives. Let us keep silent, and strive to forget. We part here!”

But the Countess answered, “No. I have nothing to conceal and nothing to forget. The blood that I have shed was entirely evil. I have saved him from himself and mankind from him. Who can bear witness against me?”

Wenk looked at her, dumb with astonishment, but slowly he understood. Then he was seized with awe. He wanted to say, “How proud, how courageous she is!” but his heart glowed within him. He spread out his arms in a gesture of self-abandonment and appeal. Life, his regained youth and vigour, came over him like a flood, and at the same moment the love which had been shaken by so many vicissitudes, but had never yet found its fulfilment, regained its sway over him.

Then they ascended the dunes together, to seek the nearest village and return to daily life.

===Chinese===

文克的双脚够到了舱内的座板，他以闪电般的速度撑起身，胸前的绳结自行绷断。他跌进舱里。飞机已在空中摇晃，文克一把抓住节流阀，将它稳住。飞机继续飞行，等辨明了方位，他便关掉发动机，任它向地面降落，沿海岸滑行。

他在东弗里西亚海岸的沙丘上着陆。他把伯爵夫人扶下飞机。她脸色苍白，但神志完全清醒。她跪倒在他面前，双手掩住脸。

他把她扶起来，说：“我们救了彼此的性命。让我们保持沉默，尽力忘却。就在此地分手吧！”

可伯爵夫人答道：“不。我无可隐瞒，也无可遗忘。我溅下的那摊血，是彻头彻尾的邪恶。我把他从他自己手中救了出来，也把人类从他手中救了出来。谁能指证我？”

文克望着她，惊得说不出话，但他慢慢地懂了。随即一种敬畏攫住了他。他想说：“她多么骄傲，多么勇敢啊！”可他的心在胸中燃烧。他张开双臂，做出一个听天由命又有所恳求的姿势。生命，连同他失而复得的青春与活力，如洪水一般漫过他全身；同一刻，那场历经无数波折却始终未曾圆满的爱情，也重新主宰了他。

随后，两人一同登上沙丘，去寻最近的村落，回到日常生活里去。
